### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个朋友 是 一个男孩、叫 Liang，（那个 就住在离我外公外婆家 隔壁没几户的），在我老家那会儿。 | So, the friend I'd like to talk about is a boy called Liang, who lived just a couple of doors down from my grandparents' place back in my hometown. |
| [how met] | 我 在一个小城市长大，大部分是 外公外婆 带大的，而 Liang 和我 最后 分到了 同一个班、在小学。 | I grew up in a small city, raised mostly by my grandparents, and Liang and I ended up in the same class in primary school. |
| [how met·细] | 我们 第一次 搭上话 是 在一个下午 走路回家的路上，然后 那之后 我们 好几年 基本上 形影不离。 | We first got chatting on the walk home one afternoon, and after that we were pretty much inseparable for years. |
| [did together] | 我们一起做的事 没什么花哨的——我们会 放学后 骑车四处逛、在附近一条小溪里 抓蝌蚪、还有 换漫画书 换上好几个小时。 | What we did together was nothing fancy — we'd cycle around after school, catch tadpoles in a little stream nearby, and swap comic books for hours. |
| [did·细] | 下雨天，我们就 窝在他家 打牌、直到天黑。 | On rainy days, we'd just hole up at his place playing cards until dark. |
| [why/EL] | 我觉得 让我这么喜欢他的东西 是 他有多随和。 | I think what made me like him so much was how easygoing he was. |
| [why·细] | 他 从不 闹脾气、也不 争强好胜，即使 他输了一局。 | He never got moody or competitive, even when he lost a game. |
| [why·细] | 除此之外，他 总是 罩着我，每当 我 惹上麻烦的时候。 | On top of that, he always had my back whenever I got into trouble. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那就是 Liang——他是那种人之一、（我 会永远 带着微笑 回想起的）。 | So yeah, that's Liang — he's one of those people I'll always look back on with a smile. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the friend I'd like to talk about is a boy called Liang, who lived just a couple of doors down from my grandparents' place back in my hometown. I grew up in a small city, raised mostly by my grandparents, and Liang and I ended up in the same class in primary school. We first got chatting on the walk home one afternoon, and after that we were pretty much inseparable for years. What we did together was nothing fancy — we'd cycle around after school, catch tadpoles in a little stream nearby, and swap comic books for hours. On rainy days, we'd just hole up at his place playing cards until dark. I think what made me like him so much was how easygoing he was. He never got moody or competitive, even when he lost a game. On top of that, he always had my back whenever I got into trouble. So yeah, that's Liang — he's one of those people I'll always look back on with a smile.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（朋友）**：我想聊的那个朋友是[X]、（那个…的）→ `the friend I'd like to talk about is [X], who …`
- **分到同一班**：最后分到同一个班 → `ended up in the same class`
- **形影不离**：基本上形影不离 → `pretty much inseparable`
- **what-裂句 ×2**：我们一起做的没什么花哨 → `What we did together was nothing fancy`；让我喜欢他的是他多随和 → `what made me like him was how easygoing he was`
- **罩着我**：他总罩着我，每当我惹麻烦 → `he always had my back whenever I got into trouble`
- **收尾**：那种我会带着微笑回想的人 → `one of those people I'll always look back on with a smile`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Do you still keep in touch with your friends from childhood? Why or why not?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，只有那么一两个。 | Honestly, only a couple of them. |
| 2 | 大多数人 慢慢就疏远了，一旦他们 为了大学或工作 搬走，而那 挺自然的。 | Most people drift apart once they move away for university or work, and that's pretty natural. |
| 3 | 那些我一直保持亲近的 是那种、（你能 直接 接着 上的、在几个月的沉默之后）。 | The ones I've stayed close to are the kind you can pick straight back up with after months of silence. |
| 4 | 但对剩下的那些，我们 就 偶尔 给个帖子点个赞，然后 也就那样了。 | But with the rest, we'll just like a post now and then, and that's about it really. |

> Honestly, only a couple of them. Most people drift apart once they move away for university or work, and that's pretty natural. The ones I've stayed close to are the kind you can pick straight back up with after months of silence. But with the rest, we'll just like a post now and then, and that's about it really.

**Q2. How important is childhood friendship to children?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 它 极其重要。 | I'd say it's hugely important. |
| 2 | 童年朋友 常常 是 孩子第一次 学会 分享、争吵、然后 再和好 的地方。 | Childhood friends are often where kids first learn to share, argue, and make up again. |
| 3 | 因为 他们的父母 不总是 在旁边，那些友谊 教会 他们 怎么 自己 应对人。 | Since their parents aren't always around, those friendships teach them how to handle people on their own. |
| 4 | 比如，摆平 一场 关于一个玩具的 小争执 是 一堂 关于妥协的 小课、（那种会伴随他们的）。 | For instance, sorting out a squabble over a toy is a tiny lesson in compromise that sticks with them. |

> I'd say it's hugely important. Childhood friends are often where kids first learn to share, argue, and make up again. Since their parents aren't always around, those friendships teach them how to handle people on their own. For instance, sorting out a squabble over a toy is a tiny lesson in compromise that sticks with them.

**Q3. What do you think of communicating via social media?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说句公道话，我 对它 挺矛盾的。 | I'm pretty mixed on it, to be fair. |
| 2 | 一方面，它 特别棒、[用来]跟人 保持联系、（那些你本来会失去的），尤其是 国外的朋友。 | On one hand, it's brilliant for staying in touch with folk you'd otherwise lose, especially friends abroad. |
| 3 | 但另一方面，人们 无止境地 刷，把自己的生活 跟 别人的高光集锦 比。 | But on the other, people scroll endlessly and compare their lives to everyone else's highlight reel. |
| 4 | 所以我觉得 它是个好工具，只要 你 别让它 吞掉 你一整天。 | So I reckon it's a great tool as long as you don't let it swallow your whole day. |

> I'm pretty mixed on it, to be fair. On one hand, it's brilliant for staying in touch with folk you'd otherwise lose, especially friends abroad. But on the other, people scroll endlessly and compare their lives to everyone else's highlight reel. So I reckon it's a great tool as long as you don't let it swallow your whole day.

**Q4. Do you think online communication through social media will replace face-to-face communication?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我真的 不这么觉得。 | I really don't think so. |
| 2 | 社交媒体 对快速的聊天 很方便，但它 比不上 坐下来 和某人 一起喝杯咖啡。 | Social media's handy for quick chats, but it can't match sitting down with someone over coffee. |
| 3 | 你 会错过 所有的肢体语言 和那些小玩笑、（那些 构成 一段真正联结的）。 | You miss all the body language and little jokes that make a real bond. |
| 4 | 说实话，在网上 忙了长长的一周 之后，大多数人 还是 渴望 真正地 见面。 | Honestly, after a long week online, most people still crave actually meeting up. |
| 5 | 所以我会说，它 会 补充 面对面，而不是 取代它。 | So I'd say it'll complement face-to-face, not replace it. |

> I really don't think so. Social media's handy for quick chats, but it can't match sitting down with someone over coffee. You miss all the body language and little jokes that make a real bond. Honestly, after a long week online, most people still crave actually meeting up. So I'd say it'll complement face-to-face, not replace it.

**Q5. What's the difference between having younger friends and older friends?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它们 挺不一样的，我觉得。 | They're quite different, I reckon. |
| 2 | 跟 小一点的朋友，你 往往 感觉 有点像 一个导师，而 他们的能量 让你 保持警觉。 | With younger friends, you tend to feel a bit like a mentor, and their energy keeps you on your toes. |
| 3 | 大一点的朋友，另一方面，带来 经验 和 更冷静的建议，当你 卡住的时候。 | Older friends, on the other hand, bring experience and calmer advice when you're stuck. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，最好的部分 是 你 从两边 都 学到 一些 真正有用的东西。 | To be fair, the best part is you learn something genuinely useful from both sides. |

> They're quite different, I reckon. With younger friends, you tend to feel a bit like a mentor, and their energy keeps you on your toes. Older friends, on the other hand, bring experience and calmer advice when you're stuck. To be fair, the best part is you learn something genuinely useful from both sides.

**Q6. Has technology changed people's friendships? How?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 变化巨大，是的。 | Massively, yeah. |
| 2 | 我们 以前 得 等 好几天 才等来 一封信，但现在 你 能 给一个哥们儿发消息、（在半个地球之外），几秒钟就到。 | We used to wait days for a letter, but now you can message a mate halfway across the world in seconds. |
| 3 | 那 特别棒、[用来]让 联结 跨越距离 活着。 | That's brilliant for keeping bonds alive over distance. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，它 也 让 友谊 感觉 浅了一点，因为 给一张照片点个赞 如今 不知怎么 就算 保持联系了。 | Having said that, it's also made friendships feel a bit shallower, since liking a photo somehow counts as staying in touch these days. |

> Massively, yeah. We used to wait days for a letter, but now you can message a mate halfway across the world in seconds. That's brilliant for keeping bonds alive over distance. Having said that, it's also made friendships feel a bit shallower, since liking a photo somehow counts as staying in touch these days.

**P3 句型/模板**：`drift apart once they move away` · `the ones I've stayed close to are the kind you can pick straight back up with`（relative层叠）· `I'm pretty mixed on it`（骑墙）· `folk you'd otherwise lose`（relative后置）· `it can't match sitting down with someone over coffee` · `jokes that make a real bond`（that后置）· `complement …, not replace it`（PREDICT 收）· `keeps you on your toes` · `liking a photo counts as staying in touch`（动名词主语）。

---

## P2-新08 · A person who'd choose a medical career — 表妹 Lin（Person）

> **Cue**: 认识她多久 / 何时开始想 / 想做什么 / 为何选这行 · 题型 Person

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个人 是 我的表妹，Lin。 | So, the person I'd like to talk about is my younger cousin, Lin. |
| [背景] | 她现在 大概 十六岁，还在上高中，而且她 挺铁了心 要 有一天 成为一名医生。 | She's about sixteen now, still in high school, and she's pretty set on becoming a doctor someday. |
| [when knew] | 我 从她还是个小孩子起 就认识她，但 我 真正 更了解她 是 几年前，当时 我们外婆 病得挺重。 | I've known her since she was a little kid, but I really got to know her better a couple of years ago, when our grandmother fell quite ill. |
| [when·细] | 全家 轮流 照顾她，而 Lin 是那一个、（在她床边 待得最久的）。 | The whole family took turns looking after her, and Lin was the one who stayed by her bed the most. |
| [started] | 那 其实 就是 她开始 认真思考 医学 的时候。 | That's actually when she started thinking seriously about medicine. |
| [what do] | 她 告诉我 她想当一名医生，也许 在一家儿童医院工作，因为 她 一直 很会跟孩子相处，而且 她 在压力下 挺冷静。 | She told me she wants to be a doctor, maybe working in a children's hospital, because she's always been good with kids and she's pretty calm under pressure. |
| [why/EL] | 我觉得 她选这条路的主要原因 是 照顾我们外婆 真的 让她开了眼。 | I think the main reason she chose this path is that caring for our grandmother really opened her eyes. |
| [EL·细] | 看着那些护士 给了她 这样一种感觉、（帮人们好起来 是 某种值得的事）。 | Watching the nurses gave her this sense that helping people get better is something worthwhile. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那就是我的表妹 Lin，而说实话，我觉得 她 有一天 会成为 一位 很棒的医生。 | So yeah, that's my cousin Lin, and honestly, I reckon she'll make a wonderful doctor one day. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the person I'd like to talk about is my younger cousin, Lin. She's about sixteen now, still in high school, and she's pretty set on becoming a doctor someday. I've known her since she was a little kid, but I really got to know her better a couple of years ago, when our grandmother fell quite ill. The whole family took turns looking after her, and Lin was the one who stayed by her bed the most. That's actually when she started thinking seriously about medicine. She told me she wants to be a doctor, maybe working in a children's hospital, because she's always been good with kids and she's pretty calm under pressure. I think the main reason she chose this path is that caring for our grandmother really opened her eyes. Watching the nurses gave her this sense that helping people get better is something worthwhile. So yeah, that's my cousin Lin, and honestly, I reckon she'll make a wonderful doctor one day.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **铁了心**：她铁了心要成为[X] → `she's pretty set on becoming [X]`
- **那一个…的**：Lin 是那一个待得最久的 → `Lin was the one who stayed … the most`
- **时间点 bullet**：那就是她开始想[X]的时候 → `That's when she started thinking about [X]`
- **原因**：她选这条路的主因是[Y] → `the main reason she chose this path is that [Y]`
- **动名词主语 + that同位**：看着护士给了她一种感觉、帮人好起来是值得的 → `Watching the nurses gave her this sense that [Y]`
- **将来断言**：她有一天会成为很棒的[X] → `she'll make a wonderful [X] one day`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Do you think being a doctor is easy or difficult?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它 挺难的。 | Honestly, it's pretty difficult. |
| 2 | 医生 肩负 巨大的责任，因为 他们 打交道的是 人的性命，所以 一个小小的错误 可能 真的 很要紧。 | Doctors carry a huge responsibility because they're dealing with people's lives, so one small mistake can really matter. |
| 3 | 此外，工时 很长，而 压力 从没 真正 松过。 | Plus, the hours are long and the pressure never really lets up. |
| 4 | 不过说句公道话，它 也 有回报，因为 你 是 真正地 帮人们 好起来。 | To be fair, though, it's also rewarding, since you're genuinely helping folk get better. |
| 5 | 所以它 辛苦，但值得。 | So it's tough, but worthwhile. |

> Honestly, it's pretty difficult. Doctors carry a huge responsibility because they're dealing with people's lives, so one small mistake can really matter. Plus, the hours are long and the pressure never really lets up. To be fair, though, it's also rewarding, since you're genuinely helping folk get better. So it's tough, but worthwhile.

**Q2. Do you think learning biology is interesting for children?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 这取决于那个孩子，但 一般来说 它 可以很迷人。 | I'd say it depends on the kid, but generally it can be fascinating. |
| 2 | 生物 就在他们周围，所以 学 他们自己的身体 怎么运作、或者 植物 为什么长，感觉 很相关。 | Biology's all around them, so learning how their own body works or why plants grow feels relevant. |
| 3 | 比如，一个爱动物的孩子 很可能 会觉得它 引人入胜。 | For instance, a child who loves animals will probably find it gripping. |
| 4 | 但如果 它 被当作 一堆要背的干巴事实 来教，他们 很快 就会走神。 | But if it's taught as dry facts to memorise, they'll quickly switch off. |

> I'd say it depends on the kid, but generally it can be fascinating. Biology's all around them, so learning how their own body works or why plants grow feels relevant. For instance, a child who loves animals will probably find it gripping. But if it's taught as dry facts to memorise, they'll quickly switch off.

**Q3. Why do some children want to become doctors?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我觉得 很多 来自 想帮助别人，因为 孩子 常常 把医生 看成 英雄、（那种在你受伤时 修好你的）。 | Well, I reckon a lot of it comes from wanting to help people, since kids often see doctors as heroes who fix you when you're hurt. |
| 2 | 此外，这份工作 承载 真正的尊重，所以 它 感觉 有意义。 | Plus, the job carries real respect, so it feels meaningful. |
| 3 | 对有些人来说，一个家庭成员 生病 是那个火花、（那种把他们 推向 医学的）。 | For some, a family member falling ill is the spark that pushes them towards medicine. |

> Well, I reckon a lot of it comes from wanting to help people, since kids often see doctors as heroes who fix you when you're hurt. Plus, the job carries real respect, so it feels meaningful. For some, a family member falling ill is the spark that pushes them towards medicine.

**Q4. Do you think governments should put a large amount of money into medical research?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对应该，我觉得 他们 应该。 | Definitely, I think they should. |
| 2 | 医学研究 长远看 拯救生命，因为 今天的资金 变成 明天的 治疗方法和疫苗。 | Medical research saves lives in the long run, since today's funding becomes tomorrow's cures and vaccines. |
| 3 | 我们 在那场疫情期间 清楚地 看到了 那一点。 | We saw that clearly during the pandemic. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，政府 不该 把一切 都砸进去、而 忽略了 学校或住房，所以 它 真的是 关于 达成 一个明智的平衡。 | Having said that, governments shouldn't pour everything into it and neglect schools or housing, so it's really about striking a sensible balance. |

> Definitely, I think they should. Medical research saves lives in the long run, since today's funding becomes tomorrow's cures and vaccines. We saw that clearly during the pandemic. Having said that, governments shouldn't pour everything into it and neglect schools or housing, so it's really about striking a sensible balance.

**Q5. Should doctors be paid more than other professionals?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 他们 应得 像样的薪水，因为 他们 训练了很多年，而 这份工作 极其 累人。 | I'd say they deserve decent pay, since they train for years and the job's incredibly demanding. |
| 2 | 但我 不确定 他们 应该 总是 比 其他所有人 挣得多，因为 老师和工程师 也 极其重要。 | But I'm not sure they should always earn more than everyone else, because teachers and engineers matter hugely too. |
| 3 | 所以我 宁愿 薪水 反映的是 责任和付出，而不只是 职位头衔 本身。 | So I'd rather pay reflected the responsibility and effort, not just the job title itself. |

> I'd say they deserve decent pay, since they train for years and the job's incredibly demanding. But I'm not sure they should always earn more than everyone else, because teachers and engineers matter hugely too. So I'd rather pay reflected the responsibility and effort, not just the job title itself.

**Q6. How can technology help doctors in the future?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，一大堆方式。 | Loads of ways, honestly. |
| 2 | AI 已经 能 扫描影像、发现 问题、（那些医生可能漏掉的），所以 它 让 诊断 更快、更安全。 | AI can already scan images and spot problems doctors might miss, so it makes diagnosis faster and safer. |
| 3 | 此外，机器人 能 协助 棘手的手术，带着 真正的精度。 | Plus, robots can assist in tricky operations with real precision. |
| 4 | 但我 不觉得 机器 会 取代 医生，因为 病人 仍然 需要 那种 人的触感 和 安慰。 | But I don't think machines will replace doctors, since patients still need that human touch and reassurance. |

> Loads of ways, honestly. AI can already scan images and spot problems doctors might miss, so it makes diagnosis faster and safer. Plus, robots can assist in tricky operations with real precision. But I don't think machines will replace doctors, since patients still need that human touch and reassurance.

**P3 句型/模板**：`carry a huge responsibility` · `the pressure never really lets up` · `so it's tough, but worthwhile`（OPINION 二面收）· `learning how their body works … feels relevant`（动名词主语+embedded）· `heroes who fix you when you're hurt`（who后置）· `the spark that pushes them towards [X]`（that后置）· `striking a sensible balance` · `I'd rather pay reflected …`（虚拟）· `machines will replace …, since patients still need that human touch`（PREDICT）。

---

## P2-新09 · A person with a successful business — Chen 咖啡馆（Person）

> **Cue**: 是谁 / 怎么认识 / 为何+怎么开始 / 什么生意 / 为何成功 · 题型 Person

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个人 是 我的一个老同事，Chen——某个人、（那个以前 跟我一起写代码、但现在 经营着 一家小咖啡店的，那种 生意 做得很好的）。 | So, the person I'd like to talk about is an old colleague of mine, Chen — someone who used to write code with me but now runs a little coffee shop that's doing really well. |
| [how met] | 我们 认识彼此 是 好多年前 在同一家公司，而 我们 常常 一起 随便吃个午饭。 | We got to know each other years ago at the same company, and we'd often grab lunch together. |
| [why start] | 那会儿 他 总是 被长工时 弄得 有点心力交瘁，而 有一天 他 就 决定 他 受够了。 | Back then he was always a bit burnt out by the long hours, and one day he just decided he'd had enough. |
| [how start] | 他 已经 迷上 在家做咖啡 很久了，所以 他 辞职、在一所大学附近 开了 他自己的店。 | He'd been into making coffee at home for ages, so he quit and opened his own place near a university. |
| [what biz] | 它是一家 小小的 精品咖啡店——他 在后面 自己 烘豆子、只卖 少数几种饮品，但 每一种 都做得 很到位。 | It's a small specialty café — he roasts his own beans in the back and only sells a handful of drinks, but each one's done properly. |
| [what·氛围] | 那氛围 真的 很温馨，配着 轻柔的音乐 和 几盆植物，而 很多学生 把它 当成 他们的 第二个家。 | The atmosphere is really cosy, with soft music and a few plants, and a lot of students treat it as their second home. |
| [why success/EL] | 我觉得 它 成功 是因为 他 真心 在乎 每一杯，而且 口碑 传开了。 | I reckon it's successful because he genuinely cares about every cup, and word's spread. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那就是 那种生意、（我 真的很佩服的）。 | So yeah, that's the kind of business I really admire. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the person I'd like to talk about is an old colleague of mine, Chen — someone who used to write code with me but now runs a little coffee shop that's doing really well. We got to know each other years ago at the same company, and we'd often grab lunch together. Back then he was always a bit burnt out by the long hours, and one day he just decided he'd had enough. He'd been into making coffee at home for ages, so he quit and opened his own place near a university. It's a small specialty café — he roasts his own beans in the back and only sells a handful of drinks, but each one's done properly. The atmosphere is really cosy, with soft music and a few plants, and a lot of students treat it as their second home. I reckon it's successful because he genuinely cares about every cup, and word's spread. So yeah, that's the kind of business I really admire.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（人+转折）**：我的一个老同事 X——某个人、以前…但现在…的 → `an old colleague of mine, X — someone who used to … but now …`
- **心力交瘁 / 受够了**：被长工时弄得心力交瘁；决定受够了 → `burnt out by the long hours` / `decided he'd had enough`
- **迷上很久**：迷上在家做咖啡很久了 → `been into making coffee for ages`
- **每一个都做到位**：每一杯都做得很到位 → `each one's done properly`
- **当成第二个家**：把它当成第二个家 → `treat it as their second home`
- **口碑传开**：口碑传开了 → `word's spread`
- **收尾**：那种我真的很佩服的生意 → `the kind of business I really admire`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Why do some people start their own business?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 它 大多是 关于自由——很多人 受够了 看老板脸色，想 做 他们自己的主人。 | Well, I'd say it's mostly about freedom — a lot of people are sick of answering to a boss and want to be their own master. |
| 2 | 此外，如果 你 有 一份热情 或者 一个聪明的点子，经营 你自己的东西 让你 建起 某种 真正属于你的东西。 | Plus, if you've got a passion or a clever idea, running your own thing lets you build something that's truly yours. |
| 3 | 对有些人来说，它 也 是 关于 挣得 比 一份固定薪水 曾经能给的 更多。 | For some, it's also about earning more than a fixed salary ever could. |

> Well, I'd say it's mostly about freedom — a lot of people are sick of answering to a boss and want to be their own master. Plus, if you've got a passion or a clever idea, running your own thing lets you build something that's truly yours. For some, it's also about earning more than a fixed salary ever could.

**Q2. Should governments provide financial support to start-ups?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我觉得 他们 应该，至少 一点点。 | Honestly, I think they should, at least a bit. |
| 2 | 创业公司 创造 就业 和 新鲜的点子，但 它们 常常 在早期 拿不到贷款，因为 银行 把它们 看得 太冒险。 | Start-ups create jobs and fresh ideas, but they often can't get loans early on because banks see them as too risky. |
| 3 | 一小笔补助 或者 一次减税 能 真的 扭转 天平。 | A small grant or a tax break can really tip the balance. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，那笔钱 得 被 仔细地 盯着，不然 它 就 白白 浪费了。 | That said, the money's got to be watched carefully, or it just gets wasted. |

> Honestly, I think they should, at least a bit. Start-ups create jobs and fresh ideas, but they often can't get loans early on because banks see them as too risky. A small grant or a tax break can really tip the balance. That said, the money's got to be watched carefully, or it just gets wasted.

**Q3. Do most people prefer shopping at big stores or small stores?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我觉得 这取决于 他们想要什么。 | I reckon it depends on what they're after. |
| 2 | 大多数人 去 大商店 买杂货，因为 它们 更便宜、而且 你 能 一趟 就把 所有东西 都拿齐。 | Most folk head to big stores for groceries since they're cheaper and you can grab everything in one trip. |
| 3 | 但 要买 一份不错的礼物 或者 一杯咖啡，人们 偏向 小店，因为 那服务 感觉 更温暖、更有人情味。 | But for a nice gift or a coffee, people lean towards small shops because the service feels warmer and more personal. |
| 4 | 所以 这 真的是 各取所需。 | So it's really horses for courses. |

> I reckon it depends on what they're after. Most folk head to big stores for groceries since they're cheaper and you can grab everything in one trip. But for a nice gift or a coffee, people lean towards small shops because the service feels warmer and more personal. So it's really horses for courses.

**Q4. What makes a business successful?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 它 归结到 真正地 解决 一个问题、（那种人们在乎的）。 | Well, I'd say it boils down to genuinely solving a problem people care about. |
| 2 | 如果 你的产品 好、而且 你 好好 对待顾客，口碑 会 自己 传开。 | If your product's good and you treat customers right, word spreads on its own. |
| 3 | 除此之外，你 得 明智地 管好 那笔钱。 | On top of that, you've got to manage the money sensibly. |
| 4 | 一个 花钱超支的生意 撑不下去，不管 它的核心点子 可能 有多 出色。 | A business that overspends won't last, no matter how brilliant its core idea might be. |

> Well, I'd say it boils down to genuinely solving a problem people care about. If your product's good and you treat customers right, word spreads on its own. On top of that, you've got to manage the money sensibly. A business that overspends won't last, no matter how brilliant its core idea might be.

**Q5. What makes a business fail?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说句公道话，一大堆原因，但 现金流 是 大的那个——公司 花光了钱，在 它们 还没 转出利润 之前。 | To be fair, plenty of reasons, but cash flow is the big one — firms run out of money before they ever turn a profit. |
| 2 | 另一个 致命的 是 忽视 顾客 真正想要什么、还有 固执地 抱着 一个有缺陷的计划。 | Another killer is ignoring what customers actually want and stubbornly sticking to a flawed plan. |
| 3 | 糟糕的时机 也伤人；一个好点子、（推出得太早的），根本 火不起来。 | Poor timing hurts too; a great idea launched too early simply won't catch on. |

> To be fair, plenty of reasons, but cash flow is the big one — firms run out of money before they ever turn a profit. Another killer is ignoring what customers actually want and stubbornly sticking to a flawed plan. Poor timing hurts too; a great idea launched too early simply won't catch on.

**Q6. Is it easy to set up a new business in your country?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，在我老家 它 好坏参半。 | Honestly, it's a mixed bag where I'm from. |
| 2 | 注册一家公司 如今 相当 快、又便宜，所以 起步 不是 难的那部分。 | Registering a company is fairly quick and cheap these days, so getting started isn't the hard part. |
| 3 | 真正的挣扎 是 那激烈的竞争 和 大城市里的房租，（那 很残酷）。 | The real struggle is the fierce competition and the rent in big cities, which is brutal. |
| 4 | 所以 虽然 起步 容易，熬过 第一年 才是 那个棘手的部分。 | So while it's easy to begin, surviving the first year's the tricky bit. |

> Honestly, it's a mixed bag where I'm from. Registering a company is fairly quick and cheap these days, so getting started isn't the hard part. The real struggle is the fierce competition and the rent in big cities, which is brutal. So while it's easy to begin, surviving the first year's the tricky bit.

**P3 句型/模板**：`sick of answering to a boss` · `running your own thing lets you build something that's truly yours`（动名词主语+that后置）· `it depends on what they're after`（embedded）· `horses for courses`（各取所需）· `it boils down to solving a problem people care about` · `A business that overspends won't last, no matter how … might be`（that后置+让步）· `run out of money before they ever turn a profit` · `a great idea launched too early won't catch on`（分词后置）· `surviving the first year's the tricky bit`（动名词主语）。

---

## P2-新10 · A plan you had to change recently — 京都行取消（speaker）

> **Cue**: 何时 / 什么让你改 / 新计划是什么 / 感受 · 题型 Event/Planning

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个计划 是 一次 短短的 家庭旅行、去京都的，（那次 我们 不得不 在最后一刻 取消），就 几周前。 | So, the plan I'd like to talk about is a short family trip to Kyoto that we had to scrap at the last minute, only a couple of weeks ago. |
| [背景] | 我们 已经 计划它 很久了——机票订了、酒店搞定了、全套都齐了。 | We'd been planning it for ages — flights booked, hotel sorted, the whole lot. |
| [背景·细] | 我和我老婆 真的 很期待 带我们五岁的儿子 Muye 去看 秋叶。 | My wife and I were really looking forward to taking our five-year-old son, Muye, to see the autumn leaves. |
| [what changed] | 但 就在 我们 该起飞的 两天前，Muye 发起了 一场 讨厌的高烧，而 医生 告诉我们 他 不适合 旅行。 | But just two days before we were due to fly out, Muye came down with a nasty fever, and the doctor told us he wasn't fit to travel. |
| [new plan] | 所以 我们 不得不 当场 把一切 重新想过。 | So we had to rethink everything on the spot. |
| [new·细] | 与其 飞去国外，我们 最后 就 待着没动、把我们的公寓 变成了 一个小小的假期。 | Instead of flying abroad, we ended up staying put and turning our flat into a little holiday. |
| [new·细] | 我们 一起 烤东西、在地板上 搭起 一座巨大的乐高城市、还 在 一个毯子堡垒 底下 看电影。 | We baked together, built a huge LEGO city on the floor, and watched films under a blanket fort. |
| [feel] | 说实话，我 一开始 挺沮丧的，但 它 慢慢 打动了我。 | Honestly, I was gutted at first, but it grew on me. |
| [closer] | 说到底，重要的 是 在一起的时间，而不是 那个目的地。 | At the end of the day, what mattered was the time together, not the destination. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the plan I'd like to talk about is a short family trip to Kyoto that we had to scrap at the last minute, only a couple of weeks ago. We'd been planning it for ages — flights booked, hotel sorted, the whole lot. My wife and I were really looking forward to taking our five-year-old son, Muye, to see the autumn leaves. But just two days before we were due to fly out, Muye came down with a nasty fever, and the doctor told us he wasn't fit to travel. So we had to rethink everything on the spot. Instead of flying abroad, we ended up staying put and turning our flat into a little holiday. We baked together, built a huge LEGO city on the floor, and watched films under a blanket fort. Honestly, I was gutted at first, but it grew on me. At the end of the day, what mattered was the time together, not the destination.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（计划）**：我想聊的那个计划是[X]、（那次我们不得不取消的）→ `the plan I'd like to talk about is [X] that we had to scrap …`
- **准备就绪罗列**：机票订了、酒店搞定、全套都齐 → `flights booked, hotel sorted, the whole lot`
- **突发**：发了场讨厌的高烧；不适合旅行 → `came down with a nasty fever` / `wasn't fit to travel`
- **当场重想**：当场把一切重新想过 → `rethink everything on the spot`
- **最后变成**：最后待着没动、把公寓变成小假期 → `ended up staying put and turning [X] into [Y]`
- **感受转折**：一开始沮丧，但慢慢打动了我 → `I was gutted at first, but it grew on me`
- **what主语收尾**：重要的是在一起的时间，不是目的地 → `what mattered was the time together, not the destination`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Do people often change their plans?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，是的，一天到晚都在改。 | Honestly, yeah, all the time. |
| 2 | 生活 就是 难以预料，所以 哪怕 一个 深思熟虑的计划 也可能 散架，因为 天气、钱、或者工作 撞车。 | Life's just unpredictable, so even a well-thought-out plan can fall apart because of weather, money or work clashes. |
| 3 | 比如，一大堆人 订了一趟旅行，然后 办公室里 冒出来 一个截止日期。 | For instance, loads of folk book a trip and then a deadline pops up at the office. |
| 4 | 我会说 应对得最好的人 是那些人、（那些保持灵活、不 死抠 每一个细节的）。 | I'd say the people who cope best are the ones who stay flexible and don't cling to every detail. |

> Honestly, yeah, all the time. Life's just unpredictable, so even a well-thought-out plan can fall apart because of weather, money or work clashes. For instance, loads of folk book a trip and then a deadline pops up at the office. I'd say the people who cope best are the ones who stay flexible and don't cling to every detail.

**Q2. Would you tell others if you change your plan?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 这 真的 取决于 谁牵扯在里面。 | It really depends who's involved. |
| 2 | 如果 这计划 影响到 别人，那 当然要说——把他们 晾在那儿 会很没礼貌。 | If the plan affects other people, then definitely — it'd be rude to leave them hanging. |
| 3 | 但 对于 纯粹私人的东西、比如 翘掉健身，我 才懒得 告诉 任何一个人。 | But for something purely personal, like skipping the gym, I wouldn't bother telling a soul. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，对于 团队计划，一条 快速的消息 帮每个人 省掉 一堆 混乱，所以 我 总会 给人们 提个醒。 | To be fair, with group plans a quick message saves everyone a lot of confusion, so I'd always give people a heads-up. |

> It really depends who's involved. If the plan affects other people, then definitely — it'd be rude to leave them hanging. But for something purely personal, like skipping the gym, I wouldn't bother telling a soul. To be fair, with group plans a quick message saves everyone a lot of confusion, so I'd always give people a heads-up.

**Q3. Why do you think parents still make plans for their children nowadays?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我觉得 这是因为 孩子 就是 还看不到 那个更大的图景。 | Well, I reckon it's because kids simply can't see the bigger picture yet. |
| 2 | 父母 规划 他们的学业 或 爱好，因为 他们 想 给他们 一个 像样的 人生起跑优势。 | Parents plan their schooling or hobbies since they want to give them a decent head start in life. |
| 3 | 比如，早早 给一个孩子 报名 游泳 建起 一项技能、（那种 以后 很难 捡起来的）。 | For example, signing a child up for swimming early builds a skill that'd be tough to pick up later. |
| 4 | 不过，最好的父母 会 留 一点空间 给 孩子 自己的选择。 | Still, the best parents leave a bit of room for the kid's own choices. |

> Well, I reckon it's because kids simply can't see the bigger picture yet. Parents plan their schooling or hobbies since they want to give them a decent head start in life. For example, signing a child up for swimming early builds a skill that'd be tough to pick up later. Still, the best parents leave a bit of room for the kid's own choices.

**Q4. How does technology help people make plans?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 哦，帮助巨大。 | Oh, massively. |
| 2 | 那些 app、比如 共享日历 和 地图，把 所有的 瞎猜 都从 安排你的一天 里 拿走。 | Apps like shared calendars and maps take all the guesswork out of organising your day. |
| 3 | 比如，你 能 跟朋友 分摊 一趟旅行、同步 每个人的 日程、还能 几秒钟 就订票。 | For instance, you can split a trip with friends, sync everyone's schedule and book tickets in seconds. |
| 4 | 此外，提醒 意味着 你 忘掉 一个约会 的可能性 小得多。 | Plus, reminders mean you're far less likely to forget an appointment. |
| 5 | 话虽如此，我觉得 过度依赖它 让我们 对 自己 记东西 有点 变懒。 | Having said that, I think over-relying on it makes us a bit lazy about remembering things ourselves. |

> Oh, massively. Apps like shared calendars and maps take all the guesswork out of organising your day. For instance, you can split a trip with friends, sync everyone's schedule and book tickets in seconds. Plus, reminders mean you're far less likely to forget an appointment. Having said that, I think over-relying on it makes us a bit lazy about remembering things ourselves.

**Q5. What kind of plans do people often make?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 挺杂的，真的。 | Quite a mix, really. |
| 2 | 大多数人 做 短期的——比如 安排 他们的周末、一次假期、或者 跟朋友 吃顿饭。 | Most people make short-term ones — like sorting out their weekend, a holiday or a dinner with mates. |
| 3 | 但 也有 那些 人生大事，比如 为一套房子 存钱、或者 规划出 一份事业。 | But there's also the big life stuff, such as saving for a house or mapping out a career. |
| 4 | 我会说 那些日常计划 让我们 维持运转，而 那些长期目标 给我们 一个 可以 瞄准的东西。 | I'd say the daily plans keep us ticking over, while the long-term goals give us something to aim for. |

> Quite a mix, really. Most people make short-term ones — like sorting out their weekend, a holiday or a dinner with mates. But there's also the big life stuff, such as saving for a house or mapping out a career. I'd say the daily plans keep us ticking over, while the long-term goals give us something to aim for.

**Q6. Do you think people like the process of making plans more, or the moment of carrying them out?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我觉得 大多数人 更享受 规划的那部分。 | Honestly, I think most people enjoy the planning bit more. |
| 2 | 有某种令人兴奋的东西、在 想象 一趟旅行或派对 可能 怎么进行 里，而 现实 很少 配得上 那份激动。 | There's something exciting about imagining how a trip or party might go, and the reality rarely lives up to that buzz. |
| 3 | 比如，人们 花上 好几周 幻想 一个假期，然后 它 一晃就过去了。 | For instance, folk spend weeks dreaming about a holiday, then it flies by in a flash. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，少数人 是 实干家、（那种 只有 在它 真正 开始 之后 才 活过来的）。 | Having said that, a few people are doers who only come alive once it actually kicks off. |

> Honestly, I think most people enjoy the planning bit more. There's something exciting about imagining how a trip or party might go, and the reality rarely lives up to that buzz. For instance, folk spend weeks dreaming about a holiday, then it flies by in a flash. Having said that, a few people are doers who only come alive once it actually kicks off.

**P3 句型/模板**：`a well-thought-out plan can fall apart` · `the people who cope best are the ones who stay flexible`（who层叠）· `it'd be rude to leave them hanging` · `give people a heads-up` · `take the guesswork out of … -ing` · `over-relying on it makes us lazy`（动名词主语）· `give us something to aim for` · `There's something exciting about … -ing`（embedded）· `doers who only come alive once it kicks off`（who后置）。

## P2-新11 · A time you worked in a group — 工作提案（speaker）

> **Cue**: 做了啥 / 跟谁 / 遇到什么问题 / 为何在团队里做 · 题型 Event

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那次 是 一个工作上的小组项目、几年前，当时 我和我的团队 不得不 拼凑出 一份 棘手的提案、在一个 特别紧的截止日期 之下。 | So, the time I'd like to talk about is a group project at work a couple of years back, when my team and I had to pull together a tricky proposal under a really tight deadline. |
| [did] | 我的工作 基本上 是 让各个部分 动起来——我 把活 拆开、催人 要进度、然后 最后 把一切 缝合进 一份文件。 | My job was basically to keep the pieces moving — I split the work up, chased people for updates, and stitched everything into one document at the end. |
| [who+problem] | 我 跟 三个同事 一起干，全都 来自 公司不同的部门，（那 正是 麻烦 开始的地方）。 | I worked with three colleagues, all from different parts of the company, which is exactly where the trouble started. |
| [problem·细] | 他们中的两个 冲突得 挺凶，关于 怎么 组织 整个东西，而 有那么几天 什么 都没 真正 做成。 | Two of them clashed pretty badly over how to structure the whole thing, and for a couple of days nothing really got done. |
| [problem·解] | 所以 我 让他们俩 坐下来、听 每一方，然后 我们 找到了 一个 折中点、（那种 让 每个人 步调一致的）。 | So I sat them both down, listened to each side, and we found a middle ground that kept everyone on the same page. |
| [why group] | 我 在一个团队里干、而不是 单干，的原因 是 这个任务 就是 对一个人来说 太大了。 | The reason I worked in a group, rather than going it alone, is that the task was just too big for one person. |
| [why·细] | 此外，每个人 都 带了 一个 不同的强项 到桌上。 | Plus, everyone brought a different strength to the table. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那就是我想聊的那次经历。 | So yeah, that's the experience I wanted to talk about. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the time I'd like to talk about is a group project at work a couple of years back, when my team and I had to pull together a tricky proposal under a really tight deadline. My job was basically to keep the pieces moving — I split the work up, chased people for updates, and stitched everything into one document at the end. I worked with three colleagues, all from different parts of the company, which is exactly where the trouble started. Two of them clashed pretty badly over how to structure the whole thing, and for a couple of days nothing really got done. So I sat them both down, listened to each side, and we found a middle ground that kept everyone on the same page. The reason I worked in a group, rather than going it alone, is that the task was just too big for one person. Plus, everyone brought a different strength to the table. So yeah, that's the experience I wanted to talk about.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（经历/工作）**：我想聊的那次是[X]，当时我… → `the time I'd like to talk about is [X], when I …`
- **让各部分动起来**：让各个部分动起来 → `keep the pieces moving`
- **which后置**：全来自不同部门、那正是麻烦开始的地方 → `…, which is exactly where the trouble started`
- **折中点**：找到一个折中点、让每个人步调一致 → `found a middle ground that kept everyone on the same page`
- **对比原因**：我在团队里干、而不是单干，的原因是… → `The reason I …, rather than going it alone, is that …`
- **各带强项**：每个人带了不同强项到桌上 → `everyone brought a different strength to the table`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Why do some people prefer to work by themselves?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 有些人 就是 自己一个人 干得更好，因为 他们 能 定 自己的节奏、也不 被 任何别人 拖住。 | Well, I'd say some folk just work better on their own because they can set their own pace and aren't held up by anyone else. |
| 2 | 比如，一个作家 常常 需要 完全的安静 才能 专注。 | For instance, a writer often needs total quiet to focus. |
| 3 | 此外，当你 单干，你 不必 催人 要进度、或者 摆平 冲突，所以 说实话 麻烦更少。 | Plus, when you're solo, you don't have to chase people for updates or smooth over clashes, so it's honestly less hassle. |

> Well, I'd say some folk just work better on their own because they can set their own pace and aren't held up by anyone else. For instance, a writer often needs total quiet to focus. Plus, when you're solo, you don't have to chase people for updates or smooth over clashes, so it's honestly less hassle.

**Q2. What should a leader do to make team members want to follow him or her?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 一个好领导 得 以身作则，我觉得，因为 人们 不会 跟随 某个人、（那种 只 坐在办公桌前 吼命令的）。 | A good leader has to lead by example, I reckon, because people won't follow someone who just barks orders from a desk. |
| 2 | 他们 应该 好好听、也 在该给功劳的地方 给功劳，这样 团队 感觉 被重视。 | They should listen properly and give credit where it's due, so the team feels valued. |
| 3 | 比如，一个老板、（那种 在 关键时刻 撸起袖子的），赢得 多得多的 忠诚，比 一个 只把一切 都甩出去的。 | For instance, a boss who rolls up their sleeves during a crunch earns way more loyalty than one who just delegates everything. |

> A good leader has to lead by example, I reckon, because people won't follow someone who just barks orders from a desk. They should listen properly and give credit where it's due, so the team feels valued. For instance, a boss who rolls up their sleeves during a crunch earns way more loyalty than one who just delegates everything.

**Q3. Should students learn to do group work?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，我觉得 它 必不可少。 | Absolutely, I think it's essential. |
| 2 | 小组作业 教 孩子 分享点子、也 处理分歧、不 闹翻，（那 他们 一辈子 都会 需要的）。 | Group work teaches kids to share ideas and handle disagreements without falling out, which they'll need their whole lives. |
| 3 | 比如，一个课堂项目 逼着 那些更安静的学生 开口、也 出一份力。 | For instance, a class project forces quieter students to speak up and pull their weight. |
| 4 | 此外，它 早早 就 给他们 展示 两个脑袋 是 真的 比一个好，当 一个任务 变棘手的时候。 | Plus, it shows them early on that two heads are genuinely better than one when a task gets tricky. |

> Absolutely, I think it's essential. Group work teaches kids to share ideas and handle disagreements without falling out, which they'll need their whole lives. For instance, a class project forces quieter students to speak up and pull their weight. Plus, it shows them early on that two heads are genuinely better than one when a task gets tricky.

**Q4. What group tasks are there in schools?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，范围挺广的，说实话。 | Well, there's quite a range, honestly. |
| 2 | 科学课 常常 有 实验室实验、（在那里 一对一对的人 一起 记录 结果）。 | Science classes often have lab experiments where pairs record results together. |
| 3 | 然后 你 有 小组展示、（在那里 每个人 拿走 话题的 一块）。 | Then you've got group presentations, where everyone takes a slice of the topic. |
| 4 | 戏剧课和音乐课 也 需要 团队合作，因为 一出戏 或 一支乐队 只有 在 人们 合上拍 的时候 才行。 | Drama and music lessons need teamwork too, since a play or a band only works if people sync up. |
| 5 | 所以 学校 覆盖它 挺广的，我会说。 | So schools cover it pretty broadly, I'd say. |

> Well, there's quite a range, honestly. Science classes often have lab experiments where pairs record results together. Then you've got group presentations, where everyone takes a slice of the topic. Drama and music lessons need teamwork too, since a play or a band only works if people sync up. So schools cover it pretty broadly, I'd say.

**Q5. What advantages are there for students experiencing teamwork at school?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 最大的一个 大概是 学会沟通，因为 你 在一个团队里 走不远，如果不 把你的点子 清楚地 解释出来。 | The biggest one's probably learning to communicate, since you can't get far in a team without explaining your ideas clearly. |
| 2 | 它 也 培养 耐心，因为 你 一定 会 撞上 一些人、（那些不同意的）。 | It also builds patience, because you're bound to hit people who disagree. |
| 3 | 比如，弄清 谁做什么 教 孩子 妥协。 | For instance, sorting out who does what teaches kids to compromise. |
| 4 | 所以 真的，它 是 在 为 职场 准备 他们，（在那里 几乎没人 完全孤立地 工作）。 | So really, it's preparing them for the workplace, where almost nobody works in total isolation. |

> The biggest one's probably learning to communicate, since you can't get far in a team without explaining your ideas clearly. It also builds patience, because you're bound to hit people who disagree. For instance, sorting out who does what teaches kids to compromise. So really, it's preparing them for the workplace, where almost nobody works in total isolation.

**Q6. How can you tell if a person is a good leader?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 你 能 看出来，从 他们周围的团队 怎么表现。 | Well, I'd say you can tell by how the team around them behaves. |
| 2 | 如果 人们 显得 有干劲、也 信任 他们的老板，那 是 一个 强的 信号。 | If people seem motivated and trust their boss, that's a strong sign. |
| 3 | 一个好领导 在压力下 保持冷静、也 在事情 出错时 揽下责任，而不是 甩锅。 | A good leader stays calm under pressure and takes the blame when things go wrong, rather than passing the buck. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，真正的考验 是 人们 是否 会 高高兴兴地 再 跟随他们 一次。 | To be fair, the real test is whether folk would happily follow them again. |

> Well, I'd say you can tell by how the team around them behaves. If people seem motivated and trust their boss, that's a strong sign. A good leader stays calm under pressure and takes the blame when things go wrong, rather than passing the buck. To be fair, the real test is whether folk would happily follow them again.

**P3 句型/模板**：`set their own pace and aren't held up by anyone else` · `someone who just barks orders from a desk`（who后置）· `give credit where it's due` · `handle disagreements without falling out, which they'll need`（which后置）· `pull their weight` · `lab experiments where pairs record results`（where后置）· `you can't get far … without explaining … clearly` · `sorting out who does what teaches kids to compromise`（动名词主语+embedded）· `takes the blame … rather than passing the buck`。

## P2-新12 · An important decision you made — 拒掉高薪 offer（speaker）

> **Cue**: 什么决定 / 怎么做的 / 结果 / 为何重要 · 题型 Event/Decision

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/23 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改 · 只练了 P2）

> 你自己练过的 P2（毕业选行业，你的真实版；这条你只练了 P2，没练 P3）。**不降档**，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · 毕业选进技术行业）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 做 的 一个 重要 决定 是 选 进 哪个 行业，就 在 我 毕业 之后。 | One important decision I made was choosing which field to go into, just after I graduated. |
| 2 | 那时候，我 完全 不知道 我 能 做 什么，所以 它 感觉 像 一个 大 决定。 | At the time, I had no idea what I could do, so it felt like a big decision. |
| 3 | 最后，我 选择 成为 一个 软件 工程师，然后 在 一家 互联网 公司 工作。 | In the end, I chose to become a software engineer, and worked at an internet company. |
| 4 | 在 我 下定 决心 之前，我 权衡 了 几样 东西——我 自己的 兴趣 和 那 工作 前景——而 我 也 问 了 一些 在 这行 工作的人 关于 他们的 经验。 | Before I made up my mind, I weighed a few things — my own interests and the job prospects — and I also asked some people working in the field about their experience. |
| 5 | 结果 呢，它 进展 得 不错，而 我 找到 一份 稳定的 工作。 | As it turned out, it worked out well, and I found a stable job. |
| 6 | 它 重要 是 因为 那 决定 完全 塑造 了 我的 职业。 | It was important because the decision completely shaped my career. |
| 7 | 有时候 我 想象，如果 我 当初 进 了 另一个 行业，我的 生活 会 完全 不同。 | Sometimes I imagine, if I'd gone into another field, my life would be totally different. |
| 8 | 回头看，我 真的 庆幸 我 花 时间 想 了 它，而不是 脑子 一热 就 冲 进去。 | Looking back, I'm really glad I spent time thinking about it, instead of rushing into it. |

> One important decision I made was choosing which field to go into, just after I graduated. At the time, I had no idea what I could do, so it felt like a big decision. In the end, I chose to become a software engineer, and worked at an internet company. Before I made up my mind, I weighed a few things — my own interests and the job prospects — and I also asked some people working in the field about their experience. As it turned out, it worked out well, and I found a stable job. It was important because the decision completely shaped my career. Sometimes I imagine, if I'd gone into another field, my life would be totally different. Looking back, I'm really glad I spent time thinking about it, instead of rushing into it.

---

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个决定 是 拒掉 一份工作机会、几年前——一份、（那种 给钱 多得多、但 会意味着 长得多的工时的）。 | So, the decision I'd like to talk about is turning down a job offer a couple of years ago — one that paid a lot more but would've meant much longer hours. |
| [背景] | 那时候，我 已经 在科技行业 工作了 十多年，而 那个 offer 说实话 挺诱人。 | At the time, I'd been working in tech for over a decade, and the offer was honestly tempting. |
| [背景·细] | 它是 一个 明显的 上升——更多的钱、更大的头衔、更多的责任。 | It was a clear step up — more money, a bigger title, more responsibility. |
| [转折] | 但它 也 意味着 我 一周里 几乎 见不到 我的家人。 | But it also meant I'd barely see my family during the week. |
| [how decided] | 所以 我和我老婆 坐下来、真正 把它 想透。 | So my wife and I sat down and really thought it through. |
| [how·细] | 我们的儿子 Muye 那会儿 还只是 个学步的小孩，而 我 意识到 我 不想 错过 那些 早年的时光。 | Our son Muye was just a toddler back then, and I realised I didn't want to miss those early years. |
| [result] | 到最后，我 拒了它。 | In the end, I turned it down. |
| [result·细] | 回头看，它是 最好的决定之一、（我做过的）——我 一直 在场、当 Muye 在长大，而 没有 哪份薪水 能 替代 那个。 | Looking back, it's one of the best calls I've ever made — I've been around while Muye's been growing up, and no salary could replace that. |
| [why/EL] | 我觉得 它 重要 是因为 它 逼着 我 弄清 我 在生活里 真正 看重 什么。 | I think it was important because it forced me to figure out what I actually value in life. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那是 一个决定、（我 一次都 没后悔过的）。 | So yeah, that's a decision I've never once regretted. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the decision I'd like to talk about is turning down a job offer a couple of years ago — one that paid a lot more but would've meant much longer hours. At the time, I'd been working in tech for over a decade, and the offer was honestly tempting. It was a clear step up — more money, a bigger title, more responsibility. But it also meant I'd barely see my family during the week. So my wife and I sat down and really thought it through. Our son Muye was just a toddler back then, and I realised I didn't want to miss those early years. In the end, I turned it down. Looking back, it's one of the best calls I've ever made — I've been around while Muye's been growing up, and no salary could replace that. I think it was important because it forced me to figure out what I actually value in life. So yeah, that's a decision I've never once regretted.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（决定）**：我想聊的那个决定是 拒掉[X]——一份、（那种…的）→ `the decision I'd like to talk about is turning down [X] — one that …`
- **上升**：一个明显的上升——更多钱、更大头衔 → `a clear step up — more money, a bigger title …`
- **想透**：真正把它想透 → `thought it through`
- **最好决定之一**：最好的决定之一、我做过的 → `one of the best calls I've ever made`
- **逼我弄清**：逼我弄清我真正看重什么 → `it forced me to figure out what I actually value in life`
- **收尾**：一个我一次都没后悔过的决定 → `a decision I've never once regretted`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Do you think children sometimes have to make important decisions?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，虽然 通常是 小的。 | Definitely, though usually small ones. |
| 2 | 我会说 孩子 每天 都面对 小小的选择，比如 该信 哪些朋友、或者 怎么花 他们的零花钱。 | I'd say kids face little choices every day, like which friends to trust or how to spend their pocket money. |
| 3 | 说实话，那些决定 重要 是因为 它们 是 孩子 学会 权衡 的方式。 | Honestly, those decisions matter because they're how children learn to weigh things up. |
| 4 | 比如，一个 挑爱好的 孩子 悄悄 就在学 他们 真正 喜欢什么，而 那 是一项技能、（他们 会 用一辈子的）。 | For instance, a kid choosing a hobby is quietly learning what they actually enjoy, and that's a skill they'll use forever. |

> Definitely, though usually small ones. I'd say kids face little choices every day, like which friends to trust or how to spend their pocket money. Honestly, those decisions matter because they're how children learn to weigh things up. For instance, a kid choosing a hobby is quietly learning what they actually enjoy, and that's a skill they'll use forever.

**Q2. What important decisions do teenagers need to make after graduation?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 其实挺多的。 | Quite a few, actually. |
| 2 | 最大的一个 大概是 是否 上大学、还是 直接 跳进 工作，因为 那 真的 塑造 接下来的 几年。 | The biggest one's probably whether to go to university or jump straight into work, since that really shapes the next few years. |
| 3 | 他们 还得 挑 一个领域、（那种他们 真正 热衷的），而不只是 一个 他们父母 偏好的。 | They've also got to pick a field they're genuinely keen on, not just one their parents prefer. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，这 是 很大的 压力，在 这么小的 年纪。 | To be fair, it's a lot of pressure at such a young age. |

> Quite a few, actually. The biggest one's probably whether to go to university or jump straight into work, since that really shapes the next few years. They've also got to pick a field they're genuinely keen on, not just one their parents prefer. To be fair, it's a lot of pressure at such a young age.

**Q3. Who can children turn to for help when making a decision?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 大多是 他们的父母，我会说，因为 爸妈 通常 最了解 他们、也 想要 对他们 合适的东西。 | Mostly their parents, I'd say, because mum and dad usually know them best and want what's right for them. |
| 2 | 老师 也帮忙，尤其 在 学校或职业 选择上。 | Teachers help too, especially with school or career choices. |
| 3 | 说实话，哥哥姐姐 也 能很棒，因为 他们 最近 才 经历过 同一个阶段、而且 不会 说教 你、（用 大人 有时那种方式）。 | Honestly, older siblings can be brilliant as well, since they've recently been through the same stage and won't lecture you the way adults sometimes do. |

> Mostly their parents, I'd say, because mum and dad usually know them best and want what's right for them. Teachers help too, especially with school or career choices. Honestly, older siblings can be brilliant as well, since they've recently been through the same stage and won't lecture you the way adults sometimes do.

**Q4. Do you think advertisements can influence our decisions when shopping?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，而且 比 人们 承认的 更多。 | Absolutely, and more than people admit. |
| 2 | 我觉得 广告 把点子 种进 你脑子里，所以 你 突然 就想要 一个牌子、（那种你 从没 甚至 听说过的）。 | I reckon ads plant ideas in your head, so you suddenly want a brand you'd never even heard of. |
| 3 | 它们 很聪明、在 把东西 弄得 看起来 必需，当 它们 其实 不是的时候。 | They're clever at making things look essential when they're really not. |
| 4 | 比如，一句 朗朗上口的 口号 能 粘在你脑子里，然后 你 会 抓起 那个产品、想都不想。 | For example, a catchy slogan can stick with you, and you'll grab that product without thinking twice. |

> Absolutely, and more than people admit. I reckon ads plant ideas in your head, so you suddenly want a brand you'd never even heard of. They're clever at making things look essential when they're really not. For example, a catchy slogan can stick with you, and you'll grab that product without thinking twice.

**Q5. Do you think the influence of advertising is good?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它 好坏参半。 | It's a bit of a mixed bag, honestly. |
| 2 | 一方面，广告 让我们 了解 新产品，而且 它们 资助 一大堆 免费服务、（那些我们 享受的）。 | On one hand, ads keep us informed about new products and they fund loads of free services we enjoy. |
| 3 | 但另一方面，它们 能 推着 我们 买 东西、（那些我们 不需要的）。 | But on the other, they can push us to buy stuff we don't need. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 它 有帮助，只要 你 保持 一点 怀疑。 | So I'd say it's helpful as long as you stay a bit sceptical. |

> It's a bit of a mixed bag, honestly. On one hand, ads keep us informed about new products and they fund loads of free services we enjoy. But on the other, they can push us to buy stuff we don't need. So I'd say it's helpful as long as you stay a bit sceptical.

**Q6. How do people usually make important decisions?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，大多数人 权衡 利弊，我会说，把 他们会得到的 记下来、对着 他们会失去的。 | Well, most folk weigh up the pros and cons, I'd say, jotting down what they'd gain against what they'd lose. |
| 2 | 他们 也 会 问 人、（那些他们信任的）要 第二意见，因为 独自 保持客观 很难。 | They'll also ask people they trust for a second opinion, since it's hard to stay objective alone. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，很多人 最后 就 跟着感觉走、然后 盼着 最好的结果。 | To be fair, plenty just go with their gut in the end and hope for the best. |

> Well, most folk weigh up the pros and cons, I'd say, jotting down what they'd gain against what they'd lose. They'll also ask people they trust for a second opinion, since it's hard to stay objective alone. To be fair, plenty just go with their gut in the end and hope for the best.

**P3 句型/模板**：`which friends to trust or how to spend their pocket money`（embedded）· `they're how children learn to weigh things up` · `a skill they'll use forever`（relative后置）· `whether to go to university or jump straight into work` · `a field they're genuinely keen on`（relative后置）· `a brand you'd never even heard of` · `making things look essential when they're really not` · `weigh up the pros and cons` · `go with their gut … and hope for the best`。

## P2-新13 · A live sports event you liked — CBA 篮球赛（speaker + zhangwei）

> **Cue**: 是什么 / 何时何地看 / 跟谁看 / 为何喜欢 · 题型 Event

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那场比赛 是 一场 CBA 篮球赛、（我在成都这儿 去看的），（那 其实 是 第一场 正经的 体育赛事、我 亲眼 看过的）。 | So, the match I'd like to talk about is a CBA basketball game I went to here in Chengdu, which was actually the first proper sports event I'd ever watched in person. |
| [when/where] | 那 大概是 一年前，一个 周六的傍晚，在 市中心那个 大体育馆。 | It was about a year ago, on a Saturday evening, at the big arena downtown. |
| [why went] | 说实话，我 自己 并不 怎么 喜欢体育，所以 我 差点 就没去。 | To be honest, I'm not really into sports myself, so I almost didn't go. |
| [why·细] | 但 一张票 掉进了 我的怀里，所以 我 想 为啥不 试一试。 | But a ticket landed in my lap, so I figured why not give it a shot. |
| [who with] | 我 跟 zhangwei 一起去，一个 大学的老朋友、（那种 是 铁杆篮球迷的）。 | I went with zhangwei, an old friend from university who's a massive basketball fan. |
| [who·细] | 他 已经 缠着 我 很久了、[要我]一起来，而且 他 花了 半个晚上 给我 解释 规则，（那 我 发现 意外地 有用）。 | He'd been bugging me for ages to come along, and he spent half the night explaining the rules to me, which I found surprisingly helpful. |
| [why liked/EL] | 我爱的东西 其实 并不是 那比赛 本身——它是 人群里的 那股热闹劲儿。 | What I loved wasn't really the game itself — it was the buzz in the crowd. |
| [EL·细] | 成千上万的人 在 同一刻 齐声呼喊、跳起来，而 你 忍不住 就 被 卷进去。 | Thousands of people were chanting and jumping up at the same moment, and you couldn't help but get swept up in it. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，甚至 像我这样的人 都 度过了 一段 很棒的时光，而 那 就是 它 为什么 一直 留在我心里。 | So yeah, even someone like me had a brilliant time, and that's why it stuck with me. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the match I'd like to talk about is a CBA basketball game I went to here in Chengdu, which was actually the first proper sports event I'd ever watched in person. It was about a year ago, on a Saturday evening, at the big arena downtown. To be honest, I'm not really into sports myself, so I almost didn't go. But a ticket landed in my lap, so I figured why not give it a shot. I went with zhangwei, an old friend from university who's a massive basketball fan. He'd been bugging me for ages to come along, and he spent half the night explaining the rules to me, which I found surprisingly helpful. What I loved wasn't really the game itself — it was the buzz in the crowd. Thousands of people were chanting and jumping up at the same moment, and you couldn't help but get swept up in it. So yeah, even someone like me had a brilliant time, and that's why it stuck with me.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（比赛）+ which**：我想聊的那场比赛是[X]、（我去看的），（那其实是第一场…的）→ `the match I'd like to talk about is [X], which was actually the first … I'd ever …`
- **票掉怀里/试试看**：一张票掉进我怀里，我想为啥不试试 → `a ticket landed in my lap, so I figured why not give it a shot`
- **缠着我很久**：他缠着我很久要我一起来 → `he'd been bugging me for ages to come along`
- **what主语 + 是热闹**：我爱的其实不是比赛本身——是人群里的热闹 → `What I loved wasn't really the game itself — it was the buzz in the crowd`
- **忍不住被卷进**：你忍不住就被卷进去 → `you couldn't help but get swept up in it`
- **收尾**：甚至像我这样的人都很尽兴，所以它留在我心里 → `even someone like me had a brilliant time, and that's why it stuck with me`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Why do some people like to watch sports events?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 它 大多是 关于那种刺激——有 这么一股 兴奋的冲劲、当 你的队 得分时，（那种 你 在别处 根本 得不到的）。 | Well, I'd say it's mostly about the thrill — there's this rush of excitement when your team scores that you just can't get anywhere else. |
| 2 | 此外，它 给人 一种 真正的 归属感，因为 你们 都 为 同一边 加油。 | Plus, it gives people a real sense of belonging, since you're all rooting for the same side. |
| 3 | 对很多人来说，它是 一个好方式、[去]放空、忘掉 他们的烦恼。 | For a lot of folk, it's a great way to switch off and forget their worries. |

> Well, I'd say it's mostly about the thrill — there's this rush of excitement when your team scores that you just can't get anywhere else. Plus, it gives people a real sense of belonging, since you're all rooting for the same side. For a lot of folk, it's a great way to switch off and forget their worries.

**Q2. Where do people normally watch sports events?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 现在，大多数人 就 在家 用电视 看，因为 它 便宜、而且 你 不必 跟人群 挤。 | These days, most people just watch from home on the telly, since it's cheap and you don't have to fight the crowds. |
| 2 | 另一些人 去 一个酒馆 或酒吧，因为 跟陌生人 一起欢呼 是 乐趣的 一半。 | Others head to a pub or a bar, because cheering with strangers is half the fun. |
| 3 | 然后 还有 那些 死忠粉、（那种 总会 买票、亲自 出现在 体育场的）。 | And then there's the die-hard fans who'll always pay for a ticket and turn up at the stadium itself. |

> These days, most people just watch from home on the telly, since it's cheap and you don't have to fight the crowds. Others head to a pub or a bar, because cheering with strangers is half the fun. And then there's the die-hard fans who'll always pay for a ticket and turn up at the stadium itself.

**Q3. What are the advantages of watching sports events online?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，最大的好处 是 方便——你 能 在手机上 看直播、（一场比赛），不管 你 在哪儿，甚至 在公交上。 | Honestly, the biggest plus is convenience — you can stream a match on your phone wherever you are, even on the bus. |
| 2 | 它 通常 也 更便宜，而且 你 不 被 绑死在 一个 转播方 上。 | It's usually cheaper too, and you're not stuck with one broadcaster. |
| 3 | 此外，你 能 暂停、回放、或者 查 数据，随时 你想，（那 你 在一个 挤满人的体育场 根本 做不到）。 | Plus, you can pause, rewind, or check the stats whenever you fancy, which you simply can't do at a packed stadium. |

> Honestly, the biggest plus is convenience — you can stream a match on your phone wherever you are, even on the bus. It's usually cheaper too, and you're not stuck with one broadcaster. Plus, you can pause, rewind, or check the stats whenever you fancy, which you simply can't do at a packed stadium.

**Q4. What sports matches are suitable for children to attend?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我觉得 更慢的、适合全家的 那种 最合适，比如 游泳 或者 一场 本地的 足球赛。 | I reckon slower, family-friendly ones work best, like swimming or a local football match. |
| 2 | 它们 不太长，所以 孩子 不会 坐不住，而且 没有 真正的 担心、关于 粗野的 人群。 | They're not too long, so kids won't get restless, and there's no real worry about rough crowds. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，任何 太吵闹的东西、比如 一个 拳击之夜，都 不理想——它 可能 又吵、又 对小孩子 有点 招架不住。 | To be fair, anything too rowdy, like a boxing night, isn't ideal — it can be loud and a bit overwhelming for little ones. |

> I reckon slower, family-friendly ones work best, like swimming or a local football match. They're not too long, so kids won't get restless, and there's no real worry about rough crowds. To be fair, anything too rowdy, like a boxing night, isn't ideal — it can be loud and a bit overwhelming for little ones.

**Q5. Why do some people spend a lot going to other countries to watch sports events?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，对铁杆粉丝来说 它 几乎 是 一次朝圣——现场看 一场世界杯决赛 是 一种 一生一次的 激动、（那种 你 会 心甘情愿地 掏空 你钱包的）。 | Well, for hardcore fans it's almost a pilgrimage — watching a World Cup final live is a once-in-a-lifetime buzz you'd happily empty your wallet for. |
| 2 | 此外，他们 能 同时 吸收 一种 新文化，所以 它是 两趟旅行 合成一趟。 | Plus, they get to soak up a new culture at the same time, so it's two trips in one. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，我觉得 那种 吹嘘的资本 同样重要。 | To be fair, I think the bragging rights matter just as much. |

> Well, for hardcore fans it's almost a pilgrimage — watching a World Cup final live is a once-in-a-lifetime buzz you'd happily empty your wallet for. Plus, they get to soak up a new culture at the same time, so it's two trips in one. To be fair, I think the bragging rights matter just as much.

**Q6. What sports games are popular in your country?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 在中国，我会说 篮球和足球 排在最前面，尤其是 年轻人群 死盯着 NBA。 | In China, I'd say basketball and football top the list, especially with younger crowds glued to the NBA. |
| 2 | 乒乓球和羽毛球 也 特别火，因为 我们 在国际上 一直 表现很好。 | Table tennis and badminton are massive too, since we've always done well at them internationally. |
| 3 | 而且说实话，你 没法 忽视 线上游戏比赛 变得 有多大——它们 如今 吸引 巨大的 观众。 | And honestly, you can't ignore how big online gaming tournaments have become — they pull in huge audiences these days. |

> In China, I'd say basketball and football top the list, especially with younger crowds glued to the NBA. Table tennis and badminton are massive too, since we've always done well at them internationally. And honestly, you can't ignore how big online gaming tournaments have become — they pull in huge audiences these days.

**P3 句型/模板**：`this rush of excitement … that you just can't get anywhere else`（that后置）· `rooting for the same side` · `a great way to switch off` · `cheering with strangers is half the fun`（动名词主语）· `die-hard fans who'll always …`（who后置）· `whenever you fancy, which you can't do …`（which后置）· `a once-in-a-lifetime buzz you'd happily empty your wallet for`（relative后置+介词悬尾）· `top the list` · `how big … tournaments have become`（embedded）· `pull in huge audiences`。

## P2-新14 · A food for special occasions — 自制生日蛋糕（wife S2）

> **Cue**: 是什么 / 什么场合 / 怎么做 / 为何那时吃 · 题型 Object/Event

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/22 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改 · 只练了 P2）

> 你自己练过的 P2（生日蛋糕 · 今日最干净一张，你的真实版；这条只练了 P2）。**不降档**，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · 生日蛋糕）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我想聊的那个 食物 是 生日 蛋糕。正如 它 名字 暗示的，人们 在 他们 生日 那天 吃 它。 | The food I'd like to talk about is birthday cake. As its name implies, people eat it on their birthday. |
| 2 | 在 我 家，生日 总是 一件 大事，因为 我 老婆 爱 烘焙、通常 自己 做。 | In my family, birthdays are always a big deal, because my wife loves baking and usually makes it herself. |
| 3 | 她 通常 先 烤 一个 海绵 底，然后 给 它 抹 上 奶油，最后 塑形、装饰——在 上面 加 一些 水果 或 糖果。 | She usually bakes a sponge base first, then covers it with cream, and finally shapes and decorates it — adding some fruit or candies on top. |
| 4 | 一个 我 真的 记得的 是 她 给 我们 儿子 做 的 那个 恐龙 蛋糕——他 高兴 坏了。 | One I really remember is the dinosaur cake she made for our son — he was over the moon. |
| 5 | 人们 吃 它 是 因为 它 是 一个 庆祝 的 方式——你 在 它 上面 插 蜡烛、许 一个 愿、然后 大家 唱歌。 | People eat it because it's a way to celebrate — you put candles on it, make a wish, and everyone sings. |
| 6 | 我 最 喜欢 的 部分 是 当 我们 关 灯、把 蛋糕 端 出来，然后 大家 开始 唱。 | My favourite bit is when we turn off the lights and bring the cake out, and everyone starts singing. |
| 7 | 所以 对 我 来说，那 蛋糕 其实 不 关于 食物，它 关于 把 大家 聚 到 一起。 | So for me, the cake isn't really about the food, it's about bringing everyone together. |

> The food I'd like to talk about is birthday cake. As its name implies, people eat it on their birthday. In my family, birthdays are always a big deal, because my wife loves baking and usually makes it herself. She usually bakes a sponge base first, then covers it with cream, and finally shapes and decorates it — adding some fruit or candies on top. One I really remember is the dinosaur cake she made for our son — he was over the moon. People eat it because it's a way to celebrate — you put candles on it, make a wish, and everyone sings. My favourite bit is when we turn off the lights and bring the cake out, and everyone starts singing. So for me, the cake isn't really about the food, it's about bringing everyone together.

---

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个食物 是 一个自制的生日蛋糕——那个、（我老婆 每一年 都给 我们儿子 Muye 烤的）。 | So, the food I'd like to talk about is a homemade birthday cake — the one my wife bakes for our son, Muye, every single year. |
| [occasion] | 那个 特别的场合 显然 是 他的生日。 | The special occasion is obviously his birthday. |
| [occasion·细] | 我们 总是 在家 办 一个 小派对、带上 几个 他的小朋友，而 那蛋糕 说实话 是 全场的主角。 | We always throw a small party at home with a few of his little friends, and the cake is honestly the star of the show. |
| [occasion·细] | 去年 她 选了 一个 恐龙形状的，因为 他 对恐龙 着了迷。 | Last year she went for a dinosaur-shaped one, since he's mad about dinosaurs. |
| [how made] | 至于 它是怎么做的，她 通常 从零 打出 一个海绵蛋糕胚——鸡蛋、面粉、一点黄油——然后 烤它、让它凉、再 把整个 裹上 奶油霜。 | As for how it's made, she usually whips up a sponge from scratch — eggs, flour, a bit of butter — then bakes it, lets it cool, and covers the whole thing in buttercream. |
| [how·细] | 好玩的部分 是 装饰，（那 能 花掉 她 好几个小时）。 | The fun part is the decorating, which can take her a good couple of hours. |
| [why/EL] | 我觉得 人们 做 这样的蛋糕 是因为 它 把 一个普通的日子 变成 某种 值得记住的东西。 | I reckon people make cakes like this because it turns an ordinary day into something memorable. |
| [EL·细] | 你 本可以 就买 一个，但 一个自制的蛋糕 承载 多得多的 爱。 | You could just buy one, but a homemade cake carries far more love. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那就是我想聊的那个食物——它 已经 变成 一个 小小的 家庭传统、（我们 都 期待的）。 | So yeah, that's the food I wanted to talk about — it's become a little family tradition we all look forward to. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the food I'd like to talk about is a homemade birthday cake — the one my wife bakes for our son, Muye, every single year. The special occasion is obviously his birthday. We always throw a small party at home with a few of his little friends, and the cake is honestly the star of the show. Last year she went for a dinosaur-shaped one, since he's mad about dinosaurs. As for how it's made, she usually whips up a sponge from scratch — eggs, flour, a bit of butter — then bakes it, lets it cool, and covers the whole thing in buttercream. The fun part is the decorating, which can take her a good couple of hours. I reckon people make cakes like this because it turns an ordinary day into something memorable. You could just buy one, but a homemade cake carries far more love. So yeah, that's the food I wanted to talk about — it's become a little family tradition we all look forward to.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（食物）**：我想聊的那个食物是[X]——那个、（…烤的）→ `the food I'd like to talk about is [X] — the one … bakes …`
- **全场主角**：蛋糕是全场的主角 → `the cake is the star of the show`
- **"怎么做" bullet**：至于它是怎么做的，… → `As for how it's made, …` + `whips up a sponge from scratch`
- **which后置**：好玩的是装饰、那能花她好几小时 → `the decorating, which can take her a good couple of hours`
- **化平凡为难忘**：把普通日子变成值得记住的东西 → `it turns an ordinary day into something memorable`
- **收尾**：一个我们都期待的小家庭传统 → `a little family tradition we all look forward to`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Why are there special foods on special occasions or events?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 它 大多是 关于意义。 | Well, I'd say it's mostly about meaning. |
| 2 | 特别的食物 其实 并不是 关于味道——它们 承载 记忆 和 一种 传统感，所以 它们 让 一个场合 感觉 跟 一个普通的日子 不一样。 | Special foods aren't really about the taste — they carry memories and a sense of tradition, so they make an occasion feel different from an ordinary day. |
| 3 | 比如，一个生日蛋糕 立刻 就 发出 庆祝的信号。 | For instance, a birthday cake instantly signals celebration. |
| 4 | 此外，分享 一道 特别的菜 是 一个 可爱的方式、[去]把 每个人 聚到一起。 | Plus, sharing a special dish is a lovely way to bring everyone together. |

> Well, I'd say it's mostly about meaning. Special foods aren't really about the taste — they carry memories and a sense of tradition, so they make an occasion feel different from an ordinary day. For instance, a birthday cake instantly signals celebration. Plus, sharing a special dish is a lovely way to bring everyone together.

**Q2. What are the differences between everyday food and festival food?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 挺多的，说实话。 | Quite a few, honestly. |
| 2 | 日常的食物 大多是 关于 快、又管饱，所以 人们 不太 在它上面 讲究。 | Everyday food is mostly about being quick and filling, so people don't fuss over it much. |
| 3 | 节日的食物，不过，往往 更丰盛、更精致，而且 它 常常 跟 一个食谱 绑在一起、（那种 一代代传下来的）。 | Festival food, though, tends to be richer and more elaborate, and it's often tied to a recipe that's been passed down. |
| 4 | 我们 会 乐意 花 好几个小时 做它，而 一顿 平日的晚饭 就只是 几分钟 随手 凑起来。 | We'll happily spend hours cooking it, whereas a weekday dinner is just thrown together in minutes. |

> Quite a few, honestly. Everyday food is mostly about being quick and filling, so people don't fuss over it much. Festival food, though, tends to be richer and more elaborate, and it's often tied to a recipe that's been passed down. We'll happily spend hours cooking it, whereas a weekday dinner is just thrown together in minutes.

**Q3. Are there any differences between the food people eat today and the food people ate in the past?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对有。 | Definitely. |
| 2 | 过去，人们 大多 吃 本地的、当季的东西，因为 他们 也 真 弄不到 别的多少。 | In the past, people mostly ate what was local and in season, since they couldn't really get much else. |
| 3 | 现在，不过，我们 有 来自世界各地的 食物 就在 家门口，所以 饮食 丰富得多。 | Now, though, we've got food from all over the world on our doorstep, so diets are far more varied. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，很多人 觉得 现代的食物 比以前 更加工、更不健康。 | Having said that, a lot of folk reckon modern food is more processed and less wholesome than before. |

> Definitely. In the past, people mostly ate what was local and in season, since they couldn't really get much else. Now, though, we've got food from all over the world on our doorstep, so diets are far more varied. Having said that, a lot of folk reckon modern food is more processed and less wholesome than before.

**Q4. Do people today prefer eating at home or in a restaurant?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 这 真的 取决于那个人，但 我会说 下馆子 最近 变得 受欢迎多了。 | It really depends on the person, but I'd say eating out has become a lot more popular lately. |
| 2 | 忙了长长的一天 之后，人们 常常 累得 做不动饭，所以 一家餐馆 感觉 像是 一种享受。 | After a long day, people are often too worn out to cook, so a restaurant feels like a treat. |
| 3 | 话虽如此，很多人 还是 更喜欢 家里做饭，因为 它 更便宜、而且 他们 确切知道 这顿饭里 放了什么。 | That said, plenty still prefer home cooking because it's cheaper and they know exactly what goes into the meal. |

> It really depends on the person, but I'd say eating out has become a lot more popular lately. After a long day, people are often too worn out to cook, so a restaurant feels like a treat. That said, plenty still prefer home cooking because it's cheaper and they know exactly what goes into the meal.

**Q5. Do you think traditional festival foods will disappear in the future?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 怀疑 它们 会 完全 消失，但 它们 可能 淡一点。 | I doubt they'll vanish completely, but they might fade a bit. |
| 2 | 年轻人 更忙、也 常常 懒得 去弄 那些 又长又琐碎的 食谱，所以 一些 传统菜 可能 被 忘掉。 | Younger folk are busier and often can't be bothered with long, fiddly recipes, so some traditional dishes could get forgotten. |
| 3 | 不过，节日 承载 强烈的 情感分量，所以 家庭 往往 让 那些关键的 食物 活着，即使 他们 把它们 简化、或者 就 买 现成的。 | Still, festivals carry strong emotional weight, so families tend to keep the key foods alive even if they simplify them or just buy them ready-made. |

> I doubt they'll vanish completely, but they might fade a bit. Younger folk are busier and often can't be bothered with long, fiddly recipes, so some traditional dishes could get forgotten. Still, festivals carry strong emotional weight, so families tend to keep the key foods alive even if they simplify them or just buy them ready-made.

**Q6. Why do many young people prefer fast food over traditional dishes?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 大多是 方便，我觉得。 | Mostly convenience, I reckon. |
| 2 | 年轻人 过着 忙乱的生活，所以 快餐 很吸引人——它 便宜、快、而且 之后 没有 碗要洗。 | Young people lead hectic lives, so fast food is appealing — it's cheap, quick, and there's no washing up afterwards. |
| 3 | 传统菜，相比之下，要 花很久 去准备。 | Traditional dishes, by contrast, take ages to prepare. |
| 4 | 此外，快餐 被 大力 营销、也 尝起来 挺上瘾，所以 它是 一个 容易 陷进去的习惯，当 你 赶时间的时候。 | Plus, fast food is heavily marketed and tastes pretty addictive, so it's an easy habit to slip into when you're rushed. |

> Mostly convenience, I reckon. Young people lead hectic lives, so fast food is appealing — it's cheap, quick, and there's no washing up afterwards. Traditional dishes, by contrast, take ages to prepare. Plus, fast food is heavily marketed and tastes pretty addictive, so it's an easy habit to slip into when you're rushed.

**P3 句型/模板**：`sharing a special dish is a lovely way to bring everyone together`（动名词主语）· `a recipe that's been passed down`（that后置）· `thrown together in minutes` · `what was local and in season`（what主语）· `food … on our doorstep` · `too worn out to cook` · `what goes into the meal`（embedded）· `keep the key foods alive` · `an easy habit to slip into when you're rushed`。
