## P2-新15 · A person good at learning languages — wife（Person）

> **Cue**: 怎么认识 / 怎么学 / 会哪些语言 / 你的感受 · 题型 Person

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/28 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改）

> 你自己练过的一整套：P2 = 老婆学语言（你的真实版）；P3 = 你当天练的 6 题（教育/学习域）。**不降档**，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · 老婆学语言）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我想聊的那个人 是 我 老婆，（她 真的 很 擅长 学 语言）。 | The person I'd like to talk about is my wife, who's really good at learning languages. |
| 2 | 我们 当年 在 高中 认识，做 了 好几年 同学，一直 到 我们 读完 大学——而 我们 现在 已经 结婚 十多年 了。 | We met back in high school and stayed classmates for years, right up until we finished college — and we've been married for over ten years now. |
| 3 | 去年，她 开始 学 日语。 | Last year, she started learning Japanese. |
| 4 | 我们 去过 日本 几次，而 她 每一次 都 很 爱，所以 我 觉得 她 想 说 流利 的 主要 原因 是 她 下次 旅行 能 更 投入。 | We've been to Japan a few times and she's loved it every single time, so I think the main reason she wants to get fluent is so she can get more involved next time she travels. |
| 5 | 她 学 的 方式 是 靠 啃 教材，而 有时候 她 也 看 卡通 和 动漫。 | The way she learns is by working through textbooks, and sometimes she watches cartoons and anime too. |
| 6 | 她 真的 坚持——她 每一天 都 啃 掉 一点点。 | She really sticks at it — she chips away at it a little every single day. |
| 7 | 她 就是 有 一个 真正的 语言 天赋。 | She's just got a real talent for languages. |
| 8 | 她 会 说 中文、英语、和 几种 方言，而 现在 她 正 把 日语 加 到 那 单子 上。 | She speaks Chinese, English and a couple of dialects, and now she's adding Japanese to the list. |
| 9 | 我 真正 佩服 她 的 是 她的 毅力。 | What I really admire about her is her perseverance. |
| 10 | 她 从不 停止 学习，即使 当 她 手头 一 大堆 事——工作、带 孩子、那种 东西。 | She never stops learning, even when she's got a lot on her plate — work, childcare, that kind of thing. |
| 11 | 说实话，她 为 我 和 我 儿子 都 树立 了 一个 很好的 榜样。 | Honestly, she sets a great example for both me and my son. |

> The person I'd like to talk about is my wife, who's really good at learning languages. We met back in high school and stayed classmates for years, right up until we finished college — and we've been married for over ten years now. Last year, she started learning Japanese. We've been to Japan a few times and she's loved it every single time, so I think the main reason she wants to get fluent is so she can get more involved next time she travels. The way she learns is by working through textbooks, and sometimes she watches cartoons and anime too. She really sticks at it — she chips away at it a little every single day. She's just got a real talent for languages. She speaks Chinese, English and a couple of dialects, and now she's adding Japanese to the list. What I really admire about her is her perseverance. She never stops learning, even when she's got a lot on her plate — work, childcare, that kind of thing. Honestly, she sets a great example for both me and my son.

**P3（你练的 6 题 · 教育/学习）**

**你Q1. Are there many people who can speak foreign languages in your country?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 是的，挺 多，我会说。 | Yeah, quite a few, I'd say. |
| 2 | 在 中国，我们 从 小学 开始 学 英语，而 它 一直 延续 到 大学。 | In China, we start learning English in primary school, and it carries on all the way through university. |
| 3 | 但 说句公道话，我们 大多数 人 还是 说 得 没那么 流利——我们 大多 专注 阅读 而不是 说 和 听，所以 我们 读 得 好好的，但 一 要 说话 就 卡住。 | But to be fair, most of us still can't speak it that fluently — we mostly focus on reading rather than speaking and listening, so we can read it fine but freeze up when we have to talk. |

> Yeah, quite a few, I'd say. In China, we start learning English in primary school, and it carries on all the way through university. But to be fair, most of us still can't speak it that fluently — we mostly focus on reading rather than speaking and listening, so we can read it fine but freeze up when we have to talk.

**你Q2. Does speaking other languages help at work?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它 取决于 那 工作，真的。 | It depends on the job, really. |
| 2 | 对 那些 跟 外国 客户 打交道 的 行业，你 显然 需要 会 说 那 语言。 | For industries that deal with foreign customers, you obviously need to speak the language. |
| 3 | 但 有 一大堆 工作 只 处理 国内的 事——比如 一个 本地 店主 或 一个 出租车 司机——（那里 你 永远 不 真的 需要 它）。 | But there are loads of jobs that only deal with domestic stuff — like a local shop owner or a taxi driver — where you'd never really need it. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，如果 他们 确实 碰巧 会 说 一门，它 仍然 能 偶尔 派上 用场，比如 帮 一个 外国 游客。 | That said, if they do happen to speak one, it can still come in handy now and then, like helping out a foreign tourist. |

> It depends on the job, really. For industries that deal with foreign customers, you obviously need to speak the language. But there are loads of jobs that only deal with domestic stuff — like a local shop owner or a taxi driver — where you'd never really need it. That said, if they do happen to speak one, it can still come in handy now and then, like helping out a foreign tourist.

**你Q3. Do people learn languages other than English?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 是的，绝对。 | Yeah, definitely. |
| 2 | 诚然，英语 是 最 通用的 语言，而 它 在 这里 从 小学 起 就是 一门 主科。 | Admittedly, English is the most universal language, and it's a core subject right from primary school here. |
| 3 | 但 有些人 更喜欢 学 别的 语言——比如 日语 或 韩语——胜过 英语，主要 因为 他们 真的 很 迷 动漫 或 韩剧。 | But some people prefer picking up other languages — Japanese or Korean, say — over English, mainly because they're really into anime or K-dramas. |
| 4 | 所以 它 常常 归结到 他们 个人 迷 什么。 | So it often comes down to what they're personally into. |

> Yeah, definitely. Admittedly, English is the most universal language, and it's a core subject right from primary school here. But some people prefer picking up other languages — Japanese or Korean, say — over English, mainly because they're really into anime or K-dramas. So it often comes down to what they're personally into.

**你Q4. Why is it easier for children to learn new things than adults?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 主要 是 习惯，我会说。 | Mainly habit, I'd say. |
| 2 | 大人 已经 见过、做过 这么 多，以至于 他们 习惯了 这 世界 本来的 样子，所以 他们 倾向于 固守 老一套、不再 逼 自己。 | Adults have already seen and done so much that they're used to the way the world is, so they tend to stay stuck in their ways and stop pushing themselves. |
| 3 | 而 小孩 灵活 得 多——他们 像 海绵，总是 在 提问、吸收 新 东西。 | Whereas kids are far more flexible — they're like sponges, always questioning and soaking up new things. |
| 4 | 此外，他们 不 怕 犯错，所以 他们 就 一头 扎 进去。 | Plus, they're not afraid of making mistakes, so they just dive in. |

> Mainly habit, I'd say. Adults have already seen and done so much that they're used to the way the world is, so they tend to stay stuck in their ways and stop pushing themselves. Whereas kids are far more flexible — they're like sponges, always questioning and soaking up new things. Plus, they're not afraid of making mistakes, so they just dive in.

**你Q5. How do people learn new things?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 一大堆 方式，真的。 | Loads of ways, really. |
| 2 | 在 网上 看 教程 大概 是 最 容易、最 便宜的 一个——几乎 任何 东西 都 有 一个 视频，从 正经的 大学 课程，到 一个 有经验的人 手把手 带 你，到 一步步 的 how-to 演示。 | Watching tutorials online is probably the easiest and cheapest one — there's a video out there for pretty much anything, from proper university courses, to someone experienced just walking you through it, to step-by-step how-to demos. |
| 3 | 除此之外，面对面 问 朋友 或 一个 有经验的人 也 很棒，因为 你 得到 实用的 提示、（那种 契合 你 自己 情况的）。 | On top of that, asking friends or someone experienced face-to-face is great too, since you get practical tips that fit your own situation. |
| 4 | 此外，老老实实 啃 一本 教材 总是 值得 一试。 | Plus, good old working through a textbook is always worth a try. |

> Loads of ways, really. Watching tutorials online is probably the easiest and cheapest one — there's a video out there for pretty much anything, from proper university courses, to someone experienced just walking you through it, to step-by-step how-to demos. On top of that, asking friends or someone experienced face-to-face is great too, since you get practical tips that fit your own situation. Plus, good old working through a textbook is always worth a try.

**你Q6. What's the most important thing for learning a language well?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 对 我 来说，那 一定 是 真正 用 它 去 交流——那 就是 我们 一开始 学 一门 语言 的 全部 原因。 | For me, it's got to be actually using it to communicate — that's the whole reason we learn a language in the first place. |
| 2 | 通过 跟 真实的人 说话，你 学会 怎么 表达 自己、理解 别人。 | By talking to real people, you learn how to express yourself and understand others. |
| 3 | 你 不该 做的 是 只 埋 在 语法书 和 单词表 里——说实话，那 就是 我们 大多数 人 掉 进 的 陷阱。 | What you shouldn't do is just bury yourself in grammar books and word lists — honestly, that's the trap most of us fall into. |
| 4 | 所以 到头来，一门 语言 是 一个 用来 跟 人 说话 的 工具；你 只有 靠 用 它 才 真正 学会 它。 | So at the end of the day, a language is a tool for talking to people; you only really learn it by using it. |

> For me, it's got to be actually using it to communicate — that's the whole reason we learn a language in the first place. By talking to real people, you learn how to express yourself and understand others. What you shouldn't do is just bury yourself in grammar books and word lists — honestly, that's the trap most of us fall into. So at the end of the day, a language is a tool for talking to people; you only really learn it by using it.

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个人 是 我老婆，（那个 说实话 是 最有天赋的 语言学习者之一、我 遇到过的）。 | So, the person I'd like to talk about is my wife, who's honestly one of the most gifted language learners I've ever come across. |
| [how met] | 我们 在一起 好些年了，而 早早 我就 注意到 她 能 用英语 聊得飞起、不费吹灰之力，（那 真的 打动了我）。 | We've been together for years now, and early on I noticed she could chat away in English without breaking a sweat, which really impressed me. |
| [how learns] | 她 学起 语言 的那种方式 挺厉害的。 | The way she picks up languages is pretty impressive. |
| [how·细] | 她 其实 不太 抱着 课本 坐下来——反而，她 刷 日剧和动漫 刷个不停，而且 她 总在 用 语言 app 练习、在 她通勤的时候。 | She doesn't really sit down with textbooks — instead, she binge-watches Japanese dramas and anime, and she's always practising on language apps during her commute. |
| [how·收] | 基本上，她 就是 一头 扎进去。 | Basically, she just throws herself into it. |
| [what speak] | 至于 她 能说什么，她 英语 流利，而且 除此之外 她 自学了 日语、到了 一个地步、（在那里 她 能 进行 一场 正经的对话）。 | As for what she can speak, she's fluent in English, and on top of that she's taught herself Japanese to the point where she can hold a proper conversation. |
| [amaze] | 她 全靠 自己 做到这一切 这件事 到现在 还 让我 惊叹。 | The fact that she did it all on her own still amazes me. |
| [feel/EL] | 我 真正 佩服她的东西 是 她 有多 自然地 跟人 建立联系。 | What I really admire about her is how naturally she connects with people. |
| [closer] | 说到底，她 是那种人、（那种 让 学习 看起来 毫不费力的），而 那 就是 我为什么 仰望她。 | At the end of the day, she's the kind of person who makes learning look effortless, and that's why I look up to her. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the person I'd like to talk about is my wife, who's honestly one of the most gifted language learners I've ever come across. We've been together for years now, and early on I noticed she could chat away in English without breaking a sweat, which really impressed me. The way she picks up languages is pretty impressive. She doesn't really sit down with textbooks — instead, she binge-watches Japanese dramas and anime, and she's always practising on language apps during her commute. Basically, she just throws herself into it. As for what she can speak, she's fluent in English, and on top of that she's taught herself Japanese to the point where she can hold a proper conversation. The fact that she did it all on her own still amazes me. What I really admire about her is how naturally she connects with people. At the end of the day, she's the kind of person who makes learning look effortless, and that's why I look up to her.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（人）+ 最…之一**：我想聊的那个人是[X]、（那个是最…之一、我遇到过的）→ `the person I'd like to talk about is [X], who's one of the most … I've ever come across`
- **不费力 + which**：她能用英语聊得飞起、不费吹灰之力，那打动了我 → `she could chat away … without breaking a sweat, which really impressed me`
- **一头扎进**：她就是一头扎进去 → `she just throws herself into it`
- **自学到…地步**：她自学日语到了能正经对话的地步 → `taught herself Japanese to the point where she can hold a proper conversation`
- **the fact that 主语**：她全靠自己做到这一切这件事让我惊叹 → `The fact that she did it all on her own still amazes me`
- **what 主语 + how**：我真正佩服她的是她多自然地跟人建立联系 → `What I really admire about her is how naturally she connects with people`
- **收尾**：那种让学习看起来毫不费力的人 → `the kind of person who makes learning look effortless`

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Are there many people who can speak foreign languages in your country?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它 在 快速增长。 | Honestly, it's growing fast. |
| 2 | 英语 现在 从小学 就教，所以 大多数 年轻人 能 应付 至少 一场 基本的聊天。 | English is taught from primary school now, so most younger folk can manage at least a basic chat. |
| 3 | 但 流利 是 另一回事——很多人 能读能写，但 一 真到 要开口说 就 卡壳。 | But fluency's another story — plenty can read and write but freeze up when they've actually got to speak. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 那数字 在纸面上 看着 不错，虽然 真正的 对话能力 还 落后 不少。 | So I'd say the numbers look good on paper, though real conversational ability still lags a fair bit behind. |

> Honestly, it's growing fast. English is taught from primary school now, so most younger folk can manage at least a basic chat. But fluency's another story — plenty can read and write but freeze up when they've actually got to speak. So I'd say the numbers look good on paper, though real conversational ability still lags a fair bit behind.

**Q2. Does speaking other languages help at work?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 帮助巨大，我会说。 | Massively, I'd say. |
| 2 | 在 很多行业 你 打交道的是 国外的客户或伙伴，所以 能 切换语言 打开 一些门、（那些 本来 会 关着的）。 | In a lot of industries you're dealing with clients or partners abroad, so being able to switch languages opens doors that'd otherwise stay shut. |
| 3 | 比如，一个 会日语的人 可能 拿到 一个职位、（别人 甚至 都 申请不了的）。 | For instance, someone who speaks Japanese might land a role others can't even apply for. |
| 4 | 此外，它 建立 信任——人们 对你 热络得 更快，当 你 用 他们的语言 下功夫 的时候。 | Plus it builds trust — people warm to you faster when you make the effort in their tongue. |

> Massively, I'd say. In a lot of industries you're dealing with clients or partners abroad, so being able to switch languages opens doors that'd otherwise stay shut. For instance, someone who speaks Japanese might land a role others can't even apply for. Plus it builds trust — people warm to you faster when you make the effort in their tongue.

**Q3. Do people learn any languages other than English?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 是的，绝对，虽然 英语 仍然 占主导。 | Yeah, definitely, though English still dominates. |
| 2 | 在我老家，挺多人 捡起 日语或韩语，大多是因为 他们 迷上了 那些剧、动漫和音乐。 | Where I'm from, quite a few pick up Japanese or Korean, mostly because they're hooked on the dramas, anime and music. |
| 3 | 另一些人 去学 法语或德语，为了 工作 或 出国留学。 | Others go for French or German for work or study abroad. |
| 4 | 所以 它 通常 是 由 要么 流行文化、要么 职业规划 驱动的，而不是 学校 硬塞 给他们。 | So it's usually driven by either pop culture or career plans, rather than school forcing it on them. |

> Yeah, definitely, though English still dominates. Where I'm from, quite a few pick up Japanese or Korean, mostly because they're hooked on the dramas, anime and music. Others go for French or German for work or study abroad. So it's usually driven by either pop culture or career plans, rather than school forcing it on them.

**Q4. Why is it easier for children to learn new things than for adults?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我觉得 主要是因为 孩子的大脑 还在 自己 搭线，所以 他们 吸收 新的模式、不 想太多。 | I reckon it's mainly because kids' brains are still wiring themselves up, so they soak up new patterns without overthinking. |
| 2 | 大人 往往 分析 每件事、也 担心 显得傻，（那 拖慢 他们）。 | Adults tend to analyse everything and worry about looking silly, which slows them down. |
| 3 | 比如，一个孩子 会 就 脱口而出 一个外语词、也不 在乎 它 对不对，而 大人 犹豫 太多了。 | For instance, a child will just blurt out a foreign word and not care if it's wrong, whereas grown-ups hesitate far too much. |

> I reckon it's mainly because kids' brains are still wiring themselves up, so they soak up new patterns without overthinking. Adults tend to analyse everything and worry about looking silly, which slows them down. For instance, a child will just blurt out a foreign word and not care if it's wrong, whereas grown-ups hesitate far too much.

**Q5. How do people learn new things?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，人们 学 用 一大堆 不同的方式。 | Well, people learn in loads of different ways. |
| 2 | 有些人 动手型、只有 靠做 才真的懂，而 另一些人 需要 先 读一读、理解 理论。 | Some are hands-on and only really get it by doing, while others need to read up first and understand the theory. |
| 3 | 我会说 重复 是 那个 共同的线索，不过——你 很少 一次 就 搞定 任何东西。 | I'd say repetition's the common thread, though — you rarely nail anything on the first go. |
| 4 | 说实话，犯错、然后 改正它们 大概 就是 我们大多数人 进步 的方式。 | Honestly, making mistakes and fixing them is probably how most of us improve. |

> Well, people learn in loads of different ways. Some are hands-on and only really get it by doing, while others need to read up first and understand the theory. I'd say repetition's the common thread, though — you rarely nail anything on the first go. Honestly, making mistakes and fixing them is probably how most of us improve.

**Q6. What is the most important thing for learning a language well?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 对我来说，是 持续——每天 一点点 地 啃它 胜过 一周一次 猛塞好几个小时。 | For me, it's consistency — chipping away at it a little every day beats cramming for hours once a week. |
| 2 | 但 如果 我 挑 一样东西，我会说 你 得 真正地 用 那语言，而不只是 学它。 | But if I'm picking one thing, I'd say you've got to actually use the language, not just study it. |
| 3 | 跟 真人 聊天，哪怕 聊得 很烂，很快 就 建立 信心、也 阻止 那些词 溜走，一旦 你 停止复习。 | Chatting with real people, even badly, builds confidence fast and stops the words slipping away the moment you stop revising. |

> For me, it's consistency — chipping away at it a little every day beats cramming for hours once a week. But if I'm picking one thing, I'd say you've got to actually use the language, not just study it. Chatting with real people, even badly, builds confidence fast and stops the words slipping away the moment you stop revising.

**P3 句型/模板**：`freeze up when they've actually got to speak` · `the numbers look good on paper, though … lags behind` · `being able to switch languages opens doors that'd otherwise stay shut`（动名词主语+that后置）· `someone who speaks Japanese might land a role others can't apply for`（who后置）· `kids' brains are still wiring themselves up` · `which slows them down`（which后置）· `chipping away at it … beats cramming …`（动名词主语比较）· `making mistakes and fixing them is how most of us improve`（动名词主语+embedded）。

## P2-新16 · A challenging tech problem — 笔记本死机（speaker）

> **Cue**: 什么问题 / 何时何地 / 多难 / 怎么解决 · 题型 Event

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/29 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改）

> 你自己练过的一整套：P2 = 系统宕机 debug（你的例子，和上面卡的"笔记本死机"同 cue、不同故事）；P3 = 你当天练的 **6 个 AI 域** follow-up（和下面 examples 的 P3 题目不同，都是你的真实产出）。这套**不降档**——你练过、你觉得好的词/结构原样保留，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · 系统宕机）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我想聊的那个问题 是 有一次、（当 我们整个 业务系统 宕机的时候）。 | The problem I'd like to talk about is one time when our whole business system went down. |
| 2 | 我 是 一个 软件工程师，而 那时候 我 负责 那些 后端 服务。 | I'm a software engineer, and back then I was in charge of the back-end services. |
| 3 | 那问题 是 我们的一个服务 停止 响应 请求，所以 用户 根本 没法 登录 或 下单——而 对 我们的业务，那 意味着 我们 每一秒 都在 亏钱。 | The problem was that one of our services stopped responding to requests, so users couldn't log in or place orders at all — and for our business, that meant we were losing money every second. |
| 4 | 那 大约 是 一年前。 | It was about a year ago. |
| 5 | 一个 深夜，我 在家，当 我的手机 开始 嗡嗡 响着 警报。 | Late one night, I was at home when my phone started buzzing with alerts. |
| 6 | 让 它 这么 难的 是 那 根本原因 不 明显——那系统 已经 好几个月 运行 正常。 | What made it so hard was that the root cause wasn't obvious — the system had been running fine for months. |
| 7 | 首先，我 试着 止血、靠 回滚 那 最新的 部署，但 那 没用，所以 我 只能 一步一步 排查。 | First, I tried to stop the bleeding by rolling back the latest deployment, but that didn't work, so I had to work through it step by step. |
| 8 | 我 查 那些 警告 和 错误 日志、什么 也 没找到，然后 我 看 那 网络流量。 | I checked the warning and error logs and found nothing, then I looked at the network traffic. |
| 9 | 在 大约 三十分钟 之内，我 追查到 那 原因：一个 节点 在 重负载下 挂了，但 我们的网关 没 察觉 到，所以 它们 一直 把 流量 路由 给 它。 | Within about thirty minutes, I tracked down the cause: one of the nodes had failed under heavy load, but our gateways didn't pick that up, so they kept routing traffic to it. |
| 10 | 我 修好 它、靠 把 那 节点 从 集群里 拔出来，而 一切 立刻 恢复了。 | I fixed it by pulling that node out of the cluster, and everything came back up right away. |
| 11 | 所以，对，差不多 就是 这样。 | So yeah, that's pretty much it. |

> The problem I'd like to talk about is one time when our whole business system went down. I'm a software engineer, and back then I was in charge of the back-end services. The problem was that one of our services stopped responding to requests, so users couldn't log in or place orders at all — and for our business, that meant we were losing money every second. It was about a year ago. Late one night, I was at home when my phone started buzzing with alerts. What made it so hard was that the root cause wasn't obvious — the system had been running fine for months. First, I tried to stop the bleeding by rolling back the latest deployment, but that didn't work, so I had to work through it step by step. I checked the warning and error logs and found nothing, then I looked at the network traffic. Within about thirty minutes, I tracked down the cause: one of the nodes had failed under heavy load, but our gateways didn't pick that up, so they kept routing traffic to it. I fixed it by pulling that node out of the cluster, and everything came back up right away. So yeah, that's pretty much it.

**P3（你练的 6 题 · AI 域）**

**你Q1. Advantages and disadvantages of AI?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它 真的 是 好坏参半，说实话。 | It's a real mixed bag, honestly. |
| 2 | 好的一面，AI 帮 人们 做 一些 事、（那种 超出 我们 能力的）——比如 处理 大量 数据 或 当场 翻译 语言。 | On the plus side, AI helps people do things that are beyond us — like crunching huge amounts of data or translating languages on the spot. |
| 3 | 它 也 把 那些 无聊的 杂活 从 我们 肩上 拿走，（这）省 一大堆 时间。 | It also takes the boring chores off our plate, which saves loads of time. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，不过，太 依赖 它 会 一点点 削掉 我们 自己的 技能，不管 是 学习 还是 工作。 | To be fair, though, relying on it too much can chip away at our own skills, whether at study or work. |
| 5 | 所以 总的来说，它 归结到 我们 怎么 用 它。 | So overall, it comes down to how we use it. |

> It's a real mixed bag, honestly. On the plus side, AI helps people do things that are beyond us — like crunching huge amounts of data or translating languages on the spot. It also takes the boring chores off our plate, which saves loads of time. To be fair, though, relying on it too much can chip away at our own skills, whether at study or work. So overall, it comes down to how we use it.

**你Q2. Should people today learn about AI?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，是的。 | Definitely, yeah. |
| 2 | AI 是 现在 最 强大的 工具 之一，所以 我们 应该 跟上 时代，不然 我们 会 落后。 | AI's one of the most powerful tools out there now, so we should keep up with the times, or we'll fall behind. |
| 3 | 你 不 需要 成为 一个 专家——只要 那些 基础 就 够了。 | You don't need to be an expert — just the basics will do. |
| 4 | 说实话，AI 会 重塑 我们 怎么 工作 和 学习，所以 我们 越早 掌握 它，越 好。 | Honestly, AI's going to reshape how we work and study, so the sooner we get to grips with it, the better. |

> Definitely, yeah. AI's one of the most powerful tools out there now, so we should keep up with the times, or we'll fall behind. You don't need to be an expert — just the basics will do. Honestly, AI's going to reshape how we work and study, so the sooner we get to grips with it, the better.

**你Q3. Should children learn to use AI?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 是的，在 某种程度上。 | Yeah, to some extent. |
| 2 | AI 其实 是 一个 强大 又 有耐心的 老师——你 可以 问 它 绝对 任何 东西、还 能 得到 一个 挺 靠谱的 答案。 | AI's actually a powerful and patient teacher — you can ask it absolutely anything and still get a pretty solid answer. |
| 3 | 但 孩子 不该 太 早 依赖 它，因为 AI 确实 会 犯 错。 | But kids shouldn't lean on it too early, because AI does make mistakes. |
| 4 | 所以 学会 独立 思考 得 排 在 第一——AI 应该 辅助 那个，而不是 取代 它。 | So learning to think for themselves has to come first — AI should back that up, not replace it. |

> Yeah, to some extent. AI's actually a powerful and patient teacher — you can ask it absolutely anything and still get a pretty solid answer. But kids shouldn't lean on it too early, because AI does make mistakes. So learning to think for themselves has to come first — AI should back that up, not replace it.

**你Q4. How can AI help in our lives?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 一大堆 方式，真的。 | Loads of ways, really. |
| 2 | AI 打理 那些 日常 杂活——比如 规划 旅行，或 自动 收拢 我 感兴趣的 新闻，那种 东西。 | AI takes care of everyday chores — like planning trips, or automatically rounding up the news I'm into, that kind of thing. |
| 3 | 它 也 能 教 我 怎么 学 一个 新 东西，（这）我 觉得 比 只是 递给 我 一个 答案 更 重要。 | It can also show me how to learn something new, which I think matters more than just handing me an answer. |
| 4 | 有时候，说实话，它 让 我 感觉 有点 没用，因为 AI 总是 做 得 比 我 好。 | Sometimes, to be honest, it makes me feel a bit useless, because AI always does it better than me. |

> Loads of ways, really. AI takes care of everyday chores — like planning trips, or automatically rounding up the news I'm into, that kind of thing. It can also show me how to learn something new, which I think matters more than just handing me an answer. Sometimes, to be honest, it makes me feel a bit useless, because AI always does it better than me.

**你Q5. Are students overly reliant on AI?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 有点，说实话。 | A bit, to be honest. |
| 2 | 对 学生 来说 用 AI 做 作业 太 方便 了——他们 做的 全部 就是 复制粘贴、不 动脑子。 | It's just so convenient for students to do their homework with AI — all they do is copy and paste without thinking. |
| 3 | 但 我 不 觉得 这 真的 是 他们的 错；说实话，没人 能 抵抗 那个 诱惑。 | But I don't think it's really their fault; honestly, nobody can resist the temptation. |
| 4 | 所以 我们 需要 做的 是 教 学生 怎么 正确 用 它，也许 再 设 一些 限制。 | So what we need to do is show students how to use it properly, and maybe put a few limits in place. |

> A bit, to be honest. It's just so convenient for students to do their homework with AI — all they do is copy and paste without thinking. But I don't think it's really their fault; honestly, nobody can resist the temptation. So what we need to do is show students how to use it properly, and maybe put a few limits in place.

**你Q6. What can teachers do to stop over-reliance on AI?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 几样 东西，真的。 | A few things, really. |
| 2 | 一来，老师 可以 布置 AI 做不了的 作业——动手的 任务，那种 东西。 | For one, teachers can set homework AI can't do — hands-on tasks, that kind of thing. |
| 3 | 除此之外，让 学生 在 课上 做 作业、在 监督下，也 是 一个 好 主意。 | On top of that, getting students to do the work in class, under supervision, is a good idea too. |
| 4 | 除此之外，老师 可以 反过来 用 AI——去 检查 学生 是否 真的 自己 做了 作业。 | Besides that, teachers can use AI the other way round — to check whether students actually did the work themselves. |

> A few things, really. For one, teachers can set homework AI can't do — hands-on tasks, that kind of thing. On top of that, getting students to do the work in class, under supervision, is a good idea too. Besides that, teachers can use AI the other way round — to check whether students actually did the work themselves.

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个问题 是 一次 科技惊魂、（我经历的），当时 我的笔记本 突然 死机、撂挑子了，就在 一个 大的工作截止日期 之前。 | So, the problem I'd like to talk about is a tech scare I had when my laptop suddenly died on me, right before a big work deadline. |
| [when/where] | 它 发生 大概 两年前，一个傍晚 在家。 | It happened about two years ago, at home one evening. |
| [problem·细] | 我 那会儿 正在 收尾 一份报告、（第二天早上要交的），而 突然间 屏幕 冻住了，然后 整个东西 甚至 都开不回来了。 | I'd been finishing off a report due the next morning, and out of nowhere the screen froze, then the whole thing wouldn't even turn back on. |
| [problem·细] | 更糟的是，我老婆 也 需要 那台笔记本、[用来]弄 我们的家庭照片。 | To make it worse, my wife also needed that laptop for our family photos. |
| [how hard] | 说实话，它 挺让人紧张的。 | It was honestly pretty stressful. |
| [how hard·细] | 我所有的文件 都在 那上面，而 我 已经 好几周 没 备份 任何东西，所以 我 确信 我 把那一堆 都丢了。 | All my files were on there, and I hadn't backed anything up for weeks, so I was convinced I'd lost the lot. |
| [how hard·细] | 我 一直 按 电源键、好像 那 会 不知怎么 就 修好它，（那 当然 没修好）。 | I kept pressing the power button like that would somehow fix it, which of course it didn't. |
| [how solved] | 到最后，我 冷静下来、在网上 查了查。 | In the end, I calmed down and looked it up online. |
| [solved·细] | 我 弄明白 它 只是 一块 没电的电池 在捣鬼，所以 我 让它 充了一整晚，然后 它 就 活过来了。 | I figured out it was just a flat battery playing tricks, so I left it charging overnight and it came back to life. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那是 一次 科技惊魂、（我 不会 很快 忘掉的）。 | So yeah, that's a tech scare I won't forget in a hurry. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the problem I'd like to talk about is a tech scare I had when my laptop suddenly died on me, right before a big work deadline. It happened about two years ago, at home one evening. I'd been finishing off a report due the next morning, and out of nowhere the screen froze, then the whole thing wouldn't even turn back on. To make it worse, my wife also needed that laptop for our family photos. It was honestly pretty stressful. All my files were on there, and I hadn't backed anything up for weeks, so I was convinced I'd lost the lot. I kept pressing the power button like that would somehow fix it, which of course it didn't. In the end, I calmed down and looked it up online. I figured out it was just a flat battery playing tricks, so I left it charging overnight and it came back to life. So yeah, that's a tech scare I won't forget in a hurry.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（问题/事件）**：我想聊的那个问题是[X]、（我经历的），当时… → `the problem I'd like to talk about is [X] I had when …`
- **撂挑子/突然**：笔记本撂挑子了；突然间 → `my laptop died on me` / `out of nowhere`
- **确信全丢了**：我确信我把那一堆都丢了 → `I was convinced I'd lost the lot`
- **which后置**：好像那会修好它，那当然没修好 → `like that would fix it, which of course it didn't`
- **弄明白/复活**：我弄明白它只是… → `I figured out it was just …` / `came back to life`
- **收尾**：一次我不会很快忘掉的科技惊魂 → `a tech scare I won't forget in a hurry`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What are the advantages and disadvantages of AI?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，好的一面，AI 帮我们 省 一大堆 时间——它 能 起草 邮件、处理 数据、甚至 发现 一些模式、（那些我们 会漏掉的）。 | Well, on the plus side, AI saves us loads of time — it can draft emails, crunch data, even spot patterns we'd miss. |
| 2 | 坏处，说实话，是 人们 开始 太依赖它、然后 停止 自己思考。 | The downside, honestly, is that people start leaning on it too much and stop thinking for themselves. |
| 3 | 此外，还有 那份担忧、关于 工作 消失。 | Plus there's the worry about jobs disappearing. |
| 4 | 所以 它 很棒，但 你 得 明智地 用它。 | So it's brilliant, but you've got to use it wisely. |

> Well, on the plus side, AI saves us loads of time — it can draft emails, crunch data, even spot patterns we'd miss. The downside, honestly, is that people start leaning on it too much and stop thinking for themselves. Plus there's the worry about jobs disappearing. So it's brilliant, but you've got to use it wisely.

**Q2. Do you think people today should learn about AI technology?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，我觉得 它 现在 差不多 无法避免了。 | Absolutely, I reckon it's pretty much unavoidable now. |
| 2 | AI 正在 悄悄 爬进 每一份工作，所以 如果 你 不 掌握 那些基础，你 会 很快 落后。 | AI's creeping into every job, so if you don't get the basics, you'll fall behind quickly. |
| 3 | 我 不是 说 每个人 都需要 编程，但 知道 它 能做什么、不能做什么 真的 很有用。 | I'm not saying everyone needs to code, but knowing what it can and can't do is really handy. |
| 4 | 比如，甚至 检查 一个答案 是否 可靠 是 一项 值得拥有的技能。 | For instance, even checking whether an answer's reliable is a skill worth having. |

> Absolutely, I reckon it's pretty much unavoidable now. AI's creeping into every job, so if you don't get the basics, you'll fall behind quickly. I'm not saying everyone needs to code, but knowing what it can and can't do is really handy. For instance, even checking whether an answer's reliable is a skill worth having.

**Q3. Should children learn to use AI?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 是的，但 带着 限制。 | I'd say yes, but with limits. |
| 2 | 现在的孩子 是 被它包围着 长大的，所以 教他们 安全地 用它 就很有道理。 | Kids these days grow up surrounded by it, so teaching them to use it safely just makes sense. |
| 3 | 那个棘手的部分 是 他们 不该 靠它 做作业，在 他们 学会 自己思考 之前。 | The tricky bit is they shouldn't rely on it for homework before they've learnt to think on their own. |
| 4 | 比如，用 AI 去 解释 一个难懂的点子 没问题；抄答案 不行。 | For example, using AI to explain a tough idea is fine; copying answers isn't. |

> I'd say yes, but with limits. Kids these days grow up surrounded by it, so teaching them to use it safely just makes sense. The tricky bit is they shouldn't rely on it for homework before they've learnt to think on their own. For example, using AI to explain a tough idea is fine; copying answers isn't.

**Q4. How can AI help in our lives?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 用 一大堆方式，真的。 | In loads of ways, really. |
| 2 | 它 把 那些无聊的杂务 从我们盘子里 拿走——整理邮件、预约、即时翻译。 | It takes the boring chores off our plate — sorting emails, booking appointments, translating on the fly. |
| 3 | 它 也 是 一个 快速的 学习方式，因为 你 能 问它 任何东西、得到 一个 清楚的答案。 | It's also a quick way to learn, since you can ask it anything and get a clear answer. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，最大的赢 是 它 怎么 腾出 时间、给 那些 真正对我们要紧的东西。 | To be fair, the biggest win is how it frees up time for what actually matters to us. |

> In loads of ways, really. It takes the boring chores off our plate — sorting emails, booking appointments, translating on the fly. It's also a quick way to learn, since you can ask it anything and get a clear answer. To be fair, the biggest win is how it frees up time for what actually matters to us.

**Q5. Do you think students are overly reliant on AI?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，相当一部分 是。 | Honestly, a fair few are. |
| 2 | 我 注意到 有些学生 就 把 一个问题 粘进去、然后 抄 蹦出来的 任何东西，没有 真正 消化它。 | I've noticed some students just paste a question in and copy whatever pops out, without really digesting it. |
| 3 | 那个危险 是 他们 从没 自己 建起 那项技能，所以 他们 卡住，当 那个工具 不在 的时候。 | The danger is they never build the skill themselves, so they're stuck when the tool isn't around. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，不是 他们所有人——很多人 还是 把它 当成 一个帮手，而不是 一根拐杖。 | That said, it's not all of them — plenty still use it as a helper, not a crutch. |

> Honestly, a fair few are. I've noticed some students just paste a question in and copy whatever pops out, without really digesting it. The danger is they never build the skill themselves, so they're stuck when the tool isn't around. That said, it's not all of them — plenty still use it as a helper, not a crutch.

**Q6. What can teachers do to stop students from relying too much on AI?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，最简单的解法 是 布置 一些任务、（那种 AI 不容易 应付的）——像 课堂讨论 或者 手写的作业。 | Well, the simplest fix is to set tasks that AI can't easily handle — things like in-class discussions or handwritten work. |
| 2 | 老师 也 可以 让 学生 出声地 解释 他们的推理，因为 你 没法 假装 理解。 | Teachers could also ask students to explain their reasoning out loud, since you can't fake understanding. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，禁掉它 行不通，所以 更聪明的做法 是 教 孩子 什么时候 靠它 真的 有帮助。 | To be fair, banning it won't work, so the smarter move is teaching kids when leaning on it actually helps. |

> Well, the simplest fix is to set tasks that AI can't easily handle — things like in-class discussions or handwritten work. Teachers could also ask students to explain their reasoning out loud, since you can't fake understanding. To be fair, banning it won't work, so the smarter move is teaching kids when leaning on it actually helps.

**P3 句型/模板**：`spot patterns we'd miss`（relative后置）· `people start leaning on it too much` · `knowing what it can and can't do is really handy`（动名词主语+embedded）· `a skill worth having` · `grow up surrounded by it` · `using AI … is fine; copying answers isn't`（动名词主语对照）· `takes the boring chores off our plate` · `how it frees up time for what actually matters`（embedded）· `as a helper, not a crutch` · `banning it won't work`（动名词主语）。

## P2-新17 · An advertisement with a famous person — 刘翔运动服广告（speaker）

> **Cue**: 是谁 / 哪里能看到 / 广告讲什么 / 你的感受 · 题型 Object/Event

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个广告 是 一个、（那种 主演 是 刘翔的），那位 著名的 中国跨栏运动员、（那个 多年前 拿了 奥运金牌的）。 | So, the advertisement I'd like to talk about is one starring Liu Xiang, the famous Chinese hurdler who won Olympic gold years ago. |
| [背景] | 他 在这儿 仍然 基本上 是 一个家喻户晓的名字。 | He's still pretty much a household name here. |
| [where see] | 你 到处 都能看到它——在 我们地铁站 附近的 广告牌上，而且 它 在视频之前 在网上 弹出来。 | You can see it all over the place — on billboards near our subway station, and it pops up online before videos. |
| [where·细] | 它是 一个广告、给 一个 知名的 运动服品牌的，那种、（很难 错过的）。 | It's a commercial for a well-known sportswear brand, the kind that's hard to miss. |
| [about] | 那广告 本身 相当 简单。 | The ad itself is fairly simple. |
| [about·细] | 刘翔 在雨中 训练，然后 他 冲刺、慢镜头 跨过 那些栏，而 在结尾 就只有 一句 短短的话、关于 永不放弃。 | Liu Xiang's training in the rain, then he sprints and clears the hurdles in slow motion, and at the end there's just a short line about never giving up. |
| [about·收] | 没有 多少字，（那 我觉得 让它 更有力量）。 | There aren't many words, which I think makes it more powerful. |
| [feel] | 说实话，我 挺喜欢它。 | Honestly, I quite like it. |
| [feel·EL] | 我 觉得 吸引人的东西 是 它 不只是 卖鞋——它 有点 在 卖 一种感觉。 | What I find appealing is that it doesn't just sell shoes — it sort of sells a feeling. |
| [feel·细] | 除此之外，它 真的 鼓舞人心，即使 它 只是 在 推销 一个产品。 | On top of that, it's genuinely inspiring, even though it's only promoting a product. |
| [feel·细] | 甚至 我儿子 都 认出了他、还 模仿了 那个 跑步的姿势。 | Even my son recognised him and copied the running pose. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那是 一个广告、（真的 一直 留在我心里的）。 | So yeah, that's an ad that really stuck with me. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the advertisement I'd like to talk about is one starring Liu Xiang, the famous Chinese hurdler who won Olympic gold years ago. He's still pretty much a household name here. You can see it all over the place — on billboards near our subway station, and it pops up online before videos. It's a commercial for a well-known sportswear brand, the kind that's hard to miss. The ad itself is fairly simple. Liu Xiang's training in the rain, then he sprints and clears the hurdles in slow motion, and at the end there's just a short line about never giving up. There aren't many words, which I think makes it more powerful. Honestly, I quite like it. What I find appealing is that it doesn't just sell shoes — it sort of sells a feeling. On top of that, it's genuinely inspiring, even though it's only promoting a product. Even my son recognised him and copied the running pose. So yeah, that's an ad that really stuck with me.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（广告）+ 主演**：我想聊的那个广告是一个、（主演是[X]的），那位…的 → `the advertisement I'd like to talk about is one starring [X], the … who …`
- **家喻户晓/到处可见**：家喻户晓的名字；到处都能看到；很难错过 → `a household name` / `all over the place` / `hard to miss`
- **which后置**：没多少字，那我觉得让它更有力量 → `…, which I think makes it more powerful`
- **what主语**：我觉得吸引人的是它不只卖鞋——它卖一种感觉 → `What I find appealing is that it doesn't just sell shoes — it sells a feeling`
- **收尾**：一个真的留在我心里的广告 → `an ad that really stuck with me`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What are the advantages and disadvantages of advertisements?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，大的好处 是 广告 让我们 知情——它们 告诉我们 外面 有什么、还 常常 标出 好的优惠、（那些我们 本来 会错过的）。 | Well, the big plus is that adverts keep us informed — they tell us what's out there and often flag good deals we'd otherwise miss. |
| 2 | 坏的一面，不过，它们 可能 很咄咄逼人、也 有点 操纵性，推着 人们 买 东西、（那些他们 其实 不需要的）。 | On the downside, though, they can be pushy and a bit manipulative, nudging people to buy stuff they don't really need. |
| 3 | 所以说实话 它 好坏参半——诚实的时候 好用，不诚实的时候 烦人。 | So honestly it's a mixed bag — handy when they're honest, annoying when they're not. |

> Well, the big plus is that adverts keep us informed — they tell us what's out there and often flag good deals we'd otherwise miss. On the downside, though, they can be pushy and a bit manipulative, nudging people to buy stuff they don't really need. So honestly it's a mixed bag — handy when they're honest, annoying when they're not.

**Q2. Why are many advertisements endorsed by celebrities? How useful are they?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 这是因为 名人 立刻 抓住 你的注意力、也 借 给 一个品牌 一些信任。 | I'd say it's because celebrities instantly grab your attention and lend a brand some trust. |
| 2 | 如果 一个人、（你 崇拜的）为一个产品 站台，你 就 有点 假设 它 不错。 | If someone you admire backs a product, you sort of assume it's decent. |
| 3 | 至于 有多有用，它 真的 看情况——它们 对 形象和知名度 起效很好，但 如果 那明星 跟 产品 毫无关系，人们 就 看穿它、它 就 砸了。 | As for how useful, it really depends — they work well for image and awareness, but if the star has nothing to do with the product, people see through it and it falls flat. |

> I'd say it's because celebrities instantly grab your attention and lend a brand some trust. If someone you admire backs a product, you sort of assume it's decent. As for how useful, it really depends — they work well for image and awareness, but if the star has nothing to do with the product, people see through it and it falls flat.

**Q3. What is the most important factor in an advertisement?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 对我来说，是 一个广告 有多 让人记得住。 | For me, it's how memorable an advert is. |
| 2 | 你 可以 有 一个聪明的点子，但 如果 事后 没人 记得它，它 基本上 是 浪费的钱。 | You can have a clever idea, but if nobody recalls it afterwards, it's basically wasted money. |
| 3 | 一句 朗朗上口的话 或者 一个 醒目的画面 让 一个品牌 卡在你脑子里。 | A catchy line or a striking image makes a brand stick in your head. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，相关性 也 重要——它 应该 真正 打动 那些人、（那些会买这产品的）。 | To be fair, relevance matters too — it should actually speak to the people who'd buy the product. |

> For me, it's how memorable an advert is. You can have a clever idea, but if nobody recalls it afterwards, it's basically wasted money. A catchy line or a striking image makes a brand stick in your head. To be fair, relevance matters too — it should actually speak to the people who'd buy the product.

**Q4. Why are some advertisements boring?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它们 很多 沉闷 是因为 它们 太 可预测了——一样的脚本、一样的 笑脸、没什么 新鲜的。 | Honestly, a lot of them are dull because they're so predictable — same script, same smiling faces, nothing fresh. |
| 2 | 当 一个品牌 玩得 太安全，它 就 融进 背景里、然后 你 把它 屏蔽掉。 | When a brand plays it too safe, it just blends into the background and you tune it out. |
| 3 | 那些无聊的 通常 推销 产品、却 不 讲 任何故事，所以 没有 任何东西 让你 停下来、真正 留意。 | The boring ones usually push the product without telling any story, so there's nothing to make you stop and actually pay attention. |

> Honestly, a lot of them are dull because they're so predictable — same script, same smiling faces, nothing fresh. When a brand plays it too safe, it just blends into the background and you tune it out. The boring ones usually push the product without telling any story, so there's nothing to make you stop and actually pay attention.

**Q5. Is advertising important for a company? Why?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，它 相当 必不可少。 | Absolutely, it's pretty essential. |
| 2 | 哪怕 有 一个 出色的产品，你 也 走不远，如果 没人 听说过 你，所以 广告 建立 知名度、也 把 顾客 拉进来。 | Even with a brilliant product, you won't get far if nobody's heard of you, so advertising builds awareness and pulls customers in. |
| 3 | 它 也 让 一个公司 塑造 人们 怎么看 他们。 | It also lets a company shape how people see them. |
| 4 | 没有它，你 基本上 是 依赖 口碑，（那 太慢了、对 任何 想成长的 生意来说）。 | Without it, you're basically relying on word of mouth, which is far too slow for any business hoping to grow. |

> Absolutely, it's pretty essential. Even with a brilliant product, you won't get far if nobody's heard of you, so advertising builds awareness and pulls customers in. It also lets a company shape how people see them. Without it, you're basically relying on word of mouth, which is far too slow for any business hoping to grow.

**Q6. Which is more effective, online advertising or offline advertising?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我觉得 网上的 这些年 更有效，主要是因为 它 更便宜、而且 你 能 精准锁定 那些人、（正是你想要的）。 | I reckon online's more effective these days, mainly because it's cheaper and you can target the exact people you want. |
| 2 | 此外，你 拿到 即时的反馈、关于 什么在起效。 | Plus you get instant feedback on what's working. |
| 3 | 话虽如此，线下广告、比如 广告牌，仍然 触及 一些人、（那些 不 死盯 屏幕的），所以 最聪明的品牌 往往 两个 都掺，而不是 押 在 一个上。 | Having said that, offline ads like billboards still reach folk who aren't glued to screens, so the smartest brands tend to mix both rather than betting on one. |

> I reckon online's more effective these days, mainly because it's cheaper and you can target the exact people you want. Plus you get instant feedback on what's working. Having said that, offline ads like billboards still reach folk who aren't glued to screens, so the smartest brands tend to mix both rather than betting on one.

**P3 句型/模板**：`good deals we'd otherwise miss`（relative后置）· `nudging people to buy stuff they don't really need` · `handy when they're honest, annoying when they're not` · `someone you admire backs a product`（relative后置）· `people see through it and it falls flat` · `how memorable an advert is`（embedded）· `makes a brand stick in your head` · `the people who'd buy the product`（who后置）· `blends into the background and you tune it out` · `folk who aren't glued to screens`（who后置）· `mix both rather than betting on one`。

## P2-新18 · A place you'd recommend — 京都（Place）

> **Cue**: 是什么 / 在哪 / 看到做了啥 / 为何推荐 · 题型 Place

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/26 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改）

> 你自己练过的一整套：P2 = 京都（你的真实版）；P3 = 你当天练的 6 题（travel/holiday 域）。**不降档**，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · 京都）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我想推荐的那个 地方 是 京都，在 日本 的 西部。 | The place I'd like to recommend is Kyoto, over in the west of Japan. |
| 2 | 我 现在 已经 去过 那儿 四次，而 我 每一次 都 享受。 | I've been there four times now, and I've enjoyed it every single time. |
| 3 | 我 在 那儿 通常 做的 是 逛 那些 老 寺庙、在 街区 里 闲逛、还 吃 当地 街头 小吃。 | What I usually do there is visit the old temples, wander around the neighbourhoods, and eat local street food. |
| 4 | 一个 真的 留 在 我 心里的 时刻 是 我 沿着 那儿 一条 河 的 一次 散步——我 说实话 念 不 出 它的 名字。 | One moment that really stuck with me was a stroll I took along a river there — I honestly can't pronounce its name. |
| 5 | 一开始，我 不 明白 为什么 这么 小 一条 河 有名，但 过 一会儿，我 完全 沉浸 在 它 那 宁静的 氛围 里。 | At first, I couldn't get why such a small river was famous, but after a while, I was completely immersed in its peaceful atmosphere. |
| 6 | 真正 让 京都 出众的 是 它 怎么 融合 那 传统 和 那 现代。 | What really makes Kyoto stand out is how it blends the traditional and the modern. |
| 7 | 你 会 看到 一大堆 人 穿着 传统 衣服 走来走去，而 你 却 能 买 那 最新的 iPhone、然后 撞见 一座 几百年 的 寺庙、就 在 街 那头。 | You'll see loads of people walking around in traditional clothes, and yet you can buy the latest iPhone and then come across a centuries-old temple just down the street. |
| 8 | 所以 总的来说，我 会 把 它 推荐 给 任何人、（那种 想要 一个 让 你 慢下来 的 地方的）——它 是 一个 我 会 永远 记得的 地方。 | So all in all, I'd recommend it to anyone who wants a place that slows you down — it's somewhere I'll always remember. |

> The place I'd like to recommend is Kyoto, over in the west of Japan. I've been there four times now, and I've enjoyed it every single time. What I usually do there is visit the old temples, wander around the neighbourhoods, and eat local street food. One moment that really stuck with me was a stroll I took along a river there — I honestly can't pronounce its name. At first, I couldn't get why such a small river was famous, but after a while, I was completely immersed in its peaceful atmosphere. What really makes Kyoto stand out is how it blends the traditional and the modern. You'll see loads of people walking around in traditional clothes, and yet you can buy the latest iPhone and then come across a centuries-old temple just down the street. So all in all, I'd recommend it to anyone who wants a place that slows you down — it's somewhere I'll always remember.

**P3（你练的 6 题 · travel/holiday）**

**你Q1. Where do people go for holidays?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 一大堆，说实话——中国 是 一个 大 国家，所以 有 很多 选择。 | Loads, honestly — China's a big country, so there are tons of options. |
| 2 | 一大堆 人 去 海边，比如 上海 或 海南，（那里 他们 能 享受 那 沙滩 和 海景）。 | A lot of people head to the seaside, like Shanghai or Hainan, where they can enjoy the sandy beaches and ocean views. |
| 3 | 另一些 更喜欢 山，比如 黄山，（那里 他们 能 躲开 那 夏天 的热、还 呼吸 一些 新鲜 空气）。 | Others prefer the mountains, like Huangshan, where they can escape the summer heat and breathe some fresh air. |
| 4 | 所以 它 真的 取决于 你 追求 什么。 | So it really depends on what you're after. |

> Loads, honestly — China's a big country, so there are tons of options. A lot of people head to the seaside, like Shanghai or Hainan, where they can enjoy the sandy beaches and ocean views. Others prefer the mountains, like Huangshan, where they can escape the summer heat and breathe some fresh air. So it really depends on what you're after.

**你Q2. What's the ideal length for a holiday?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 一到 两周 是 理想的，我会说。 | One to two weeks is ideal, I'd say. |
| 2 | 如果 你 只 有 几天，那 旅行 感觉 太 匆忙——你 几乎 来不及 体会 那 当地 文化 或 那 风景，它 就 结束了。 | If you only have a few days, the trip feels too rushed — you barely get to take in the local culture or the scenery before it's over. |
| 3 | 一周 左右 是 那个 最佳点：你 有 足够 时间 慢下来、真正 享受 它，不管 是 那 自然 风景 还是 那 历史 古迹。 | A week or so is the sweet spot: you've got enough time to slow down and really enjoy it, whether it's the natural scenery or the historical sights. |

> One to two weeks is ideal, I'd say. If you only have a few days, the trip feels too rushed — you barely get to take in the local culture or the scenery before it's over. A week or so is the sweet spot: you've got enough time to slow down and really enjoy it, whether it's the natural scenery or the historical sights.

**你Q3. How do people plan holidays?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 这些天，大多 在 网上，我会说。 | These days, mostly online, I'd say. |
| 2 | 你 能 在 网上 找到 所有 答案——怎么 去 那儿、哪些 餐厅 好、那 当地 文化 是 什么 样的，全部。 | You can find all the answers on the internet — how to get there, which restaurants are good, what the local culture's like, all of it. |
| 3 | 此外，问 朋友 也 是 一个 好 方式——你 得到 更 真实的 信息、而不是 只 广告。 | Plus, asking friends is a good way too — you get more honest info instead of just ads. |
| 4 | 所以 真的，大多数 人 两样 都 做 一点。 | So really, most people do a bit of both. |

> These days, mostly online, I'd say. You can find all the answers on the internet — how to get there, which restaurants are good, what the local culture's like, all of it. Plus, asking friends is a good way too — you get more honest info instead of just ads. So really, most people do a bit of both.

**你Q4. Is it important to plan ahead?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，是的——至少 那些 基础。 | Definitely, yes — at least the basics. |
| 2 | 即使 是 一个 轻松的 假期，值得 有 一个 大致的 计划，尤其 为 交通 和 你 住 哪儿。 | Even for a chilled-out holiday, it's worth having a rough plan, especially for transport and where you're staying. |
| 3 | 否则 你 可能 最后 被 困住——比如 在 一个 陌生 地方 晚上 转来转去、无处 可去。 | Otherwise you can end up stuck — like wandering around a strange place at night with nowhere to go. |
| 4 | 所以 我 会 总是 提前 搞定 那些 大事，即使 我 让 其余 保持 灵活。 | So I'd always sort out the big stuff in advance, even if I keep the rest flexible. |

> Definitely, yes — at least the basics. Even for a chilled-out holiday, it's worth having a rough plan, especially for transport and where you're staying. Otherwise you can end up stuck — like wandering around a strange place at night with nowhere to go. So I'd always sort out the big stuff in advance, even if I keep the rest flexible.

**你Q5. Why do countries attract tourists?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 主要 是 钱，我会说。 | Mainly money, I'd say. |
| 2 | 旅游业 带 进 一 大笔 收入——游客 花钱 在 交通、酒店、和 食物 上，所以 它 全 加起来。 | Tourism brings in a huge amount of income — visitors spend on transport, hotels and food, so it all adds up. |
| 3 | 此外，它 创造 一大堆 工作 给 普通 人。 | Plus, it creates loads of jobs for ordinary people. |
| 4 | 而 它 对 那 国家 的 形象 也 很好——游客 回家 然后 到处 说。 | And it's great for the country's image too — visitors go home and spread the word. |
| 5 | 所以 总的来说，外国 游客 对 那 经济 真的 很 好。 | So overall, foreign tourists are really good for the economy. |

> Mainly money, I'd say. Tourism brings in a huge amount of income — visitors spend on transport, hotels and food, so it all adds up. Plus, it creates loads of jobs for ordinary people. And it's great for the country's image too — visitors go home and spread the word. So overall, foreign tourists are really good for the economy.

**你Q6. How do people decide when to travel?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它 大多 归结到 时机，我会说。 | It mostly comes down to timing, I'd say. |
| 2 | 对 小孩，是 学校 假期；对 大人，是 无论 什么时候 他们 能 请 到 假，或 当 事情 稍微 清闲 一点。 | For kids, it's the school holidays; for adults, it's whenever they can get time off work, or when things are a bit quieter. |
| 3 | 人们 也 会 考虑 那 目的地——哪个 季节 最好，或 什么时候 人 更 少。 | People also think about the destination — which season's best, or when it's less crowded. |
| 4 | 所以 真的，它 是 你 什么时候 有空 和 什么时候 是 去 的 最佳时机 的 一个 混合。 | So really, it's a mix of when you're free and when's the best time to go. |

> It mostly comes down to timing, I'd say. For kids, it's the school holidays; for adults, it's whenever they can get time off work, or when things are a bit quieter. People also think about the destination — which season's best, or when it's less crowded. So really, it's a mix of when you're free and when's the best time to go.

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个地方 是 京都，一座 日本的 老城市、（那座 我 到现在 去过 三次的、跟我家人一起）。 | So, the place I'd like to talk about is Kyoto, an old city in Japan that I've been to three times now with my family. |
| [where] | 它 在 这个国家的 西部那边，离 大阪 不太远，而且 它 以前 是 日本的首都，所以 它 绝对 塞满了 老庙 和 安静的花园。 | It's down in the western part of the country, not too far from Osaka, and it used to be Japan's capital, so it's absolutely packed with old temples and quiet gardens. |
| [saw/did] | 在我们的旅行里，我们 大多 带 我们的儿子 去看 春天的樱花，而 有一年秋天 我们 赶上了 枫叶 变红，（那 说实话 惊艳）。 | On our trips, we mostly took our son to see the cherry blossoms in spring, and one autumn we caught the maple leaves turning red, which was honestly stunning. |
| [saw/did·细] | 我们 在 清水寺 四处逛、拍了 一大堆照片、还 在 岚山的竹林里 待了 一整个下午。 | We wandered around Kiyomizu Temple, took loads of photos, and spent a whole afternoon in the bamboo groves at Arashiyama. |
| [saw/did·细] | 那儿的食物 也 相当 惊艳。 | The food there was pretty amazing too. |
| [why/EL] | 我 会推荐它 的原因 是 它 真的 让你 慢下来。 | The reason I'd recommend it is that it really slows you down. |
| [EL·细] | 此外，它 有 这么一种 永恒的感觉、（那种 你 在 大的现代城市里 得不到的）。 | Plus, it's got this timeless feel you don't get in big modern cities. |
| [closer] | 所以 总的来说，京都 是那种地方之一、（我 会永远 记得的），而且 我 很想 有天 再 带 我的家人 回去。 | So all in all, Kyoto's one of those places I'll always remember, and I'd love to take my family back again sometime. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the place I'd like to talk about is Kyoto, an old city in Japan that I've been to three times now with my family. It's down in the western part of the country, not too far from Osaka, and it used to be Japan's capital, so it's absolutely packed with old temples and quiet gardens. On our trips, we mostly took our son to see the cherry blossoms in spring, and one autumn we caught the maple leaves turning red, which was honestly stunning. We wandered around Kiyomizu Temple, took loads of photos, and spent a whole afternoon in the bamboo groves at Arashiyama. The food there was pretty amazing too. The reason I'd recommend it is that it really slows you down. Plus, it's got this timeless feel you don't get in big modern cities. So all in all, Kyoto's one of those places I'll always remember, and I'd love to take my family back again sometime.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（地方）+ 同位 + that**：我想聊的那个地方是[X]，一座…的城市、（那座我去过…次的）→ `the place I'd like to talk about is [X], an … city … that I've been to …`
- **塞满**：绝对塞满了老庙和安静的花园 → `packed with old temples and quiet gardens`
- **赶上 + which**：我们赶上枫叶变红，那说实话惊艳 → `we caught the maple leaves turning red, which was honestly stunning`
- **推荐原因**：我推荐它的原因是它真的让你慢下来 → `The reason I'd recommend it is that it really slows you down`
- **永恒感 + relative**：那种你在大现代城市得不到的永恒感 → `this timeless feel you don't get in big modern cities`
- **收尾**：那种我会永远记得的地方之一 → `one of those places I'll always remember`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Where do people in your country often go for holidays?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 这儿 大多数人 去 海边 或 山里，当 他们 有假的时候。 | Well, I'd say most people here head to the coast or the mountains when they get time off. |
| 2 | 海滨小镇 在夏天 特别火，因为 人们 就 想 放松、凉快一下。 | Beach towns are hugely popular in summer because folk just want to relax and cool down. |
| 3 | 但 也有 一群 在增长的人、（那些 更喜欢 乡下 更安静的地方的），因为 城市生活 可能 变得 挺累人，而 他们 渴望 一个 真正的 休息。 | But there's also a growing crowd who prefer quieter spots in the countryside, since city life can get pretty exhausting and they crave a real break. |

> Well, I'd say most people here head to the coast or the mountains when they get time off. Beach towns are hugely popular in summer because folk just want to relax and cool down. But there's also a growing crowd who prefer quieter spots in the countryside, since city life can get pretty exhausting and they crave a real break.

**Q2. What is the ideal length for a holiday?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我觉得 大概 一周 是 那个 最佳点。 | Honestly, I reckon around a week is the sweet spot. |
| 2 | 任何 更短的 你 都 几乎 还没放松 它 就结束了，尤其 如果 有 一趟 长途飞行 掺在里面。 | Anything shorter and you barely unwind before it's over, especially if there's a long flight involved. |
| 3 | 一周 给你 足够的时间、[去]好好 探索 一个地方、不 赶时间。 | A week gives you enough time to properly explore a place without rushing. |
| 4 | 但 对于 一次 大的出国旅行，我 会 把它 推到 两周，因为 反正 你 已经 走了 那么远。 | But for a big trip abroad, I'd push it to two weeks, since you've travelled so far anyway. |

> Honestly, I reckon around a week is the sweet spot. Anything shorter and you barely unwind before it's over, especially if there's a long flight involved. A week gives you enough time to properly explore a place without rushing. But for a big trip abroad, I'd push it to two weeks, since you've travelled so far anyway.

**Q3. How do people usually plan holidays?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 现在，大多数人 从网上 开始，浏览 评价、在旅行 app 上 比价。 | These days, most folk start online, browsing reviews and comparing prices on travel apps. |
| 2 | 我会说 那 已经 成了 每个人的 第二天性。 | I'd say that's become second nature for everyone. |
| 3 | 有些人 喜欢 提前 把一切 都订好，从 机票 到 酒店，而 另一些人 就 搞定 那些基本的、然后 弄清 剩下的，一旦 他们 到了。 | Some like to book everything in advance, from flights to hotels, while others just sort out the basics and figure out the rest once they arrive. |
| 4 | 它 真的 取决于 你 有多 随性，我猜。 | It really depends on how spontaneous you are, I suppose. |

> These days, most folk start online, browsing reviews and comparing prices on travel apps. I'd say that's become second nature for everyone. Some like to book everything in advance, from flights to hotels, while others just sort out the basics and figure out the rest once they arrive. It really depends on how spontaneous you are, I suppose.

**Q4. Is it important to plan a holiday ahead?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说句公道话，我觉得 它 真的 有帮助，尤其 对 一趟 出国的旅行。 | To be fair, I think it really helps, especially for a trip overseas. |
| 2 | 提前规划 意味着 你 锁定 更便宜的机票、也 避开 那种压力、（一切 都 被订光的）。 | Planning ahead means you lock in cheaper flights and avoid the stress of everything being booked out. |
| 3 | 话虽如此，你 不想 排得太满、然后 不 留 空间 去闲逛。 | That said, you don't want to over-schedule and leave no room to wander. |
| 4 | 所以 我 会 早早 规划 那些大的东西，但 让 每天的 安排 保持灵活。 | So I'd plan the big stuff early, but keep the daily routine flexible. |

> To be fair, I think it really helps, especially for a trip overseas. Planning ahead means you lock in cheaper flights and avoid the stress of everything being booked out. That said, you don't want to over-schedule and leave no room to wander. So I'd plan the big stuff early, but keep the daily routine flexible.

**Q5. Why do many countries try to attract people to visit?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，它 大多是 关于钱，真的。 | Well, it's mostly about money, really. |
| 2 | 游客 带来 巨大的收入，（那 支撑 本地企业、也 给 普通人 创造 一大堆 工作）。 | Tourists bring in a huge amount of income, which supports local businesses and creates loads of jobs for ordinary people. |
| 3 | 此外，它 对 一个国家的形象 很好，因为 游客 回家 会 帮着传话。 | Plus, it's great for a country's image, since visitors go home and spread the word. |
| 4 | 所以 国家 把钱 砸进 广告和地标，[去]让 人群 不断 回来。 | So countries pour money into adverts and landmarks to keep the crowds coming back. |

> Well, it's mostly about money, really. Tourists bring in a huge amount of income, which supports local businesses and creates loads of jobs for ordinary people. Plus, it's great for a country's image, since visitors go home and spread the word. So countries pour money into adverts and landmarks to keep the crowds coming back.

**Q6. How do people decide when to travel?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 它 通常 归结到 天气 和 工作的假期。 | I'd say it usually comes down to the weather and time off work. |
| 2 | 大多数人 等 公共假期 或者 暑假，就因为 那 是 他们 真正 能 走开 的时候。 | Most folk wait for public holidays or the summer break, simply because that's when they can actually get away. |
| 3 | 花费 也 重要，因为 价格 在淡季 会降。 | Cost matters too, since prices drop in the off-season. |
| 4 | 所以 很多人 把 他们的旅行 安排 围绕着 更便宜的机票 和 更好的优惠。 | So a lot of people time their trips around cheaper flights and better deals. |

> I'd say it usually comes down to the weather and time off work. Most folk wait for public holidays or the summer break, simply because that's when they can actually get away. Cost matters too, since prices drop in the off-season. So a lot of people time their trips around cheaper flights and better deals.

**P3 句型/模板**：`a growing crowd who prefer quieter spots`（who后置）· `crave a real break` · `the sweet spot` · `enough time to properly explore a place without rushing` · `how spontaneous you are`（embedded）· `Planning ahead means you lock in cheaper flights`（动名词主语）· `the stress of everything being booked out` · `which supports local businesses`（which后置）· `spread the word` · `to keep the crowds coming back` · `that's when they can actually get away`。

## P2-新19 · A home you like to visit but not live in — 外公外婆家（Place）

> **Cue**: 在哪 / 什么样 / 为何爱去 / 为何不想住 · 题型 Place

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个家 是 我外公外婆的地方，在 那个小镇里、（那个我 长大的）。 | So, the home I'd like to talk about is my grandparents' place, in the small town where I grew up. |
| [背景] | 他们 在那儿 把我养大，所以 它 会 永远 感觉 像家。 | They raised me there, so it'll always feel like home. |
| [what like] | 它是 一栋 朴素的 小房子、带着 一个 后面的 小花园、（在那里 我外公 种 西红柿和辣椒）。 | It's a modest little house with a small garden out the back, where my grandfather grows tomatoes and chillies. |
| [what·细] | 里面，它 有点 老派，但 它 温馨 又 暖和，而且 它 总是 闻着 家常菜的味道。 | Inside, it's a bit old-fashioned, but it's cosy and warm, and it always smells of home-cooked food. |
| [why visit] | 我 爱去 的原因 是 一切 都 彻底 慢下来。 | The reason I love visiting is that everything slows right down. |
| [why·细] | 我外公 在花园里 慢悠悠地 忙活，我外婆 做 好大桌 的饭，而 我们 就 聊上 好几个小时。 | My grandfather putters around the garden, my grandmother cooks huge meals, and we just chat for hours. |
| [why·收] | 它是 那种地方、（在那里 没人 赶着 去 任何地方），而且 说实话，它 每次 都 给我 充电。 | It's the kind of place where nobody's rushing anywhere, and honestly, it recharges me every time. |
| [why not live] | 话虽如此，我 不会想 在那儿 全职 住着。 | That said, I wouldn't want to live there full-time. |
| [why not·细] | 那镇子 挺安静、也 远离 我在 成都的 生活，周围 没什么 工作。 | The town's pretty quiet and far from my life in Chengdu, with few jobs around. |
| [why not·细] | 那慢节奏 对一个周末 很可爱，但 日复一日 我 会 想念 城市生活的 那股热闹劲儿。 | The slow pace is lovely for a weekend, but day to day I'd miss the buzz of city life. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那就是 那个家、（我 爱去 但 没法 安顿下来的）。 | So yeah, that's the home I love to visit but couldn't settle in. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the home I'd like to talk about is my grandparents' place, in the small town where I grew up. They raised me there, so it'll always feel like home. It's a modest little house with a small garden out the back, where my grandfather grows tomatoes and chillies. Inside, it's a bit old-fashioned, but it's cosy and warm, and it always smells of home-cooked food. The reason I love visiting is that everything slows right down. My grandfather putters around the garden, my grandmother cooks huge meals, and we just chat for hours. It's the kind of place where nobody's rushing anywhere, and honestly, it recharges me every time. That said, I wouldn't want to live there full-time. The town's pretty quiet and far from my life in Chengdu, with few jobs around. The slow pace is lovely for a weekend, but day to day I'd miss the buzz of city life. So yeah, that's the home I love to visit but couldn't settle in.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（家）+ where**：我想聊的那个家是[X]，在那个小镇、（我长大的）→ `the home I'd like to talk about is [X], in the small town where I grew up`
- **带…where后置**：一个后花园、在那里外公种菜 → `a small garden out the back, where my grandfather grows …`
- **闻着…味道**：总是闻着家常菜的味道 → `it always smells of home-cooked food`
- **爱去的原因**：我爱去的原因是一切都彻底慢下来 → `The reason I love visiting is that everything slows right down`
- **那种…where**：那种没人赶着去任何地方的地方 → `the kind of place where nobody's rushing anywhere`
- **收尾对照**：那个我爱去但没法安顿的家 → `the home I love to visit but couldn't settle in`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Do Chinese people like to visit others' homes?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 是的，非常。 | Yes, very much so. |
| 2 | 我会说 互相 串门 是 中国文化 的 一大块，尤其 在 节日附近、比如 春节。 | I'd say visiting each other's homes is a big part of Chinese culture, especially around festivals like Spring Festival. |
| 3 | 它 被看作 温暖 又 亲切，比 在餐馆见面 友好 得多。 | It's seen as warm and personal, way friendlier than meeting in a restaurant. |
| 4 | 说实话，请 一个人 过来 吃 一顿家常饭 是 最好的方式之一、[去]表示 你 真的 看重 他们。 | Honestly, inviting someone over for a home-cooked meal is one of the nicest ways to show you really value them. |

> Yes, very much so. I'd say visiting each other's homes is a big part of Chinese culture, especially around festivals like Spring Festival. It's seen as warm and personal, way friendlier than meeting in a restaurant. Honestly, inviting someone over for a home-cooked meal is one of the nicest ways to show you really value them.

**Q2. What do Chinese people do when they visit others?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，主人 通常 全力以赴 做 一大桌饭，而 客人 很少 空手 出现。 | Well, the host usually goes all out cooking a big meal, and guests rarely show up empty-handed. |
| 2 | 人们 就着 茶 或 水果 聊天，而且 常常 有 一大堆 客气的 你来我往、关于 谁 付 什么。 | People chat over tea or fruit, and there's often a lot of polite back-and-forth about who pays for what. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，一旦 每个人 安顿下来 就 挺放松，孩子们 到处跑、大人们 交换 新闻。 | To be fair, it's pretty relaxed once everyone settles in, with kids running around and grown-ups swapping news. |

> Well, the host usually goes all out cooking a big meal, and guests rarely show up empty-handed. People chat over tea or fruit, and there's often a lot of polite back-and-forth about who pays for what. To be fair, it's pretty relaxed once everyone settles in, with kids running around and grown-ups swapping news.

**Q3. What kind of place do people in your country like to live in?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我们国家 大多数人 偏向 大城市，说实话。 | Most people in my country lean towards big cities, honestly. |
| 2 | 年轻人 尤其 想 住 在 一个地方、（那种 有 好工作、交通 和 夜生活的），所以 像 成都 或 上海 这样的地方 把他们 拉进来。 | Younger folk especially want to live somewhere with good jobs, transport and nightlife, so places like Chengdu or Shanghai pull them in. |
| 3 | 话虽如此，很多 老年人 更喜欢 一个 更安静的地方、在老家，（在那里 生活 更便宜、节奏 也 更 适合他们）。 | That said, plenty of older people prefer a quieter spot back home, where life's cheaper and the pace suits them far better. |

> Most people in my country lean towards big cities, honestly. Younger folk especially want to live somewhere with good jobs, transport and nightlife, so places like Chengdu or Shanghai pull them in. That said, plenty of older people prefer a quieter spot back home, where life's cheaper and the pace suits them far better.

**Q4. What's the difference between homes in cities and those in the countryside?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 最大的差距 是 空间，我觉得。 | The biggest gap is space, I reckon. |
| 2 | 城市的家 大多是 挤挤的公寓、在高塔里，而 乡下的房子 往往 更大，常常 带着 一个院子 或 一点地。 | City homes are mostly cramped flats in tall towers, while country houses tend to be bigger, often with a yard or a bit of land. |
| 3 | 此外，乡下的家 感觉 更悠闲、更有邻里气，而 城市生活 更快、也 私密得多，因为 人们 几乎 不认识 他们的邻居。 | Plus, rural homes feel more laid-back and neighbourly, whereas city living's faster and a lot more private, since people barely know their neighbours. |

> The biggest gap is space, I reckon. City homes are mostly cramped flats in tall towers, while country houses tend to be bigger, often with a yard or a bit of land. Plus, rural homes feel more laid-back and neighbourly, whereas city living's faster and a lot more private, since people barely know their neighbours.

**Q5. What kind of gifts do people usually bring when they visit others?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它 真的 看情况，但 水果 是 一个 稳妥的选择，不错的零食 或 盒装茶 也是。 | It really depends, but fruit's a safe bet, and so are nice snacks or boxed tea. |
| 2 | 如果 你 去看 一个 有孩子的人，人们 常常 抓 玩具 或 糖果。 | If you're visiting someone with kids, people often grab toys or sweets. |
| 3 | 对 更亲近的朋友，我会说 一瓶 像样的酒 挺合适。 | For closer friends, I'd say a decent bottle of wine works well. |
| 4 | 那条 黄金法则 是 永远 别 空手 出现，因为 那 会 显得 有点 没礼貌。 | The golden rule is never turning up with nothing, since that'd come across as a bit rude. |

> It really depends, but fruit's a safe bet, and so are nice snacks or boxed tea. If you're visiting someone with kids, people often grab toys or sweets. For closer friends, I'd say a decent bottle of wine works well. The golden rule is never turning up with nothing, since that'd come across as a bit rude.

**Q6. How often do you visit your relatives or friends?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 没有 我想的 那么频繁，遗憾地说。 | Not as often as I'd like, sadly. |
| 2 | 我 通常 在 大节日 见 我的亲戚，也许 一年 几次，因为 他们 大多数 住得 挺远。 | I usually see my relatives during big holidays, maybe a few times a year, since most of them live quite far away. |
| 3 | 跟 朋友 就 容易些，所以 我们 每隔 一两周 就 吃个饭。 | With friends it's easier, so we'll grab dinner every couple of weeks. |
| 4 | 说实话，生活 变得 那么忙，以至于 拜访 在 清单上 往下滑，比 它们 应该的 更多。 | Honestly, life gets so busy that visits slip down the list more than they should. |

> Not as often as I'd like, sadly. I usually see my relatives during big holidays, maybe a few times a year, since most of them live quite far away. With friends it's easier, so we'll grab dinner every couple of weeks. Honestly, life gets so busy that visits slip down the list more than they should.

**P3 句型/模板**：`visiting each other's homes is a big part of …`（动名词主语）· `way friendlier than meeting in a restaurant` · `inviting someone over … is one of the nicest ways to show …`（动名词主语）· `goes all out cooking a big meal` · `show up empty-handed` · `polite back-and-forth about who pays for what`（embedded）· `with kids running around and grown-ups swapping news`（with-tag）· `somewhere with good jobs, transport and nightlife` · `The golden rule is never turning up with nothing`（动名词主语）· `visits slip down the list more than they should`。
