## P2-新20 · A story/book with animals — 《好饿的毛毛虫》（Object）

> **Cue**: 有什么动物 / 讲什么 / 为何读 / 你的看法 · 题型 Object

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那本书 是 一本 儿童绘本、叫《好饿的毛毛虫》，（那本 我五岁的儿子 Muye 绝对 爱的）。 | So, the book I'd like to talk about is a children's picture book called The Very Hungry Caterpillar, which my five-year-old son Muye absolutely loves. |
| [what animals] | 主角 是 一只 小小的毛毛虫，而 也有 一些 别的动物 的 零零碎碎、散在 书页 各处。 | The main character is a tiny caterpillar, and there are bits and pieces of other animals dotted around the pages too. |
| [about] | 基本上，那毛毛虫 从 一个蛋里 孵出来、然后 一路 啃穿 各种各样的食物，直到 他 变成 一只 漂亮的蝴蝶。 | Basically, the caterpillar hatches from an egg and munches through all sorts of food, until he turns into a beautiful butterfly. |
| [about·收] | 故事 简单，但 那插画 很鲜艳，而 那结尾 总能 从 Muye 那儿 逗出 一个笑。 | The story's simple, but the artwork's colourful and the ending always gets a smile out of Muye. |
| [why read] | 我读它 是因为 它 已经 成了 我们睡前流程的 一个固定部分。 | I read it because it's become a fixed part of our bedtime routine. |
| [why·细] | 他 喜欢 动物，所以 任何 里面有生物的东西 都 抓住 他的注意力。 | He's into animals, so anything with creatures in it grabs his attention. |
| [why·细] | 说实话，他 会 把这一本 从架子上 拿下来、一晚 又一晚，而 我 到现在 差不多 已经 把它 背下来了。 | Honestly, he'll pick this one off the shelf night after night, and I've pretty much got it memorised by now. |
| [what think/EL] | 我爱的东西 是 它 塞进 一个小道理 的那种方式、不 说教——它 悄悄地 教他 关于 数数 和 长大。 | What I love is the way it slips in a little lesson without being preachy — it quietly teaches him about counting and growing up. |
| [closer] | 所以 总的来说，它是 那种书之一、（我 会永远 记得 跟他一起读的）。 | So all in all, it's one of those books I'll always remember reading with him. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the book I'd like to talk about is a children's picture book called The Very Hungry Caterpillar, which my five-year-old son Muye absolutely loves. The main character is a tiny caterpillar, and there are bits and pieces of other animals dotted around the pages too. Basically, the caterpillar hatches from an egg and munches through all sorts of food, until he turns into a beautiful butterfly. The story's simple, but the artwork's colourful and the ending always gets a smile out of Muye. I read it because it's become a fixed part of our bedtime routine. He's into animals, so anything with creatures in it grabs his attention. Honestly, he'll pick this one off the shelf night after night, and I've pretty much got it memorised by now. What I love is the way it slips in a little lesson without being preachy — it quietly teaches him about counting and growing up. So all in all, it's one of those books I'll always remember reading with him.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（书）+ which**：我想聊的那本书是[X]、（那本…爱的）→ `the book I'd like to talk about is [X], which … loves`
- **散在书页**：一些别的动物的零碎、散在书页各处 → `bits and pieces … dotted around the pages`
- **逗出笑**：结尾总能从Muye那儿逗出一个笑 → `the ending always gets a smile out of Muye`
- **任何有…的都抓住注意力**：任何里面有生物的东西都抓住他的注意力 → `anything with creatures in it grabs his attention`
- **what主语 + the way**：我爱的是它塞进小道理的那种方式、不说教 → `What I love is the way it slips in a little lesson without being preachy`
- **收尾**：那种我会永远记得跟他一起读的书之一 → `one of those books I'll always remember reading with him`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Should schools teach children about animals?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对应该。 | Definitely. |
| 2 | 我会说 动物 是 一个 绝佳的方式、[去]点燃 一个孩子 对 更广阔世界 的好奇。 | I'd say animals are a brilliant way to spark a child's curiosity about the wider world. |
| 3 | 学 生物 住在哪儿、吃什么 培养 同理心，因为 孩子 开始 在乎 某种 超出他们自己的东西。 | Learning where creatures live and what they eat builds empathy, since kids start caring about something beyond themselves. |
| 4 | 比如，一堂 关于蜜蜂的 快课 能 教 一整个班 为什么 大自然 重要。 | For instance, a quick lesson on bees can teach a whole class why nature matters. |
| 5 | 它 也 就是 真的 好玩。 | It's also just genuinely fun. |

> Definitely. I'd say animals are a brilliant way to spark a child's curiosity about the wider world. Learning where creatures live and what they eat builds empathy, since kids start caring about something beyond themselves. For instance, a quick lesson on bees can teach a whole class why nature matters. It's also just genuinely fun.

**Q2. Some people think pets should not be kept in cities. What do you think?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它 取决于 那个动物。 | Honestly, it depends on the animal. |
| 2 | 我会说 大狗 在 小公寓里 不公平，因为 它们 需要 空间 [去]到处跑。 | I'd say big dogs in tiny flats isn't fair, since they need space to run around. |
| 3 | 但 一只猫 或 一条金鱼 在城市里 完全 没问题。 | But a cat or a goldfish does perfectly fine in a city. |
| 4 | 所以 与其 干脆 禁掉 宠物，我觉得 人们 只是 需要 挑 某种东西、（那种 明智地 适合 一个 更小的家的）。 | So rather than banning pets outright, I reckon people just need to pick something that suits a smaller home sensibly. |

> Honestly, it depends on the animal. I'd say big dogs in tiny flats isn't fair, since they need space to run around. But a cat or a goldfish does perfectly fine in a city. So rather than banning pets outright, I reckon people just need to pick something that suits a smaller home sensibly.

**Q3. Many people regard pets as members of their family. What do you think?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，而且 我觉得 那 很可爱。 | Absolutely, and I think that's lovely. |
| 2 | 一只宠物 分享 你的日常、在门口 迎接你、还 捕捉到 你的情绪，所以 它 自然 感觉 像 家里的一员。 | A pet shares your daily routine, greets you at the door, and picks up on your moods, so it naturally feels like one of the family. |
| 3 | 比如，人们 常常 为 一只狗 哀伤，就跟 一个亲戚 一样深。 | For instance, people often grieve a dog just as deeply as a relative. |
| 4 | 对我来说，那种纽带 是 完全 真实的、也 值得 尊重。 | To me, that bond is completely real and worth respecting. |

> Absolutely, and I think that's lovely. A pet shares your daily routine, greets you at the door, and picks up on your moods, so it naturally feels like one of the family. For instance, people often grieve a dog just as deeply as a relative. To me, that bond is completely real and worth respecting.

**Q4. Do many people keep pets in your country?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 是的，这些年 一大堆人 养，比 一代人之前 多得多。 | Yeah, loads of people do these days, far more than a generation ago. |
| 2 | 猫和狗 轻松 是 最受欢迎的，虽然 更小的公寓 意味着 兔子和鱼 也 在 流行起来。 | Cats and dogs are easily the most popular, though smaller flats mean rabbits and fish are catching on too. |
| 3 | 我会说 一部分是因为 城市生活 可能 感觉 孤独，所以 一只宠物的 陪伴 真的 有帮助。 | I'd say it's partly because city life can feel lonely, so a pet's company really helps. |
| 4 | 它们 基本上 已经 变成了 小小的 伴儿。 | They've basically become little companions. |

> Yeah, loads of people do these days, far more than a generation ago. Cats and dogs are easily the most popular, though smaller flats mean rabbits and fish are catching on too. I'd say it's partly because city life can feel lonely, so a pet's company really helps. They've basically become little companions.

**Q5. What are the advantages of keeping a pet?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 有一大堆，说实话。 | There are loads, honestly. |
| 2 | 最大的一个 是 陪伴——一只宠物 总是 高兴 见到你，（那 真的 提振 你的心情、在 糟糕的一天之后）。 | The biggest one's companionship — a pet's always happy to see you, which really lifts your mood after a rough day. |
| 3 | 此外，狗 尤其 让你 去户外、动起来，所以 你 保持活跃。 | Plus, dogs especially get you outdoors and moving, so you stay active. |
| 4 | 对孩子来说，照顾 一只动物 也 教 责任。 | For kids, looking after an animal teaches responsibility too. |
| 5 | 总的来说，它们 给 一个家 带来 很多 温暖。 | All round, they bring a lot of warmth into a home. |

> There are loads, honestly. The biggest one's companionship — a pet's always happy to see you, which really lifts your mood after a rough day. Plus, dogs especially get you outdoors and moving, so you stay active. For kids, looking after an animal teaches responsibility too. All round, they bring a lot of warmth into a home.

**Q6. Why do people always tell children stories with animals?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 动物 就是 立刻 抓住 一个孩子的 想象力——它们 鲜艳、搞笑、又 容易 想象出来。 | Well, I'd say animals just grab a child's imagination instantly — they're colourful, funny, and easy to picture. |
| 2 | 此外，会说话的 生物 让 作家 温柔地 塞进 一个道理，不 直接 说教 那孩子。 | Plus, talking creatures let writers slip in a moral gently, without lecturing the kid directly. |
| 3 | 比如，一只 狡猾的狐狸 能 教 诚实、比 一条 干巴巴的规则 好得多。 | For instance, a sly fox can teach honesty far better than a plain rule. |
| 4 | 所以 它 真的是 一个 更软、更友好的方式、[去]把 道理 传达出去。 | So it's really a softer, friendlier way to get lessons across. |

> Well, I'd say animals just grab a child's imagination instantly — they're colourful, funny, and easy to picture. Plus, talking creatures let writers slip in a moral gently, without lecturing the kid directly. For instance, a sly fox can teach honesty far better than a plain rule. So it's really a softer, friendlier way to get lessons across.

**P3 句型/模板**：`a brilliant way to spark a child's curiosity` · `Learning where creatures live and what they eat builds empathy`（动名词主语+embedded）· `why nature matters`（embedded）· `something that suits a smaller home sensibly`（that后置）· `picks up on your moods` · `just as deeply as a relative` · `worth respecting` · `looking after an animal teaches responsibility`（动名词主语）· `slip in a moral gently, without lecturing the kid directly` · `a softer, friendlier way to get lessons across`。

## P2-新21 · Someone who helped you solve a problem — zhangwei 修网络（Event）

> **Cue**: 是谁 / 什么问题 / 怎么帮 / 你的感受 · 题型 Event

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个人 是 一个 大学的 老朋友，zhangwei，（那个 真的 帮我 解了围、当 我 卡在 一个问题上的时候）。 | So, the person I'd like to talk about is an old friend from university, zhangwei, who really bailed me out when I was stuck with a problem. |
| [problem] | 那个问题 是 我们家的网络 已经 好几周 慢得像爬。 | The problem was that our home internet had been crawling for weeks. |
| [problem·细] | 视频通话 一直 卡住，而 说实话 它 快 把我 逼疯了。 | Video calls kept freezing, and honestly it was driving me up the wall. |
| [problem·细] | 我 已经 重启了 路由器 无数次，但 好像 什么 都不管用。 | I'd restarted the router countless times, but nothing seemed to work. |
| [how helped] | 所以 我 给 zhangwei 打了个电话，因为 他 是 个开发者、而 那种事 正是 他的 拿手好戏。 | So I gave zhangwei a call, since he's a developer and that sort of thing is right up his alley. |
| [how·细] | 与其 只是 告诉我 该按什么，他 在电话里 带着我 走了一遍，一步一步来。 | Instead of just telling me what to press, he walked me through it step by step over the phone. |
| [how·细] | 他 全程 保持耐心，即使 当 我 明显 跟不上，而且 他 让我 检查 哪些设备 在 抢带宽。 | He stayed patient the whole time, even when I clearly didn't follow, and he got me to check which devices were hogging the bandwidth. |
| [how·细] | 结果发现 我的智能电视 在后台 悄悄地 下载更新。 | It turned out my smart TV was quietly downloading updates in the background. |
| [feel] | 回头看，我 感觉 挺松了口气、也 真心 感激。 | Looking back, I felt pretty relieved and genuinely grateful. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那是 那种时刻之一、（我 会记得的），因为 他 本可以 自己 修好它，却 选择 教我 代替。 | So yeah, that's one of those moments I'll remember, because he could've fixed it himself but chose to teach me instead. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the person I'd like to talk about is an old friend from university, zhangwei, who really bailed me out when I was stuck with a problem. The problem was that our home internet had been crawling for weeks. Video calls kept freezing, and honestly it was driving me up the wall. I'd restarted the router countless times, but nothing seemed to work. So I gave zhangwei a call, since he's a developer and that sort of thing is right up his alley. Instead of just telling me what to press, he walked me through it step by step over the phone. He stayed patient the whole time, even when I clearly didn't follow, and he got me to check which devices were hogging the bandwidth. It turned out my smart TV was quietly downloading updates in the background. Looking back, I felt pretty relieved and genuinely grateful. So yeah, that's one of those moments I'll remember, because he could've fixed it himself but chose to teach me instead.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（帮我的人）+ who**：我想聊的那个人是[X]、（那个真的帮我解围的）→ `the person I'd like to talk about is [X], who really bailed me out …`
- **that主语从句**：那个问题是我们家网络慢得像爬 → `The problem was that our home internet had been crawling`
- **逼疯我 / 拿手好戏**：快把我逼疯了；正是他的拿手好戏 → `driving me up the wall` / `right up his alley`
- **与其只告诉…，他带我走一遍**：与其只是告诉我该按什么，他带我一步步走 → `Instead of just telling me what to press, he walked me through it step by step`
- **结果发现**：结果发现我的电视在后台下载更新 → `It turned out my smart TV was … downloading in the background`
- **收尾**：他本可以自己修，却选择教我 → `he could've fixed it himself but chose to teach me instead`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. How important is it for schools to help children become smarter?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，它是 学校有的 主要工作之一。 | Definitely, it's one of the main jobs schools have. |
| 2 | 我会说 它们真正的价值 不只是 把事实 塞进 孩子的脑袋，而是 教 他们 怎么 思考、怎么 自己 解决问题。 | I'd say their real value isn't just stuffing facts into kids' heads, but teaching them how to think and solve problems on their own. |
| 3 | 比如，一个好老师 不会 直接 交出 答案——他们 会 把一个孩子 推向 自己 想通它。 | For instance, a good teacher won't hand over answers — they'll nudge a child towards working it out. |
| 4 | 那种 训练 伴随 一辈子。 | That kind of training sticks for life. |

> Definitely, it's one of the main jobs schools have. I'd say their real value isn't just stuffing facts into kids' heads, but teaching them how to think and solve problems on their own. For instance, a good teacher won't hand over answers — they'll nudge a child towards working it out. That kind of training sticks for life.

**Q2. Why are some people reluctant to ask others for help?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，很多人 觉得 它 尴尬。 | Honestly, a lot of folk find it awkward. |
| 2 | 我觉得 它 大多是 面子——人们 担心 求助 让 他们 显得 软弱 或 无能。 | I reckon it's mostly pride — people worry that asking for help makes them look weak or incapable. |
| 3 | 在 一些文化里，你 是 被 养成 去 收拾 你自己的烂摊子。 | In some cultures you're raised to sort your own mess out. |
| 4 | 但 我会说 那 很可惜，因为 早早 伸手 求助 常常 帮你 省掉 一大堆麻烦、往后。 | But I'd say that's a shame, because reaching out early often saves you loads of hassle down the line. |

> Honestly, a lot of folk find it awkward. I reckon it's mostly pride — people worry that asking for help makes them look weak or incapable. In some cultures you're raised to sort your own mess out. But I'd say that's a shame, because reaching out early often saves you loads of hassle down the line.

**Q3. Can technology help people solve their problems?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 当然，用 一大堆方式。 | For sure, in loads of ways. |
| 2 | 我会说 技术 很擅长 处理 那些无聊的、重复的活儿——一次 快速的搜索 或 一个 app 能 修好 那些 以前 要花很久的事。 | I'd say technology's brilliant at handling the boring, repetitive jobs — a quick search or an app can fix what used to take ages. |
| 3 | 但 说句公道话，它 不 适合 每一个问题。 | But to be fair, it doesn't suit every problem. |
| 4 | 有些问题 需要 一种 人的触感，比如 安慰 一个朋友，而 没有 哪个 小工具 能 真的 替代 那个。 | Some issues need a human touch, like comforting a friend, and no gadget can really replace that. |

> For sure, in loads of ways. I'd say technology's brilliant at handling the boring, repetitive jobs — a quick search or an app can fix what used to take ages. But to be fair, it doesn't suit every problem. Some issues need a human touch, like comforting a friend, and no gadget can really replace that.

**Q4. What's the difference between teaching someone and doing something for them?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 有 一个巨大的差距，说实话。 | There's a huge gap, honestly. |
| 2 | 替别人 做 更快，但 他们 什么 都学不到，而 你 被 困着 永远 帮下去。 | Doing it for someone is quicker, but they learn nothing and you're stuck helping forever. |
| 3 | 教 需要 更多 耐心，因为 你 得 慢下来、让 他们 磕绊 一下。 | Teaching takes more patience, since you've got to slow down and let them stumble a bit. |
| 4 | 但 我会说 它 有回报——一旦 他们 掌握了 它，他们 就 能 靠自己 站起来。 | But I'd say it pays off — once they grasp it, they can stand on their own feet. |

> There's a huge gap, honestly. Doing it for someone is quicker, but they learn nothing and you're stuck helping forever. Teaching takes more patience, since you've got to slow down and let them stumble a bit. But I'd say it pays off — once they grasp it, they can stand on their own feet.

**Q5. Do you think intelligence is something people are born with or learn?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 它 是 两者 都有点，真的。 | I'd say it's a bit of both, really. |
| 2 | 有些人 显然 生来 机敏，但 大多数的聪明 来自 努力——广泛地 读、问问题、永不放弃。 | Some folk are clearly born quick-witted, but most cleverness comes from effort — reading widely, asking questions, never giving up. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，我 佩服的人 不是 那些 天生有天赋的；他们 是 那些人、（那些 就 一直 苦干、慢慢 随时间 变得 更敏锐的）。 | To be fair, the people I admire aren't the naturally gifted ones; they're the ones who just keep grafting and slowly get sharper over time. |

> I'd say it's a bit of both, really. Some folk are clearly born quick-witted, but most cleverness comes from effort — reading widely, asking questions, never giving up. To be fair, the people I admire aren't the naturally gifted ones; they're the ones who just keep grafting and slowly get sharper over time.

**Q6. How does helping each other affect relationships between people?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 影响巨大，我觉得。 | Massively, I reckon. |
| 2 | 当 一个同事 帮你 从 一个困境里 脱身，你 往往 信任 他们 多得多、也 会 高高兴兴地 以后 还这个人情。 | When a colleague bails you out of a tight spot, you tend to trust them far more and you'll happily return the favour later. |
| 3 | 它 建起 一种 无声的 忠诚。 | It builds a sort of quiet loyalty. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，反过来 也成立——如果 某人 无视 你的 求助，那纽带 挺快 就 冷掉。 | To be fair, the opposite's true too — if someone ignores your call for help, that bond cools off pretty fast. |

> Massively, I reckon. When a colleague bails you out of a tight spot, you tend to trust them far more and you'll happily return the favour later. It builds a sort of quiet loyalty. To be fair, the opposite's true too — if someone ignores your call for help, that bond cools off pretty fast.

**P3 句型/模板**：`stuffing facts into kids' heads`（动名词）· `nudge a child towards working it out` · `sticks for life` · `asking for help makes them look weak`（动名词主语）· `sort your own mess out` · `saves you loads of hassle down the line` · `fix what used to take ages`（embedded）· `Doing it for someone is quicker`（动名词主语）· `stand on their own feet` · `the ones who just keep grafting`（who后置）· `bails you out of a tight spot` / `return the favour`。

## P2-新22 · An environmental protection law — 垃圾分类（wife S3 信息来源）

> **Cue**: 是什么 / 怎么最先了解 / 谁受益 / 感受 · 题型 Object/现行法

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那条法律 是 那条 垃圾分类的规则、（我们城市里有的）——基本上，你 得 把 你的垃圾 分进 不同的桶，比如 厨余、可回收物、和 其他一切，而不是 就 把它 全 一股脑扔一起。 | So, the law I'd like to talk about is the waste-sorting rule we've got in our city — basically, you have to separate your rubbish into different bins, like food waste, recyclables and everything else, instead of just chucking it all together. |
| [how learned] | 我 最早 听说它 其实是 从我老婆那儿。 | I first heard about it from my wife, actually. |
| [how·细] | 她 在城市管理部门工作，所以 她 早就 一直 跟我讲 它 要来了，远早于 它 上新闻。 | She works in city management, so she'd been telling me it was coming long before it hit the news. |
| [how·细] | 一开始 我 觉得 它 听起来 像 个麻烦，但 她 带着我 弄清了 它为什么重要。 | At first I thought it sounded like a hassle, but she walked me through why it matters. |
| [who benefits] | 至于 谁受益，我会说 长远看 差不多 每个人。 | As for who benefits, I'd say pretty much everyone in the long run. |
| [benefits·细] | 城市 最后 更干净，更少的垃圾 去 填埋场，而 那些 能回收的东西 真的 被回收了，（那 对 我们 都 共享的 环境 更好）。 | The city ends up cleaner, less rubbish goes to landfill, and the stuff that can be recycled actually gets recycled, which is better for the environment we all share. |
| [feel] | 说实话，我 现在 是它的 大粉丝。 | Honestly, I'm a big fan of it now. |
| [closer] | 它 曾经 有点 让人头疼、去习惯，但 说到底，它是 那种 小习惯、（那种 累积成 某种 巨大东西的）。 | It was a bit of a pain to get used to, but at the end of the day, it's the kind of small habit that adds up to something huge. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the law I'd like to talk about is the waste-sorting rule we've got in our city — basically, you have to separate your rubbish into different bins, like food waste, recyclables and everything else, instead of just chucking it all together. I first heard about it from my wife, actually. She works in city management, so she'd been telling me it was coming long before it hit the news. At first I thought it sounded like a hassle, but she walked me through why it matters. As for who benefits, I'd say pretty much everyone in the long run. The city ends up cleaner, less rubbish goes to landfill, and the stuff that can be recycled actually gets recycled, which is better for the environment we all share. Honestly, I'm a big fan of it now. It was a bit of a pain to get used to, but at the end of the day, it's the kind of small habit that adds up to something huge.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（现行法）**：我想聊的那条法律是[X]、（我们城市里有的）→ `the law I'd like to talk about is [X] we've got in our city`
- **怎么了解 bullet**：我最早听说它是从…那儿 → `I first heard about it from [X]`
- **带我弄清**：她带着我弄清了它为什么重要 → `she walked me through why it matters`
- **谁受益 bullet**：至于谁受益，长远看差不多每个人 → `As for who benefits, … in the long run`
- **which后置**：那对我们都共享的环境更好 → `which is better for the environment we all share`
- **收尾**：那种累积成巨大东西的小习惯 → `the kind of small habit that adds up to something huge`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What kinds of rules do schools in China have?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，这儿的学校 有 一大堆规则，说实话。 | Well, schools here have loads of rules, honestly. |
| 2 | 有 一个严格的 着装要求，所以 你 每天 都得 穿校服。 | There's a strict dress code, so you've got to wear a uniform every day. |
| 3 | 然后 有 一些规则、关于 准时到、课上 不许手机、还有 上课时 保持安静。 | Then there are rules about turning up on time, no phones in class, and keeping quiet during lessons. |
| 4 | 它们 对 作业截止日期 也 挺严的，而 大多数学校 期待 学生 无条件地 尊重老师。 | They're pretty firm on homework deadlines too, and most schools expect students to respect teachers without question. |

> Well, schools here have loads of rules, honestly. There's a strict dress code, so you've got to wear a uniform every day. Then there are rules about turning up on time, no phones in class, and keeping quiet during lessons. They're pretty firm on homework deadlines too, and most schools expect students to respect teachers without question.

**Q2. Do you think school rules are important?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对。 | Definitely. |
| 2 | 我会说 校规 给 孩子 一种 秩序感，而且 他们 早早 就学到 行为 有后果。 | I'd say school rules give kids a sense of order, and they learn early that actions have consequences. |
| 3 | 没有它们，教室 就 会 陷入 混乱、而 没人 会学到 任何东西。 | Without them, classrooms would just descend into chaos and nobody'd learn a thing. |
| 4 | 比如，一条 简单的 无手机规则 让 每个人 专注。 | For instance, a simple no-phones rule keeps everyone focused. |
| 5 | 此外，在学校 遵守规则 让 他们 为 以后的职场 做好准备。 | Plus, following rules at school gets them ready for the workplace later on. |

> Definitely. I'd say school rules give kids a sense of order, and they learn early that actions have consequences. Without them, classrooms would just descend into chaos and nobody'd learn a thing. For instance, a simple no-phones rule keeps everyone focused. Plus, following rules at school gets them ready for the workplace later on.

**Q3. Are children unhappy with the school rules?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 有时候，是的。 | Sometimes, yeah. |
| 2 | 我觉得 孩子 抱怨 那些规则、（那些 感觉 太严 或 没意义的），比如 禁 某些发型。 | I reckon kids grumble about rules that feel too strict or pointless, like banning certain hairstyles. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，在那个年纪 他们 渴望 一点自由，所以 任何 限制 都感觉 不公平。 | To be fair, at that age they crave a bit of freedom, so any limit feels unfair. |
| 4 | 但 说实话，他们 大多数 会 想通，一旦 他们 理解 它背后的 道理，即使 他们 不肯 承认。 | But honestly, most of them come round once they understand the reasoning behind it, even if they won't admit it. |

> Sometimes, yeah. I reckon kids grumble about rules that feel too strict or pointless, like banning certain hairstyles. To be fair, at that age they crave a bit of freedom, so any limit feels unfair. But honestly, most of them come round once they understand the reasoning behind it, even if they won't admit it.

**Q4. How can parents and teachers help children understand and follow rules?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 关键 是 解释 那个为什么，而不只是 吼命令。 | I'd say the key is explaining the why, not just barking orders. |
| 2 | 当 大人 花时间 聊 那些原因，孩子 就 买账 容易得多。 | When adults take time to chat about the reasons, kids buy in far more easily. |
| 3 | 比如，与其 只 说"不许手机"，你 解释 它 怎么 毁掉 专注力。 | For example, instead of just saying "no phones", you explain how it wrecks concentration. |
| 4 | 此外，父母和老师 应该 自己 树立 一个 好榜样，因为 孩子 模仿 他们看到的。 | Plus, parents and teachers should set a good example themselves, since kids copy what they see. |

> I'd say the key is explaining the why, not just barking orders. When adults take time to chat about the reasons, kids buy in far more easily. For example, instead of just saying "no phones", you explain how it wrecks concentration. Plus, parents and teachers should set a good example themselves, since kids copy what they see.

**Q5. What are the rules people should obey at work?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，每个 工作场所 有 它自己的，但 有些 挺普遍。 | Well, every workplace has its own, but some are pretty universal. |
| 2 | 你 被期待 准时到、赶上截止日期、还 尊重地 对待 同事。 | You're expected to show up on time, meet deadlines, and treat colleagues with respect. |
| 3 | 通常 也有 一个 着装要求，取决于 那份工作。 | There's usually a dress code too, depending on the job. |
| 4 | 此外，大多数公司 对 保密 很严，所以 你 不能 去 把 敏感信息 泄露给 外人。安全规则 也 极其重要。 | Plus, most companies are strict about confidentiality, so you can't go leaking sensitive information to outsiders. Safety rules matter loads as well. |

> Well, every workplace has its own, but some are pretty universal. You're expected to show up on time, meet deadlines, and treat colleagues with respect. There's usually a dress code too, depending on the job. Plus, most companies are strict about confidentiality, so you can't go leaking sensitive information to outsiders. Safety rules matter loads as well.

**Q6. What is the purpose of punishment?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我觉得 它 主要是 [为了]阻止 人们 重复 同样的错误。 | Honestly, I think it's mainly to deter people from repeating the same mistake. |
| 2 | 当 有 一个 真正的后果，人们 会 三思，在 再次 违规 之前。 | When there's a real consequence, folk think twice before breaking the rules again. |
| 3 | 但 它 不该 只是 关于报复——理想上 惩罚 教 一堂课、也 帮 某人 改进。 | But it shouldn't just be about revenge — ideally punishment teaches a lesson and helps someone improve. |
| 4 | 比如，社区服务 能 让 犯错的人 反省，而不是 只 感到 怨恨。 | For instance, community service can make wrongdoers reflect rather than just feel resentful. |

> Honestly, I think it's mainly to deter people from repeating the same mistake. When there's a real consequence, folk think twice before breaking the rules again. But it shouldn't just be about revenge — ideally punishment teaches a lesson and helps someone improve. For instance, community service can make wrongdoers reflect rather than just feel resentful.

**P3 句型/模板**：`a strict dress code` · `respect teachers without question` · `descend into chaos` · `following rules … gets them ready for the workplace`（动名词主语）· `rules that feel too strict or pointless`（that后置）· `come round once they understand the reasoning` · `explaining the why, not just barking orders`（动名词主语）· `how it wrecks concentration`（embedded）· `deter people from repeating the same mistake` · `make wrongdoers reflect rather than just feel resentful`。

## P2-新23 · A message that got no reply — 发小 Liang（Event）

> **Cue**: 发给谁 / 内容 / 是否终收到回复 / 感受 · 题型 Event

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/22 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改 · 只练了 P2）

> 你自己练过的 P2（给老同学发邮件求建议、久等回复，你的真实版；这条只练了 P2）。**不降档**，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · 发邮件求建议久等回复）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 想 到 的 一次 是 当 我 给 一个 老同学 发 邮件 求 一些 建议。 | One time that comes to mind is when I emailed an old classmate to ask for some advice. |
| 2 | 那时候，我 在 考虑 转 进 他 那个 行业，所以 我 想 他 会 是 那个 该 问 的 合适 人选。 | At the time, I was thinking about moving into his industry, so I thought he'd be the right person to ask. |
| 3 | 我 想 问 他 一些 行业 消息，因为 他 在 一个 我 感兴趣的 领域 工作。 | I wanted to ask him about some industry news, because he works in a field I'm interested in. |
| 4 | 我们 好些年 没 说过话 了，所以 突然 给 他 发消息 感觉 有点 尴尬，而 我 花 了 老半天 斟酌 措辞。 | We hadn't talked in years, so it felt a bit awkward to message him out of the blue, and I spent ages wording it. |
| 5 | 但 在 我 发 出去 之后，我 很久 都 没 收到 回复。 | But after I sent it, I didn't hear back for a long time. |
| 6 | 一天天 过去，然后 好几周，而 我 开始 琢磨 我 是不是 说 错 了 什么。 | Days went by, then a couple of weeks, and I started to wonder if I'd said something wrong. |
| 7 | 我 一天 查 手机 好几次，我 甚至 回去 看 他 是不是 读 了。 | I checked my phone a few times a day, and I even went back to see if he'd read it. |
| 8 | 最后，他 确实 回 了——大约 一个月 后——而 结果 是 他 只是 一直 很 忙。 | In the end, he did reply — about a month later — and it turned out he'd just been really busy. |
| 9 | 说实话，那 等待 的 部分 有点 让人 焦虑，因为 我 一直 想 太多。 | Honestly, the waiting part was a bit stressful, because I kept overthinking it. |
| 10 | 所以 现在 当 人们 回 得 慢，我 尽量 不 往 心里 去。 | So now I try not to take it personally when people are slow to reply. |

> One time that comes to mind is when I emailed an old classmate to ask for some advice. At the time, I was thinking about moving into his industry, so I thought he'd be the right person to ask. I wanted to ask him about some industry news, because he works in a field I'm interested in. We hadn't talked in years, so it felt a bit awkward to message him out of the blue, and I spent ages wording it. But after I sent it, I didn't hear back for a long time. Days went by, then a couple of weeks, and I started to wonder if I'd said something wrong. I checked my phone a few times a day, and I even went back to see if he'd read it. In the end, he did reply — about a month later — and it turned out he'd just been really busy. Honestly, the waiting part was a bit stressful, because I kept overthinking it. So now I try not to take it personally when people are slow to reply.

---

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那次 是 当 我 给 一个 童年老朋友 发消息、叫 Liang，（那个 我 基本上 已经 失联 好几年了的）。 | So, the time I'd like to talk about is when I messaged an old childhood friend of mine called Liang, who I'd basically lost touch with for years. |
| [背景] | 我们 在我老家 是 邻居 长大的，就 隔 几户，但 生活 把我们 拉向 不同的方向、在毕业后。 | We grew up as neighbours in my hometown, just a couple of doors apart, but life pulled us in different directions after school. |
| [about] | 一个傍晚 我 偶然 翻到 一张 我们的老照片，然后 一时兴起 我 给他 发了 一条 长长的短信——问 他 过得怎么样、还 提议 我们 吃个饭，每当 我 回老家的时候。 | One evening I came across an old photo of us, and on a whim I sent him a long text — asking how he'd been and suggesting we grab a meal whenever I was back home. |
| [whether reply] | 但 事情是这样的：我 几乎 三周 都 没 收到 回信。 | But here's the thing: I heard nothing back for almost three weeks. |
| [whether·细] | 我 一直 查 我的手机，半 猜想着 他 是不是 换了号码、或者 就是 不在乎了。 | I kept checking my phone, half wondering if he'd changed his number or simply didn't care anymore. |
| [feel] | 说实话，它 有点 刺痛。 | Honestly, it stung a little. |
| [whether·反转] | 然后，出乎意料地，他 回了 这么一条 温暖的、絮絮叨叨的消息——结果发现 他 换了手机、错过了它。 | Then, out of the blue, he replied with this warm, rambling message — turns out he'd switched phones and missed it. |
| [whether·收] | 从那以后 我们 就 一直 在聊。 | We've been chatting ever since. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那是 那种时刻之一、（我 会永远 记得的），因为 它 提醒了我 好的友谊 从不 真正 褪色。 | So yeah, that's one of those moments I'll always remember, because it reminded me good friendships never really fade. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the time I'd like to talk about is when I messaged an old childhood friend of mine called Liang, who I'd basically lost touch with for years. We grew up as neighbours in my hometown, just a couple of doors apart, but life pulled us in different directions after school. One evening I came across an old photo of us, and on a whim I sent him a long text — asking how he'd been and suggesting we grab a meal whenever I was back home. But here's the thing: I heard nothing back for almost three weeks. I kept checking my phone, half wondering if he'd changed his number or simply didn't care anymore. Honestly, it stung a little. Then, out of the blue, he replied with this warm, rambling message — turns out he'd switched phones and missed it. We've been chatting ever since. So yeah, that's one of those moments I'll always remember, because it reminded me good friendships never really fade.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（事件）+ when + who**：我想聊的那次是当我给[X]发消息、（那个我失联多年的）→ `the time I'd like to talk about is when I messaged [X], who I'd lost touch with for years`
- **生活把我们拉散**：生活把我们拉向不同的方向 → `life pulled us in different directions`
- **一时兴起 + 分词**：一时兴起我发了长短信——问…、提议… → `on a whim I sent him a long text — asking … and suggesting …`
- **事情是这样的**：但事情是这样的：我几乎三周没收到回信 → `here's the thing: I heard nothing back for …`
- **出乎意料 + 结果发现**：出乎意料地他回了…——结果发现他换了手机 → `out of the blue, he replied … — turns out he'd switched phones`
- **收尾**：那种我会永远记得的时刻，因为它提醒我… → `one of those moments I'll always remember, because it reminded me …`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. In what situations do people spend a long time responding to others' messages?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 它 通常 发生 当 人们 被 工作 淹没、或者 埋在 截止日期 之下，所以 一条 回复 就 在 他们的清单上 往下滑。 | Well, I'd say it usually happens when people are swamped with work or buried under deadlines, so a reply just slips down their list. |
| 2 | 有时候 那消息 不 紧急，所以 他们 拖着它、然后 彻底 忘掉。 | Sometimes the message isn't urgent, so they put it off and then forget completely. |
| 3 | 说实话，另一些人 就是 需要 时间 [去]想，在 他们 好好 回答 之前，尤其 如果 它 是 某种 有点 敏感的东西。 | Honestly, others simply need time to think before they answer properly, especially if it's something a bit sensitive. |

> Well, I'd say it usually happens when people are swamped with work or buried under deadlines, so a reply just slips down their list. Sometimes the message isn't urgent, so they put it off and then forget completely. Honestly, others simply need time to think before they answer properly, especially if it's something a bit sensitive.

**Q2. In what situations do people not respond to messages at all?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我觉得 人们 保持沉默 当 他们 恼火、或者 想 避开 一场 尴尬的对话，比如 一个 棘手的人情、（那种 他们 宁愿 躲开的）。 | Honestly, I reckon people stay silent when they're annoyed or want to avoid an awkward conversation, like a tricky favour they'd rather dodge. |
| 2 | 垃圾信息 或 随机的推销短信 也 被 忽视，显然。 | Spam or random sales texts get ignored too, obviously. |
| 3 | 此外，有时候 一条消息 就 淹没在 一堆 通知里，所以 它 从不是 故意的——他们 是真的 从没 注意到 它 到了。 | Plus, sometimes a message just gets lost in a flood of notifications, so it's never deliberate — they genuinely never noticed it landed. |

> Honestly, I reckon people stay silent when they're annoyed or want to avoid an awkward conversation, like a tricky favour they'd rather dodge. Spam or random sales texts get ignored too, obviously. Plus, sometimes a message just gets lost in a flood of notifications, so it's never deliberate — they genuinely never noticed it landed.

**Q3. What would you do if you did not receive a reply after sending out a message?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我 会 先 等 几天，因为 大多数人 只是 忙，而不是 没礼貌。 | Well, I'd give it a few days first, since most folk are just busy rather than rude. |
| 2 | 如果 它 重要，我 会 发 一个 温和的 提醒、或者 也许 就 打电话给 他们。 | If it's important, I'd send a gentle nudge or maybe ring them instead. |
| 3 | 但 如果 他们 一直 无视 我，我 会 领会 那个暗示、然后 退开。 | But if they keep ignoring me, I'd take the hint and back off. |
| 4 | 没有意义 去 追 某个人、（那种 明显 不 热衷于 回复的）。 | There's no point chasing someone who clearly isn't keen on replying. |

> Well, I'd give it a few days first, since most folk are just busy rather than rude. If it's important, I'd send a gentle nudge or maybe ring them instead. But if they keep ignoring me, I'd take the hint and back off. There's no point chasing someone who clearly isn't keen on replying.

**Q4. Why do some people prefer sending a message instead of making a call?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 它 主要是 关于方便——一条短信 让你 回复、随时 合适你，不 [为一个电话]放下 一切。 | I'd say it's mainly about convenience — a text lets you reply whenever suits you, without dropping everything for a call. |
| 2 | 它 也 压力更小，因为 你 能 仔细地 措辞、还 躲开 那种 尴尬的 寒暄。 | It's also less pressure, since you can word things carefully and dodge that awkward small talk. |
| 3 | 此外，消息 留下 一个 书面记录，所以 你 不会 忘掉 到底 商定了什么、往后。 | Plus, messages leave a written record, so you won't forget what was actually agreed on later. |

> I'd say it's mainly about convenience — a text lets you reply whenever suits you, without dropping everything for a call. It's also less pressure, since you can word things carefully and dodge that awkward small talk. Plus, messages leave a written record, so you won't forget what was actually agreed on later.

**Q5. How do you show your respect in your messages?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 主要 靠 及时地 回复，我会说，因为 把 某人 晾着 感觉 挺不当回事的。 | Mainly by replying promptly, I'd say, because leaving someone hanging feels pretty dismissive. |
| 2 | 我 也 试着 让 我的语气 礼貌、还 加上 一句 像样的 问候，而不是 只 甩出 一句 生硬的话。 | I also try to keep my tone polite and add a proper greeting rather than just firing off one blunt line. |
| 3 | 对 长辈 或 我老板，我 对 措辞 格外 小心，因为 一条 潦草的短信 可能 显得 没礼貌 或 粗心。 | With elders or my boss, I'm extra careful with wording, since a sloppy text can come across as rude or careless. |

> Mainly by replying promptly, I'd say, because leaving someone hanging feels pretty dismissive. I also try to keep my tone polite and add a proper greeting rather than just firing off one blunt line. With elders or my boss, I'm extra careful with wording, since a sloppy text can come across as rude or careless.

**Q6. Why do some people feel angry when others don't reply to their message?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说句公道话，沉默 可能 感觉 像 被忽视，而 没人 享受 那个。 | To be fair, silence can feel like being ignored, and nobody enjoys that. |
| 2 | 人们 常常 把它 读作 不尊重，好像 他们的消息 不值 一个 快速的回复。 | People often read it as disrespect, like their message wasn't worth a quick reply. |
| 3 | 它 更糟，当 你 能 看到 那条短信 被读了，因为 那 有点 刺痛。 | It's worse when you can see the text was read, since that stings a bit. |
| 4 | 说实话，它 就 让你 一直 猜想 你 是不是 不知怎么 惹恼了 他们。 | Honestly, it just leaves you guessing whether you've upset them somehow. |

> To be fair, silence can feel like being ignored, and nobody enjoys that. People often read it as disrespect, like their message wasn't worth a quick reply. It's worse when you can see the text was read, since that stings a bit. Honestly, it just leaves you guessing whether you've upset them somehow.

**P3 句型/模板**：`swamped with work or buried under deadlines` · `slips down their list` · `put it off and then forget` · `a tricky favour they'd rather dodge`（relative后置）· `gets lost in a flood of notifications` · `take the hint and back off` · `someone who clearly isn't keen on replying`（who后置）· `a text lets you reply whenever suits you` · `what was actually agreed on`（embedded）· `leaving someone hanging feels pretty dismissive`（动名词主语）· `come across as rude or careless` · `leaves you guessing whether you've upset them`（embedded）。

## P2-新24 · A long-term goal — 独立研究梦（speaker）

> **Cue**: 有多久 / 是什么 / 怎么实现 / 为何设它 · 题型 Object/Goal

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个目标 是 终于 挤出 时间、[去]做 我自己的 独立研究，有一天，而不是 总 给 别人 造东西。 | So, the goal I'd like to talk about is finally carving out time to do my own independent research one day, instead of always building things for other people. |
| [how long] | 我会说 这个念头 已经 静静地 待在 我脑子的深处、大概 五六年了。 | I'd say this idea has been quietly sitting at the back of my mind for about five or six years now. |
| [how long·细] | 它 真正 成形 是 在 科技行业 十多年之后，当 我 开始 感觉 我想 钻进 一些问题、纯粹 出于好奇，不是 因为 一个截止日期 那么说。 | It really took shape after over a decade in tech, when I started feeling that I wanted to dig into questions purely out of curiosity, not because a deadline says so. |
| [how achieve] | 至于 我 会 怎么 达到那儿，我 一步一步来。 | As for how I'll get there, I'm taking it step by step. |
| [how·细] | 我 每个月 存 一点钱、这样 我 以后 不 在压力下，而且 我 在业余 学习，捡起 那些数学和理论、（那些我 从没 好好 学过的）。 | I'm setting a bit of money aside each month so I'm not under pressure later, and I'm studying on the side, picking up the maths and theory I never properly learned. |
| [how·细] | 它 慢，但 我 在 一点一点 啃它。 | It's slow, but I'm chipping away at it. |
| [why/EL] | 我 定 这个目标的 原因 挺简单——我 想要 工作、（那种 感觉 真正 属于我的）。 | The reason I set this goal is pretty simple — I want work that feels genuinely mine. |
| [closer] | 说到底，那种 归属感 就是 让我 坚持下去 的东西。 | At the end of the day, that sense of ownership is what keeps me going. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the goal I'd like to talk about is finally carving out time to do my own independent research one day, instead of always building things for other people. I'd say this idea has been quietly sitting at the back of my mind for about five or six years now. It really took shape after over a decade in tech, when I started feeling that I wanted to dig into questions purely out of curiosity, not because a deadline says so. As for how I'll get there, I'm taking it step by step. I'm setting a bit of money aside each month so I'm not under pressure later, and I'm studying on the side, picking up the maths and theory I never properly learned. It's slow, but I'm chipping away at it. The reason I set this goal is pretty simple — I want work that feels genuinely mine. At the end of the day, that sense of ownership is what keeps me going.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（目标）**：我想聊的那个目标是 挤出时间[去做X]，而不是… → `the goal I'd like to talk about is finally carving out time to [do X], instead of …`
- **搁在心底**：静静待在我脑子深处五六年 → `sitting at the back of my mind for … years`
- **成形**：它真正成形是在…之后，当我开始感觉… → `It really took shape after …, when I started feeling that …`
- **怎么实现 bullet**：至于我会怎么达到那儿，我一步一步来 → `As for how I'll get there, I'm taking it step by step`
- **that后置 + what表语**：我想要那种感觉真正属于我的工作；那种归属感就是让我坚持的东西 → `work that feels genuinely mine` / `that sense of ownership is what keeps me going`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Why should children have ambitions?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 它 给 他们 一个 可以 瞄准的东西，（那 让 他们 保持 有动力、当 学校 变枯燥的时候）。 | I'd say it gives them something to aim for, which keeps them motivated when school gets dull. |
| 2 | 一个孩子、（那种 梦想 当兽医的），比如，会 乐意 熬过 生物课。 | A kid who dreams of being a vet, for instance, will happily slog through biology. |
| 3 | 没有 任何目标，很容易 漂着、就 混日子。 | Without any goal, it's easy to drift and just coast. |
| 4 | 所以 抱负 给 年轻人 一点 方向 和 目标感，早早地。 | So ambitions give young people a bit of direction and purpose early on. |

> I'd say it gives them something to aim for, which keeps them motivated when school gets dull. A kid who dreams of being a vet, for instance, will happily slog through biology. Without any goal, it's easy to drift and just coast. So ambitions give young people a bit of direction and purpose early on.

**Q2. What do you think of people going after high positions?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我 对它 没意见，只要 他们 不是 踩着 别人 去 到那儿。 | Honestly, I'm fine with it as long as they're not stepping on others to get there. |
| 2 | 想要 一份 顶级工作 常常 显示 冲劲，而 一个人、带着 那种 渴望，通常 推动 一整个团队 往前。 | Wanting a top job often shows drive, and someone with that hunger usually pushes a whole team forward. |
| 3 | 但 如果 它 倾向 变成 纯粹的 自我，（在那里 头衔 比 实际工作 更要紧），那 它 就 开始 感觉 有点 空洞。 | But if it tips into pure ego, where titles matter more than the actual work, then it starts to feel a bit hollow. |

> Honestly, I'm fine with it as long as they're not stepping on others to get there. Wanting a top job often shows drive, and someone with that hunger usually pushes a whole team forward. But if it tips into pure ego, where titles matter more than the actual work, then it starts to feel a bit hollow.

**Q3. Why are some young people keen on being fans of superstars?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我觉得 明星 给 他们 一个人、[去]仰望、[去]梦想。 | Well, I reckon stars give them someone to look up to and dream about. |
| 2 | 一个青少年、（那种 崇拜 一个歌手的），常常 学到 那个人的 自信 和 风格，几乎 像 一个榜样。 | A teenager who idolises a singer often picks up that person's confidence and style, almost like a role model. |
| 3 | 此外，当粉丝 是 社交的——你 跟 别的粉丝 结伴、交换故事、感觉 是 某种东西的 一部分。 | Plus, being a fan is social — you bond with other fans, swap stories, feel part of something. |
| 4 | 它 是 一种 归属感，真的。 | It's a sense of belonging, really. |

> Well, I reckon stars give them someone to look up to and dream about. A teenager who idolises a singer often picks up that person's confidence and style, almost like a role model. Plus, being a fan is social — you bond with other fans, swap stories, feel part of something. It's a sense of belonging, really.

**Q4. Is it good for a person to be ambitious?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 大体上，是的。 | Mostly, yes. |
| 2 | 有野心 推着 你 成长、也 不 将就于 比 你 本可以 成为的 更少。 | Being ambitious pushes you to grow and not settle for less than you could be. |
| 3 | 一个人、带着目标，往往 醒来 带着 一种 更清楚的感觉、关于 他们 为什么 做那些事。 | Someone with goals tends to wake up with a clearer sense of why they're doing things. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，它 可能 适得其反，如果 你 从不 满足，所以 它 最起效、当 它 被 平衡、跟 知道 什么时候 够了就是够了。 | Having said that, it can backfire if you're never satisfied, so it works best when it's balanced with knowing when enough is enough. |

> Mostly, yes. Being ambitious pushes you to grow and not settle for less than you could be. Someone with goals tends to wake up with a clearer sense of why they're doing things. Having said that, it can backfire if you're never satisfied, so it works best when it's balanced with knowing when enough is enough.

**Q5. Do you think it is necessary to be ambitious when working in a team in a company?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 不是 对每个人，不。 | Not for everyone, no. |
| 2 | 一个团队 也 需要 稳的球员，一些人、（那种 就 做 扎实的活儿、不 追 那束聚光灯的）。 | A team needs steady players too, people who just do solid work without chasing the spotlight. |
| 3 | 如果 每个人 都 极其 有野心，你 会 得到 冲突的 自我 和 不断的 摩擦。 | If everybody's fiercely ambitious, you'd get clashing egos and constant friction. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 一两个 有干劲的成员 有帮助，但 其余的 就 能 可靠、又 合作。平衡 比 一刀切的野心 更要紧。 | So I'd say one or two driven members help, but the rest can simply be reliable and cooperative. Balance matters more than blanket ambition. |

> Not for everyone, no. A team needs steady players too, people who just do solid work without chasing the spotlight. If everybody's fiercely ambitious, you'd get clashing egos and constant friction. So I'd say one or two driven members help, but the rest can simply be reliable and cooperative. Balance matters more than blanket ambition.

**Q6. Should parents support their children in pursuing their ambitions?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，在合理范围内。 | Absolutely, within reason. |
| 2 | 一点 支持——鼓励、也许 一些课——能 把 一个 模糊的梦 变成 某种 真实的东西。 | A bit of backing — encouragement, maybe some lessons — can turn a vague dream into something real. |
| 3 | 没有它，孩子 常常 在 第一个 障碍 就放弃。 | Without it, kids often give up at the first hurdle. |
| 4 | 但 父母 不该 把 他们自己 未实现的目标 硬压 给 他们。那个诀窍 是 支持 无论 孩子 真心 想要什么，而不是 父母 私下 希望 他们 选的。 | But parents shouldn't push their own unfulfilled goals onto them. The trick is supporting whatever the child genuinely wants, not what the parent secretly wishes they'd chosen. |

> Absolutely, within reason. A bit of backing — encouragement, maybe some lessons — can turn a vague dream into something real. Without it, kids often give up at the first hurdle. But parents shouldn't push their own unfulfilled goals onto them. The trick is supporting whatever the child genuinely wants, not what the parent secretly wishes they'd chosen.

**P3 句型/模板**：`something to aim for, which keeps them motivated`（which后置）· `A kid who dreams of being a vet`（who后置）· `drift and just coast` · `Wanting a top job often shows drive`（动名词主语）· `if it tips into pure ego, where …`（where后置）· `someone to look up to and dream about` · `being a fan is social`（动名词主语）· `not settle for less than you could be` · `knowing when enough is enough` · `supporting whatever the child genuinely wants, not what the parent … wishes`（动名词主语+embedded）。

## P2-新25 · A person who met difficulties but succeeded — zhangwei 自学 ML（Person）

> **Cue**: 是谁 / 遇到什么困难 / 怎么克服 / 你的感受 · 题型 Person

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/23 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改 · 只练了 P2）

> 你自己练过的 P2（张伟高压救火，你的真实版；这条你只练了 P2，没练 P3）。**不降档**，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · 张伟高压救火）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我想聊的那个人 是 我的 一个 大学 同学，张伟。 | The person I'd like to talk about is one of my classmates at university, Zhangwei. |
| 2 | 我们 认识 彼此 十多年 了。 | We've known each other for over ten years. |
| 3 | 那时候，我们 通常 做的 是 泡 在 学校 电脑 社团 里，而 我们 会 瞎 鼓捣、写 一些 小 程序。 | Back then, what we usually did was hang out in the school computer club, and we'd mess around building little programs. |
| 4 | 让 他 出众的 是 他 怎么 在 压力下 解决 问题。 | What makes him stand out is how he solves problems under pressure. |
| 5 | 我 记得 有一次，在 一个 小组 项目 上，那 程序 就 在 展示 之前 崩了。 | I remember one time, on a group project, the program crashed right before the presentation. |
| 6 | 当 我们 其他人 都 在 慌，他 就 保持 冷静、坐下、然后 一行一行 过 那 代码 和 日志。 | While the rest of us were panicking, he just stayed calm, sat down, and went through the code and logs line by line. |
| 7 | 大约 二十分钟 里，他 修好 了 它——他 基本上 救 了 场。 | In about twenty minutes, he fixed it — he basically saved the day. |
| 8 | 而 不 只是 那 一次——无论 什么时候 有 东西 坏了，他 就是 那种 人、（大家 都 会 求助的），因为 他 稳得住、还 总是 乐意 帮忙。 | And it's not just that one time — whenever something breaks, he's the kind of person everyone turns to, because he keeps his cool and is always happy to help. |
| 9 | 这些天 他 做 得 很好——他 是 一个 出色的 开发者，而 我 觉得 那 是 因为 他 总能 在 压力下 保持 冷静。 | These days he's doing really well — he's an excellent developer, and I think it's because he can always stay calm under pressure. |
| 10 | 那 是 一个 我 真的 佩服 他 的 地方。 | That's something I really admire about him. |

> The person I'd like to talk about is one of my classmates at university, Zhangwei. We've known each other for over ten years. Back then, what we usually did was hang out in the school computer club, and we'd mess around building little programs. What makes him stand out is how he solves problems under pressure. I remember one time, on a group project, the program crashed right before the presentation. While the rest of us were panicking, he just stayed calm, sat down, and went through the code and logs line by line. In about twenty minutes, he fixed it — he basically saved the day. And it's not just that one time — whenever something breaks, he's the kind of person everyone turns to, because he keeps his cool and is always happy to help. These days he's doing really well — he's an excellent developer, and I think it's because he can always stay calm under pressure. That's something I really admire about him.

---

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个人 是 一个 大学的 老朋友，zhangwei。 | So, the person I'd like to talk about is an old friend from university, zhangwei. |
| [背景] | 我们 认识彼此 快 二十年了，而 他 做 一个 开发者。 | We've known each other for almost twenty years, and he works as a developer. |
| [difficulty] | 几年前，他的公司 决定 转向 AI，而 那 把他 置于 一个 真正 艰难的 境地。 | A few years back, his company decided to pivot towards AI, and that put him in a really tough spot. |
| [difficulty·细] | 他 完全 没有 机器学习的 背景，所以 他 基本上 面对 一个选择——要么 自学 这一整个 新领域，要么 冒 被落下 的风险。 | He had no background in machine learning at all, so he was basically facing a choice — either teach himself this whole new field, or risk being left behind. |
| [how overcame] | 我佩服的东西 是 他 怎么 正面 迎战它。 | What I admired was how he tackled it head-on. |
| [overcame·细] | 他 花了 大概 六个月 下班后 学习，慢慢地 把 东西 拼凑 起来，从 网课 和 副业项目。 | He spent about six months studying after work, slowly piecing things together from online courses and side projects. |
| [overcame·细] | 有些时候 他 想 认输，但 他 就是 一直坚持。 | There were times he wanted to throw in the towel, but he just kept at it. |
| [overcame·收] | 到最后，他 完成了 那个 转变、还 拿下了 一个 扎实的职位、在 那个新团队。 | In the end, he pulled off the switch and landed a solid role on the new team. |
| [feel/EL] | 说实话，我 非常 仰望他。他 向我 展示了 只要 有 足够的 韧劲，你 就能 重塑 你自己。 | Honestly, I look up to him a great deal. He showed me that with enough grit, you can reinvent yourself. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那就是我想聊的那个朋友。 | So yeah, that's the friend I wanted to talk about. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the person I'd like to talk about is an old friend from university, zhangwei. We've known each other for almost twenty years, and he works as a developer. A few years back, his company decided to pivot towards AI, and that put him in a really tough spot. He had no background in machine learning at all, so he was basically facing a choice — either teach himself this whole new field, or risk being left behind. What I admired was how he tackled it head-on. He spent about six months studying after work, slowly piecing things together from online courses and side projects. There were times he wanted to throw in the towel, but he just kept at it. In the end, he pulled off the switch and landed a solid role on the new team. Honestly, I look up to him a great deal. He showed me that with enough grit, you can reinvent yourself. So yeah, that's the friend I wanted to talk about.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **置于困境**：那把他置于一个真正艰难的境地 → `that put him in a really tough spot`
- **要么…要么冒…风险**：要么自学这新领域，要么冒被落下的风险 → `either teach himself this whole new field, or risk being left behind`
- **what主语 + how**：我佩服的是他怎么正面迎战它 → `What I admired was how he tackled it head-on`
- **想认输但一直坚持**：有些时候他想认输，但他就是一直坚持 → `There were times he wanted to throw in the towel, but he just kept at it`
- **完成转变**：完成了那个转变、拿下扎实职位 → `pulled off the switch and landed a solid role`
- **收尾**：只要有足够韧劲，你就能重塑自己 → `with enough grit, you can reinvent yourself`

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. In your country, what industry is it easier to be successful in?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 科技 大概 是 这些年 最容易的，主要是因为 它 增长 那么快、而 薪水 也扎实。 | Well, I'd say tech is probably the easiest these days, mainly because it's growing so fast and the pay's solid. |
| 2 | 如果 你 有 那些技能，有 一大堆 空缺，而 没人 真的 在乎 你的背景。 | If you've got the skills, there's loads of openings and nobody really cares about your background. |
| 3 | 但 说实话，金融 也 做得好，因为 钱 总是 往那儿 流。 | But honestly, finance does well too, since money always moves there. |
| 4 | 无论如何，你 都得 一直 学，否则 你 会 很快 落后。 | Either way, you've got to keep learning or you'll fall behind quickly. |

> Well, I'd say tech is probably the easiest these days, mainly because it's growing so fast and the pay's solid. If you've got the skills, there's loads of openings and nobody really cares about your background. But honestly, finance does well too, since money always moves there. Either way, you've got to keep learning or you'll fall behind quickly.

**Q2. Do you think difficulties are good for people's growth?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对。 | Definitely. |
| 2 | 我觉得 面对 挫折 就是 真正 塑造 一个人 的东西，因为 任何人 都能 做得好、当 一切 顺利的时候。 | I reckon facing setbacks is what really shapes a person, because anyone can do well when everything's smooth. |
| 3 | 是 那些 艰难的时期 考验 你。 | It's the rough patches that test you. |
| 4 | 比如，一个人、（那种 失败过 几次的），通常 应付 压力 好得多、以后。所以，对，艰难 不 愉快，但 它 建起 那种 韧劲、（那种 你 装不出来的）。 | For instance, someone who's failed a few times usually handles pressure far better later on. So yeah, hardship isn't pleasant, but it builds the kind of grit you can't fake. |

> Definitely. I reckon facing setbacks is what really shapes a person, because anyone can do well when everything's smooth. It's the rough patches that test you. For instance, someone who's failed a few times usually handles pressure far better later on. So yeah, hardship isn't pleasant, but it builds the kind of grit you can't fake.

**Q3. What qualities help people overcome difficulties and succeed?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我会说 坚持 最要紧。 | Honestly, I'd say persistence matters most. |
| 2 | 天赋 很好，但 一大堆 有天赋的人 放弃，就在 事情 变棘手的那一刻。 | Talent's great, but plenty of gifted people give up the moment things get tricky. |
| 3 | 那些人、（那些 真正 成功的），就是 拒绝 退出。他们 一直 出现，一天 又一天，即使 没有 快速的 回报。 | The ones who actually make it just refuse to quit. They keep showing up, day after day, even when there's no quick payoff. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，一点 运气 也 有帮助，但 你 不能 依靠 那个。 | To be fair, a bit of luck helps too, but you can't rely on that. |

> Honestly, I'd say persistence matters most. Talent's great, but plenty of gifted people give up the moment things get tricky. The ones who actually make it just refuse to quit. They keep showing up, day after day, even when there's no quick payoff. To be fair, a bit of luck helps too, but you can't rely on that.

**Q4. Is it harder for young people to succeed nowadays than in the past?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我觉得 对 年轻人 现在 难得多。 | I think it's loads harder for young people now. |
| 2 | 竞争 残酷，而 住房成本 疯狂，所以 哪怕 一份 好工作 也 撑不了 多远。 | The competition's brutal and housing costs are crazy, so even a good job doesn't stretch far. |
| 3 | 我父母 能 靠 一份薪水 买 一套公寓，（那 在今天 感觉 不可想象）。 | My parents could buy a flat on one salary, which feels unthinkable today. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，网上 有 多得多的 机会，所以 那些聪明的 确实 找到 他们自己的 路子。 | Having said that, there's way more opportunity online, so the smart ones do find their own routes. |

> I think it's loads harder for young people now. The competition's brutal and housing costs are crazy, so even a good job doesn't stretch far. My parents could buy a flat on one salary, which feels unthinkable today. Having said that, there's way more opportunity online, so the smart ones do find their own routes.

**Q5. How important is support from others when facing hard times?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 是的，支持 造成 巨大的 不同。 | Yeah, support makes a huge difference. |
| 2 | 当 你 有 人 撑着你，你 从 失败中 反弹 快得多，因为 你 不是 独自 扛着 这一切。 | When you've got people backing you, you bounce back from failure way quicker, because you're not carrying it all alone. |
| 3 | 一点 鼓励、在 对的时刻，能 阻止 某人 彻底 放弃。 | A bit of encouragement at the right moment can stop someone giving up entirely. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，有些人 顽固到 足以 独自 挺过去，但 我们 大多数 需要 那一推。 | That said, some folk are stubborn enough to push through solo, but most of us need that nudge. |

> Yeah, support makes a huge difference. When you've got people backing you, you bounce back from failure way quicker, because you're not carrying it all alone. A bit of encouragement at the right moment can stop someone giving up entirely. That said, some folk are stubborn enough to push through solo, but most of us need that nudge.

**Q6. Should schools teach children how to deal with failure?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 学校 太 关注 分数、而 对 韧性 不够。 | I'd say schools focus way too much on grades and not enough on resilience. |
| 2 | 孩子 学会 害怕 失败，而不是 把它 看作 正常。 | Kids learn to fear failure instead of seeing it as normal. |
| 3 | 所以 当 他们 终于 撞上 一个 真正的障碍，他们 就 崩了。 | So when they finally hit a real obstacle, they crumble. |
| 4 | 说实话，让 孩子 挣扎 一点、自己 弄明白 事情，会 让 他们 准备得 好得多、为 那个 工作世界。 | Honestly, letting children struggle a little and figure things out themselves would prepare them far better for the working world. |

> I'd say schools focus way too much on grades and not enough on resilience. Kids learn to fear failure instead of seeing it as normal. So when they finally hit a real obstacle, they crumble. Honestly, letting children struggle a little and figure things out themselves would prepare them far better for the working world.

**P3 句型/模板**：`there's loads of openings` · `facing setbacks is what really shapes a person`（动名词主语+what表语）· `It's the rough patches that test you`（强调句）· `the kind of grit you can't fake`（relative后置）· `give up the moment things get tricky` · `The ones who actually make it just refuse to quit`（who后置）· `doesn't stretch far` · `which feels unthinkable today`（which后置）· `bounce back from failure` · `letting children struggle a little … would prepare them far better`（动名词主语）。
