## P2-新26 · A time you changed an important opinion — 育儿观转变（speaker + Muye）

> **Cue**: 何时改的 / 原来的想法 / 为何改 / 感受 · 题型 Event/Opinion

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个想法 是 一个、（我 完全 扭转过来的、大约 三年前），当时 我儿子 Muye 还只是 个学步的小孩——它 全是 关于 怎么 养 小孩子。 | So, the opinion I'd like to talk about is one I completely turned around about three years ago, when my son Muye was just a toddler — it was all about how to raise young kids. |
| [original] | 那会儿，我 真的 相信 孩子 需要 被 从 很早的 年纪 使劲 推。 | Back then, I honestly believed children needed to be pushed hard from a really early age. |
| [original·细] | 我 觉得 越多 课外班 和 识字卡，越好，还觉得 让 一个孩子 就 整天 瞎玩 基本上 是 在 浪费 宝贵的时间。 | I thought the more extra classes and flashcards, the better, and that letting a kid just mess around all day was basically wasting precious time. |
| [original·细] | 我 身边 很多 父母 都 这么想。 | A lot of parents around me felt the same way. |
| [why changed] | 改变 我想法的东西 是 看着 Muye 他自己。 | What changed my mind was watching Muye himself. |
| [why·细] | 我们 推掉了 所有那些课、让 他 自由地 画画和玩，而 让我惊讶的是 他 绝对 蓬勃发展。 | We held off on all those classes and let him draw and play freely, and to my surprise he absolutely flourished. |
| [why·细] | 他 变得 好奇、专注、又 充满 想象力——远远 超过 那些 孩子、（那些 被 不停地 灌训的）。 | He became curious, focused, and full of imagination — far more than the kids who were drilled non-stop. |
| [feel] | 说实话，我 为 之前 那么死板 感觉 有点 傻，但 大多是 松了口气。 | To be honest, I felt a bit foolish for being so rigid before, but mostly relieved. |
| [closer] | 说到底，那个 转变 让我 成了 一个 冷静得多的 爸爸。 | At the end of the day, that shift made me a much calmer dad. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the opinion I'd like to talk about is one I completely turned around about three years ago, when my son Muye was just a toddler — it was all about how to raise young kids. Back then, I honestly believed children needed to be pushed hard from a really early age. I thought the more extra classes and flashcards, the better, and that letting a kid just mess around all day was basically wasting precious time. A lot of parents around me felt the same way. What changed my mind was watching Muye himself. We held off on all those classes and let him draw and play freely, and to my surprise he absolutely flourished. He became curious, focused, and full of imagination — far more than the kids who were drilled non-stop. To be honest, I felt a bit foolish for being so rigid before, but mostly relieved. At the end of the day, that shift made me a much calmer dad.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（改想法）**：我想聊的那个想法是一个、（我完全扭转过来的），当时…——它全是关于… → `the opinion I'd like to talk about is one I completely turned around …, when … — it was all about …`
- **越…越好**：越多课外班和识字卡，越好 → `the more extra classes and flashcards, the better`
- **what主语 + 动名词**：改变我想法的是看着Muye自己 → `What changed my mind was watching Muye himself`
- **推掉…让他自由…他蓬勃发展**：我们推掉那些课、让他自由画画玩，他绝对蓬勃发展 → `We held off on all those classes and let him … freely, and … he absolutely flourished`
- **收尾**：那个转变让我成了冷静得多的爸爸 → `that shift made me a much calmer dad`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. When do most children begin to have their own opinions?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 它 出人意料地 早 就开始，也许 三四岁 左右。 | I'd say it kicks in surprisingly early, maybe around three or four. |
| 2 | 那 就是 孩子 开始 说不、挑 他们自己的 衣服 或 零食 的时候。 | That's when kids start saying no and picking their own clothes or snacks. |
| 3 | 在那之前，他们 大多 模仿 大人 做的 任何东西。 | Before that, they mostly copy whatever adults do. |
| 4 | 但 一旦 他们 上学、跟 别人 混在一起，他们的看法 变得 更鲜明，而且 他们 更热衷于 为 自己的立场 争辩。 | But once they hit school and mix with others, their views get sharper and they're far keener to argue their corner. |

> I'd say it kicks in surprisingly early, maybe around three or four. That's when kids start saying no and picking their own clothes or snacks. Before that, they mostly copy whatever adults do. But once they hit school and mix with others, their views get sharper and they're far keener to argue their corner.

**Q2. Whose opinions are more important to children, their parents' or teachers'?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它 随年龄 变。 | Honestly, it shifts with age. |
| 2 | 当 他们 小的时候，父母的意见 最要紧，因为 孩子 基本上 把 他们 看成 他们的 整个世界。 | When they're little, parents' opinions matter most, since kids basically see them as their whole world. |
| 3 | 但 一旦 他们 是 青少年，老师 常常 分量 更重，尤其 在 学习或职业 选择上。 | But once they're teenagers, teachers often carry more weight, especially on study or career choices. |
| 4 | 我觉得 聪明的做法 是 双方 大致 保持 步调一致，这样 那孩子 不 被 撕扯。 | I reckon the smart move is for both sides to stay roughly on the same page, so the kid isn't pulled apart. |

> Honestly, it shifts with age. When they're little, parents' opinions matter most, since kids basically see them as their whole world. But once they're teenagers, teachers often carry more weight, especially on study or career choices. I reckon the smart move is for both sides to stay roughly on the same page, so the kid isn't pulled apart.

**Q3. Do children communicate more with teachers or with parents?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它 真的 取决于 那个孩子，但 我 倾向 父母、对大多数来说。 | It really depends on the kid, but I'd lean towards parents for most. |
| 2 | 他们 每一天 都在 那儿，所以 孩子 自然 对 他们 敞开心扉、关于 小小的 烦恼。 | They're there every single day, so children naturally open up to them about little worries. |
| 3 | 老师 往往 拿到 那些 跟学校相关的东西。 | Teachers tend to get the school-related stuff. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，有些 害羞的孩子 其实 觉得 一个老师 更容易 说话，尤其 如果 家里 感觉 有点 紧张 或 严厉。 | That said, some shy kids actually find a teacher easier to talk to, especially if home feels a bit tense or strict. |

> It really depends on the kid, but I'd lean towards parents for most. They're there every single day, so children naturally open up to them about little worries. Teachers tend to get the school-related stuff. That said, some shy kids actually find a teacher easier to talk to, especially if home feels a bit tense or strict.

**Q4. Who do young people like to share opinions with?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 大多是 他们的 亲近朋友，我会说。 | Mostly their close friends, I'd say. |
| 2 | 年轻人 觉得 被 大人 评判，所以 他们 宁愿 对 哥们儿 倒苦水、（那些 就 懂的）。 | Young people feel judged by adults, so they'd rather vent to mates who just get it. |
| 3 | 社交媒体 也 起 一个 巨大的作用，因为 他们 能 发 一个 快速的看法、拿到 即时的反应。 | Social media plays a huge part too, since they can post a quick view and get instant reactions. |
| 4 | 但 对于 真正 大的事，他们 很多 还是 悄悄地 转回 家人。 | But for the really big stuff, a lot of them still quietly turn back to family. |

> Mostly their close friends, I'd say. Young people feel judged by adults, so they'd rather vent to mates who just get it. Social media plays a huge part too, since they can post a quick view and get instant reactions. But for the really big stuff, a lot of them still quietly turn back to family.

**Q5. Why do people sometimes change their important opinions?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 通常 因为 现实生活 证明 他们 错了，我觉得。 | Usually because real life proves them wrong, I reckon. |
| 2 | 你 能 抱着 一个信念 好多年，然后 一次经历 就 一夜之间 把它 翻过来。 | You can cling to a belief for years, then one experience just flips it overnight. |
| 3 | 遇到 不同的人 也 起作用，因为 你 突然 看到 一面、（那种 你 从没 考虑过的）。 | Meeting different people does it too, since you suddenly see a side you'd never considered. |
| 4 | 说实话，那些人、（那些 一点 都不 松动的），往往 显得 挺 固执、又 封闭。 | Honestly, the ones who never budge a bit tend to come across as pretty stubborn and closed-minded. |

> Usually because real life proves them wrong, I reckon. You can cling to a belief for years, then one experience just flips it overnight. Meeting different people does it too, since you suddenly see a side you'd never considered. Honestly, the ones who never budge a bit tend to come across as pretty stubborn and closed-minded.

**Q6. Is it easy for people to change their minds once they're adults?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 不太，它 随 你 变老 变难。 | Not really, it gets harder as you age. |
| 2 | 到那时 你 已经 建起 牢固的 习惯，而且 你 挺 依恋 你的看法，所以 承认 你 错了 感觉 几乎 尴尬。 | By then you've built solid habits and you're pretty attached to your views, so admitting you're wrong feels almost embarrassing. |
| 3 | 孩子 一瞬间 就 改主意，但 大人 死守。 | Kids change their minds in a heartbeat, but grown-ups dig in. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，一次 真正的 震动，比如 丢了工作，仍然 能 把 哪怕 最固执的人 也 撬松。 | Having said that, a genuine shock, like losing a job, can still shake even the most fixed person loose. |

> Not really, it gets harder as you age. By then you've built solid habits and you're pretty attached to your views, so admitting you're wrong feels almost embarrassing. Kids change their minds in a heartbeat, but grown-ups dig in. Having said that, a genuine shock, like losing a job, can still shake even the most fixed person loose.

**P3 句型/模板**：`it kicks in surprisingly early` · `That's when kids start …` · `copy whatever adults do` · `argue their corner` · `carry more weight` · `for both sides to stay roughly on the same page` · `open up to them about little worries` · `mates who just get it`（who后置）· `a side you'd never considered`（relative后置）· `admitting you're wrong feels almost embarrassing`（动名词主语）· `shake even the most fixed person loose`。

---

## P2-新27 · An environmental law you'd introduce — 一次性塑料禁令（speaker + wife light context）

> **Cue**: 什么法 / 为何要守 / 是否受欢迎 / 感受 · 题型 Hypothetical

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那条法律 是 一个 正儿八经的 禁令、针对 一次性塑料——基本上，不再有 塑料袋、吸管、或 用完就扔的包装，在 全国的 商店和餐馆里。 | So, the law I'd like to talk about is a proper ban on single-use plastics — basically, no more plastic bags, straws or throwaway packaging in shops and restaurants across the country. |
| [why follow] | 人们 应该 支持它 的原因 挺简单，真的。 | The reason people should get behind it is pretty simple, really. |
| [why·细] | 那种 塑料 被 用 几分钟，但 然后 逗留 几百年，堵塞 河流、伤害 野生动物。 | That kind of plastic gets used for a few minutes but then sticks around for hundreds of years, clogging up rivers and harming wildlife. |
| [why·细] | 如果 每个人 就 换成 可重复用的袋子 和 纸吸管，我们 会 减掉 巨大量的 垃圾，没有 多少 麻烦。 | If everyone just switched to reusable bags and paper straws, we'd cut down a huge amount of waste without much hassle. |
| [popular] | 它 会 受欢迎吗？说实话，我觉得 它 一开始 会 有点 好坏参半。 | Would it be popular? Honestly, I reckon it'd be a bit of a mixed bag at first. |
| [popular·细] | 店主 可能 抱怨 那成本，而 人们 确实 爱 那份方便。 | Shop owners might grumble about the cost, and people do love the convenience. |
| [popular·细] | 但 我老婆 在城市管理部门工作，而 她 总说 人们 会 想通，一旦 一条规则 真的 让 他们的街道 更干净。 | But my wife works in city management, and she always says folks come round once a rule actually makes their streets cleaner. |
| [feel] | 所以，对，说到底，我 对 这一个 感觉 强烈。 | So yeah, at the end of the day, I feel strongly about this one. |
| [closer] | 它 在纸面上 是 一个 小改变，但 它 会 给 这个星球 带来 一个 真正的 不同。 | It's a small change on paper, but it'd make a real difference for the planet. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the law I'd like to talk about is a proper ban on single-use plastics — basically, no more plastic bags, straws or throwaway packaging in shops and restaurants across the country. The reason people should get behind it is pretty simple, really. That kind of plastic gets used for a few minutes but then sticks around for hundreds of years, clogging up rivers and harming wildlife. If everyone just switched to reusable bags and paper straws, we'd cut down a huge amount of waste without much hassle. Would it be popular? Honestly, I reckon it'd be a bit of a mixed bag at first. Shop owners might grumble about the cost, and people do love the convenience. But my wife works in city management, and she always says folks come round once a rule actually makes their streets cleaner. So yeah, at the end of the day, I feel strongly about this one. It's a small change on paper, but it'd make a real difference for the planet.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（假设法）**：我想聊的那条法律是一个正儿八经的禁令、针对[X]——基本上，不再有… → `the law I'd like to talk about is a proper ban on [X] — basically, no more …`
- **为何守 bullet**：人们应该支持它的原因挺简单 → `The reason people should get behind it is pretty simple`
- **用几分钟却逗留几百年 + 分词**：被用几分钟但逗留几百年，堵塞河流、伤害野生动物 → `gets used for a few minutes but sticks around for hundreds of years, clogging up rivers and harming wildlife`
- **虚拟条件**：如果每个人换成…，我们会减掉… → `If everyone just switched to …, we'd cut down …`
- **人们会想通**：人们会想通，一旦规则真的让街道更干净 → `folks come round once a rule actually makes their streets cleaner`
- **收尾**：纸面上小改变，却给星球带来真正不同 → `a small change on paper, but it'd make a real difference for the planet`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. How does technology affect the law?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 技术 帮助 很大，说实话。 | I'd say technology helps a lot, honestly. |
| 2 | 有 到处都是的 摄像头 和 传感器 现在，抓 违规的人 容易 得多，比如 乱倒垃圾 或 超速。 | With cameras and sensors everywhere now, it's far easier to catch people breaking rules, like dumping rubbish or speeding. |
| 3 | 所以 执法 变得 更便宜、更公平。此外，app 让 普通人 立刻 报告 问题。 | So enforcement gets cheaper and fairer. Plus, apps let ordinary folk report problems straight away. |
| 4 | 不过 说句公道话，它 也 引出 一些 棘手的问题、关于 隐私，（那些 立法者 还没 解决的）。 | To be fair, though, it also raises tricky questions about privacy that lawmakers haven't sorted out yet. |

> I'd say technology helps a lot, honestly. With cameras and sensors everywhere now, it's far easier to catch people breaking rules, like dumping rubbish or speeding. So enforcement gets cheaper and fairer. Plus, apps let ordinary folk report problems straight away. To be fair, though, it also raises tricky questions about privacy that lawmakers haven't sorted out yet.

**Q2. What kinds of rules do schools in China have?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，中国学校 往往 挺严，我觉得。 | Well, Chinese schools tend to be pretty strict, I reckon. |
| 2 | 通常 有 一个 着装要求、固定的座位、还有 一些规则、关于 手机 在课上 被禁。 | There's usually a dress code, fixed seating, and rules about phones being banned in class. |
| 3 | 学生 也 有 设定好的时间、[用来]打扫 他们自己的 教室。 | Students also have set times for cleaning their own classrooms. |
| 4 | 说实话，其中有些 感觉 有点 死板，但 我会说 它 确实 教 纪律、也 让 每个人 步调一致。 | Honestly, some of it feels a bit rigid, but I'd say it does teach discipline and keeps everyone on the same page. |

> Well, Chinese schools tend to be pretty strict, I reckon. There's usually a dress code, fixed seating, and rules about phones being banned in class. Students also have set times for cleaning their own classrooms. Honestly, some of it feels a bit rigid, but I'd say it does teach discipline and keeps everyone on the same page.

**Q3. Will there be a law that is universally accepted?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我 很怀疑。 | Honestly, I doubt it. |
| 2 | 人们 来自 如此 不同的 文化 和 信仰，以至于 你 总能 找到 某个人、（那种 不同意的），甚至 对 基本的东西、比如 死刑。 | People come from such different cultures and beliefs that you'll always find someone who disagrees, even with basic things like the death penalty. |
| 3 | 更重要的是，在一个国家 感觉公平的东西 在别处 可能 显得 严苛。 | What's more, what feels fair in one country might seem harsh somewhere else. |
| 4 | 不过 说句公道话，某些规则、比如 禁止 奴隶制，确实 挺接近 在 各处 都被接受。 | To be fair, though, certain rules, like banning slavery, do come pretty close to being accepted everywhere. |

> Honestly, I doubt it. People come from such different cultures and beliefs that you'll always find someone who disagrees, even with basic things like the death penalty. What's more, what feels fair in one country might seem harsh somewhere else. To be fair, though, certain rules, like banning slavery, do come pretty close to being accepted everywhere.

**Q4. What environmental laws does your country already have?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 其实挺多的。 | Quite a few, actually. |
| 2 | 中国 现在 有 相当 严的 空气质量法，所以 工厂 面对 排放的 限制，而 很多城市 运行 垃圾分类方案、（在那里 你 把 你的垃圾 分开）。 | China's got fairly tough air-quality laws now, so factories face limits on emissions, and a lot of cities run waste-sorting schemes where you separate your rubbish. |
| 3 | 也有 一个 推动、关于 保护 河流和森林。 | There's also a push on protecting rivers and forests. |
| 4 | 我会说 执法 因地 而异，但 总的来说 那些规则 这些年 肯定 收紧了。 | I'd say enforcement varies from place to place, but overall the rules have definitely tightened up over recent years. |

> Quite a few, actually. China's got fairly tough air-quality laws now, so factories face limits on emissions, and a lot of cities run waste-sorting schemes where you separate your rubbish. There's also a push on protecting rivers and forests. I'd say enforcement varies from place to place, but overall the rules have definitely tightened up over recent years.

**Q5. Whose responsibility is it to protect the environment — governments or individuals?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我会说 是 两者。 | Honestly, I'd say it's both. |
| 2 | 政府 得 定 那些大规则、也 资助 清洁能源，因为 个人 修不了 某种 这么大的东西、独自。 | Governments have to set the big rules and fund clean energy, since individuals can't fix something this huge alone. |
| 3 | 但 日常的 选择 也 要紧，比如 回收 或 减少 开车。如果 我们 就 把它 全 交给 政客，什么 都 不 真正 动。 | But everyday choices matter too, like recycling or cutting back on driving. If we just leave it all to politicians, nothing really shifts. |
| 4 | 所以我觉得 真正的进步 需要 每个人 往 同一个方向 使劲。 | So I reckon real progress needs everyone pulling in the same direction. |

> Honestly, I'd say it's both. Governments have to set the big rules and fund clean energy, since individuals can't fix something this huge alone. But everyday choices matter too, like recycling or cutting back on driving. If we just leave it all to politicians, nothing really shifts. So I reckon real progress needs everyone pulling in the same direction.

**Q6. Do you think people are willing to pay more for eco-friendly products?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 看情况，真的。 | It depends, really. |
| 2 | 有些人 乐意 多付钱、为 环保产品，因为 它 跟 他们的价值观 对得上，尤其 年轻的 消费者。 | Some people happily pay extra for eco-friendly products because it lines up with their values, especially younger shoppers. |
| 3 | 但 对 很多 预算紧的家庭，价格 每次 都赢，而 那 完全 可以理解。 | But for a lot of families on a tight budget, price wins every time, and that's totally understandable. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 意愿 在增长，但 它 不会 真正 起飞，直到 绿色的选择 变得 对 每个人 真正 负担得起。 | So I'd say willingness is growing, but it won't take off properly until green options become genuinely affordable for everyone. |

> It depends, really. Some people happily pay extra for eco-friendly products because it lines up with their values, especially younger shoppers. But for a lot of families on a tight budget, price wins every time, and that's totally understandable. So I'd say willingness is growing, but it won't take off properly until green options become genuinely affordable for everyone.

**P3 句型/模板**：`With cameras and sensors everywhere now, it's far easier to catch …`（with-absolute）· `tricky questions … that lawmakers haven't sorted out yet`（that后置）· `phones being banned in class` · `someone who disagrees`（who后置）· `what feels fair in one country might seem harsh somewhere else`（what主语）· `come pretty close to being accepted everywhere` · `waste-sorting schemes where you separate your rubbish`（where后置）· `varies from place to place` · `fix something this huge alone` · `everyone pulling in the same direction` · `won't take off properly until …`。

---

## P2-老01 · A perfect job you'd like — 自主灵活的技术角色（speaker）

> **Cue**: 是什么 / 怎么知道 / 需学什么 / 为何完美 · 题型 Object/Job

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那份工作 是 一个 相当 灵活的、自主导向的 角色、（在那里 我 能 挑 我 做的 那些问题、然后 按 我自己的方式 解决它们），而不是 就 一整天 听 别人 的命令。 | So, the job I'd like to talk about is a fairly flexible, self-directed role where I'd get to pick the problems I work on and solve them on my own terms, rather than just following someone else's orders all day. |
| [how knew] | 我 已经 知道 这 就是 我想要的、有 好一阵子了。 | I've known this is what I want for quite a while now. |
| [how·细] | 在 科技行业 干了 好些年 之后，我 注意到 我 总是 在 我最佳状态、当 没人 微管理 我的时候——给我 一个 棘手的问题 和 一些自由，我 就会 乐意地 忘掉时间。 | After years working in tech, I noticed I'm always at my best when nobody's micromanaging me — give me a tricky problem and some freedom, and I'll happily lose track of time. |
| [need learn] | 为了 拿下 一份 那样的工作，我 需要 磨 几样东西。 | To land a job like that, I'd need to sharpen a couple of things. |
| [need·细] | 主要是 那种 自律、[去]好好 管理 我自己的时间，加上 那种 自信、[去]支持 我自己的判断，当 没有 老板 告诉我 什么 是对的。 | Mainly the discipline to manage my own time well, plus the confidence to back my own judgement when there's no boss telling me what's right. |
| [why/EL] | 它 会 完美的 原因 归结到 我是谁。 | The reason it'd be perfect comes down to who I am. |
| [why·细] | 我 天生 好奇，而且 我 真的 珍视 我的自由——但 同时，那种 灵活性 意味着 我 仍然 会 在 我家人 身边。 | I'm curious by nature, and I really value my freedom — but at the same time, that kind of flexibility means I'd still be around for my family. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那就是 那份工作、（我 很想有的）。 | So yeah, that's the job I'd love to have. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the job I'd like to talk about is a fairly flexible, self-directed role where I'd get to pick the problems I work on and solve them on my own terms, rather than just following someone else's orders all day. I've known this is what I want for quite a while now. After years working in tech, I noticed I'm always at my best when nobody's micromanaging me — give me a tricky problem and some freedom, and I'll happily lose track of time. To land a job like that, I'd need to sharpen a couple of things. Mainly the discipline to manage my own time well, plus the confidence to back my own judgement when there's no boss telling me what's right. The reason it'd be perfect comes down to who I am. I'm curious by nature, and I really value my freedom — but at the same time, that kind of flexibility means I'd still be around for my family. So yeah, that's the job I'd love to have.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（工作）+ where**：我想聊的那份工作是一个…角色、（在那里我能…），而不是… → `the job I'd like to talk about is a … role where I'd get to …, rather than …`
- **最佳状态 when**：我总在最佳状态、当没人微管理我 → `I'm always at my best when nobody's micromanaging me`
- **忘掉时间**：乐意地忘掉时间 → `happily lose track of time`
- **需学 bullet**：为了拿下那样的工作，我需要磨几样东西 → `To land a job like that, I'd need to sharpen a couple of things`
- **归结到我是谁**：它完美的原因归结到我是谁 → `The reason it'd be perfect comes down to who I am`
- **天生好奇**：我天生好奇 → `I'm curious by nature`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What kind of job can be called a 'dream job'?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我会说 一份 梦想工作 不是 关于 钱 或 地位——它是 工作、（那种 你 会 乐意 做的、哪怕 在 糟糕的一天）。 | Honestly, I'd say a dream job isn't about money or status — it's work you'd happily do even on a rough day. |
| 2 | 对我来说，它是 某种 有意义的东西、（那种 给你 自由、[去]做 你自己的决定的）。 | For me, it's something meaningful that gives you freedom to make your own calls. |
| 3 | 如果 你 醒来 好奇、而不是 害怕 那办公室，那 是 一个 挺可靠的 信号、（你 真的 找到了它）。 | If you wake up curious rather than dreading the office, that's a pretty solid sign you've actually found it. |

> Honestly, I'd say a dream job isn't about money or status — it's work you'd happily do even on a rough day. For me, it's something meaningful that gives you freedom to make your own calls. If you wake up curious rather than dreading the office, that's a pretty solid sign you've actually found it.

**Q2. What jobs do children want to do when they grow up?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 大多是 那些 光鲜的、显眼的，我觉得。 | Mostly the glamorous, visible ones, I reckon. |
| 2 | 一大堆 孩子 想 当 宇航员、医生、或 足球运动员，因为 那 就是 他们 在电视上、在电影里 看到的。 | Loads of kids want to be astronauts, doctors, or footballers because that's what they see on telly and in films. |
| 3 | 他们 被 那种 兴奋 吸引，而不是 那种 日常的 苦活。 | They're drawn to the excitement rather than the daily grind. |
| 4 | 它 说得通——在那个年纪 你 并不 真的 领会 一份工作 涉及什么，所以 你 挑 无论什么 看起来 最酷的。 | It makes sense — at that age you don't really grasp what a job involves, so you pick whatever looks coolest. |

> Mostly the glamorous, visible ones, I reckon. Loads of kids want to be astronauts, doctors, or footballers because that's what they see on telly and in films. They're drawn to the excitement rather than the daily grind. It makes sense — at that age you don't really grasp what a job involves, so you pick whatever looks coolest.

**Q3. Do people's ideal jobs change as they grow up?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，它们 变 挺多。 | Absolutely, they shift quite a lot. |
| 2 | 小时候 你 追 无论什么 听起来 令人兴奋的，但 一旦 你 大了 你 开始 权衡 钱、稳定性、还有 一份工作 怎么 契合 你的生活方式。 | As a kid you chase whatever sounds exciting, but once you're older you start weighing money, stability, and how a job fits your lifestyle. |
| 3 | 经历 也 改变 你——你 弄清 你 实际上 擅长什么。 | Experience changes you too — you figure out what you're actually good at. |
| 4 | 所以 它 会 很奇怪，如果 你的理想工作 永远 完全 保持 一样。 | So it'd be odd if your ideal job stayed exactly the same forever. |

> Absolutely, they shift quite a lot. As a kid you chase whatever sounds exciting, but once you're older you start weighing money, stability, and how a job fits your lifestyle. Experience changes you too — you figure out what you're actually good at. So it'd be odd if your ideal job stayed exactly the same forever.

**Q4. What should people consider when choosing jobs?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 几样 关键的东西，我会说。 | A few key things, I'd say. |
| 2 | 薪水 要紧，显然，但 你 也 应该 权衡 那工作 是否 契合 你的性格、还 留 空间 给 一个 它之外的 生活。 | Pay matters, obviously, but you should also weigh whether the work suits your personality and leaves room for a life outside it. |
| 3 | 工作 稳定性 也 算。 | Job security counts too. |
| 4 | 说实话，最聪明的 一步 是 挑 某种东西、（那种 你 不会 耗竭在上面的），因为 一份 肥薪水 意义不大，如果 你 害怕 每个 周一 早晨。 | Honestly, the smartest move is picking something you won't burn out on, because a fat salary means little if you dread every Monday morning. |

> A few key things, I'd say. Pay matters, obviously, but you should also weigh whether the work suits your personality and leaves room for a life outside it. Job security counts too. Honestly, the smartest move is picking something you won't burn out on, because a fat salary means little if you dread every Monday morning.

**Q5. Is salary the main reason why people choose a certain job?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 对很多人，当然，它是 一个 大因素——账单 不会 自己 付，毕竟。 | For a lot of people, sure, it's a big factor — bills don't pay themselves, after all. |
| 2 | 但 我 不会 把它 叫做 主要的那个。 | But I wouldn't call it the main one. |
| 3 | 很多人 乐意 拿 减薪、为了 更短的工时 或 一份工作、（那种 他们 真心 享受的）。 | Plenty of folk happily take a pay cut for shorter hours or a job they genuinely enjoy. |
| 4 | 说实话，一旦 你 挣得 足够 生活，像 尊重 和 自由 这样的东西 常常 更要紧。 | Honestly, once you earn enough to live on, things like respect and freedom often matter more. |

> For a lot of people, sure, it's a big factor — bills don't pay themselves, after all. But I wouldn't call it the main one. Plenty of folk happily take a pay cut for shorter hours or a job they genuinely enjoy. Honestly, once you earn enough to live on, things like respect and freedom often matter more.

**Q6. What kind of jobs are the most popular in your country?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 在中国，政府 和 公务员 岗位 极其 抢手，主要是因为 它们 稳定、也 带着 扎实的福利。 | In China, government and civil-service posts are hugely sought after, mainly because they're stable and come with solid benefits. |
| 2 | 科技岗 也 受欢迎，因为 它们 薪水好，虽然 工时 可能 残酷。 | Tech roles are popular too, since they pay well, though the hours can be brutal. |
| 3 | 说实话，很多 归结到 安全感——在 疫情之后，人们 真的 开始 渴望 一份 稳定、可靠的 薪水。 | Honestly, a lot of it comes down to security — after the pandemic, people really started craving a steady, reliable paycheque. |

> In China, government and civil-service posts are hugely sought after, mainly because they're stable and come with solid benefits. Tech roles are popular too, since they pay well, though the hours can be brutal. Honestly, a lot of it comes down to security — after the pandemic, people really started craving a steady, reliable paycheque.

**P3 句型/模板**：`work you'd happily do even on a rough day`（relative后置）· `something meaningful that gives you freedom to make your own calls`（that后置）· `that's what they see on telly`（what表语）· `the daily grind` · `what a job involves` / `whatever looks coolest`（embedded）· `how a job fits your lifestyle`（embedded）· `picking something you won't burn out on`（动名词主语+relative）· `take a pay cut for … a job they genuinely enjoy` · `hugely sought after` / `come with solid benefits` · `craving a steady, reliable paycheque`。

---

## P2-老02 · A famous person you'd like to meet — Christopher Nolan（Person）

> **Cue**: 是谁 / 怎么知道 / 想在哪见 / 为何想见 · 题型 Person

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个人 是 Christopher Nolan，那位 电影导演。 | So, the person I'd like to talk about is Christopher Nolan, the film director. |
| [背景] | 他 是 那个人、（在 像《盗梦空间》和《星际穿越》这样的 电影 背后的），而 说实话 他 是 我 最喜欢的 导演之一、有史以来。 | He's the guy behind movies like Inception and Interstellar, and honestly he's one of my favourite directors of all time. |
| [how knew] | 我 最早 接触到 他的作品 是 好多年前，当 一个朋友 拖着 我 去看《盗梦空间》。 | I first came across his work years ago when a friend dragged me to watch Inception. |
| [how·细] | 我 完全 不知道 我 在 卷入 什么，但 到结尾 我的脑子 被 彻底 炸了，而 从那以后 我 就 一直 迷上 他的电影。 | I had no idea what I was getting into, but by the end my mind was completely blown, and I've been hooked on his films ever since. |
| [how meet] | 如果 我 有天 有 机会 见到他，我 会 很想 它 是 在 某个 放松的地方，比如 一个 安静的咖啡馆，而不是 一个 花哨的 红毯活动。 | If I ever got the chance to meet him, I'd love it to be somewhere relaxed, like a quiet café rather than a fancy red-carpet event. |
| [meet·细] | 那样 我们 就能 好好 聊聊，没有 一群 粉丝 在 我们周围 挤进来。 | That way we could just chat properly, without a crowd of fans pushing in around us. |
| [why meet/EL] | 我 想见 他的 主要原因 是 我 是 科幻的 大粉丝，而 他的故事 真的 拧 你的脑子。 | The main reason I'd want to meet him is that I'm a big fan of sci-fi, and his stories really bend your brain. |
| [why·细] | 我 会 问他 他 究竟 怎么 想出 那么 扭曲的 情节。 | I'd ask him how on earth he dreams up plots that twisted. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那就是 那个名人、（我 最想 坐下来 一起聊的）。 | So yeah, that's the famous person I'd most love to sit down with. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the person I'd like to talk about is Christopher Nolan, the film director. He's the guy behind movies like Inception and Interstellar, and honestly he's one of my favourite directors of all time. I first came across his work years ago when a friend dragged me to watch Inception. I had no idea what I was getting into, but by the end my mind was completely blown, and I've been hooked on his films ever since. If I ever got the chance to meet him, I'd love it to be somewhere relaxed, like a quiet café rather than a fancy red-carpet event. That way we could just chat properly, without a crowd of fans pushing in around us. The main reason I'd want to meet him is that I'm a big fan of sci-fi, and his stories really bend your brain. I'd ask him how on earth he dreams up plots that twisted. So yeah, that's the famous person I'd most love to sit down with.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **opener（名人）+ 同位**：我想聊的那个人是[X]，那位…导演；他是那个在…电影背后的人 → `the person I'd like to talk about is [X], the …` / `He's the guy behind movies like …`
- **接触到 + when 拖我去看**：我最早接触到他的作品是当一个朋友拖我去看X → `I first came across his work … when a friend dragged me to watch X`
- **脑子被炸/迷上**：我的脑子被彻底炸了；从那以后一直迷上 → `my mind was completely blown` / `hooked on his films ever since`
- **假设见面**：如果我有天有机会见他，我会想它在某个放松的地方 → `If I ever got the chance to meet him, I'd love it to be somewhere relaxed`
- **embedded 疑问**：我会问他究竟怎么想出那么扭曲的情节 → `how on earth he dreams up plots that twisted`
- **收尾**：那个我最想坐下来一起聊的名人 → `the famous person I'd most love to sit down with`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What are the advantages and disadvantages of being a famous child?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它 是 一个 真正的 好坏参半。 | Honestly, it's a real mixed bag. |
| 2 | 好的一面，一个 出名的孩子 拿到 惊人的 机会 和 钱、挺早地，（那 大多数孩子 只能 梦想）。 | On the plus side, a famous kid gets amazing opportunities and money pretty early, which most children can only dream of. |
| 3 | 但 那坏处 巨大——他们 失去 任何 正常的童年，媒体 不停地 追逐 他们，而 他们 在 零隐私 中 长大。 | But the downside's huge — they lose any normal childhood, the press hounds them non-stop, and they grow up with zero privacy. |
| 4 | 我会说 那压力 常常 压过 那些好处。 | I'd say the pressure often outweighs the perks. |

> Honestly, it's a real mixed bag. On the plus side, a famous kid gets amazing opportunities and money pretty early, which most children can only dream of. But the downside's huge — they lose any normal childhood, the press hounds them non-stop, and they grow up with zero privacy. I'd say the pressure often outweighs the perks.

**Q2. What can today's children do to become famous?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 现在 我会说 社交媒体 是 那个 明显的 路子。 | These days I'd say social media's the obvious route. |
| 2 | 一个孩子 能 发视频、建立 一批粉丝、还能 一夜之间 爆火，如果 他们 走运。 | A kid can post videos, build a following, and go viral overnight if they're lucky. |
| 3 | 但 除此之外，天生的 才华 仍然 要紧——在 唱歌、体育、或 编程上 出色 让 你 被注意到。 | But beyond that, raw talent still matters — being brilliant at singing, sport or coding gets you noticed. |
| 4 | 说实话，持续性 也 是 关键，因为 发 一次 不管用。你 得 一直 出现。 | Honestly, consistency's key too, since posting once won't cut it. You've got to keep showing up. |

> These days I'd say social media's the obvious route. A kid can post videos, build a following, and go viral overnight if they're lucky. But beyond that, raw talent still matters — being brilliant at singing, sport or coding gets you noticed. Honestly, consistency's key too, since posting once won't cut it. You've got to keep showing up.

**Q3. What can children do with their fame?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，那些聪明的 用它 做好事，我觉得。 | Well, the smart ones use it for good, I reckon. |
| 2 | 他们 能 为 慈善机构 提高关注、为 重要的事业 发声、还能 成为 更小的孩子 的 榜样。 | They can raise awareness for charities, speak up about causes that matter, and become role models for younger kids. |
| 3 | 此外，名气 打开 门——赞助、奖学金、那种 机会。 | Plus, fame opens doors — sponsorships, scholarships, that sort of opportunity. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，有些 就 追 关注、然后 浪费了它，（那 说实话 有点 可惜）。 | Having said that, some just chase attention and waste it, which is honestly a bit of a shame. |

> Well, the smart ones use it for good, I reckon. They can raise awareness for charities, speak up about causes that matter, and become role models for younger kids. Plus, fame opens doors — sponsorships, scholarships, that sort of opportunity. Having said that, some just chase attention and waste it, which is honestly a bit of a shame.

**Q4. Do people become famous because of their talent?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 不总是，说句公道话。 | Not always, to be fair. |
| 2 | 天赋 肯定 有帮助，但 一大堆 出名的人 并不 特别 有天赋——他们 只是 擅长 营销 自己、或者 他们 在 时机上 走了运。 | Talent definitely helps, but plenty of famous people aren't especially gifted — they're just good at marketing themselves or they got lucky with timing. |
| 3 | 想想 那些 真人秀明星、（那种 纯粹 因为 在屏幕上 而 出名的）。 | Think of reality TV stars who're famous purely for being on screen. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 它是 天赋、苦干、人脉、还有 说实话 相当一点 运气 的 一种混合。 | So I'd say it's a mix of talent, hard graft, connections, and honestly a fair bit of luck. |

> Not always, to be fair. Talent definitely helps, but plenty of famous people aren't especially gifted — they're just good at marketing themselves or they got lucky with timing. Think of reality TV stars who're famous purely for being on screen. So I'd say it's a mix of talent, hard graft, connections, and honestly a fair bit of luck.

**Q5. Is it easy to become famous in your country?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 比 以前 容易，我觉得。 | Easier than it used to be, I reckon. |
| 2 | 有 像 抖音 和 哔哩哔哩 这样的平台，任何 有手机的人 都能 从零 建起 一批观众。 | With platforms like Douyin and Bilibili, anyone with a phone can build an audience from scratch. |
| 3 | 但 变得 真正 出名——不只是 昙花一现——仍然 难。 | But becoming properly famous — not just a flash in the pan — is still tough. |
| 4 | 竞争 残酷，所以 你 需要 一个 真正的 钩子 [去]脱颖而出。大多数人 挺快 就 淡出，说实话。 | The competition's brutal, so you need a real hook to stand out. Most people fade pretty fast, to be honest. |

> Easier than it used to be, I reckon. With platforms like Douyin and Bilibili, anyone with a phone can build an audience from scratch. But becoming properly famous — not just a flash in the pan — is still tough. The competition's brutal, so you need a real hook to stand out. Most people fade pretty fast, to be honest.

**Q6. Do you want to be a famous person?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，不太想。 | Honestly, not really. |
| 2 | 我 太 喜欢 我的隐私，而 名气 看起来 累人——到处 被 认出来、你的每一个动作 在网上 被 评判。 | I like my privacy too much, and fame seems exhausting — being recognised everywhere, having your every move judged online. |
| 3 | 我 宁愿 在 我自己的领域 悄悄地 成功，也不 被 摄像头 追。 | I'd rather be quietly successful in my own field than chased by cameras. |
| 4 | 也许 一点 认可、为 做 某种 真正有用的事，会 不错，但 全面的 名人？不了，谢谢，那 不适合 我。 | Maybe a little recognition for doing something genuinely useful would be nice, but full-blown celebrity? No thanks, that's not for me. |

> Honestly, not really. I like my privacy too much, and fame seems exhausting — being recognised everywhere, having your every move judged online. I'd rather be quietly successful in my own field than chased by cameras. Maybe a little recognition for doing something genuinely useful would be nice, but full-blown celebrity? No thanks, that's not for me.

**P3 句型/模板**：`which most children can only dream of`（which后置）· `the press hounds them non-stop` · `the pressure often outweighs the perks` · `build a following` / `go viral overnight` · `being brilliant at … gets you noticed`（动名词主语）· `posting once won't cut it` · `causes that matter`（that后置）· `reality TV stars who're famous purely for being on screen`（who后置）· `not just a flash in the pan` · `a real hook to stand out` · `I'd rather be quietly successful … than chased by cameras`。

---

## P2-老03 · An occasion when you couldn't use your phone — 京都寺庙大殿（Event）

> **Cue**: 何时 / 何地 / 为何不许 / 感受 · 题型 Event

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个场合 是 一次 家庭旅行 去京都、几年前，当 我 不被 允许 用 我的手机、在 一座 古老寺庙大殿 里面。 | So, the occasion I'd like to talk about is a family trip to Kyoto a couple of years back, when I wasn't allowed to use my phone inside one of the old temple halls. |
| [where] | 我们 那时候 已经 去过 京都 三次了，但 这 特定的一座 大殿 对我们 是 新的。 | We'd been to Kyoto three times by then, but this particular hall was new to us. |
| [why] | 我们 一踏进去 的那一刻，一个 牌子 说得 明明白白：不许 拍照、不许 手机。 | The moment we stepped in, a sign made it clear: no photos, no phones. |
| [why·细] | 那 是 出于 对 那地方 的尊重，还有 [去]保护 一些 真的 很老的 壁画、在 墙上。 | It was out of respect for the place, and to protect some really old artwork on the walls. |
| [feel] | 说实话，我的 第一反应 是 一点 恼火。 | Honestly, my first reaction was a bit of annoyance. |
| [feel·细] | 我的 本能 是 拍 一张 每样东西 的照片，所以 被 告知 [去]把 我的手机 收起来 感觉 怪怪的。 | My instinct is to snap a photo of everything, so being told to put my phone away felt weird. |
| [turn] | 但 几分钟 之后，某种东西 变了。 | But after a few minutes, something shifted. |
| [turn·细] | 没有 一个 屏幕 在 我面前，我 开始 真正地 看——那些 褪色的 画、那些 木梁、那份 静。 | Without a screen in front of me, I started to actually look — the faded paintings, the wooden beams, the stillness. |
| [细] | 连 我的妻子 和 Muye 都 保持 安静。 | Even my wife and Muye stayed quiet. |
| [EL] | 回头看，我 真心 感激 那条规矩。 | Looking back, I'm genuinely grateful for that rule. |
| [EL·细] | 我是说，它 逼 我 [去]在场、[去]沉浸其中。 | I mean, it forced me to be present and soak it in. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，差不多 就是 这样。 | So yeah, that's pretty much it. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the occasion I'd like to talk about is a family trip to Kyoto a couple of years back, when I wasn't allowed to use my phone inside one of the old temple halls. We'd been to Kyoto three times by then, but this particular hall was new to us. The moment we stepped in, a sign made it clear: no photos, no phones. It was out of respect for the place, and to protect some really old artwork on the walls. Honestly, my first reaction was a bit of annoyance. My instinct is to snap a photo of everything, so being told to put my phone away felt weird. But after a few minutes, something shifted. Without a screen in front of me, I started to actually look — the faded paintings, the wooden beams, the stillness. Even my wife and Muye stayed quiet. Looking back, I'm genuinely grateful for that rule. I mean, it forced me to be present and soak it in. So yeah, that's pretty much it.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **occasion opener + when**：我想聊的场合是…几年前，当我不被允许用手机… → `the occasion I'd like to talk about is …, when I wasn't allowed to …`
- **The moment 从句**：我们一踏进去那一刻，一个牌子说得明白 → `The moment we stepped in, a sign made it clear`
- **动名词主语（被动）**：被告知把手机收起来感觉怪 → `being told to put my phone away felt weird`
- **转折信号**：某种东西变了 → `something shifted`
- **without 开头状语**：没有屏幕在我面前，我开始真正地看 → `Without a screen in front of me, I started to actually look`
- **EL**：回头看我真心感激那规矩；它逼我在场、沉浸其中 → `Looking back, I'm genuinely grateful …` / `it forced me to be present and soak it in`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. How do young and old people use mobile phones differently?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 有 一个 明显的 差距。 | Well, I'd say there's a clear gap. |
| 2 | 年轻人 倾向于 活在 他们的手机上——打游戏、刷社交媒体、不停地 发消息。 | Younger folk tend to live on their phones — gaming, scrolling social media, messaging non-stop. |
| 3 | 老年人 偏向 那些 基本的——比如 打电话 或 偶尔一张照片。 | Older people lean towards the basics, like calls or the odd photo. |
| 4 | 比如，我的 祖父母 真的 只 用 他们的（手机）[去]给 家人 打电话，而 一个 青少年 一整天 粘 在 屏幕上，同时 玩 五个 app。 | For instance, my grandparents only really use theirs to ring family, whereas a teenager's glued to a screen all day, juggling five apps at once. |

> Well, I'd say there's a clear gap. Younger folk tend to live on their phones — gaming, scrolling social media, messaging non-stop. Older people lean towards the basics, like calls or the odd photo. For instance, my grandparents only really use theirs to ring family, whereas a teenager's glued to a screen all day, juggling five apps at once.

**Q2. What positive and negative impact do mobile phones have on friendship?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它 两面都切。 | Honestly, it cuts both ways. |
| 2 | 好的一面，手机 让 你 保持 靠近 朋友、（那种 住得 远的），有 一条 快消息 或 一个 视频通话 让 事情 活着。 | On the plus side, phones let you stay close to friends who live far off, with a quick text or video call keeping things alive. |
| 3 | 但 它们 也 能 冲淡 真实的 连接——人们 坐 一起 却 盯着 各自的 屏幕。 | But they can also water down real connection — people sit together yet stare at their own screens. |
| 4 | 所以 它 是 一个 顺手的 工具，虽然 它 不该 取代 真正地 在场。 | So it's a handy tool, though it shouldn't replace actually being there. |

> Honestly, it cuts both ways. On the plus side, phones let you stay close to friends who live far off, with a quick text or video call keeping things alive. But they can also water down real connection — people sit together yet stare at their own screens. So it's a handy tool, though it shouldn't replace actually being there.

**Q3. Is it a waste of time to take pictures with mobile phones?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 一点也不，在 合理范围内。 | Not at all, within reason. |
| 2 | 拍 一张照片 让 你 抓住 一个 时刻、（那种 你 否则 会 忘掉的），而且 它 一秒 就 完成。 | Snapping a photo lets you hold onto a moment you'd otherwise forget, and it's done in a second. |
| 3 | 那问题 是 当 人们 沉迷于 它——把 整趟旅行 花 在 一个 镜头 后面、而不是 沉浸其中。 | The problem's when people obsess over it — spending the whole trip behind a lens instead of soaking it in. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 几张 快照 没问题，但 你 不该 让 那相机 主导 一切。 | So I'd say a few quick shots are fine, but you shouldn't let the camera run the show. |

> Not at all, within reason. Snapping a photo lets you hold onto a moment you'd otherwise forget, and it's done in a second. The problem's when people obsess over it — spending the whole trip behind a lens instead of soaking it in. So I'd say a few quick shots are fine, but you shouldn't let the camera run the show.

**Q4. Do you think it is necessary to have laws on the use of mobile phones?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，尤其 是 围绕 安全。 | Definitely, especially around safety. |
| 2 | 拿 开车时 发短信 来说——那 是 鲁莽的，所以 禁 它 就是 有道理、还 救 人命。 | Take texting while driving — that's reckless, so banning it just makes sense and saves lives. |
| 3 | 我 也 会 支持 关于 学校里 手机 的 规矩，因为 它们 对 孩子 是 一个 巨大的 分心物。 | I'd also back rules on phones in schools, since they're a huge distraction for kids. |
| 4 | 但 除了 像那些 清楚的 情况，我觉得 它 归 常识、而不是 那法律 [去]规定 每一个 小习惯。 | But beyond clear cases like those, I reckon it's down to common sense rather than the law dictating every little habit. |

> Definitely, especially around safety. Take texting while driving — that's reckless, so banning it just makes sense and saves lives. I'd also back rules on phones in schools, since they're a huge distraction for kids. But beyond clear cases like those, I reckon it's down to common sense rather than the law dictating every little habit.

**Q5. What are examples of good and poor phone manners?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 好礼貌 会 是 在 一顿饭 期间 把 你的手机 收起来、给 人们 你 全部的 注意力。 | Good manners would be putting your phone away during a meal and giving people your full attention. |
| 2 | 差礼貌？对话中 发短信，或者 在 一列 安静的火车上 接 一个 大声的 电话。 | Poor manners? Texting mid-conversation, or taking a loud call on a quiet train. |
| 3 | 比如，没有 什么 比 某人 刷手机、当 你 正 掏心掏肺 的时候 更粗鲁。 | For instance, nothing's ruder than someone scrolling while you're pouring your heart out. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 它 真的 归结到 读 那房间 的气氛、还有 尊重 别人。 | So I'd say it really comes down to reading the room and respecting others. |

> Good manners would be putting your phone away during a meal and giving people your full attention. Poor manners? Texting mid-conversation, or taking a loud call on a quiet train. For instance, nothing's ruder than someone scrolling while you're pouring your heart out. So I'd say it really comes down to reading the room and respecting others.

**Q6. How does the internet benefit people?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 在 一大堆 方面，说实话。 | In loads of ways, honestly. |
| 2 | 它 把 一个 信息的世界 放 在 你的 指尖——你 能 学 几乎 任何东西、免费地、从 一个 快搜索。 | It puts a world of information at your fingertips — you can learn pretty much anything for free off a quick search. |
| 3 | 它 也 让 人们 跨越 边界 保持 连接，还 让 日常生活 更 顺滑，比如 银行业务 或 购物、从 你的 沙发。 | It also keeps people connected across borders and makes daily life smoother, like banking or shopping from your sofa. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 它 重塑了 我们 怎么 工作、学习 和 保持联系。 | So I'd say it's reshaped how we work, study and stay in touch. |

> In loads of ways, honestly. It puts a world of information at your fingertips — you can learn pretty much anything for free off a quick search. It also keeps people connected across borders and makes daily life smoother, like banking or shopping from your sofa. So I'd say it's reshaped how we work, study and stay in touch.

**P3 句型/模板**：`there's a clear gap`（dummy-there）· `live on their phones` · `lean towards the basics` / `the odd photo` · `glued to a screen all day, juggling five apps at once`（-ing 伴随）· `it cuts both ways` · `friends who live far off`（who后置）· `with a quick text … keeping things alive`（with-absolute）· `water down real connection` · `Snapping a photo lets you hold onto a moment you'd otherwise forget`（动名词主语+relative）· `within reason` · `let the camera run the show` · `banning it just makes sense`（动名词主语）· `it's down to common sense rather than the law dictating …` · `putting your phone away … and giving people your full attention`（动名词主语）· `reading the room` · `puts a world of information at your fingertips` · `reshaped how we work, study and stay in touch`（embedded）。

## P2-老04 · A time you gave advice to others — 劝妻子别硬扛工作（Event/Person）

> **Cue**: 何时 / 给谁 / 什么建议 / 为何给 · 题型 Event/Person

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个时刻 是 几个月前，当 我的妻子 在 工作上 绷得 太紧，而 我 让她 坐下 好好 聊一场。 | So, the time I'd like to talk about is a few months ago, when my wife was stretched too thin at work and I sat her down for a proper chat. |
| [背景] | 她 是 一个 公务员、在 城市管理 部门、在 市政府，而 虽然 那工作 稳定，它 说实话 挺 有压力，有 加班 堆积。 | She's a civil servant in urban management at the city government, and although the job's stable, it's honestly pretty stressful, with overtime piling up. |
| [背景·细] | 那时候 她 在 考虑 争取 一个 更大的 职位，而 她 看起来 一直 精疲力竭。 | Back then she was thinking about pushing for a bigger role, and she looked exhausted all the time. |
| [advice] | 所以 一天晚上 我 告诉她 [去]退 一步、别 咬下 比 她 能嚼的 更多。 | So one evening I told her to take a step back and not bite off more than she could chew. |
| [advice·细] | 我 说 她 不必 打动 每个人，而且 她的健康 和 我们的家 要紧 得多、比 一个 更花哨的 头衔。 | I said she didn't have to impress everyone, and that her health and our family mattered way more than a fancier title. |
| [结果] | 她 一开始 犹豫，但 最终 她 松了 一点，而 几周之内 她 看起来 快乐 多了。 | She was hesitant at first, but eventually she eased off a bit, and within weeks she seemed much happier. |
| [why/EL] | 我 给 她 那个建议 因为 我 能 看出 她 就要 把 自己 烧垮。 | I gave her that advice because I could see she was about to burn herself out. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那 差不多 就是 那个时刻、（我 庆幸 我 开了口的）。 | So yeah, that's pretty much the moment I'm glad I spoke up. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the time I'd like to talk about is a few months ago, when my wife was stretched too thin at work and I sat her down for a proper chat. She's a civil servant in urban management at the city government, and although the job's stable, it's honestly pretty stressful, with overtime piling up. Back then she was thinking about pushing for a bigger role, and she looked exhausted all the time. So one evening I told her to take a step back and not bite off more than she could chew. I said she didn't have to impress everyone, and that her health and our family mattered way more than a fancier title. She was hesitant at first, but eventually she eased off a bit, and within weeks she seemed much happier. I gave her that advice because I could see she was about to burn herself out. So yeah, that's pretty much the moment I'm glad I spoke up.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **time opener + when 双动作**：我想聊的时刻是几个月前，当我妻子绷得太紧，而我让她坐下聊 → `the time I'd like to talk about is …, when … and I sat her down for a proper chat`
- **although 让步 + with-absolute**：虽然工作稳定，有加班堆积 → `although the job's stable … with overtime piling up`
- **成语 bullet**：退一步、别咬下超过能嚼的 → `take a step back and not bite off more than she could chew`
- **that-名词从句**：我说她不必打动每个人，而且她健康和家要紧得多 → `I said she didn't have to …, and that … mattered way more than …`
- **就要烧垮**：我能看出她就要把自己烧垮 → `she was about to burn herself out`
- **closer**：那个我庆幸开了口的时刻 → `the moment I'm glad I spoke up`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Should people prepare before giving advice?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 是的，我 真的 觉得 他们 应该。 | Yeah, I really think they should. |
| 2 | 如果 你 给 建议 顺口 就说，你 可能 错过 那 更大的 图景、最后 把 某人 引 错了。 | If you give advice off the top of your head, you might miss the bigger picture and end up steering someone wrong. |
| 3 | 一点 事先的 思考 显示 你 真的 在乎。 | A bit of thought beforehand shows you actually care. |
| 4 | 比如，在 告诉 一个朋友 [去]辞掉 他们的工作 之前，你 会 想 先 理解 为什么 他们 不开心。 | For instance, before telling a friend to quit their job, you'd want to understand why they're unhappy first. |

> Yeah, I really think they should. If you give advice off the top of your head, you might miss the bigger picture and end up steering someone wrong. A bit of thought beforehand shows you actually care. For instance, before telling a friend to quit their job, you'd want to understand why they're unhappy first.

**Q2. Is it good to ask advice from strangers online?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它 好坏参半。 | Honestly, it's a mixed bag. |
| 2 | 网上的 陌生人 能 对 小众的东西 很棒，比如 修 一台笔记本，因为 某人 很可能 之前 解决过 它。 | Strangers online can be brilliant for niche stuff, like fixing a laptop, since someone's probably solved it before. |
| 3 | 但 对于 个人的 决定 我 会 谨慎，因为 他们 不 知道 你的处境，而且 任何人 都能 假装 是 一个 专家。 | But for personal decisions I'd be cautious, because they don't know your situation and anyone can pretend to be an expert. |
| 4 | 所以 我 会 对它 打个 折扣。 | So I'd take it with a pinch of salt. |

> Honestly, it's a mixed bag. Strangers online can be brilliant for niche stuff, like fixing a laptop, since someone's probably solved it before. But for personal decisions I'd be cautious, because they don't know your situation and anyone can pretend to be an expert. So I'd take it with a pinch of salt.

**Q3. What are the personalities of people whose job is to give advice to others?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 他们 倾向于 是 好的 倾听者、还 挺 有耐心。 | I'd say they tend to be good listeners and pretty patient. |
| 2 | 一个 咨询师 或 教练，比如，不能 只 推 他们 自己的 意见；他们 得 保持 冷静、还 把 人 读得 好。 | A counsellor or coach, for example, can't just push their own opinions; they have to stay calm and read people well. |
| 3 | 他们 通常 也 有 同理心，所以 人们 感觉 安全 [去]敞开。 | They're usually empathetic too, so folk feel safe opening up. |
| 4 | 有点 客观 有帮助，因为 他们 没 在 情感上 缠 在 里面。 | Being a bit objective helps, since they aren't emotionally tangled up in it. |

> I'd say they tend to be good listeners and pretty patient. A counsellor or coach, for example, can't just push their own opinions; they have to stay calm and read people well. They're usually empathetic too, so folk feel safe opening up. Being a bit objective helps, since they aren't emotionally tangled up in it.

**Q4. What are the problems if you ask too many people for advice?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 那 主要问题 是 你 最后 被 淹没。 | The main problem is you end up overwhelmed. |
| 2 | 每个人 都 有 他们 自己的 看法，所以 你 得到 一大堆 冲突的 意见、然后 僵住、而不是 决定。 | Everyone's got their own take, so you get loads of conflicting opinions and freeze up instead of deciding. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，它 也 能 一点点 削掉 你的自信，因为 你 开始 怀疑 你自己。 | To be fair, it can also chip away at your confidence, because you start doubting yourself. |
| 4 | 有时候 两三个 信任的 看法 就 够了，而 更多 只是 搅浑 那水。 | Sometimes two or three trusted views are plenty, and more just muddies the water. |

> The main problem is you end up overwhelmed. Everyone's got their own take, so you get loads of conflicting opinions and freeze up instead of deciding. To be fair, it can also chip away at your confidence, because you start doubting yourself. Sometimes two or three trusted views are plenty, and more just muddies the water.

**Q5. Why do some people think it is better to ask for advice from friends than from parents?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，朋友 常常 感觉 更 能 共鸣，因为 他们 大致 同龄、还 懂 你 正在 经历什么。 | Well, friends often feel more relatable, since they're roughly the same age and get what you're going through right now. |
| 2 | 父母 可能 有点 脱节、跟 现代的 压力，而 他们的建议 有时候 带着 评判。 | Parents can be a bit out of touch with modern pressures, and their advice sometimes comes with judgement. |
| 3 | 跟 哥们儿，你 能 诚实、而 不用 担心 他们 会 教训 你 或者 无休止地 为 你 担心。 | With mates, you can be honest without worrying they'll lecture you or worry endlessly about you. |

> Well, friends often feel more relatable, since they're roughly the same age and get what you're going through right now. Parents can be a bit out of touch with modern pressures, and their advice sometimes comes with judgement. With mates, you can be honest without worrying they'll lecture you or worry endlessly about you.

**Q6. When would old people ask young people for advice?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 大多 当 涉及 技术，我觉得。 | Mostly when it comes to technology, I reckon. |
| 2 | 老年人 常常 问 那些 更年轻的 怎么 用 一部 智能手机、怎么 设置 视频通话、或 怎么 网购。 | Older folk often ask the younger ones how to use a smartphone, set up video calls, or shop online. |
| 3 | 他们 也 会 转向 年轻人、为 新鲜的 视角、关于 趋势 或 职业道路，因为 那世界 变得 这么快、而 年轻人 正 在 它的 正中央。 | They'll also turn to youngsters for fresh perspectives on trends or career paths, since the world's changed so fast and young people are right in the thick of it. |

> Mostly when it comes to technology, I reckon. Older folk often ask the younger ones how to use a smartphone, set up video calls, or shop online. They'll also turn to youngsters for fresh perspectives on trends or career paths, since the world's changed so fast and young people are right in the thick of it.

**P3 句型/模板**：`off the top of your head` · `end up steering someone wrong` · `A bit of thought beforehand shows you actually care` · `why they're unhappy`（embedded）· `it's a mixed bag` · `anyone can pretend to be an expert` · `take it with a pinch of salt` · `push their own opinions` / `read people well` · `folk feel safe opening up` · `Being a bit objective helps`（动名词主语）· `you end up overwhelmed` · `Everyone's got their own take` / `freeze up` · `chip away at your confidence` · `muddies the water` · `get what you're going through`（embedded）· `out of touch with modern pressures` · `without worrying they'll lecture you`（without+动名词）· `when it comes to technology` · `how to use a smartphone`（embedded）· `right in the thick of it`。

## P2-老05 · A piece of technology (not a phone) you'd like to own — 无反相机拍 Muye（Object）

> **Cue**: 是什么 / 多少钱 / 怎么知道 / 为何想要 · 题型 Object

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/22 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改 · 只练了 P2）

> 你自己练过的 P2（高端 3D 打印机，你的真实版 · 首次独立全 cold 里程碑；这条只练了 P2）。**不降档**，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · 高端 3D 打印机）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 想 拥有 的 一件 科技产品 是 一台 高端的 3D 打印机。 | One piece of technology I'd like to own is a high-end 3D printer. |
| 2 | 我 入 这个 坑 是 通过 我 一个 同事，（他 真的 很 迷 3D 打印）——他 甚至 用 零件 造 了 他 自己的 机器。 | I got into it through a colleague of mine, who's really into 3D printing — he even built his own machine from spare parts. |
| 3 | 他 给 我 看 他 那台 能 做 什么，而 我 立刻 就 上瘾了。 | He showed me what his one could do, and I was hooked straight away. |
| 4 | 据 我 所知，一台 入门级 的 大约 花 四千 块。 | As far as I know, an entry-level one costs around four thousand yuan. |
| 5 | 它 挺 贵，但 我 觉得 它 会 完全 值得。 | It's pretty expensive, but I think it'd be completely worth it. |
| 6 | 有 几个 原因 我 想 拥有 一台。 | There are a couple of reasons why I'd like to own one. |
| 7 | 首先，它 能 打印 各种 玩具，比如 五彩的 恐龙、乐高 积木、那种 东西。 | First, it can print all sorts of toys, like colourful dinosaurs, Lego blocks, that kind of thing. |
| 8 | 除此之外，它 是 一个 偷偷的 方式、[去]让 我 儿子 对 工程 感兴趣——他 能 看到 屏幕上 的 一个 东西 怎么 变成 一个 真的 物体。 | On top of that, it's a sneaky way to get my son interested in engineering — he could see how something on the screen turns into a real object. |
| 9 | 让 它 出众的 是 一台 3D 打印机 其实 是 我们 俩 的 一个 巨大的 玩具。 | What makes it stand out is that a 3D printer is really a giant toy for both of us. |
| 10 | 周末，我 儿子 坐 在 我 旁边，我们 一起 设计 一个 模型，然后 那 打印机 一层一层 把 它 造 出来。 | On weekends, my son sits right next to me, we design a model together, and the printer builds it layer by layer. |
| 11 | 所以 它 会 让 我们 亲近 得 多。 | So it'd bring us much closer. |

> One piece of technology I'd like to own is a high-end 3D printer. I got into it through a colleague of mine, who's really into 3D printing — he even built his own machine from spare parts. He showed me what his one could do, and I was hooked straight away. As far as I know, an entry-level one costs around four thousand yuan. It's pretty expensive, but I think it'd be completely worth it. There are a couple of reasons why I'd like to own one. First, it can print all sorts of toys, like colourful dinosaurs, Lego blocks, that kind of thing. On top of that, it's a sneaky way to get my son interested in engineering — he could see how something on the screen turns into a real object. What makes it stand out is that a 3D printer is really a giant toy for both of us. On weekends, my son sits right next to me, we design a model together, and the printer builds it layer by layer. So it'd bring us much closer.

---

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个小设备 是 一台 正经的 无反相机——一台 真的，不 只是 我的手机。 | So, the gadget I'd like to talk about is a proper mirrorless camera — a real one, not just my phone. |
| [cost] | 它 会 花 我 不少，说实话——大约 八九千 块钱，所以 它 会 有点 是 一次 挥霍。 | It'd cost me a fair bit, honestly — around eight or nine thousand yuan, so it'd be a bit of a splurge. |
| [how knew] | 我 最早 知道 它 是 从 一个 亲密的朋友、（那种 真的 很迷 摄影的）。 | I first knew about it from a close friend who's really into photography. |
| [how·细] | 他 给 我 看了 一些 照片、（他 拍过的），而 我 就 被 震住了。 | He showed me some shots he'd taken, and I was just blown away. |
| [why] | 我 想要 一台 的 主要原因 是 我的 五岁的 儿子，Muye。 | The main reason I want one is my five-year-old son, Muye. |
| [why·细] | 我 爱 拍 他的照片，但 我的 手机照片 就是 配不上 他。 | I love snapping photos of him, but my phone pictures just don't do him justice. |
| [why·细2] | 有 一台 正经的相机，我 就能 真正地 抓住 那些 小时刻、在 它们 溜走 之前——比如 他 第一次 骑 自行车。 | With a proper camera, I could actually capture those little moments before they slip away — like his first time riding a bike. |
| [细] | 我的妻子 也 觉得 那 是 一个 贴心的 主意。 | My wife thinks it's a sweet idea too. |
| [EL] | 我想 我 就是 一个 多愁善感的 爸爸。 | I guess I'm just a sentimental dad. |
| [EL·细] | 孩子 长得 这么快，而 我 会 很讨厌 回头看、只有 模糊的 照片。 | Kids grow up so fast, and I'd hate to look back with only blurry photos. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那台相机 已经 坐 在 我的 愿望清单 顶端 有 一阵子了。 | So yeah, that camera's been sitting at the top of my wishlist for a while now. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the gadget I'd like to talk about is a proper mirrorless camera — a real one, not just my phone. It'd cost me a fair bit, honestly — around eight or nine thousand yuan, so it'd be a bit of a splurge. I first knew about it from a close friend who's really into photography. He showed me some shots he'd taken, and I was just blown away. The main reason I want one is my five-year-old son, Muye. I love snapping photos of him, but my phone pictures just don't do him justice. With a proper camera, I could actually capture those little moments before they slip away — like his first time riding a bike. My wife thinks it's a sweet idea too. I guess I'm just a sentimental dad. Kids grow up so fast, and I'd hate to look back with only blurry photos. So yeah, that camera's been sitting at the top of my wishlist for a while now.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **gadget opener 对比**：我想聊的设备是一台正经的X——真的，不只是手机 → `the gadget I'd like to talk about is a proper …, not just my phone`
- **who后置 + relative后置**：一个真迷摄影的朋友；他拍过的一些照片 → `a close friend who's really into photography` / `some shots he'd taken`
- **配不上**：我的手机照片配不上他 → `my phone pictures just don't do him justice`
- **With 开头 + before**：有台正经相机我就能抓住那些小时刻，在它们溜走之前 → `With a proper camera, I could … capture those little moments before they slip away`
- **多愁善感的爸爸 / 讨厌回头看**：我会很讨厌回头看只有模糊照片 → `I'd hate to look back with only blurry photos`
- **closer**：坐在我愿望清单顶端有一阵子了 → `sitting at the top of my wishlist for a while now`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What are the differences between the technology of the past and that of today?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 那 最大的 差距 是 速度 和 便捷。 | Well, I'd say the biggest gap is speed and ease. |
| 2 | 更老的 小设备 又 笨重 又 慢，而 你 经常 等 老半天、让 它们 做 任何事。 | Older gadgets were bulky and slow, and you'd often wait ages for them to do anything. |
| 3 | 今天的 科技 又 光滑、又 即时、还 塞满 功能。 | Today's tech is sleek, instant, and packed with features. |
| 4 | 拿 相机 来说——胶片的 意味着 猜测 和 等待，但 现在 你 看见 你的照片、就 在 你 拍它 的那一秒。 | Take cameras — film ones meant guesswork and waiting, but now you see your shot the second you take it. |

> Well, I'd say the biggest gap is speed and ease. Older gadgets were bulky and slow, and you'd often wait ages for them to do anything. Today's tech is sleek, instant, and packed with features. Take cameras — film ones meant guesswork and waiting, but now you see your shot the second you take it.

**Q2. What technology do young people like to use?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，年轻人 倾向 任何 社交的、视觉的 东西。 | Honestly, young people gravitate towards anything social and visual. |
| 2 | 他们 粘 在 短视频 app、流媒体 平台、和 游戏装备上，因为 那些 感觉 又 快 又 好玩。 | They're glued to short-video apps, streaming platforms, and gaming gear, since those feel fast and fun. |
| 3 | 我觉得 它 大多是 关于 保持 连接 和 被 娱乐。 | I reckon it's mostly about staying connected and entertained. |
| 4 | 比如，一个 青少年 会 挑 一个 智能手表 或 耳机、胜过 一台 老台式机、任何时候，因为 便携性 对 他们 真的 要紧。 | For instance, a teenager would pick a smartwatch or earbuds over an old desktop any day, because portability really matters to them. |

> Honestly, young people gravitate towards anything social and visual. They're glued to short-video apps, streaming platforms, and gaming gear, since those feel fast and fun. I reckon it's mostly about staying connected and entertained. For instance, a teenager would pick a smartwatch or earbuds over an old desktop any day, because portability really matters to them.

**Q3. What are the differences between online and face-to-face communication?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 那 主要差别 是 深度。 | I'd say the main difference is depth. |
| 2 | 面对面，你 接收到 语气、肢体语言、和 小停顿，所以 它 感觉 更 温暖、更 真诚。 | Face-to-face, you pick up tone, body language, and little pauses, so it feels warmer and more genuine. |
| 3 | 网上 聊天 可能 更 快、更 顺手，但 它们 经常 平淡、还 容易 被 误读。 | Online chats can be quicker and handier, but they're often flat and easy to misread. |
| 4 | 比如，一个 文字 玩笑 可能 落 得 很糟，当 没人 听见 你的 声音、在 它 背后。 | For example, a text joke might land badly when nobody hears your voice behind it. |

> I'd say the main difference is depth. Face-to-face, you pick up tone, body language, and little pauses, so it feels warmer and more genuine. Online chats can be quicker and handier, but they're often flat and easy to misread. For example, a text joke might land badly when nobody hears your voice behind it.

**Q4. Do you think technology has changed the way people communicate?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，它 改造了 一切。 | Absolutely, it's transformed everything. |
| 2 | 我们 现在 全天候 可被 联系到，而 一条 快消息 几秒 就 跨过 那地球，（那 以前 会 不可想象）。 | We're now reachable round the clock, and a quick message crosses the globe in seconds, which would've been unthinkable before. |
| 3 | 但 我会说 它 好坏参半——人们 发短信 而不是 打电话，所以 对话 感觉 更短、更浅。 | But I'd say it's a mixed bag — people text instead of calling, so conversations feel shorter and shallower. |
| 4 | 说实话，我们 连接 更频繁，但 也许 更浅、比 我们 以前。 | Honestly, we connect more often but maybe less deeply than we used to. |

> Absolutely, it's transformed everything. We're now reachable round the clock, and a quick message crosses the globe in seconds, which would've been unthinkable before. But I'd say it's a mixed bag — people text instead of calling, so conversations feel shorter and shallower. Honestly, we connect more often but maybe less deeply than we used to.

**Q5. What negative effects does technology have on people's relationships?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，那 明显的一个 是 分心。 | Well, the obvious one is distraction. |
| 2 | 人们 在 晚饭时 刷 他们的手机，所以 他们 身体上 在一起、但 精神上 在 别处。 | People scroll through their phones at dinner, so they're physically together but mentally elsewhere. |
| 3 | 那 慢慢地 一点点 侵蚀 真正的 亲密。 | That slowly chips away at real closeness. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，它 也 滋生 一点 嫉妒，因为 人们 不断地 拿 他们的生活 比 那些 光鲜的 帖子、在网上，而 那 能 悄悄地 拉紧 真诚的 友谊、随着 时间。 | To be fair, it also breeds a bit of envy, since folk constantly compare their lives to those polished posts online, and that can quietly strain genuine friendships over time. |

> Well, the obvious one is distraction. People scroll through their phones at dinner, so they're physically together but mentally elsewhere. That slowly chips away at real closeness. To be fair, it also breeds a bit of envy, since folk constantly compare their lives to those polished posts online, and that can quietly strain genuine friendships over time.

**Q6. What are the differences between making friends in real life and online?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 现实的 友谊 慢慢 建立、通过 共享的 时刻，所以 它们 倾向于 感觉 更 结实、更 扎实。 | I'd say real-life friendships build slowly through shared moments, so they tend to feel sturdier and more grounded. |
| 2 | 网上的 很快 形成、围绕 共同的 兴趣，（那 对于 找到 你的 同类 很棒）。 | Online ones form fast around common interests, which is brilliant for finding your tribe. |
| 3 | 但 它们 可能 有点 脆弱，因为 你 从不 完全 知道 谁 在 那屏幕 后面。 | But they can be a bit fragile, since you never fully know who's behind the screen. |
| 4 | 两者 都 有 它们的 位置，说实话。 | Both have their place, honestly. |

> I'd say real-life friendships build slowly through shared moments, so they tend to feel sturdier and more grounded. Online ones form fast around common interests, which is brilliant for finding your tribe. But they can be a bit fragile, since you never fully know who's behind the screen. Both have their place, honestly.

**P3 句型/模板**：`bulky and slow` / `wait ages for them to do anything` · `sleek, instant, and packed with features` · `you see your shot the second you take it` · `gravitate towards anything social and visual` · `glued to short-video apps` · `staying connected and entertained` · `pick … over … any day` · `you pick up tone, body language, and little pauses` · `flat and easy to misread` · `a text joke might land badly when nobody hears your voice behind it` · `reachable round the clock` · `which would've been unthinkable before`（which后置）· `we connect more often but maybe less deeply than we used to` · `physically together but mentally elsewhere` · `chips away at real closeness` · `breeds a bit of envy` · `who's behind the screen`（embedded）· `Both have their place`。

---

## P2-老06 · A person who is good at planning — zhangwei（Person）

> **Cue**: 是谁 / 怎么认识 / 做什么计划 / 你的感受 · 题型 Person

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 我想聊的那个人 是 一个 大学的 老朋友，zhangwei。 | The person I'd like to talk about is an old friend from university, zhangwei. |
| [how knew] | 我们 认识 彼此 快 二十年了，而 他 做 一个 开发者。 | We've known each other for almost twenty years, and he works as a developer. |
| [what plans] | 他 计划 的方式 说实话 令人佩服。 | The way he plans is honestly impressive. |
| [细] | 他 把 一切 放 在 Notion 里——年度的、季度的、月度的 计划，全 摆开。 | He keeps everything in Notion — yearly, quarterly and monthly plans, all laid out. |
| [细2] | 每个 周日 晚上 他 坐下、回顾 他的一周 怎么过的、然后 规划 出 下一个。 | Every Sunday evening he sits down, reviews how his week went, and maps out the next one. |
| [细3] | 他 基本上 把 它 练 到 出神入化。 | He's basically got it down to a fine art. |
| [强调] | 但 真正 打动 我的 不是 那 计划 本身——是 他 真的 执行到底。 | But what gets me isn't the planning itself — it's that he actually follows through. |
| [e.g.] | 比如，他 会 定 一个 目标、然后 就 安静地 把 它 做完，一周 又 一周。 | For example, he'll set a goal and just quietly get it done, week after week. |
| [对比] | 我 是 完全 相反，更 是 一个 随机应变的人，所以 我 真的 佩服 他 怎么 把 事情 掌控住。 | I'm the total opposite, more of a wing-it person, so I really admire how he stays on top of things. |
| [feel] | 我 感到 一种 佩服 和 一点 嫉妒 的 混合，说实话。 | I feel a mix of admiration and a bit of envy, to be honest. |
| [feel·细] | 跟 他 一起 混，一些 那种 自律 蹭 到 我 身上。 | Hanging out with him, some of that discipline rubs off on me. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那 就是 zhangwei——我 认识的 最有条理的 人，还 是 一个 人、（那种 真的 让 计划 发生的）。 | So yeah, that's zhangwei — the most organised person I know, and someone who genuinely makes plans happen. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> The person I'd like to talk about is an old friend from university, zhangwei. We've known each other for almost twenty years, and he works as a developer. The way he plans is honestly impressive. He keeps everything in Notion — yearly, quarterly and monthly plans, all laid out. Every Sunday evening he sits down, reviews how his week went, and maps out the next one. He's basically got it down to a fine art. But what gets me isn't the planning itself — it's that he actually follows through. For example, he'll set a goal and just quietly get it done, week after week. I'm the total opposite, more of a wing-it person, so I really admire how he stays on top of things. I feel a mix of admiration and a bit of envy, to be honest. Hanging out with him, some of that discipline rubs off on me. So yeah, that's zhangwei — the most organised person I know, and someone who genuinely makes plans happen.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **the way + 主谓**：他计划的方式说实话令人佩服 → `The way he plans is honestly impressive`
- **embedded how + 并列动词**：坐下、回顾他一周怎么过的、然后规划下一个 → `sits down, reviews how his week went, and maps out the next one`
- **got it down to a fine art**：练到出神入化
- **cleft 强调**：真正打动我的不是计划本身——是他真的执行到底 → `what gets me isn't the planning itself — it's that he actually follows through`
- **how 从句 + on top of**：我佩服他怎么把事情掌控住 → `I really admire how he stays on top of things`
- **rubs off on me**：那种自律蹭到我身上
- **closer 后置定语叠加**：我认识的最有条理的人，还是一个真让计划发生的人 → `the most organised person I know, and someone who genuinely makes plans happen`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Do you think it's important to plan ahead?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对。 | Definitely. |
| 2 | 我觉得 提前 计划 省 你 很多 压力，因为 你 不 在 最后一刻 手忙脚乱。 | I reckon planning ahead saves you a lot of stress, since you're not scrambling at the last minute. |
| 3 | 比如，如果 你 事先 规划 一趟旅行，你 避免 那些 傻错误、比如 重复预订。 | For example, if you map out a trip beforehand, you avoid those silly mistakes like double-booking. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，我 不会 计划 每一个 小细节——留 一点 空间 给 随性 让 生活 好玩。 | Having said that, I wouldn't plan every tiny detail — leaving a bit of room for spontaneity keeps life fun. |

> Definitely. I reckon planning ahead saves you a lot of stress, since you're not scrambling at the last minute. For example, if you map out a trip beforehand, you avoid those silly mistakes like double-booking. Having said that, I wouldn't plan every tiny detail — leaving a bit of room for spontaneity keeps life fun.

**Q2. What activities do we need to plan ahead?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，那些 大的。 | Honestly, the big ones. |
| 2 | 我会说 大旅行、婚礼、或 任何 涉及 钱的东西 真的 需要 计划，因为 那 花费 加起来 很快、而 错误 昂贵。 | I'd say major trips, weddings, or anything involving money really needs planning, because the costs add up fast and mistakes are pricey. |
| 3 | 日常 杂事，不过，你 可以 就 随机应变。 | Daily errands, though, you can just wing it. |
| 4 | 所以 基本上，赌注 越高，你 就 得 越 小心、关于 事先 把 一切 规划 出来。 | So basically, the higher the stakes, the more careful you've got to be about mapping everything out beforehand. |

> Honestly, the big ones. I'd say major trips, weddings, or anything involving money really needs planning, because the costs add up fast and mistakes are pricey. Daily errands, though, you can just wing it. So basically, the higher the stakes, the more careful you've got to be about mapping everything out beforehand.

**Q3. Do you think children should plan their future careers?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 在 某种程度上，是的，但 我 不会 推 它 太 用力。 | To some extent, yeah, but I wouldn't push it too hard. |
| 2 | 我觉得 有 一个 大致的 方向 帮 孩子 在学校 保持 有动力。 | I reckon having a rough direction helps kids stay motivated at school. |
| 3 | 但 在那个年纪，兴趣 不断 变，所以 锁定 一个 职业 感觉 有风险。 | But at that age, interests shift constantly, so locking into one career feels risky. |
| 4 | 比如，一大堆人 最后 做 工作、（那种 他们 小时候 甚至 从没 听说过的）。 | For instance, plenty of people end up doing jobs they'd never even heard of as children. |

> To some extent, yeah, but I wouldn't push it too hard. I reckon having a rough direction helps kids stay motivated at school. But at that age, interests shift constantly, so locking into one career feels risky. For instance, plenty of people end up doing jobs they'd never even heard of as children.

**Q4. Should children ask their teachers or parents for advice when making plans?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 两者，理想地。 | Both, ideally. |
| 2 | 我会说 父母 最 了解 那孩子的 性格，而 老师 看到 他们 学业上 怎么 表现。 | I'd say parents know the kid's personality best, while teachers see how they perform academically. |
| 3 | 所以 从 每一方 得到 输入 给 一个 更全的 图景。 | So getting input from each gives a fuller picture. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，孩子 不该 只 盲目地 跟 建议——那 最终决定 应该 是 他们的，因为 他们 是 那些人、（那种 真的 会 承受 那决定的）。 | That said, kids shouldn't just follow advice blindly — the final call should be theirs, because they're the ones who'll actually live with the decision. |

> Both, ideally. I'd say parents know the kid's personality best, while teachers see how they perform academically. So getting input from each gives a fuller picture. That said, kids shouldn't just follow advice blindly — the final call should be theirs, because they're the ones who'll actually live with the decision.

**Q5. Is making study plans popular among young people?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 比 以前 更多，我觉得。 | More than it used to be, I reckon. |
| 2 | 有 所有 这些 计划 app 和 学习 追踪器 在，年轻人 现在 爱 规划 出 他们的复习。 | With all these planner apps and study trackers around, young people love mapping out their revision now. |
| 3 | 比如，很多 学生 分享 他们的 彩色编码的 日程、在网上。 | For instance, lots of students share their colour-coded schedules online. |
| 4 | 但 说句公道话，坚持 那计划 是 那 棘手的 部分——把 它 拟 出来 说实话 是 那 容易的 部分。 | But to be fair, sticking to the plan is the tricky part — drawing it up is honestly the easy bit. |

> More than it used to be, I reckon. With all these planner apps and study trackers around, young people love mapping out their revision now. For instance, lots of students share their colour-coded schedules online. But to be fair, sticking to the plan is the tricky part — drawing it up is honestly the easy bit.

**Q6. Do you think choosing a college major is closely related to a person's future career?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 常常，是的，但 它 不是 板上钉钉。 | Often, yeah, but it's not set in stone. |
| 2 | 我会说 你的专业 打开 某些 门，比如 一个 工程 学位 通向 科技岗。 | I'd say your major opens certain doors, like an engineering degree leading into tech roles. |
| 3 | 但 一大堆人 也 飘 进 完全 不相关的 领域。 | But loads of people drift into totally unrelated fields too. |
| 4 | 所以 我觉得 它 塑造 你的 起点 多过 它 固定 你 实际上 会 在哪 结束。 | So I reckon it shapes your starting point more than it fixes where you'll actually end up. |

> Often, yeah, but it's not set in stone. I'd say your major opens certain doors, like an engineering degree leading into tech roles. But loads of people drift into totally unrelated fields too. So I reckon it shapes your starting point more than it fixes where you'll actually end up.

**P3 句型/模板**：`planning ahead saves you a lot of stress`（动名词主语）· `scrambling at the last minute` · `map out a trip beforehand` · `leaving a bit of room for spontaneity keeps life fun`（动名词主语）· `anything involving money`（动名词后置）· `the costs add up fast` · `wing it` · `the higher the stakes, the more careful …`（the-more 结构）· `having a rough direction helps kids stay motivated`（动名词主语）· `locking into one career feels risky`（动名词主语）· `jobs they'd never even heard of`（relative后置）· `how they perform academically`（embedded）· `getting input from each gives a fuller picture`（动名词主语）· `the ones who'll actually live with the decision`（who后置）· `sticking to the plan is the tricky part`（动名词主语）· `colour-coded schedules` · `it's not set in stone` · `your major opens certain doors` · `where you'll actually end up`（embedded）。
