## P2-老07 · A child who loves drawing — Muye 画恐龙（Person）

> **Cue**: 是谁 / 怎么/何时知道 / 多久画一次 / 为何爱画 · 题型 Person

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个孩子 是 我的 五岁的 儿子，Muye。 | So, the child I'd like to talk about is my five-year-old son, Muye. |
| [what] | 他 真的 很迷 画画——它 差不多 是 他 在家 最爱 做的事。 | He's really into drawing — it's pretty much his favourite thing to do at home. |
| [how knew] | 我 最早 注意到 它 是 大约 一年前，当 他 开始 填满 每一张 空白的纸、（他 能 找到的）。 | I first noticed it about a year ago, when he started filling every blank piece of paper he could find. |
| [how often] | 这些天 他 几乎 每个 周末 画，通常 一两个小时 一口气。 | These days he draws almost every weekend, usually for an hour or two at a stretch. |
| [细] | 他 最爱的 是 画 动物，尤其 恐龙。 | What he loves most is drawing animals, especially dinosaurs. |
| [细2] | 他 有 这个 笔记本、（塞满了 五彩的、有点 歪歪扭扭的 霸王龙的），而 他 会 骄傲地 给 我们 看 每一只。 | He's got this notebook full of colourful, slightly wonky T-Rexes, and he'll proudly show each one to us. |
| [细3] | 有一次 他 花 一整个 下午 在 一张 单独的画上——一只 恐龙 在 开 生日派对——而 他 那么 专注、他 忘了 他的零食。 | One time he spent a whole afternoon on a single picture — a dinosaur having a birthday party — and he was so focused he forgot about his snacks. |
| [why/EL] | 我 觉得 他 爱 它 的原因 是 它 让 他 建 他 自己的 小世界。 | I think the reason he loves it is that it lets him build his own little world. |
| [why·细] | 五岁，他 还 不能 真的 把 他的感受 放进 词语，但 通过 画画 他 表达 无论 什么 在 他脑子里。 | At five, he can't really put his feelings into words yet, but through drawing he expresses whatever's in his head. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那 就是 为什么 它 对 他 意义 这么大。 | So yeah, that's why it means so much to him. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the child I'd like to talk about is my five-year-old son, Muye. He's really into drawing — it's pretty much his favourite thing to do at home. I first noticed it about a year ago, when he started filling every blank piece of paper he could find. These days he draws almost every weekend, usually for an hour or two at a stretch. What he loves most is drawing animals, especially dinosaurs. He's got this notebook full of colourful, slightly wonky T-Rexes, and he'll proudly show each one to us. One time he spent a whole afternoon on a single picture — a dinosaur having a birthday party — and he was so focused he forgot about his snacks. I think the reason he loves it is that it lets him build his own little world. At five, he can't really put his feelings into words yet, but through drawing he expresses whatever's in his head. So yeah, that's why it means so much to him.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **child opener**：我想聊的孩子是我五岁的儿子；他真的很迷画画 → `the child I'd like to talk about is my five-year-old son … He's really into drawing`
- **when 从句 + relative后置**：当他开始填满每张能找到的空白纸 → `when he started filling every blank piece of paper he could find`
- **一口气**：一两个小时一口气 → `for an hour or two at a stretch`
- **what 主语**：他最爱的是画动物 → `What he loves most is drawing animals`
- **full of 后置定语**：塞满五彩歪扭霸王龙的笔记本 → `this notebook full of colourful, slightly wonky T-Rexes`
- **so…（that省略）**：他那么专注他忘了零食 → `he was so focused he forgot about his snacks`
- **the reason … is that …**：他爱它的原因是它让他建自己的小世界 → `the reason he loves it is that it lets him build his own little world` / `whatever's in his head`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What is the right age for a child to learn drawing?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我 不 觉得 有 一个 固定的 年龄。 | Honestly, I don't think there's a fixed age. |
| 2 | 孩子 从 学步期 就 开始 乱涂，而 那 某种程度上 已经 是 画画。 | Kids start scribbling as toddlers, and that's already drawing in a way. |
| 3 | 我会说 大约 三四岁 是 当 它 变得 有意义，因为 他们 有 足够的 控制、对 他们的手。 | I'd say around three or four is when it gets meaningful, since they've got enough control over their hands. |
| 4 | 但 它 不该 被 强迫——他们 会 自然地 学会 它，一旦 他们 有兴趣。 | But it shouldn't be forced — they'll pick it up naturally once they're keen. |

> Honestly, I don't think there's a fixed age. Kids start scribbling as toddlers, and that's already drawing in a way. I'd say around three or four is when it gets meaningful, since they've got enough control over their hands. But it shouldn't be forced — they'll pick it up naturally once they're keen.

**Q2. Why do most children draw more often than adults do?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我觉得 那 是 因为 孩子 有 一大堆 空闲时间 和 更少的 烦恼。 | Well, I reckon it's because kids have loads of free time and fewer worries. |
| 2 | 画画 也 是 少数 方式之一、（那种 他们 能 表达 自己的、在 他们 词语流利 之前）。 | Drawing's also one of the few ways they can express themselves before they're fluent in words. |
| 3 | 大人，另一方面，被 工作 淹没、然后 就 丢了 那习惯。 | Adults, on the other hand, get swamped with work and just lose the habit. |
| 4 | 对 大多数 成年人，它 感觉 幼稚 或 无意义，可惜。 | For most grown-ups, it feels childish or pointless, sadly. |

> Well, I reckon it's because kids have loads of free time and fewer worries. Drawing's also one of the few ways they can express themselves before they're fluent in words. Adults, on the other hand, get swamped with work and just lose the habit. For most grown-ups, it feels childish or pointless, sadly.

**Q3. Why do some people visit galleries or museums instead of viewing artworks online?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 它 是 关于 那 体验。 | I'd say it's about the experience. |
| 2 | 亲眼 看 一幅画，你 捕捉到 那 尺度、那 质感、那 真实的 笔触——一个 屏幕 把 那全部 压平。 | Seeing a painting in person, you catch the scale, the texture, the actual brushstrokes — a screen flattens all that. |
| 3 | 还 有 一个 安静画廊 的 氛围、（那种 你 就是 在家 得不到的）。 | There's also the atmosphere of a quiet gallery that you just can't get at home. |
| 4 | 对 真正的 艺术爱好者，在网上 看 感觉 有点 空洞，像 一张 明信片。 | For real art lovers, viewing online feels a bit hollow, like a postcard. |

> I'd say it's about the experience. Seeing a painting in person, you catch the scale, the texture, the actual brushstrokes — a screen flattens all that. There's also the atmosphere of a quiet gallery that you just can't get at home. For real art lovers, viewing online feels a bit hollow, like a postcard.

**Q4. Do you think galleries and museums should be free of charge?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 理想地，是的。 | Ideally, yes. |
| 2 | 艺术 和 文化 不该 只 为 人们、（那种 买得起 一张票的），所以 免费入场 让 它们 对 每个人 开放。 | Art and culture shouldn't only be for people who can afford a ticket, so free entry makes them open to everyone. |
| 3 | 话虽如此，画廊 确实 需要 资金 [去]生存，所以 一个 小费用、为 特别 展览 看起来 公平。 | That said, galleries do need funding to survive, so a small charge for special exhibitions seems fair. |
| 4 | 我 会 保持 那 主藏品 免费、但 让 捐款 覆盖 其余。 | I'd keep the main collection free but let donations cover the rest. |

> Ideally, yes. Art and culture shouldn't only be for people who can afford a ticket, so free entry makes them open to everyone. That said, galleries do need funding to survive, so a small charge for special exhibitions seems fair. I'd keep the main collection free but let donations cover the rest.

**Q5. How do artworks inspire people?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 在 一大堆 方面，真的。 | In loads of ways, really. |
| 2 | 一幅 有力的画 或 一首歌 能 搅起 情绪、（那种 你 甚至 不知道 你 有的），而 它 让 你 看 那世界 有点 不一样。 | A powerful painting or song can stir up emotions you didn't even know you had, and it makes you see the world a bit differently. |
| 3 | 有时候 它 推 人们 [去]创造 某种东西 自己。 | Sometimes it pushes people to create something themselves. |
| 4 | 对我来说，一件 好的 艺术品 逗留 在 你脑子里 很久、在 你 看过 它 之后。 | For me, a good piece of art lingers in your head long after you've seen it. |

> In loads of ways, really. A powerful painting or song can stir up emotions you didn't even know you had, and it makes you see the world a bit differently. Sometimes it pushes people to create something themselves. For me, a good piece of art lingers in your head long after you've seen it.

**Q6. What are the differences between reading a book and visiting a museum?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它们 挺 不同，说实话。 | They're pretty different, honestly. |
| 2 | 一本书 把 你 拉 进 一个 故事、经过 几小时，而 一个 博物馆 让 你 闲逛、还 吸收 一大堆 碎片、按 你 自己的 节奏。 | A book pulls you into one story over hours, while a museum lets you wander and take in loads of bits at your own pace. |
| 3 | 阅读 更 私人、更 有想象力，而 一个 博物馆 是 视觉的、还 常常 社交的。 | Reading's more private and imaginative, whereas a museum's visual and often social. |
| 4 | 但 两者 都 喂养 你的 头脑，只是 用 它们 自己的 方式。 | But both feed your mind, just in their own ways. |

> They're pretty different, honestly. A book pulls you into one story over hours, while a museum lets you wander and take in loads of bits at your own pace. Reading's more private and imaginative, whereas a museum's visual and often social. But both feed your mind, just in their own ways.

**P3 句型/模板**：`there's a fixed age`（dummy-there）· `start scribbling as toddlers` · `when it gets meaningful` / `control over their hands` · `pick it up naturally once they're keen` · `one of the few ways they can express themselves`（relative后置）· `get swamped with work` · `Seeing a painting in person`（动名词状语）· `a screen flattens all that` · `the atmosphere … that you just can't get at home`（that后置）· `viewing online feels a bit hollow`（动名词主语）· `people who can afford a ticket`（who后置）· `free entry makes them open to everyone` · `let donations cover the rest` · `stir up emotions you didn't even know you had`（relative后置）· `lingers in your head long after you've seen it` · `pulls you into one story over hours` / `take in loads of bits at your own pace` · `both feed your mind`。

## P2-老08 · A program or app on your phone — 阅读 app 看科幻（Object）

> **Cue**: 是什么 / 多久用 / 何时怎么用 / 怎么发现 / 感受 · 题型 Object

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/25 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改）

> 你自己练过的一整套：P2 = 小红书 Rednote（你的例子，同 cue、不同 app）；P3 = 你当天练的 6 题（和下面 examples 的 P3 题目不同，都是你的真实产出）。**不降档**，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · Rednote）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我想聊的那个 app 是 小红书。 | The app I'd like to talk about is Rednote. |
| 2 | 我 用 它 挺 频繁——我 花 一两个 小时 在 它 上面。 | I use it quite often — I spend an hour or two on it. |
| 3 | 我 真的 上瘾，因为 它 给 我 这么 多 信息、（那种 我 感兴趣的），比如 短的 旅行 视频。 | I'm really hooked, because it gives me so much information I'm interested in, like short travel videos. |
| 4 | 有时候 我 也 在 它 上面 搜 东西——比如，如果 我 在 出差，我 会 用 小红书 [去]找 一家 好的 餐厅 吃 晚饭。 | Sometimes I also search for things on it — for example, if I'm on a business trip, I'll use Rednote to find a nice restaurant for dinner. |
| 5 | 至于 我 怎么 发现 它的，这 是 一个 有意思的 故事：小红书 本来 是 为 女性 设计的，而 我 老婆 很爱 它。 | As for how I found it, it's an interesting story: Rednote was originally designed for women, and my wife loved it. |
| 6 | 几年前，那 公司 想 扩大 它的 市场，所以 他们 重新 设计 它、也 加 了 给 男性的 内容——而 那 就是 我 老婆 推荐 它 给 我 的时候。 | A few years ago, the company wanted to expand its market, so they redesigned it with content for men too — and that's when my wife recommended it to me. |
| 7 | 所以 说实话，它 是 一个 真的 很 有用的 工具。 | So honestly, it's a really useful tool. |

> The app I'd like to talk about is Rednote. I use it quite often — I spend an hour or two on it. I'm really hooked, because it gives me so much information I'm interested in, like short travel videos. Sometimes I also search for things on it — for example, if I'm on a business trip, I'll use Rednote to find a nice restaurant for dinner. As for how I found it, it's an interesting story: Rednote was originally designed for women, and my wife loved it. A few years ago, the company wanted to expand its market, so they redesigned it with content for men too — and that's when my wife recommended it to me. So honestly, it's a really useful tool.

**P3（你练的 6 题）**

**你Q1. Differences between old and young people using apps?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它们 挺 不同。 | They're quite different. |
| 2 | 年轻人 瞬间 就 上手 新 app，而 老年人 有点 更 谨慎、还 坚持 他们 认识的 那些。 | Young people pick new apps up instantly, whereas older people are a bit more cautious and stick with the ones they know. |
| 3 | 所以 它 真的 归结到 他们 是 伴随 什么 长大的。 | So it really comes down to what they grew up with. |

> They're quite different. Young people pick new apps up instantly, whereas older people are a bit more cautious and stick with the ones they know. So it really comes down to what they grew up with.

**你Q2. Why do some people not like using apps?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 主要 是 信任 和 习惯，我会说。 | Mainly trust and habit, I'd say. |
| 2 | 有些人 担心 那些 app 公司 知道 关于 他们的 一切——他们 喜欢 什么、他们 吃 什么——（这）感觉 有点 瘆人。 | Some people worry the app companies know everything about them — what they like, what they eat — which feels a bit creepy. |
| 3 | 另一些 就 更喜欢 老 方式；我 父母 还 用 现金 付钱，因为 那 就是 他们 习惯的。 | Others just prefer the old way; my parents still pay with cash because that's what they're used to. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，对 他们 一个 app 感觉 像 麻烦 多过 帮助。 | To be fair, for them an app feels like more hassle than help. |

> Mainly trust and habit, I'd say. Some people worry the app companies know everything about them — what they like, what they eat — which feels a bit creepy. Others just prefer the old way; my parents still pay with cash because that's what they're used to. To be fair, for them an app feels like more hassle than help.

**你Q3. What apps are popular in your country, and why?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 哦，一大堆。 | Oh, loads. |
| 2 | 那 最大的 是 通讯 和 支付 app、比如 微信——基本上 每个人 每天 用 它们，因为 你 能 在 一个 地方 做 一切：聊天、付钱、甚至 订 一辆 出租车。 | The biggest ones are messaging and payment apps like WeChat — basically everyone uses them every day, because you can do everything in one place: chat, pay, even book a taxi. |
| 3 | 短视频 app 也 巨大，主要 因为 它们 令人上瘾、还 给 你 一个 快 笑、当 你 无聊 的时候。 | Short-video apps are huge too, mainly because they're addictive and give you a quick laugh when you're bored. |
| 4 | 所以 真的，它 归结到 便捷 和 打发 时间。 | So really, it comes down to convenience and killing time. |

> Oh, loads. The biggest ones are messaging and payment apps like WeChat — basically everyone uses them every day, because you can do everything in one place: chat, pay, even book a taxi. Short-video apps are huge too, mainly because they're addictive and give you a quick laugh when you're bored. So really, it comes down to convenience and killing time.

**你Q4. Should parents limit children's use of apps and games?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，是的——但 用 一个 轻手，不是 一个 彻底的 禁令。 | Definitely, yes — but with a light touch, not a total ban. |
| 2 | 当然，有些 app 帮 孩子 学习、游戏 让 他们 放松，但 它们 也 能 真的 令人上瘾。 | Sure, some apps help kids learn and games let them relax, but they can also be really addictive. |
| 3 | 所以 我 会 设 一个 每日 时间 限制，还 把 屏幕 时间 配 上 户外 玩 或 阅读。 | So I'd set a daily time limit, and pair screen time with outdoor play or reading. |
| 4 | 如果 你 太 严，他们 反正 会 就 偷偷 绕过。 | If you're too strict, they'll just sneak around anyway. |

> Definitely, yes — but with a light touch, not a total ban. Sure, some apps help kids learn and games let them relax, but they can also be really addictive. So I'd set a daily time limit, and pair screen time with outdoor play or reading. If you're too strict, they'll just sneak around anyway.

**你Q5. Are young people more reliant on these apps?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 是的，我 会 这么 说。 | Yes, I'd say so. |
| 2 | 我 花 很多 时间 在 各种 app 上——比如，微信 用来 跟 朋友 保持 联系，美团 用来 点 外卖。 | I spend a lot of time on various apps — for example, WeChat for staying in touch with friends, Meituan for ordering takeout. |
| 3 | 说实话，我 没法 想象 那 世界 会 是 什么 样，如果 这些 app 消失了。 | Honestly, I can't imagine what the world would be like if these apps disappeared. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，有时候 我 觉得 我 对 它们 太 关注、还 忽略了 那 真实 世界。 | To be fair, sometimes I think I pay too much attention to them and ignore the real world. |

> Yes, I'd say so. I spend a lot of time on various apps — for example, WeChat for staying in touch with friends, Meituan for ordering takeout. Honestly, I can't imagine what the world would be like if these apps disappeared. To be fair, sometimes I think I pay too much attention to them and ignore the real world.

**你Q6. What about countries banning children from social media?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 大体上 同意。 | I largely agree. |
| 2 | 当然，社交媒体 帮 人们 连接，但 对 孩子 那些 坏处 真的 压过 那些 好处。 | Sure, social media helps people connect, but for children the drawbacks really outweigh the benefits. |
| 3 | 网上 聊天 是 肤浅的，而 它 会 伤害 孩子 面对面 交流 的 能力。 | Chatting online is shallow, and it can hurt kids' ability to communicate in person. |
| 4 | 此外，外面 有 一大堆 负面 内容、（那种 孩子 真的 应付不了的）。 | Plus, there's loads of negative content out there that kids can't really handle. |
| 5 | 所以 我会说 它 是 必要的，至少 到 某个 年龄 之前。 | So I'd say it's necessary, at least up to a certain age. |

> I largely agree. Sure, social media helps people connect, but for children the drawbacks really outweigh the benefits. Chatting online is shallow, and it can hurt kids' ability to communicate in person. Plus, there's loads of negative content out there that kids can't really handle. So I'd say it's necessary, at least up to a certain age.

---

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个 app 是 一个 阅读 app、在 我手机上——基本上 一个 数字图书馆、（在那里 我 读 科幻 小说）。 | So, the app I'd like to talk about is a reading app on my phone — basically a digital library where I read sci-fi novels. |
| [how often] | 我 在 它上面 差不多 每一天。 | I'm on it pretty much every single day. |
| [when/how] | 我 通常 打开 它、在 我通勤时，然后 又 在 晚上、[去]放松、在 睡前。 | I usually open it on my commute, and then again at night to wind down before bed. |
| [when·细] | 我 爱 科幻，所以 它 是 那个地方、（那种 我 挤 几页、无论 什么时候 我 得到 一个 空闲时刻）。 | I love sci-fi, so it's where I squeeze in a few pages whenever I get a spare moment. |
| [how found] | 我 其实 完全 偶然地 找到 它。 | I actually found it completely by accident. |
| [found·细] | 一个 亲密的朋友 分享 一个 书链接 给 我、一个下午，我 点 它、出于 纯 好奇，而 那 单独一本书 让 我 彻底 上瘾。 | A close friend shared a book link with me one afternoon, I tapped it out of pure curiosity, and that single book got me completely hooked. |
| [细] | 而且，它 同步 跨 我所有的 设备，所以 我 能 接着 就 从 我 停下的地方，而 它 甚至 朗读 给 我、当 我的眼睛 累了。 | What's more, it syncs across all my devices, so I can pick up right where I left off, and it even reads aloud to me when my eyes are tired. |
| [细2] | 我的妻子 也 用 它，说来 好笑。 | My wife uses it too, funnily enough. |
| [EL] | 说实话，它 重新 点燃 我 对 阅读 的爱、在 好些年 几乎 没 碰 一本书 之后。 | Honestly, it's rekindled my love of reading after years of barely touching a book. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，它 一直 是 一个 真正的 救星、对 一个 像 我 这样的 忙碌爸爸。 | So yeah, it's been a real lifesaver for a busy dad like me. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the app I'd like to talk about is a reading app on my phone — basically a digital library where I read sci-fi novels. I'm on it pretty much every single day. I usually open it on my commute, and then again at night to wind down before bed. I love sci-fi, so it's where I squeeze in a few pages whenever I get a spare moment. I actually found it completely by accident. A close friend shared a book link with me one afternoon, I tapped it out of pure curiosity, and that single book got me completely hooked. What's more, it syncs across all my devices, so I can pick up right where I left off, and it even reads aloud to me when my eyes are tired. My wife uses it too, funnily enough. Honestly, it's rekindled my love of reading after years of barely touching a book. So yeah, it's been a real lifesaver for a busy dad like me.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **app opener + where**：我想聊的app是一个阅读app——基本上一个数字图书馆，在那里我读科幻 → `a reading app … a digital library where I read sci-fi novels`
- **to + 目的 + before**：晚上打开去放松、在睡前 → `at night to wind down before bed`
- **where 表语**：它是那个我一有空就挤几页的地方 → `it's where I squeeze in a few pages whenever I get a spare moment`
- **偶然发现**：我完全偶然地找到它 → `found it completely by accident`
- **三段叙事链**：朋友分享链接，我出于好奇点了，那本书让我上瘾 → `A friend shared … I tapped it … and that book got me completely hooked`
- **从停下处接着读**：我能接着从我停下的地方 → `pick up right where I left off`
- **EL/closer**：重新点燃我对阅读的爱；对像我这样的忙碌爸爸是真救星 → `rekindled my love of reading` / `a real lifesaver for a busy dad like me`

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What are the differences between old and young people when using apps?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，有 一个 真正的 差距。 | Honestly, there's a real gap. |
| 2 | 年轻人 倾向于 弄懂 新 app 几乎 瞬间，因为 他们 是 滑着、点着 长大的，所以 它 是 第二天性。 | Younger folk tend to figure out new apps almost instantly because they've grown up swiping and tapping, so it's second nature. |
| 3 | 老年人 通常 更 谨慎——他们 会 读 每一个 提示、还 担心 按 错 按钮。 | Older people are usually more cautious — they'll read every prompt and worry about pressing the wrong button. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，一旦 他们 摸熟 它，他们 常常 忠诚地 坚持 一个 app。 | That said, once they get the hang of it, they often stick with one app loyally. |

> Honestly, there's a real gap. Younger folk tend to figure out new apps almost instantly because they've grown up swiping and tapping, so it's second nature. Older people are usually more cautious — they'll read every prompt and worry about pressing the wrong button. That said, once they get the hang of it, they often stick with one app loyally.

**Q2. Why do some people not like using apps?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 它 归结到 信任 和 习惯。 | Well, I'd say it comes down to trust and habit. |
| 2 | 有些人 担心 app 在 不断地 收割 他们的数据，所以 他们 宁愿 不 费心。 | Some people worry apps are constantly harvesting their data, so they'd rather not bother. |
| 3 | 另一些 就 觉得 那些 布局 令人困惑、还 更喜欢 用 老方式 做事，比如 打给 一个 商店、而不是 网上 下单。 | Others just find the layouts confusing and prefer doing things the old way, like ringing a shop instead of ordering online. |
| 4 | 对 他们，一个 app 感觉 像 额外的 麻烦，不是 一个 捷径。 | For them, an app feels like extra hassle, not a shortcut. |

> Well, I'd say it comes down to trust and habit. Some people worry apps are constantly harvesting their data, so they'd rather not bother. Others just find the layouts confusing and prefer doing things the old way, like ringing a shop instead of ordering online. For them, an app feels like extra hassle, not a shortcut.

**Q3. What apps are popular in your country? Why?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 在 我国，超级app、为 消息 和 支付 巨大——基本上 每个人 每天 用 它们。 | In my country, super-apps for messaging and payment are massive — basically everyone uses them daily. |
| 2 | 我觉得 那 是 因为 它们 把 一切 捆 进 一个 地方，所以 你 能 聊天、付账单、订 一辆 出租车、不用 切换 app。 | I reckon it's because they bundle everything into one place, so you can chat, pay bills and book a taxi without switching apps. |
| 3 | 短视频 app 也 巨大，大多 因为 它们 令人上瘾、还 给 你 一个 快笑、在 死时间 期间。 | Short-video apps are huge too, mostly because they're addictive and give you a quick laugh during dead time. |

> In my country, super-apps for messaging and payment are massive — basically everyone uses them daily. I reckon it's because they bundle everything into one place, so you can chat, pay bills and book a taxi without switching apps. Short-video apps are huge too, mostly because they're addictive and give you a quick laugh during dead time.

**Q4. Should parents limit their children's use of computer programs and computer games? Why and how?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，但 用 一个 轻手、而不是 一个 彻底的 禁令。 | Definitely, but with a light touch rather than an outright ban. |
| 2 | 我会说 设 一个 每日 时间限制 最 有效，因为 孩子 需要 一些 屏幕时间 [去]放松、甚至 学习。 | I'd say setting a daily time limit works best, since kids need some screen time to relax and even learn. |
| 3 | 那 关键 是 平衡——把 游戏 配 上 户外 玩 或 阅读。 | The key is balance — pairing games with outdoor play or reading. |
| 4 | 如果 你 太 严，他们 反正 会 就 偷偷 在 你 背后 溜。 | If you're too strict, they'll just sneak around behind your back anyway. |

> Definitely, but with a light touch rather than an outright ban. I'd say setting a daily time limit works best, since kids need some screen time to relax and even learn. The key is balance — pairing games with outdoor play or reading. If you're too strict, they'll just sneak around behind your back anyway.

**Q5. Do you think young people are more and more reliant on these programs?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 是的，我 觉得 他们 一年年 变得 更 依赖。 | Yeah, I think they're getting more dependent by the year. |
| 2 | 一大堆 年轻人 不能 导航 一座 新城市、没有 一个 地图 app，或 甚至 做 基本 算术、没有 一个 计算器。 | Loads of young people can't navigate a new city without a maps app, or even do basic sums without a calculator. |
| 3 | 它 顺手，当然，但 我会说 它 在 一点点 侵蚀 一些 日常 技能。 | It's handy, sure, but I'd say it's chipping away at some everyday skills. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，你 可以 争辩 它 只是 把 他们的脑子 解放 出来、为 其他 任务。 | Having said that, you could argue it just frees their brains up for other tasks. |

> Yeah, I think they're getting more dependent by the year. Loads of young people can't navigate a new city without a maps app, or even do basic sums without a calculator. It's handy, sure, but I'd say it's chipping away at some everyday skills. Having said that, you could argue it just frees their brains up for other tasks.

**Q6. What do you think about some countries banning children from using social media?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 能 看到 它 背后的 逻辑，说句公道话。 | I can see the logic behind it, to be fair. |
| 2 | 社交媒体 能 真的 搞乱 一个 青少年的 自信 和 睡眠，所以 保护 他们 几年 不是 一个 坏主意。 | Social media can really mess with a teenager's confidence and sleep, so shielding them for a few years isn't a bad shout. |
| 3 | 但 说实话，那 执行 的部分 变得 棘手——孩子 轻易 伪造 他们的年龄。 | But honestly, the enforcing part gets tricky — kids easily fake their age. |
| 4 | 我 宁愿 看到 更好的 教育、关于 安全 使用，而不是 一个 一刀切的 禁令。 | I'd rather see better education on safe use than a blanket ban. |

> I can see the logic behind it, to be fair. Social media can really mess with a teenager's confidence and sleep, so shielding them for a few years isn't a bad shout. But honestly, the enforcing part gets tricky — kids easily fake their age. I'd rather see better education on safe use than a blanket ban.

**P3 句型/模板**：`there's a real gap`（dummy-there）· `grown up swiping and tapping` / `second nature` · `worry about pressing the wrong button` · `get the hang of it` / `stick with one app loyally` · `it comes down to trust and habit` · `harvesting their data` / `they'd rather not bother` · `find the layouts confusing` · `ringing a shop instead of ordering online` · `extra hassle, not a shortcut` · `super-apps … are massive` · `bundle everything into one place` / `without switching apps` · `give you a quick laugh during dead time` · `with a light touch rather than an outright ban` · `setting a daily time limit works best`（动名词主语）· `pairing games with outdoor play or reading` · `sneak around behind your back` · `getting more dependent by the year` · `chipping away at some everyday skills` · `frees their brains up for other tasks` · `the logic behind it`（behind后置）· `shielding them for a few years isn't a bad shout`（动名词主语）· `the enforcing part gets tricky` / `fake their age` · `better education on safe use than a blanket ban`。

## P2-老09 · An occasion when many people were smiling — Muye 幼儿园表演（Event）

> **Cue**: 何时 / 和谁 / 发生什么 / 为何都在笑 · 题型 Event

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个场合 是 我儿子 Muye 的 幼儿园 新年 表演，（它 发生 在 几个月前，靠近 上学期 结尾）。 | So, the occasion I'd like to talk about is my son Muye's kindergarten New Year show, which happened a few months back, near the end of last term. |
| [who] | 我 跟 我妻子 一起 去，而 那 小礼堂 完全 挤满了 其他 家长。 | I went along with my wife, and the little hall was absolutely packed with other parents. |
| [who·细] | 我们 设法 抢到 两个 座位、就 在 前排，我们的手机 准备好 [去]拍 整个 过程。 | We managed to grab two seats right in the front, with our phones ready to film the whole thing. |
| [what] | 当 音乐 开始，所有 这些 小小的 五岁孩子 上场、穿着 配套的 服装、做了 一个 载歌载舞的 节目。 | When the music started, all these tiny five-year-olds came on in matching costumes and did a song-and-dance number. |
| [what·细] | 说实话，它 有点 是 一团糟，以 最好的 方式。 | Honestly, it was a bit of a mess, in the best way. |
| [what·细2] | 一个 男孩 就 冲 他妈 挥手 一整个 期间，几个 孩子 忘了 他们的 舞步，而 Muye 僵了 一秒、在 继续 之前。 | One boy just waved at his mum the entire time, a couple of kids forgot their steps, and Muye froze for a second before carrying on. |
| [细3] | 那 整个 房间 忍不住 微笑。 | The whole room couldn't help but smile. |
| [why/EL] | 大家 微笑 的原因，我 觉得，是 那些 孩子 那么 天真、那么 认真，而 那些 小错误 让 它 更 迷人。 | The reason everyone was smiling, I think, is that the kids were so innocent and earnest, and those little mistakes made it even more charming. |
| [why·细] | 我是说，他们的脸 就 亮了。 | I mean, their faces just lit up. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，差不多 就是 这样。 | So yeah, that's pretty much it. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the occasion I'd like to talk about is my son Muye's kindergarten New Year show, which happened a few months back, near the end of last term. I went along with my wife, and the little hall was absolutely packed with other parents. We managed to grab two seats right in the front, with our phones ready to film the whole thing. When the music started, all these tiny five-year-olds came on in matching costumes and did a song-and-dance number. Honestly, it was a bit of a mess, in the best way. One boy just waved at his mum the entire time, a couple of kids forgot their steps, and Muye froze for a second before carrying on. The whole room couldn't help but smile. The reason everyone was smiling, I think, is that the kids were so innocent and earnest, and those little mistakes made it even more charming. I mean, their faces just lit up. So yeah, that's pretty much it.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **occasion opener + which 后置**：我想聊的场合是Muye的幼儿园新年表演，发生在几个月前 → `… kindergarten New Year show, which happened a few months back`
- **挤满 / with-absolute**：小礼堂挤满家长；抢到两个前排座，手机准备好拍 → `packed with other parents` / `grab two seats … with our phones ready to film the whole thing`
- **上场穿着…做了…**：孩子上场穿配套服装做了载歌载舞节目 → `came on in matching costumes and did a song-and-dance number`
- **正面翻转的"糟"**：有点一团糟，以最好的方式 → `a bit of a mess, in the best way`
- **忍不住笑**：整个房间忍不住微笑 → `couldn't help but smile`
- **The reason … is that …**：大家微笑的原因是孩子太天真认真 → `The reason everyone was smiling … is that …`
- **脸亮了**：他们的脸就亮了 → `their faces just lit up`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Do you think people who like to smile are more friendly?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 总的来说，是的。 | Generally, yes. |
| 2 | 我会说 人们、（那种 微笑 很多的）显得 更 温暖、更 好接近，所以 陌生人 在 他们 周围 感觉 放松。 | I'd say people who smile a lot come across as warmer and more approachable, so strangers feel relaxed around them. |
| 3 | 一个 简单的 微笑 基本上 传达 你 是 开放、友好的，甚至 在 你 说话 之前。 | A simple smile basically signals you're open and friendly, even before you speak. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，它 不 总是 真的——有些人 微笑 出于 礼貌、而 底下 保持 挺 内敛。 | Having said that, it isn't always true — some folk smile out of politeness while staying quite reserved underneath. |

> Generally, yes. I'd say people who smile a lot come across as warmer and more approachable, so strangers feel relaxed around them. A simple smile basically signals you're open and friendly, even before you speak. Having said that, it isn't always true — some folk smile out of politeness while staying quite reserved underneath.

**Q2. Why do most people smile in photographs?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，大多 因为 一个 微笑 让 一张照片 看起来 更 有生气、更 友好。 | Well, mostly because a smile makes a photo look livelier and friendlier. |
| 2 | 没人 想 看起来 脾气坏、在 一张照片里、（那种 他们 会 保存 好些年的）。 | Nobody wants to look grumpy in a picture they'll keep for years. |
| 3 | 此外，摄影师 通常 告诉 你 [去]微笑，所以 它 现在 几乎 是 一个 习惯。 | Plus, photographers usually tell you to smile, so it's almost a habit now. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，一个 真诚的 微笑 也 捕捉 那一刻 的 快乐 心情 很好地。 | To be fair, a genuine smile also captures the happy mood of the moment really nicely. |

> Well, mostly because a smile makes a photo look livelier and friendlier. Nobody wants to look grumpy in a picture they'll keep for years. Plus, photographers usually tell you to smile, so it's almost a habit now. To be fair, a genuine smile also captures the happy mood of the moment really nicely.

**Q3. Do women smile more than men? Why?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我 觉得 她们 是，至少 一点。 | Honestly, I think they do, at least a bit. |
| 2 | 女性 常常 被 养成 更 有表现力、还 [去]让 别人 自在，所以 微笑 来得 更 自然。 | Women are often raised to be more expressive and to put others at ease, so smiling comes more naturally. |
| 3 | 男性 有时候 觉得 他们 应该 看起来 强硬 或 严肃、反而。 | Men sometimes feel they should look tough or serious instead. |
| 4 | 但 它 真的 因人而异——一大堆 开朗的 家伙 微笑 跟 任何人 一样 多。 | But it really varies — plenty of cheerful blokes smile just as much as anyone. |

> Honestly, I think they do, at least a bit. Women are often raised to be more expressive and to put others at ease, so smiling comes more naturally. Men sometimes feel they should look tough or serious instead. But it really varies — plenty of cheerful blokes smile just as much as anyone.

**Q4. Do people smile more when they are younger or older?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 年轻人 微笑 更 自由，只是 因为 他们 有 更少的 烦恼 压 着 他们。 | I'd say younger people smile more freely, simply because they've fewer worries weighing them down. |
| 2 | 孩子 笑 几乎 任何东西，而 大人 陷 在 压力 和 账单 里。 | Kids laugh at almost anything, while adults get caught up in stress and bills. |
| 3 | 但 老年人、（那种 已经 跟 生活 和解了的）也 能 出奇地 开朗。 | But older folk who've made peace with life can be surprisingly cheerful too. |
| 4 | 所以 它 更少 关于 年龄、更多 关于 你的 心态，真的。 | So it's less about age and more about your mindset, really. |

> I'd say younger people smile more freely, simply because they've fewer worries weighing them down. Kids laugh at almost anything, while adults get caught up in stress and bills. But older folk who've made peace with life can be surprisingly cheerful too. So it's less about age and more about your mindset, really.

**Q5. Is smiling important in your culture?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对。 | Definitely. |
| 2 | 在 我来自的地方，一个 温暖的 微笑 被 看作 礼貌、欢迎，尤其 当 你 遇见 某个 新人 或 迎接 客人。 | Where I'm from, a warm smile is seen as polite and welcoming, especially when you meet someone new or greet guests. |
| 3 | 它 也 抚平 尴尬的 时刻。 | It smooths over awkward moments too. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，我们 比 一些 文化 更 内敛 一点，所以 人们 不 那么 轻易地 冲 完全 陌生人 咧嘴笑、在 街上。 | That said, we're a bit more reserved than some cultures, so people don't grin at total strangers on the street as readily. |

> Definitely. Where I'm from, a warm smile is seen as polite and welcoming, especially when you meet someone new or greet guests. It smooths over awkward moments too. That said, we're a bit more reserved than some cultures, so people don't grin at total strangers on the street as readily.

**Q6. Are there any occasions when people need to pretend to smile?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 哦，绝对。 | Oh, absolutely. |
| 2 | 在 客服，员工 基本上 得 微笑 一整天、甚至 当 他们 精疲力竭 或 应付 粗鲁的 客户。 | In customer service, staff basically have to smile all day even when they're exhausted or dealing with rude clients. |
| 3 | 同样 适用于 摆姿势、在 正式 活动 或 家庭聚会、（那种 你 宁愿 跳过的）。 | The same goes for posing at formal events or family gatherings you'd rather skip. |
| 4 | 它 是 一个 社交 面具，真的——你 假装 [去]保持 事情 愉快、避免 冒犯 你 周围的 任何人。 | It's a social mask, really — you fake it to keep things pleasant and avoid offending anyone around you. |

> Oh, absolutely. In customer service, staff basically have to smile all day even when they're exhausted or dealing with rude clients. The same goes for posing at formal events or family gatherings you'd rather skip. It's a social mask, really — you fake it to keep things pleasant and avoid offending anyone around you.

**P3 句型/模板**：`people who smile a lot come across as warmer and more approachable`（who后置）· `signals you're open and friendly` · `smile out of politeness while staying quite reserved underneath` · `makes a photo look livelier and friendlier` · `a picture they'll keep for years`（relative后置）· `captures the happy mood of the moment` · `raised to be more expressive and to put others at ease` · `smiling comes more naturally`（动名词主语）· `plenty of cheerful blokes smile just as much as anyone` · `fewer worries weighing them down`（-ing后置）· `get caught up in stress and bills` · `older folk who've made peace with life`（who后置）· `less about age and more about your mindset` · `a warm smile is seen as polite and welcoming` · `smooths over awkward moments` · `grin at total strangers … as readily` · `The same goes for …` · `family gatherings you'd rather skip`（relative后置）· `a social mask` / `fake it to keep things pleasant`。

## P2-老10 · A time you felt proud of a family member — Muye 公园善举（Person）

> **Cue**: 何时 / 是谁 / 做了什么 / 为何骄傲 · 题型 Person

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个时刻 是 去年夏天，当 我的 小男孩 Muye 真的 让 我 骄傲。 | So, the time I'd like to talk about is last summer, when my little boy Muye really made me proud. |
| [where] | 它 发生 在 一个 公园、（在 我们 住处 附近的）。 | It happened at a park near our place. |
| [背景] | 我妻子 和 我 带 他 去 那儿 度 那 下午，而 他 就 在 溜达。 | My wife and I had taken him there for the afternoon, and he was just pottering about. |
| [背景·细] | 现在，Muye 才 五岁，而 他 是 一个 挺 害羞的 孩子——他 通常 往后 缩、等 我。 | Now, Muye's only five, and he's quite a shy kid — he usually hangs back and waits for me. |
| [what] | 但 那天，一个 更小的 学步孩子 绊倒 了、开始 哭得 撕心裂肺。 | But that day, a smaller toddler tripped over and started crying his eyes out. |
| [what·细] | 没有 一秒 的 犹豫，Muye 直接 走 过去、帮 那 小家伙 重新 站起来、甚至 分享 他 自己的 饼干 [去]安抚 他。 | Without a second thought, Muye walked straight over, helped the little one back up, and even shared his own biscuits to calm him down. |
| [细] | 我是说，那孩子 几秒 就 停止 哭。 | I mean, the kid stopped crying in seconds. |
| [why/EL] | 我 感到 这么 骄傲 的原因 是 我的 害羞的 儿子 直接 走 出 他的 舒适区、为 一个 陌生人。 | The reason I felt so proud is that my shy son stepped right out of his comfort zone for a stranger. |
| [why·细] | 它 说实话 融化 我的心——他 有 一个 真正的 软肋、为 别人，而 他 也 显示 一点 勇气。 | It honestly melted my heart — he's got a real soft spot for others, and he showed a bit of courage too. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，差不多 就是 这样。 | So yeah, that's pretty much it. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the time I'd like to talk about is last summer, when my little boy Muye really made me proud. It happened at a park near our place. My wife and I had taken him there for the afternoon, and he was just pottering about. Now, Muye's only five, and he's quite a shy kid — he usually hangs back and waits for me. But that day, a smaller toddler tripped over and started crying his eyes out. Without a second thought, Muye walked straight over, helped the little one back up, and even shared his own biscuits to calm him down. I mean, the kid stopped crying in seconds. The reason I felt so proud is that my shy son stepped right out of his comfort zone for a stranger. It honestly melted my heart — he's got a real soft spot for others, and he showed a bit of courage too. So yeah, that's pretty much it.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **time opener + when**：我想聊的时刻是去年夏天，当我小男孩真的让我骄傲 → `the time I'd like to talk about is last summer, when … really made me proud`
- **过去完成设背景**：我们带他去度那下午，他就在溜达 → `had taken him there for the afternoon … pottering about`
- **害羞铺垫**：他通常往后缩、等我 → `he usually hangs back and waits for me`
- **转折事件**：一个更小的孩子绊倒了、哭得撕心裂肺 → `a smaller toddler tripped over and started crying his eyes out`
- **Without a second thought + 一串动作**：没一秒犹豫，走过去、扶起、分享饼干去安抚 → `Without a second thought, … walked straight over, helped … up, and … shared … to calm him down`
- **The reason … is that … + 走出舒适区**：我骄傲的原因是害羞的儿子走出舒适区 → `The reason I felt so proud is that … stepped right out of his comfort zone`
- **EL**：融化我的心；他有真正的软肋、为别人 → `melted my heart` / `a real soft spot for others`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. When would parents feel proud of their children?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 父母 最 骄傲、当 他们的 孩子 展现 好品格，不 只是 好成绩。 | I'd say parents feel proudest when their kids show good character, not just good marks. |
| 2 | 比如，当 一个孩子 对 别人 善良 或 承认 一个 错误，那 就是 那 真正的 胜利。 | For instance, when a child's kind to others or owns up to a mistake, that's the real win. |
| 3 | 说实话，一个 顶尖的 考试分数 不错，但 看到 你的孩子 长成 一个 正派、有爱心的人 意义 大得多、长远来看。 | Honestly, a top exam score is nice, but seeing your kid grow into a decent, caring person means far more in the long run. |

> I'd say parents feel proudest when their kids show good character, not just good marks. For instance, when a child's kind to others or owns up to a mistake, that's the real win. Honestly, a top exam score is nice, but seeing your kid grow into a decent, caring person means far more in the long run.

**Q2. Should parents reward children? Why and how?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，在 合理范围内。 | Definitely, within reason. |
| 2 | 一个 小奖励 告诉 一个孩子 他们的 努力 被 注意到，而 那 让 他们 保持 有动力。 | A small reward tells a kid their effort gets noticed, and that keeps them motivated. |
| 3 | 但 我 会 避开 现金 或 昂贵的 玩具，因为 那 把 一切 变成 一场 交易。 | But I'd steer clear of cash or pricey toys, since that turns everything into a deal. |
| 4 | 反而，我觉得 表扬、一次 好玩的 外出、或 额外的 玩耍时间 更 有效——它 感觉 温暖、而不是 像 一场 交易。 | Instead, I reckon praise, a fun outing, or extra playtime works better — it feels warm rather than like a transaction. |

> Definitely, within reason. A small reward tells a kid their effort gets noticed, and that keeps them motivated. But I'd steer clear of cash or pricey toys, since that turns everything into a deal. Instead, I reckon praise, a fun outing, or extra playtime works better — it feels warm rather than like a transaction.

**Q3. Is it good to reward children too often? Why?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 不，我 不 觉得 这样。 | No, I don't reckon so. |
| 2 | 如果 你 奖励 一个孩子、为 每一个 小任务，他们 开始 期待 一个 奖品、在 他们 肯 动 一根手指 之前。 | If you reward a kid for every little task, they start expecting a prize before they'll lift a finger. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，它 扼杀 那 乐趣、（做 某事、只是 因为 它 是 对的）。 | To be fair, it kills the joy of doing something just because it's right. |
| 4 | 所以 我 会 把 奖励 留 给 真正的 努力，否则 它们 挺快 就 失去 它们 全部的 意义。 | So I'd save rewards for genuine effort, otherwise they lose all their meaning pretty fast. |

> No, I don't reckon so. If you reward a kid for every little task, they start expecting a prize before they'll lift a finger. To be fair, it kills the joy of doing something just because it's right. So I'd save rewards for genuine effort, otherwise they lose all their meaning pretty fast.

**Q4. On what occasions would adults be proud of themselves?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，大人 最 骄傲、在 挺过 某种 艰难的东西 之后——比如 完成 一场 马拉松 或 搞定 一个 项目、（那种 他们 差点 放弃的）。 | Honestly, adults feel proudest after pushing through something tough — like finishing a marathon or nailing a project they nearly gave up on. |
| 2 | 是 那种 "我 真的 做到了" 的感觉 留 下来。 | It's that sense of "I actually did it" that sticks. |
| 3 | 此外，帮 别人 也 能 激起 真正的 骄傲，因为 你 意识到 你 已经 做出 一个 小小的 不同、在 他们的 一天里。 | Plus, helping someone else can spark real pride too, since you realise you've made a small difference in their day. |

> Honestly, adults feel proudest after pushing through something tough — like finishing a marathon or nailing a project they nearly gave up on. It's that sense of "I actually did it" that sticks. Plus, helping someone else can spark real pride too, since you realise you've made a small difference in their day.

**Q5. Do rewards help a child become better?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 在 某种程度上，是的。 | To some extent, yes. |
| 2 | 一个 及时的 奖励 能 推 一个孩子 [去]更 努力、还 早早地 建 一个 好习惯。 | A timely reward can nudge a child to try harder and build a good habit early on. |
| 3 | 但 它 有点 是 一把 双刃剑——太 依赖 它，而 他们 就 只 为 那奖品 干活。 | But it's a bit of a double-edged sword — lean on it too much, and they only work for the prize. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 它 最好 作为 一个 温和的 助推、不是 那 主要的 驱动。 | So I'd say it helps best as a gentle boost, not the main driver. |

> To some extent, yes. A timely reward can nudge a child to try harder and build a good habit early on. But it's a bit of a double-edged sword — lean on it too much, and they only work for the prize. So I'd say it helps best as a gentle boost, not the main driver.

**Q6. What do you think about children working hard just for grades?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我 觉得 它 有点 悲哀。 | Honestly, I find it a bit sad. |
| 2 | 成绩 要紧，当然，但 如果 一个孩子 只 学习 [去]追 数字，他们 错过 那 乐趣、（真正 学到 某种东西的）。 | Grades matter, sure, but if a kid only studies to chase numbers, they miss the joy of actually learning something. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，那 压力 常常 来自 父母 或 学校。 | To be fair, the pressure's often from parents or schools. |
| 4 | 我觉得 真正的 好奇心 持续 得 远远 更久、比 任何 成绩单 曾经 能。 | I reckon real curiosity lasts far longer than any report card ever could. |

> Honestly, I find it a bit sad. Grades matter, sure, but if a kid only studies to chase numbers, they miss the joy of actually learning something. To be fair, the pressure's often from parents or schools. I reckon real curiosity lasts far longer than any report card ever could.

**P3 句型/模板**：`show good character, not just good marks` · `owns up to a mistake` / `the real win` · `seeing your kid grow into a decent, caring person means far more`（动名词主语）· `their effort gets noticed` / `keeps them motivated` · `steer clear of cash or pricey toys` / `turns everything into a deal` · `it feels warm rather than like a transaction` · `expecting a prize before they'll lift a finger` · `kills the joy of doing something just because it's right` · `save rewards for genuine effort` · `pushing through something tough` / `a project they nearly gave up on`（relative后置）· `It's that sense of "I actually did it" that sticks`（强调）· `helping someone else can spark real pride`（动名词主语）· `made a small difference in their day` · `nudge a child to try harder` · `a double-edged sword` / `lean on it too much` · `a gentle boost, not the main driver` · `studies to chase numbers` / `the joy of actually learning something` · `real curiosity lasts far longer than any report card ever could`。

## P2-老11 · Something kept in your family for a long time — 外公的旧机械表（Object）

> **Cue**: 是什么 / 何时拥有 / 怎么得到 / 为何重要 · 题型 Object

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/22 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改 · 只练了 P2）

> 你自己练过的 P2（外公的旧机械表，你的真实版；这条只练了 P2）。**不降档**，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · 外公的旧机械表）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我想聊的那个 东西 是 一块 旧 手表。 | The thing I'd like to talk about is an old watch. |
| 2 | 它 是 一块 机械 表，（它 看起来 有点 磨损、甚至 已经 走 不 准 了）。 | It's a mechanical watch, which looks a bit worn and doesn't even keep perfect time anymore. |
| 3 | 它 属于 我 外公，（他 在 娶 我 外婆 的时候 买 了 它）。 | It belonged to my grandfather, who bought it when he married my grandmother. |
| 4 | 我 外公 几乎 每天 戴 它，而 他 在 我 童年 期间 把 我 带大。 | My grandfather wore it almost every day, and he raised me during my childhood. |
| 5 | 它 在 我们 家 五十多年 了，而 后来 他 把 它 传 给 了 我。 | It's been in our family for over fifty years, and later he passed it down to me. |
| 6 | 它 重要 不 是 因为 它 值 很多 钱，而 是 因为 它 代表 的 东西——看着 它 总是 勾起 我 小时候 的 回忆。 | It's important not because it's worth a lot, but because of what it represents — looking at it always brings back memories of when I was a kid. |
| 7 | 说实话，每次 我 看 它，我 就 想起 那些 时候、（他 会 坐 在 我 旁边 陪 我、当 我 做 作业 的时候）。 | Honestly, every time I look at it, I remember the times when he would sit right next to me and keep me company while I did my homework. |
| 8 | 所以 有一天，我 想 把 它 传 给 我 儿子。 | So one day, I'd like to pass it down to my son. |

> The thing I'd like to talk about is an old watch. It's a mechanical watch, which looks a bit worn and doesn't even keep perfect time anymore. It belonged to my grandfather, who bought it when he married my grandmother. My grandfather wore it almost every day, and he raised me during my childhood. It's been in our family for over fifty years, and later he passed it down to me. It's important not because it's worth a lot, but because of what it represents — looking at it always brings back memories of when I was a kid. Honestly, every time I look at it, I remember the times when he would sit right next to me and keep me company while I did my homework. So one day, I'd like to pass it down to my son.

---

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那件东西 是 我 外公的 旧 机械 手表——一块 简单的 银色的、带 一条 磨损的 皮表带。 | So, the item I'd like to talk about is my grandfather's old mechanical wristwatch — a simple silver one with a worn leather strap. |
| [when] | 它 在 我家 已经 很久了，远 在 我 甚至 出生 之前。 | It's been in my family for ages, way before I was even born. |
| [how got·1] | 我外公 买 它 是 在 他 二十几岁，用 那 第一份 正经工资、（他 曾 挣的）。 | My grandad bought it back in his twenties, with the first proper salary he ever earned. |
| [细] | 他 以前 几乎 每一天 戴 它、戴 了 几十年，而 不知怎么 它 还 一直 在 走。 | He used to wear it nearly every single day for decades, and somehow it still keeps on ticking. |
| [how got·2] | 我 得到 它 的方式 其实 挺 感人。 | The way I got it is actually quite touching. |
| [how got·细] | 他 帮 养 我、当 我 小的时候，在 一个 小城市，而 在 他 去世 之前，他 把 它 传 给 了 我。 | He helped raise me when I was little, in a small city, and before he passed away, he handed it down to me. |
| [细2] | 我是说，它 不 值 多少 钱，但 对我来说 它 无价。 | I mean, it isn't worth much money, but to me it's priceless. |
| [why 提问] | 那 为什么 它 意义 这么 大？ | So why does it matter so much? |
| [why/EL] | 嗯，基本上 它 是 一个 通往 过去 的 链接——一种 跨 代际 的 桥。 | Well, basically it's a link to the past — a kind of bridge across the generations. |
| [why·细] | 每次 我 瞥 它 一眼，它 把 我的童年 涌 回来。 | Every time I glance at it, it brings my childhood flooding back. |
| [closer] | 总的来说，它 对 我 值 得 远远 多过 任何 钱，而 我 永远 不会 放手。 | All in all, it's worth far more to me than any money, and I'd never let it go. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the item I'd like to talk about is my grandfather's old mechanical wristwatch — a simple silver one with a worn leather strap. It's been in my family for ages, way before I was even born. My grandad bought it back in his twenties, with the first proper salary he ever earned. He used to wear it nearly every single day for decades, and somehow it still keeps on ticking. The way I got it is actually quite touching. He helped raise me when I was little, in a small city, and before he passed away, he handed it down to me. I mean, it isn't worth much money, but to me it's priceless. So why does it matter so much? Well, basically it's a link to the past — a kind of bridge across the generations. Every time I glance at it, it brings my childhood flooding back. All in all, it's worth far more to me than any money, and I'd never let it go.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **item opener + with 后置**：外公的旧机械表——一块简单银色的、带磨损皮表带 → `… wristwatch — a simple silver one with a worn leather strap`
- **way before I was even born**：远在我出生之前
- **relative 后置**：用他曾挣的第一份正经工资 → `with the first proper salary he ever earned`
- **伪分裂主语**：我得到它的方式挺感人 → `The way I got it is actually quite touching`
- **传家动词**：他把它传给了我 → `he handed it down to me` / `keeps on ticking`
- **比喻**：通往过去的链接——跨代际的桥 → `a link to the past — a kind of bridge across the generations`
- **涌回来**：每次我瞥一眼，它把我的童年涌回来 → `it brings my childhood flooding back`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What things do families keep for a long time?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 它 大多是 旧照片 和 一些 首饰，比如 戒指 或 项链、（从 祖父母 传下来的）。 | Well, I'd say it's mostly old photos and bits of jewellery, like rings or necklaces passed down from grandparents. |
| 2 | 家庭 也 保留 手写的 信、有时候 家具。 | Families also hang onto handwritten letters and sometimes furniture. |
| 3 | 说实话，他们 保留的 通常 不 值 多少 钱——它 更 关于 那些 记忆、（系在 它上面的），一小块 家族历史、（他们 真的 不能 替换的）。 | Honestly, what they keep usually isn't worth much money — it's more about the memories tied to it, a little piece of family history they can't really replace. |

> Well, I'd say it's mostly old photos and bits of jewellery, like rings or necklaces passed down from grandparents. Families also hang onto handwritten letters and sometimes furniture. Honestly, what they keep usually isn't worth much money — it's more about the memories tied to it, a little piece of family history they can't really replace.

**Q2. What's the difference between things valued by people in the past and today?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说句公道话，过去的 人 珍视 东西、（那种 持久的）——结实的 家具、手工的 工具、物件、（他们 会 修 然后 保留 一辈子的）。 | To be fair, people in the past valued things that lasted — solid furniture, handmade tools, items they'd repair and keep for life. |
| 2 | 这些天，不过，我们 倾向于 珍视 便捷 和 最新的 小设备，（那 我们 挺快 就 扔掉）。 | These days, though, we tend to prize convenience and the latest gadgets, which we toss out pretty fast. |
| 3 | 所以 我觉得 那 大转变 是 从 耐用性 朝向 新奇 和 不断的 升级。 | So I reckon the big shift is from durability towards novelty and constant upgrades. |

> To be fair, people in the past valued things that lasted — solid furniture, handmade tools, items they'd repair and keep for life. These days, though, we tend to prize convenience and the latest gadgets, which we toss out pretty fast. So I reckon the big shift is from durability towards novelty and constant upgrades.

**Q3. What kinds of things are kept in museums?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 一大堆 不同的 东西，真的——古代 陶器、旧 硬币、绘画、化石、甚至 恐龙 骨头。 | Loads of different stuff, really — ancient pottery, old coins, paintings, fossils, even dinosaur bones. |
| 2 | 基本上 任何 稀有的 或 历史上 重要的东西、（那种 帮 讲 一个 关于 过去 的故事的）。 | Basically anything rare or historically important that helps tell a story about the past. |
| 3 | 有些 博物馆 也 保留 日常 物件，比如 旧 厨房 工具，因为 它们 展示 普通人 那时候 实际上 怎么 生活。 | Some museums also keep everyday objects, like old kitchen tools, since they show how ordinary folk actually lived back then. |
| 4 | 它 挺 是 一个 混合。 | It's quite a mix. |

> Loads of different stuff, really — ancient pottery, old coins, paintings, fossils, even dinosaur bones. Basically anything rare or historically important that helps tell a story about the past. Some museums also keep everyday objects, like old kitchen tools, since they show how ordinary folk actually lived back then. It's quite a mix.

**Q4. What's the influence of technology on museums?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它 一直 是 巨大的，说实话。 | It's been huge, honestly. |
| 2 | 很多 博物馆 现在 用 触屏、虚拟现实、和 互动 展示，所以 参观者 能 探索 展品、以 一种 真正 亲手的 方式。 | A lot of museums now use touchscreens, virtual reality and interactive displays, so visitors can explore exhibits in a really hands-on way. |
| 3 | 此外，它们 很多 已经 数字化了，让 你 在线 逛 那 藏品、从 家里。 | Plus, plenty of them have gone digital, letting you tour the collection online from home. |
| 4 | 我觉得 它 让 历史 远远 更 吸引人，尤其 对 更年轻的 人群。 | I reckon it makes history far more engaging, especially for younger crowds. |

> It's been huge, honestly. A lot of museums now use touchscreens, virtual reality and interactive displays, so visitors can explore exhibits in a really hands-on way. Plus, plenty of them have gone digital, letting you tour the collection online from home. I reckon it makes history far more engaging, especially for younger crowds.

**Q5. What are the benefits of technology for learning history?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 相当 几个，我会说。 | Quite a few, I'd say. |
| 2 | 技术 把 过去 带 活——纪录片、3D 重建、和 app 让 你 真正地 想象 人们 怎么 生活。 | Technology brings the past to life — documentaries, 3D reconstructions and apps let you actually picture how people lived. |
| 3 | 它 也 让 学习 更 可及，因为 你 能 挖 进 档案 或 看 讲座、从 任何地方。 | It also makes learning more accessible, since you can dig into archives or watch lectures from anywhere. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，我觉得 没有东西 完全 替代 亲手 摆弄 一个 真的 文物、亲自地。 | Having said that, I reckon nothing fully replaces handling a real artefact in person. |

> Quite a few, I'd say. Technology brings the past to life — documentaries, 3D reconstructions and apps let you actually picture how people lived. It also makes learning more accessible, since you can dig into archives or watch lectures from anywhere. Having said that, I reckon nothing fully replaces handling a real artefact in person.

**Q6. Why do people visit museums?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，大多 [为了] 学 点东西、满足 他们的 好奇心、关于 历史 或 艺术。 | Well, mostly to learn something and satisfy their curiosity about history or art. |
| 2 | 亲近地 看 那些 真实的 物件 感觉 远远 更 有力、比 在 一本书里 读 它们。 | Seeing the real objects up close feels way more powerful than reading about them in a book. |
| 3 | 此外，它 是 一个 可爱的 方式、[去]度过 一个 下午，尤其 跟 家人 或 朋友。 | Plus, it's a lovely way to spend an afternoon, especially with family or friends. |
| 4 | 对 游客，它 也 是 一个 快速的 窗口、通向 当地 文化。 | For tourists, it's also a quick window into local culture. |

> Well, mostly to learn something and satisfy their curiosity about history or art. Seeing the real objects up close feels way more powerful than reading about them in a book. Plus, it's a lovely way to spend an afternoon, especially with family or friends. For tourists, it's also a quick window into local culture.

**P3 句型/模板**：`rings or necklaces passed down from grandparents`（分词后置）· `hang onto handwritten letters` · `what they keep usually isn't worth much money`（what主语）· `the memories tied to it`（分词后置）/ `a little piece of family history they can't really replace`（relative后置）· `things that lasted`（that后置）· `items they'd repair and keep for life`（relative后置）· `the latest gadgets, which we toss out pretty fast`（which后置）· `from durability towards novelty and constant upgrades` · `anything rare or historically important that helps tell a story`（that后置）· `how ordinary folk actually lived back then`（embedded）· `explore exhibits in a really hands-on way` · `gone digital, letting you tour the collection online`（-ing结果）· `brings the past to life` / `picture how people lived`（embedded）· `dig into archives` · `nothing fully replaces handling a real artefact in person`（动名词宾语）· `Seeing the real objects up close feels way more powerful`（动名词主语）· `a quick window into local culture`。

## P2-老12 · A car/bike/motorcycle trip you'd like to go on — 川西自驾（Event/Place）

> **Cue**: 和谁 / 去哪 / 何时 / 为何选这种交通 · 题型 Event/Place

### 🎤 你的实产版（6/22 session 练过 · 你的词/结构，未降未改 · 只练了 P2）

> 你自己练过的 P2（川西自驾带家人，你的真实版 · 固定短语零翻车；这条只练了 P2）。**不降档**，只加英式中文珠子。

**P2（你的版 · 川西自驾带家人）**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 你的实产英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 想 去 的 那趟 公路旅行 是 川西 的 某个 地方。 | The road trip I'd like to take is somewhere in western Sichuan. |
| 2 | 我 大概 会 等 到 我 儿子 长 大 一点，也许 一两年 内，然后 在 暑假 期间 去。 | I'd probably wait until my son grows a bit older, maybe in a year or two, and go during the summer holiday. |
| 3 | 它 是 那种 地方、（那里 有 很多 雪盖的 山）。 | It's the kind of place where there are plenty of snow-capped mountains. |
| 4 | 我 会 跟 我 家人 一起 去——我 老婆 和 我们 儿子，因为 他们 是 我 盼着 旅行 的 主要 原因。 | I'd go with my family — my wife and our son, because they're the main reason I look forward to traveling. |
| 5 | 当 我们 到 那儿，我们 会 沿着 那些 山路 开，然后 在 任何 看起来 不错的 地方 停。 | When we get there, we'd drive along the mountain roads and stop anywhere that looks nice. |
| 6 | 我们 也 会 找 一家 不错的 餐厅、尝 一些 当地 食物。 | We'd also find a nice restaurant and try some local food. |
| 7 | 我 开车 而不是 坐飞机 的 原因 是 那 自由——因为 当 你 开车，你 不 被 一个 日程 拴住，你 可以 只要 看到 一个 好 景 就 停、拍照、让 我们 儿子 到处 跑。 | The reason I'd go by car rather than fly is the freedom — because when you drive you're not stuck to a schedule, you can stop whenever you see a nice view, take photos and let our son run around. |
| 8 | 说实话，那 最好的 部分 是 我们 不必 赶——通常 我们 总是 匆匆忙忙，但 这次 我们 就 慢慢来。 | Honestly, the best part is we don't have to rush — usually we're always in a hurry, but this time we'd just take our time. |

> The road trip I'd like to take is somewhere in western Sichuan. I'd probably wait until my son grows a bit older, maybe in a year or two, and go during the summer holiday. It's the kind of place where there are plenty of snow-capped mountains. I'd go with my family — my wife and our son, because they're the main reason I look forward to traveling. When we get there, we'd drive along the mountain roads and stop anywhere that looks nice. We'd also find a nice restaurant and try some local food. The reason I'd go by car rather than fly is the freedom — because when you drive you're not stuck to a schedule, you can stop whenever you see a nice view, take photos and let our son run around. Honestly, the best part is we don't have to rush — usually we're always in a hurry, but this time we'd just take our time.

---

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那趟 旅行 是 一次 自驾 公路旅行、穿过 川西，（它 就 在 我家门口，因为 我 住 成都）。 | So, the trip I'd like to talk about is a self-drive road trip through western Sichuan, which is right on my doorstep since I live in Chengdu. |
| [who/where] | 我 会 很想 跟 我妻子 和 我们的 儿子 Muye 一起 做 它，而 我们 会 往 西边 上 到 山里，（那里 有 雪盖的 山峰、宽阔的 草原、和 这些 蜿蜒的 路、那种 穿过 那些 山谷 扭 的）。 | I'd love to do it with my wife and our son Muye, and we'd head up into the mountains out west, where there's snow-capped peaks, wide grasslands and these winding roads that twist through the valleys. |
| [when] | 我 打算 去 一个 长假、一旦 Muye 大 一点、能 应付 那 长途 驾驶。 | I'm planning to go on a long holiday once Muye's a bit older and can handle the long drive. |
| [why] | 我 会 开车 而不是 坐 火车 或 飞机 的原因 是 那 自由——我们 能 靠边停、无论 什么时候 我们 想，吸收 那 风景，还 到达 那些 偏远的 地点、（远离 常规路线的），（那种 公共交通 就是 到不了的）。 | The reason I'd go by car rather than by train or plane is the freedom — we could pull over whenever we felt like it, take in the scenery, and reach those remote spots off the beaten track that public transport just can't get to. |
| [why·细] | 此外，它 是 可爱的 家庭 时光、在 路上。 | Plus, it's lovely family time on the road. |
| [EL] | 真正 吸引 我的 是 按 我们 自己的 节奏 做 它、还 一起 制造 回忆。 | What really appeals to me is doing it at our own pace and making memories together. |
| [EL·细] | 我是说，它 是 关于 那 旅程，不是 那 目的地。 | I mean, it's about the journey, not the destination. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，差不多 就是 这样。 | So yeah, that's pretty much it. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the trip I'd like to talk about is a self-drive road trip through western Sichuan, which is right on my doorstep since I live in Chengdu. I'd love to do it with my wife and our son Muye, and we'd head up into the mountains out west, where there's snow-capped peaks, wide grasslands and these winding roads that twist through the valleys. I'm planning to go on a long holiday once Muye's a bit older and can handle the long drive. The reason I'd go by car rather than by train or plane is the freedom — we could pull over whenever we felt like it, take in the scenery, and reach those remote spots off the beaten track that public transport just can't get to. Plus, it's lovely family time on the road. What really appeals to me is doing it at our own pace and making memories together. I mean, it's about the journey, not the destination. So yeah, that's pretty much it.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **trip opener + which 后置**：川西自驾，就在我家门口 → `a self-drive road trip through western Sichuan, which is right on my doorstep`
- **where 后置 + that 后置**：往山里上，那里有雪山草原和蜿蜒穿谷的路 → `head up into the mountains …, where there's … and these winding roads that twist through the valleys`
- **once 从句**：一旦Muye大一点能应付长途 → `once Muye's a bit older and can handle the long drive`
- **The reason I'd … rather than … is the freedom**：我开车而不坐火车飞机的原因是自由
- **拆珠子**：随时靠边停、吸收风景、到达公共交通到不了的偏远地点 → `pull over whenever we felt like it, take in the scenery … off the beaten track that public transport just can't get to`
- **What 主语 + 动名词表语**：真正吸引我的是按自己节奏做、一起制造回忆 → `What really appeals to me is doing it at our own pace and making memories together`
- **金句**：关于旅程，不是目的地 → `it's about the journey, not the destination`

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Which form of vehicle is more popular in your country, bikes, cars or motorcycles?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 汽车 是 目前 最 受欢迎的、在这里，尤其 在 城市，因为 它们 被 看作 一个 生活 混得好 的 标志。 | Well, I'd say cars are by far the most popular here, especially in cities, since they're seen as a sign of doing well in life. |
| 2 | 自行车 还 在、为 短途，但 摩托车 已经 过时了。 | Bikes are still around for short trips, but motorcycles have fallen out of fashion. |
| 3 | 说句公道话，电动 踏板车 现在 到处 都是，因为 它们 便宜、还 躲开 那 交通。 | To be fair, electric scooters are everywhere now because they're cheap and dodge the traffic. |

> Well, I'd say cars are by far the most popular here, especially in cities, since they're seen as a sign of doing well in life. Bikes are still around for short trips, but motorcycles have fallen out of fashion. To be fair, electric scooters are everywhere now because they're cheap and dodge the traffic.

**Q2. Do you think air pollution comes mostly from mobile vehicles?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我 觉得 车辆 是 它的 一大部分，但 它们 不是 那 全部。 | Honestly, I think vehicles are a big part of it, but they're not the whole story. |
| 2 | 在 很多 城市，工厂 和 燃煤 电厂 排出 一样多，如果 不是 更多。 | In a lot of cities, factories and coal-fired plants pump out just as much, if not more. |
| 3 | 所以 虽然 汽车 和 卡车 肯定 增加 那 雾霾，只 怪 它们 有点 牵强，真的。 | So while cars and lorries definitely add to the smog, blaming them alone is a bit of a stretch, really. |

> Honestly, I think vehicles are a big part of it, but they're not the whole story. In a lot of cities, factories and coal-fired plants pump out just as much, if not more. So while cars and lorries definitely add to the smog, blaming them alone is a bit of a stretch, really.

**Q3. Do you think people need to change the way of transportation drastically to protect the environment?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 一个 剧烈的 改变 大概 必要，是的。 | I'd say a drastic change is probably necessary, yeah. |
| 2 | 只 微调 习惯 不管用，如果 每个人 还 到处 开 汽油车。 | Just tweaking habits won't cut it if everyone's still driving petrol cars everywhere. |
| 3 | 我们 会 需要 正经的 投资、在 公共交通 和 自行车道，好让 人们 真的 有 一个 真正的 替代。 | We'd need proper investment in public transport and cycling lanes so people actually have a real alternative. |
| 4 | 比如，城市、（那种 有 好 地铁系统的）看到 远远 更少的 车、在 路上。 | For instance, cities with good metro systems see way fewer cars on the road. |

> I'd say a drastic change is probably necessary, yeah. Just tweaking habits won't cut it if everyone's still driving petrol cars everywhere. We'd need proper investment in public transport and cycling lanes so people actually have a real alternative. For instance, cities with good metro systems see way fewer cars on the road.

**Q4. How are the transportation systems in urban areas and rural areas different?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它们 天壤之别，真的。 | They're worlds apart, really. |
| 2 | 在 城市 你 有 地铁、公交、和 打车 app、全天候 运行，所以 出行 极其 容易。 | In cities you've got metros, buses and ride-hailing apps running round the clock, so getting about is dead easy. |
| 3 | 在 乡下，不过，选择 挺 稀薄——也许 一天 一班 公交——所以 大多数人 依赖 他们 自己的 车 或 摩托、只 为了 去 任何地方。 | Out in the countryside, though, options are pretty thin — maybe one bus a day — so most folk rely on their own car or motorbike just to get anywhere at all. |

> They're worlds apart, really. In cities you've got metros, buses and ride-hailing apps running round the clock, so getting about is dead easy. Out in the countryside, though, options are pretty thin — maybe one bus a day — so most folk rely on their own car or motorbike just to get anywhere at all.

**Q5. Why do more people own and drive private vehicles now?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 主要 因为 汽车 变得 远远 更 买得起，加上 生活水平 在 过去 十年 飙升了。 | Mainly because cars have got far more affordable, plus living standards have shot up over the past decade. |
| 2 | 一辆 私家车 也 给 你 真正的 自由——你 不 被 困 着 等 一辆 公交、在 雨里。 | A private car also gives you real freedom — you're not stuck waiting for a bus in the rain. |
| 3 | 除此之外，拥有 一辆 带 一点 地位，所以 人们 把 它 看作 证明、（他们 成功了的）。 | On top of that, owning one carries a bit of status, so people see it as proof they've made it. |

> Mainly because cars have got far more affordable, plus living standards have shot up over the past decade. A private car also gives you real freedom — you're not stuck waiting for a bus in the rain. On top of that, owning one carries a bit of status, so people see it as proof they've made it.

**Q6. What do you think of the future of electric cars?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 挺 乐观，说实话。 | I'm pretty optimistic, honestly. |
| 2 | 它们 每年 变得 更 便宜、而 那 充电 网络 一直 增长，所以 里程 焦虑 慢慢 消退。 | They're getting cheaper every year and the charging network keeps growing, so range anxiety's slowly fading. |
| 3 | 我觉得 十年 之内 它们 会 是 那 默认 选择、为 大多数 买家。 | I reckon within a decade they'll be the default choice for most buyers. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，那 电池 仍然 引起 环境 问题，所以 它们 还 不是 一个 完美的 解决、暂时。 | Having said that, the batteries still raise environmental questions, so they're not a perfect fix just yet. |

> I'm pretty optimistic, honestly. They're getting cheaper every year and the charging network keeps growing, so range anxiety's slowly fading. I reckon within a decade they'll be the default choice for most buyers. Having said that, the batteries still raise environmental questions, so they're not a perfect fix just yet.

**P3 句型/模板**：`seen as a sign of doing well in life` · `fallen out of fashion` · `dodge the traffic` · `not the whole story` · `pump out just as much, if not more` · `blaming them alone is a bit of a stretch`（动名词主语）· `Just tweaking habits won't cut it`（动名词主语）· `proper investment … so people actually have a real alternative` · `cities with good metro systems see way fewer cars`（with后置）· `worlds apart` · `running round the clock` / `getting about is dead easy`（动名词主语）· `options are pretty thin` / `just to get anywhere at all` · `cars have got far more affordable` / `living standards have shot up` · `you're not stuck waiting for a bus in the rain` · `owning one carries a bit of status`（动名词主语）/ `proof they've made it`（relative后置）· `the charging network keeps growing` / `range anxiety's slowly fading` · `the default choice for most buyers` · `not a perfect fix just yet`。
