## P2-老13 · A person who solved a problem in a smart way — zhangwei 查网络日志（Person）

> **Cue**: 是谁 / 什么问题 / 怎么解决 / 为何机智 · 题型 Person

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 我想聊的那个人 是 一个 大学的 老朋友，zhangwei。 | The person I'd like to talk about is an old friend from university, zhangwei. |
| [铺垫] | 我们 认识 彼此 快 二十年了，而 他 做 一个 开发者。 | We've known each other for almost twenty years, and he works as a developer. |
| [problem] | 那问题 发生 在 他 公司、不久前——他们 整个 系统 突然 变慢，而 用户 到处 在 抱怨。 | The problem happened at his company a while back — their whole system suddenly slowed down, and users were complaining everywhere. |
| [problem·细] | 团队上 每个人 都 慌了、直接 扎 进 代码，猎 bug。 | Everyone on the team panicked and dove straight into the code, hunting for bugs. |
| [how solved] | 但 zhangwei 采取 一个 完全 不同的 方式。 | But zhangwei took a completely different approach. |
| [how·细] | 与其 像 其他人 一样 翻 那 代码库，他 退 一步、开始 看 那 网络 日志。 | Instead of digging through the codebase like everyone else, he stepped back and started looking at the network logs. |
| [how·细2] | 在 大约 三十分钟 之内，他 弄清 一台 有故障的 服务器 在 把 请求 发 到 半个 地球 之外。 | Within about thirty minutes, he figured out that one faulty server was sending requests halfway across the world. |
| [结果] | 他 修 那 路由，而 那 整个东西 回到 正常。 | He fixed the routing, and the whole thing was back to normal. |
| [why/EL] | 让 它 机智的 不 只是 他的 技术 技能——是 他的 本能、[去]停下 然后 问，"要是 那问题 不在 大家 找的 地方 呢？" | What made it smart wasn't just his technical skill — it was his instinct to stop and ask, "What if the problem isn't where everyone's looking?" |
| [closer] | 所以，对，那 就是 zhangwei。他 是 那种人、（那种 靠 想 得 不一样 解决 事情的，不 只是 干 得 更 努力）。 | So yeah, that's zhangwei. He's the kind of person who solves things by thinking differently, not just working harder. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> The person I'd like to talk about is an old friend from university, zhangwei. We've known each other for almost twenty years, and he works as a developer. The problem happened at his company a while back — their whole system suddenly slowed down, and users were complaining everywhere. Everyone on the team panicked and dove straight into the code, hunting for bugs. But zhangwei took a completely different approach. Instead of digging through the codebase like everyone else, he stepped back and started looking at the network logs. Within about thirty minutes, he figured out that one faulty server was sending requests halfway across the world. He fixed the routing, and the whole thing was back to normal. What made it smart wasn't just his technical skill — it was his instinct to stop and ask, "What if the problem isn't where everyone's looking?" So yeah, that's zhangwei. He's the kind of person who solves things by thinking differently, not just working harder.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **-ing 伴随**：每个人慌了、扎进代码，猎bug → `panicked and dove straight into the code, hunting for bugs`
- **Instead of + 动名词**：与其翻代码库，他退一步 → `Instead of digging through the codebase …, he stepped back`
- **figured out that …**：他弄清一台故障服务器把请求发到半个地球外 → `he figured out that one faulty server was sending requests halfway across the world`
- **what 主语强调**：让它机智的不只是技术——是他停下问的本能 → `What made it smart wasn't just his technical skill — it was his instinct to …`
- **the kind of person who … 后置**：靠想得不一样解决事情、不只干得更努力 → `the kind of person who solves things by thinking differently, not just working harder`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Do you think children are born smart or they learn to become smart?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我会说 它 有点 是 两者。 | Honestly, I'd say it's a bit of both. |
| 2 | 有些 孩子 确实 看起来 天生 快，但 我觉得 它 大部分 归结到 他们 怎么 被 养、还有 他们 被 暴露于 什么。 | Some kids do seem naturally quick, but I reckon most of it comes down to how they're raised and what they're exposed to. |
| 3 | 如果 一个孩子 在 好奇的人 周围 长大、（那种 问 问题的），他们 会 学到 那习惯。 | If a child grows up around curious people who ask questions, they'll pick up that habit. |
| 4 | 所以 天性 给 一个 起点，但 学习 塑造 其余。 | So nature gives a start, but learning shapes the rest. |

> Honestly, I'd say it's a bit of both. Some kids do seem naturally quick, but I reckon most of it comes down to how they're raised and what they're exposed to. If a child grows up around curious people who ask questions, they'll pick up that habit. So nature gives a start, but learning shapes the rest.

**Q2. How do children become smart at school?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我 觉得 它 更少 关于 记 事实、更多 关于 他们 怎么 被 教。 | Well, I think it's less about memorising facts and more about how they're taught. |
| 2 | 一个 好学校 推 孩子 [去]问 为什么、小组 工作、还 解决 真实 问题。 | A good school pushes kids to ask why, work in groups, and solve real problems. |
| 3 | 比如，一个 科学课、（那里 你 真的 做 实验的）胜过 一个、（那里 你 只 抄 笔记的）。 | For instance, a science class where you actually run experiments beats one where you just copy notes. |
| 4 | 那 就是 好奇心 和 思考 技能 真正 发展 的 方式。 | That's how curiosity and thinking skills really develop. |

> Well, I think it's less about memorising facts and more about how they're taught. A good school pushes kids to ask why, work in groups, and solve real problems. For instance, a science class where you actually run experiments beats one where you just copy notes. That's how curiosity and thinking skills really develop.

**Q3. Why are some people well-rounded and others only good at one thing?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 它 大多 归结到 兴趣 和 选择。 | I'd say it mostly comes down to interests and choices. |
| 2 | 有些人 保持 好奇、对 一大堆 领域，所以 他们 把 他们的 精力 铺 得 宽。 | Some people stay curious about loads of areas, so they spread their energy wide. |
| 3 | 另一些 早早 找到 一个 热情、然后 把 一切 倾 进 它。 | Others find one passion early and pour everything into it. |
| 4 | 两者 都 不 错，真的——它 只是 性格。此外，有些 职业 奖励 深度 专家，而 另一些 需要 人、（那种 能 兼顾 许多 角色的）。 | Neither's wrong, really — it's just personality. Plus, some careers reward deep specialists, while others need people who can juggle many roles. |

> I'd say it mostly comes down to interests and choices. Some people stay curious about loads of areas, so they spread their energy wide. Others find one passion early and pour everything into it. Neither's wrong, really — it's just personality. Plus, some careers reward deep specialists, while others need people who can juggle many roles.

**Q4. Why does modern society need talents of all kinds?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 因为 没有 单一的 技能 让 一切 运转，真的。 | Because no single skill keeps everything running, really. |
| 2 | 一座 城市 需要 医生、建筑工、老师、艺术家——你 说得出的 都要。 | A city needs doctors, builders, teachers, artists — you name it. |
| 3 | 如果 每个人 都 追 同一份 工作，整片 区域 会 就 崩溃。 | If everyone chased the same job, whole areas would just collapse. |
| 4 | 比如，出色的 工程师 仍然 需要 农民 [去]喂 他们。所以 一个 健康的 社会 说实话 依赖 一个 宽的 混合、各种 才能、全 一起 工作。 | For instance, brilliant engineers still need farmers to feed them. So a healthy society honestly depends on a wide mix of talents all working together. |

> Because no single skill keeps everything running, really. A city needs doctors, builders, teachers, artists — you name it. If everyone chased the same job, whole areas would just collapse. For instance, brilliant engineers still need farmers to feed them. So a healthy society honestly depends on a wide mix of talents all working together.

**Q5. Do you think smart children are happier than other children?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 不 一定，我会说。 | Not necessarily, I'd say. |
| 2 | 聪明 其实 能 堆 上 压力，因为 父母 和 老师 期待 更多、从 他们。 | Being smart can actually pile on pressure, because parents and teachers expect more from them. |
| 3 | 有些 聪明的 孩子 也 感觉 有点 孤独，因为 他们 不 总是 和 同学 合拍。 | Some bright kids feel a bit lonely too, since they don't always click with classmates. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，快乐 远远 更 依赖 友谊 和 感到 被 支持、比 一个人 恰好 有 多 聪明。 | To be fair, happiness depends way more on friendships and feeling supported than on how clever someone happens to be. |

> Not necessarily, I'd say. Being smart can actually pile on pressure, because parents and teachers expect more from them. Some bright kids feel a bit lonely too, since they don't always click with classmates. To be fair, happiness depends way more on friendships and feeling supported than on how clever someone happens to be.

**Q6. Is it important for schools to identify and develop each student's talents?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，我觉得 它 是 他们 关键 工作 之一。 | Definitely, I reckon it's one of their key jobs. |
| 2 | 每个 孩子 擅长 某种 不同的东西，而 如果 学校 只 追 考试 分数，一大堆 才能 从 缝隙 溜走。 | Every kid's good at something different, and if schools only chase exam marks, loads of talent slips through the cracks. |
| 3 | 比如，一个孩子、（那种 数学 挣扎的）可能 在 音乐 或 体育上 出色。 | For instance, a child who struggles with maths might be brilliant at music or sport. |
| 4 | 早早 发现 那个 给 他们 自信 和 一个 真正的 方向。 | Spotting that early gives them confidence and a real direction. |

> Definitely, I reckon it's one of their key jobs. Every kid's good at something different, and if schools only chase exam marks, loads of talent slips through the cracks. For instance, a child who struggles with maths might be brilliant at music or sport. Spotting that early gives them confidence and a real direction.

**P3 句型/模板**：`it's a bit of both` · `how they're raised and what they're exposed to`（embedded ×2）· `curious people who ask questions`（who后置）· `nature gives a start, but learning shapes the rest` · `less about memorising facts and more about how they're taught` · `a science class where you actually run experiments beats one where you just copy notes`（where后置 ×2）· `That's how … really develop`（embedded）· `spread their energy wide` / `pour everything into it` · `people who can juggle many roles`（who后置）· `no single skill keeps everything running` / `you name it` · `whole areas would just collapse` · `a wide mix of talents all working together` · `Being smart can actually pile on pressure`（动名词主语）· `don't always click with classmates` · `how clever someone happens to be`（embedded）· `slips through the cracks` · `a child who struggles with maths`（who后置）· `Spotting that early gives them confidence`（动名词主语）。

---

## P2-老14 · A friend who learned something without a teacher — zhangwei 自学 ML（Person）

> **Cue**: 是谁 / 学了什么 / 为何学 / 有老师会不会更容易 · 题型 Person

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 我想聊的那个朋友 是 一个 大学的 老哥们儿，zhangwei。 | The friend I'd like to talk about is an old mate from university, zhangwei. |
| [铺垫] | 我们 认识 彼此 快 二十年了，而 他 做 一个 开发者。 | We've known each other for almost twenty years, and he works as a developer. |
| [what] | 他 自学的 是 机器学习，基本上 整个 AI 那 一面。 | What he taught himself was machine learning, basically the whole AI side of things. |
| [why] | 他 公司 突然 决定 转向 AI，而 那问题 是，他 有 零 背景、在 它上面。 | His company suddenly decided to pivot towards AI, and the problem was, he had zero background in it. |
| [how] | 与其 干等 一个 培训 课程，他 就 一头 扎 进 它、从零 学。 | Instead of waiting around for a training course, he just threw himself into it and learned from scratch. |
| [how·细] | 他 花 大约 六个月 在 它上面，做 在线 课程、读 论文、还 建 副项目、下班后。 | He spent roughly six months at it, working through online courses, reading papers and building side projects after work. |
| [结果] | 有 一大堆 死胡同，但 他 慢慢 弄懂 它，而 它 真的 有回报。 | There were loads of dead ends, but he slowly got his head around it, and it really paid off. |
| [评价·提问] | 一个 老师 会 让 它 更容易 吗？说实话，大概 会——一个 老师 把 你 指 向 那 正确的 方向、还 救 你 脱离 死胡同。 | Would a teacher have made it easier? Honestly, probably yes — a teacher points you in the right direction and saves you from dead ends. |
| [评价·反面] | 但 用 那 艰难 的方式 自学 逼 他 [去]深深 理解 它，而 它 建 了 真正的 韧性。 | But teaching himself the hard way forced him to understand it deeply, and it built real grit. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，我会说 两者 都 有 它们的 价值。 | So yeah, I'd say both have their value. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> The friend I'd like to talk about is an old mate from university, zhangwei. We've known each other for almost twenty years, and he works as a developer. What he taught himself was machine learning, basically the whole AI side of things. His company suddenly decided to pivot towards AI, and the problem was, he had zero background in it. Instead of waiting around for a training course, he just threw himself into it and learned from scratch. He spent roughly six months at it, working through online courses, reading papers and building side projects after work. There were loads of dead ends, but he slowly got his head around it, and it really paid off. Would a teacher have made it easier? Honestly, probably yes — a teacher points you in the right direction and saves you from dead ends. But teaching himself the hard way forced him to understand it deeply, and it built real grit. So yeah, I'd say both have their value.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **what 主语**：他自学的是机器学习 → `What he taught himself was machine learning`
- **Instead of + 动名词 + 一头扎进**：与其干等培训课，他一头扎进、从零学 → `Instead of waiting around for a training course, he just threw himself into it and learned from scratch`
- **三个 -ing 伴随**：做在线课程、读论文、建副项目 → `working through online courses, reading papers and building side projects`
- **got his head around it / it really paid off**：慢慢弄懂、真的有回报
- **修辞问句 + 反思答**：有老师会更容易吗？说实话大概会 → `Would a teacher have made it easier? Honestly, probably yes`
- **动名词主语（反面）**：用艰难方式自学逼他深深理解 → `teaching himself the hard way forced him to understand it deeply`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Is it necessary to keep learning after graduating from school?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对。 | Absolutely. |
| 2 | 我会说 学校 只 给 你 那 基础，而 那世界 变 得 这么快、以至于 你 学过的 能 很快 过时。 | I'd say school only gives you the basics, and the world keeps changing so fast that what you learned can go out of date quickly. |
| 3 | 比如，新 科技 每年 冒 出来，所以 你 得 一直 捡 起 东西、只 为了 保持 相关。 | For instance, new tech pops up every year, so you've got to keep picking things up just to stay relevant. |
| 4 | 说实话，你 停止 学习 的那天 就是 你 落后 的那天。 | Honestly, the day you stop learning is the day you fall behind. |

> Absolutely. I'd say school only gives you the basics, and the world keeps changing so fast that what you learned can go out of date quickly. For instance, new tech pops up every year, so you've got to keep picking things up just to stay relevant. Honestly, the day you stop learning is the day you fall behind.

**Q2. Should teachers make learning in their classes fun?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，在 合理范围内。 | Definitely, within reason. |
| 2 | 当 一节课 好玩，孩子 真的 集中 注意、而 那东西 黏 得 远远 更好、比 枯燥的 讲课。 | When a class is fun, kids actually pay attention and the stuff sticks far better than dry lectures do. |
| 3 | 比如，一个 老师、（那种 用 游戏 或 现实 例子的）得到 多得多的 参与。 | For example, a teacher who uses games or real-life examples gets way more engagement. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，它 不该 变成 纯 娱乐，因为 学生 仍然 需要 抓住 那 严肃的 内容、在 这一切 底下。 | Having said that, it shouldn't turn into pure entertainment, because students still need to grasp the serious content underneath it all. |

> Definitely, within reason. When a class is fun, kids actually pay attention and the stuff sticks far better than dry lectures do. For example, a teacher who uses games or real-life examples gets way more engagement. Having said that, it shouldn't turn into pure entertainment, because students still need to grasp the serious content underneath it all.

**Q3. Do you think there are too many subjects for students to learn?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说句公道话，我 觉得 可能 有，尤其 在 高中。 | To be fair, I think there can be, especially in high school. |
| 2 | 孩子 同时 兼顾 八九门 学科，所以 他们 最后 绷得 太紧、压力 山大。 | Kids juggle eight or nine subjects at once, so they end up stretched thin and stressed out. |
| 3 | 比如，为 那么多 考试 死记硬背 留 不 出 空间 [去]真正 享受 学习。 | For example, cramming for that many exams leaves no room to actually enjoy learning. |
| 4 | 我 宁愿 看到 更少的 学科 被 好好 教，也不 一大堆 匆匆 覆盖。 | I'd rather see fewer subjects taught properly than loads covered in a rush. |

> To be fair, I think there can be, especially in high school. Kids juggle eight or nine subjects at once, so they end up stretched thin and stressed out. For example, cramming for that many exams leaves no room to actually enjoy learning. I'd rather see fewer subjects taught properly than loads covered in a rush.

**Q4. Is it better to focus on a few subjects or to learn many subjects?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它 取决于 那 阶段。 | Honestly, it depends on the stage. |
| 2 | 早期，一个 宽的 混合 帮 你 弄清 你 实际上 享受 什么，所以 我 会 倾向 多样性、在那里。 | Early on, a broad mix helps you figure out what you actually enjoy, so I'd lean towards variety there. |
| 3 | 但 后来，一旦 你 找到 你的 那个东西，缩窄 更 有道理，因为 真正的 专长 需要 深度。 | But later, once you've found your thing, narrowing down makes more sense because real expertise needs depth. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 从 宽 开始，然后 逐渐 聚焦、随着 你 走。 | So I'd say start wide, then gradually focus as you go. |

> Honestly, it depends on the stage. Early on, a broad mix helps you figure out what you actually enjoy, so I'd lean towards variety there. But later, once you've found your thing, narrowing down makes more sense because real expertise needs depth. So I'd say start wide, then gradually focus as you go.

**Q5. Do you think enterprises should provide training for their employees?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 当然。 | For sure. |
| 2 | 我觉得 它 是 一个 双赢，因为 训练好的 员工 做 更好的 工作、而 公司 从 他们 得到 更多。 | I reckon it's a win-win, since well-trained staff do better work and the company gets more out of them. |
| 3 | 拿 一个 公司、（转向 新 科技的）来说——没有 培训，员工 就 手足无措。 | Take a firm shifting towards new tech — without training, employees just flounder. |
| 4 | 此外，它 让 人 感到 被 重视，所以 他们 远远 更 可能 留 下来、而不是 跳槽。 | Plus, it makes people feel valued, so they're far more likely to stick around rather than jump ship. |

> For sure. I reckon it's a win-win, since well-trained staff do better work and the company gets more out of them. Take a firm shifting towards new tech — without training, employees just flounder. Plus, it makes people feel valued, so they're far more likely to stick around rather than jump ship.

**Q6. Do you think it is good for older adults to continue learning?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，它 对 他们 很棒。 | Definitely, it's brilliant for them. |
| 2 | 捡 起 一个 新 爱好 或 技能 保持 那 头脑 敏锐、还 挡开 那种 被 卡住 的 感觉。 | Picking up a new hobby or skill keeps the mind sharp and wards off that feeling of being stuck. |
| 3 | 比如，我的 祖父母 退休后 开始 画画，而 它 给 了 他们 一个 真正的 目的感。 | For instance, my grandparents took up painting after retiring, and it gave them a real sense of purpose. |
| 4 | 此外，它 是 一个 可爱的 方式、[去]认识 人、保持 社交 活跃。 | Plus, it's a lovely way to meet people and stay socially active. |

> Definitely, it's brilliant for them. Picking up a new hobby or skill keeps the mind sharp and wards off that feeling of being stuck. For instance, my grandparents took up painting after retiring, and it gave them a real sense of purpose. Plus, it's a lovely way to meet people and stay socially active.

**P3 句型/模板**：`the world keeps changing so fast that what you learned can go out of date`（so…that + what主语）· `new tech pops up every year` / `keep picking things up just to stay relevant` · `the day you stop learning is the day you fall behind` · `the stuff sticks far better than dry lectures do` · `a teacher who uses games or real-life examples`（who后置）· `grasp the serious content underneath it all` · `juggle eight or nine subjects at once` / `stretched thin and stressed out` · `cramming for that many exams leaves no room to actually enjoy learning`（动名词主语）· `fewer subjects taught properly than loads covered in a rush` · `helps you figure out what you actually enjoy`（embedded）· `narrowing down makes more sense`（动名词主语）· `start wide, then gradually focus as you go` · `well-trained staff do better work` · `a firm shifting towards new tech`（分词后置）/ `employees just flounder` · `stick around rather than jump ship` · `Picking up a new hobby or skill keeps the mind sharp`（动名词主语）/ `wards off that feeling of being stuck` · `a real sense of purpose`。

## P2-老15 · An event where you didn't enjoy the music — 太吵的现场演出（Event）

> **Cue**: 是什么 / 和谁去 / 为何去 / 为何不享受 · 题型 Event

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个 活动 是 一场 吵闹的 现场 音乐会、（我 去年 去的），那些 摇滚 和 电子 演出 之一、在 一个 挤满的 小场地、就 在 成都 这儿。 | So, the event I'd like to talk about is a loud live music gig I went to last year, one of those rock and electronic shows in a packed little venue here in Chengdu. |
| [who] | 我 跟 我的 一个 老朋友 一起 去。 | I went with an old friend of mine. |
| [why go] | 他 弄到 几张 免费 票、不想 自己 去，所以 说实话 我 就 跟着去 图 个 合群。 | He'd got a couple of free tickets and didn't want to go on his own, so honestly I just tagged along to be sociable. |
| [why·细] | 我妻子 明智地 待 家里，而 我 去 主要 好让 我 不 让 他 失望。 | My wife wisely stayed home, and I went mainly so I wouldn't let him down. |
| [why disliked] | 那问题 是，它 真的 不是 我的 菜。 | The problem was, it really wasn't my scene. |
| [细] | 我 更 喜欢 安静的、原声的 东西，但 那 音乐 从 一开始 就 痛苦地 吵。 | I'm more into quiet, acoustic stuff, but the music was painfully loud from the start. |
| [细2] | 比如，我 听 不见 我 自己 想，更别提 跟 他 聊，而 到 结尾 我的 耳朵 在 嗡嗡响、我 有 一个 撕裂般的 头疼。 | For example, I couldn't hear myself think, let alone chat with him, and by the end my ears were ringing and I had a splitting headache. |
| [EL] | 回头看，我 只 得 咬牙 忍着。 | Looking back, I just had to grin and bear it. |
| [EL·细] | 我是说，我 大概 老了，但 一个 平静的 晚上、在家 更 适合 我。 | I mean, I'm probably getting old, but a calm evening at home suits me far better. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，差不多 就是 这样。 | So yeah, that's pretty much it. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the event I'd like to talk about is a loud live music gig I went to last year, one of those rock and electronic shows in a packed little venue here in Chengdu. I went with an old friend of mine. He'd got a couple of free tickets and didn't want to go on his own, so honestly I just tagged along to be sociable. My wife wisely stayed home, and I went mainly so I wouldn't let him down. The problem was, it really wasn't my scene. I'm more into quiet, acoustic stuff, but the music was painfully loud from the start. For example, I couldn't hear myself think, let alone chat with him, and by the end my ears were ringing and I had a splitting headache. Looking back, I just had to grin and bear it. I mean, I'm probably getting old, but a calm evening at home suits me far better. So yeah, that's pretty much it.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **event opener + relative后置 + one of those …**：我想聊的活动是一场去年去的吵闹现场，那些摇滚电子演出之一 → `a loud live music gig I went to last year, one of those … shows in a packed little venue`
- **跟着去图合群**：我就跟着去图个合群 → `I just tagged along to be sociable`
- **目的从句**：我去主要好让我不让他失望 → `I went mainly so I wouldn't let him down`
- **不是我的菜**：它真的不是我的菜 → `it really wasn't my scene`
- **let alone**：我听不见自己想，更别提跟他聊 → `I couldn't hear myself think, let alone chat with him`
- **咬牙忍着**：我只得咬牙忍着 → `I just had to grin and bear it`
- **更适合我**：一个平静的晚上在家更适合我 → `a calm evening at home suits me far better`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What kind of music events do people like today?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，这些天 我会说 音乐节 极其 受欢迎——大 户外的、（那里 一大堆 乐队 演 一整个 周末）。 | Well, these days I'd say festivals are massively popular — big outdoor ones where loads of bands play across a weekend. |
| 2 | 人们 爱 那种 兴奋、（在 人群里、全 一起 跟着 唱的）。 | People love the buzz of being in a crowd, all singing along together. |
| 3 | 流媒体 也 巨大，但 它 真的 匹配 不了 那 现场 能量。 | Streaming's huge too, but it can't really match that live energy. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，更小的 原声 演出、在 咖啡馆 也 在 卷土重来。 | To be fair, smaller acoustic gigs in cafes are making a comeback as well. |

> Well, these days I'd say festivals are massively popular — big outdoor ones where loads of bands play across a weekend. People love the buzz of being in a crowd, all singing along together. Streaming's huge too, but it can't really match that live energy. To be fair, smaller acoustic gigs in cafes are making a comeback as well.

**Q2. Do you think children should receive some musical education?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对。 | Definitely. |
| 2 | 我觉得 学 一件 乐器 教 孩子 耐心 和 自律，因为 你 没有 每日 练习 变 不 好。 | I reckon learning an instrument teaches kids patience and discipline, since you can't get good without daily practice. |
| 3 | 它 也 给 他们 一个 创意的 出口、远离 屏幕。 | It also gives them a creative outlet away from screens. |
| 4 | 比如，一个孩子、（那种 弹 钢琴的）学会 读 谱 和 专注。此外，它 是 一个 可爱的 技能、（那种 会 陪 他们 一辈子的）。 | For instance, a child who plays piano learns to read music and concentrate. Plus, it's a lovely skill that'll stay with them for life. |

> Definitely. I reckon learning an instrument teaches kids patience and discipline, since you can't get good without daily practice. It also gives them a creative outlet away from screens. For instance, a child who plays piano learns to read music and concentrate. Plus, it's a lovely skill that'll stay with them for life.

**Q3. What are the differences between old and young people's music preferences?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，它们 挺 不同。 | Honestly, they're quite different. |
| 2 | 年轻人 倾向于 追 无论 什么 正 流行的——流行、说唱、那些 最新的 热门、（大家 都在 播的）。 | Younger folk tend to chase whatever's trending — pop, rap, the latest hits everyone's streaming. |
| 3 | 老年人 通常 坚持 那些 经典、（他们 伴随 长大的），比如 摇滚 或 民谣。 | Older people usually stick with the classics they grew up with, like rock or folk. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，现在 有 很多 交叉，因为 老 曲子 一直 被 采样、被 年轻人 重新 发现。 | That said, there's a lot of crossover now, since older tunes keep getting sampled and rediscovered by the young. |

> Honestly, they're quite different. Younger folk tend to chase whatever's trending — pop, rap, the latest hits everyone's streaming. Older people usually stick with the classics they grew up with, like rock or folk. That said, there's a lot of crossover now, since older tunes keep getting sampled and rediscovered by the young.

**Q4. What kind of music events are there in your country?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 相当 几个，其实。 | Quite a few, actually. |
| 2 | 在 更大的 城市 有 演唱会、音乐节、和 很多 小 livehouse 演出、为 独立 乐队。 | In bigger cities there're concerts, music festivals, and plenty of small live-house gigs for indie bands. |
| 3 | 传统 戏曲 演出 也 仍然 吸引 更年长的 人群。 | Traditional opera shows still draw older crowds too. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，那 圈子 最近 长 了 很多，因为 更多 年轻人 热衷于 看 演出 现场 表演、而不是 只 播放 它们。 | To be fair, the scene's grown a lot lately, since more young people are keen to see acts perform live rather than just streaming them. |

> Quite a few, actually. In bigger cities there're concerts, music festivals, and plenty of small live-house gigs for indie bands. Traditional opera shows still draw older crowds too. To be fair, the scene's grown a lot lately, since more young people are keen to see acts perform live rather than just streaming them.

**Q5. Why do many people like listening to music while doing sports?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 音乐 让 你 保持 有动力、当 你 在 锻炼。 | Well, I'd say music keeps you motivated when you're working out. |
| 2 | 一个 强的 节拍 有点 推 你 [去]动 得 更快、还 泵 起 你的 能量。 | A strong beat sort of pushes you to move faster and pumps up your energy. |
| 3 | 它 也 把 你 从 感到 累 分神开，所以 一场 艰难的 跑 感觉 更容易。 | It also distracts you from feeling tired, so a tough run feels easier. |
| 4 | 比如，一大堆 跑者 发誓 他们 撑 不住、没有 一个 好的 欢快 播放列表 放着。 | For instance, plenty of runners swear they can't last without a good upbeat playlist going. |

> Well, I'd say music keeps you motivated when you're working out. A strong beat sort of pushes you to move faster and pumps up your energy. It also distracts you from feeling tired, so a tough run feels easier. For instance, plenty of runners swear they can't last without a good upbeat playlist going.

**Q6. What are the differences between listening to music at home and at a live concert?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它们 天壤之别，说实话。 | They're worlds apart, honestly. |
| 2 | 在家 它 放松、私密——你 控制 那 音量、还能 重放 你 最爱的 曲目。 | At home it's relaxed and private — you control the volume and can replay your favourite track. |
| 3 | 一场 现场 演唱会 全 关于 那 氛围、那 人群、和 那 原始的 能量、（那种 你 就是 在 室内 重现 不了的）。 | A live concert's all about the atmosphere, the crowd, and that raw energy you simply can't recreate indoors. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，在家 听 便宜 得多、而 你 不必 挤 过 巨大的 人群。 | Having said that, home listening's far cheaper and you don't have to fight through massive crowds. |

> They're worlds apart, honestly. At home it's relaxed and private — you control the volume and can replay your favourite track. A live concert's all about the atmosphere, the crowd, and that raw energy you simply can't recreate indoors. Having said that, home listening's far cheaper and you don't have to fight through massive crowds.

**P3 句型/模板**：`big outdoor ones where loads of bands play across a weekend`（where后置）· `the buzz of being in a crowd, all singing along together` · `can't really match that live energy` · `making a comeback` · `learning an instrument teaches kids patience and discipline`（动名词主语）· `a creative outlet away from screens` · `a child who plays piano`（who后置）/ `a lovely skill that'll stay with them for life`（that后置）· `whatever's trending` / `the latest hits everyone's streaming`（relative后置）· `the classics they grew up with`（relative后置）· `keep getting sampled and rediscovered by the young` · `there're concerts, music festivals …`（dummy-there）· `draw older crowds` · `keen to see acts perform live rather than just streaming them` · `keeps you motivated when you're working out` · `pushes you to move faster and pumps up your energy` · `distracts you from feeling tired` · `swear they can't last without a good upbeat playlist going` · `worlds apart` · `that raw energy you simply can't recreate indoors`（relative后置）· `home listening's far cheaper`（动名词主语）/ `fight through massive crowds`。

## P2-老16 · A movie you watched and enjoyed recently — 科幻片（Object）

> **Cue**: 何时何地看 / 和谁看 / 讲什么 / 为何看 · 题型 Object

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那部 电影 是 一部 科幻 电影、（我 最近 看的）——一个 近未来的 太空 故事、（那种 真的 留 在 我 心里的）。 | So, the film I'd like to talk about is a science-fiction movie I caught recently — a near-future space story that really stuck with me. |
| [when/where] | 我 就 上 周末 看的，在家 沙发上，流媒体 播 它、在 我儿子 Muye 已经 上床 之后。 | I watched it just last weekend, at home on the sofa, streaming it after my son Muye had gone to bed. |
| [who] | 我 跟 我妻子 一起 看，（那 很好）——我们 俩、灯 关着、一碗 零食。 | I watched it with my wife, which was nice — the two of us with the lights off and a bowl of snacks. |
| [about] | 基本上，它 跟着 一小队 宇航员、在 一次 深空 任务，而 在 它 的核心 它 是 关于 他们 之一，一个 父亲，拼命 试着 找 一条 回家 的路、回到 他家人。 | Basically, it follows a small crew of astronauts on a deep-space mission, and at its heart it's about one of them, a father, desperately trying to find a way back home to his family. |
| [why] | 我 看 它 因为 我 是 一个 巨大的 科幻 粉丝，加上 一个 亲密的朋友 推荐了 它，而 我 爱 电影、（那种 真的 让 我 思考的）。 | I watched it because I'm a huge sci-fi fan, plus a close friend had recommended it, and I love films that actually make me think. |
| [EL] | 不过，真正 打动 我的 不 只是 那 惊艳的 画面——是 那 情感的 分量。 | What got me, though, wasn't just the stunning visuals — it was the emotional weight. |
| [EL·细] | 比如，那 结尾 是 一记 真正的 重拳。 | For example, the ending was a real gut-punch. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，它 震撼 了 我，而 我 好几天 停 不 下 想 它。 | So yeah, it blew me away, and I couldn't stop thinking about it for days. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the film I'd like to talk about is a science-fiction movie I caught recently — a near-future space story that really stuck with me. I watched it just last weekend, at home on the sofa, streaming it after my son Muye had gone to bed. I watched it with my wife, which was nice — the two of us with the lights off and a bowl of snacks. Basically, it follows a small crew of astronauts on a deep-space mission, and at its heart it's about one of them, a father, desperately trying to find a way back home to his family. I watched it because I'm a huge sci-fi fan, plus a close friend had recommended it, and I love films that actually make me think. What got me, though, wasn't just the stunning visuals — it was the emotional weight. For example, the ending was a real gut-punch. So yeah, it blew me away, and I couldn't stop thinking about it for days.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **film opener + relative后置 + that后置**：一部我最近看的科幻片——一个真留在我心里的近未来太空故事 → `a science-fiction movie I caught recently — a near-future space story that really stuck with me`
- **过去完成**：流媒体播它，在我儿子已经上床之后 → `streaming it after my son Muye had gone to bed`
- **which 后置评论**：我跟妻子一起看，那很好 → `I watched it with my wife, which was nice`
- **同位 + 分词**：关于他们之一，一个父亲，拼命试着找回家的路 → `about one of them, a father, desperately trying to find a way back home`
- **that后置**：我爱那种真让我思考的电影 → `films that actually make me think`
- **what 主语强调**：真正打动我的不只是画面——是情感的分量 → `What got me … wasn't just the stunning visuals — it was the emotional weight`
- **金句**：一记真正的重拳 / 震撼了我 / 好几天停不下想它 → `a real gut-punch` / `it blew me away` / `couldn't stop thinking about it for days`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What kinds of movies do you think are successful in your country?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 喜剧 和 大 动作 大片 倾向于 在这里 表现 很好，主要 因为 人们 想要 一个 逃离、在 漫长的 一天 之后。 | I'd say comedies and big action blockbusters tend to do really well here, mainly because people want an escape after a long day. |
| 2 | 它们 容易 享受、不 对 你 要求 太多。 | They're easy to enjoy and don't ask too much of you. |
| 3 | 比如，一部 好笑的 假日 上映 能 挤爆 影院。 | For example, a funny holiday release can pack out cinemas. |
| 4 | 话虽如此，扣人心弦的 历史 剧 也 能 真的 火。 | That said, gripping historical dramas can really take off too. |

> I'd say comedies and big action blockbusters tend to do really well here, mainly because people want an escape after a long day. They're easy to enjoy and don't ask too much of you. For example, a funny holiday release can pack out cinemas. That said, gripping historical dramas can really take off too.

**Q2. What are the factors that make a successful movie?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 对我来说，它 大多是 一个 强的 故事——如果 那 情节 抓 住 你、而 那些 角色 感觉 真实，你 会 原谅 几乎 任何 别的。 | For me, it's mostly a strong story — if the plot grips you and the characters feel real, you'll forgive almost anything else. |
| 2 | 像样的 节奏 也 要紧，因为 一部 电影、（那种 拖沓的）很快 失去 人。 | Decent pacing matters too, since a film that drags loses people fast. |
| 3 | 比如，甚至 一部 低成本 电影 能 靠 犀利的 写作 发光。 | For instance, even a low-budget movie can shine with sharp writing. |
| 4 | 营销 有帮助，但 它 救 不了 一个 沉闷的 剧本。 | Marketing helps, but it can't save a dull script. |

> For me, it's mostly a strong story — if the plot grips you and the characters feel real, you'll forgive almost anything else. Decent pacing matters too, since a film that drags loses people fast. For instance, even a low-budget movie can shine with sharp writing. Marketing helps, but it can't save a dull script.

**Q3. Do Chinese people prefer to watch domestic movies or foreign movies?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，这些天 它 是 一个 真正的 混合。 | Honestly, it's a real mix these days. |
| 2 | 一大堆 年轻人 仍然 蜂拥 去 好莱坞 大片、为 那 视觉 奇观，但 国产 电影 已经 大大 进步。 | A lot of younger folk still flock to Hollywood blockbusters for the spectacle, but domestic films have come on hugely. |
| 3 | 它们 讲 故事、（那种 击 得 更贴近 家的），所以 观众 更 有共鸣。 | They tell stories that hit closer to home, so audiences relate more. |
| 4 | 比如，本土 喜剧 和 节日 上映 现在 打破 票房 纪录，（那 以前 并不 真的 是 那样）。 | For example, local comedies and festival releases now smash box-office records, which wasn't really the case before. |

> Honestly, it's a real mix these days. A lot of younger folk still flock to Hollywood blockbusters for the spectacle, but domestic films have come on hugely. They tell stories that hit closer to home, so audiences relate more. For example, local comedies and festival releases now smash box-office records, which wasn't really the case before.

**Q4. Do you think only well-known directors can create the best movies?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 一点也不，说实话。 | Not at all, honestly. |
| 2 | 一个 有名的 名字 帮 吸引 一群人、还 拿到 资金，但 才华 不 系于 名气。 | A famous name helps draw a crowd and secure funding, but talent isn't tied to fame. |
| 3 | 一大堆 新人 已经 做出 惊艳的 处女作、（那种 完全 盖过 那些 大导演的）。 | Plenty of newcomers have made stunning debuts that completely outshone the big directors. |
| 4 | 比如，一个 新鲜的 视角 常常 感觉 更 大胆、更 原创。所以我会说 热情 和 一个 清晰的 愿景 要紧 得 远远 多过 一个 名声。 | For instance, a fresh perspective often feels bolder and more original. So I'd say passion and a clear vision matter far more than a reputation. |

> Not at all, honestly. A famous name helps draw a crowd and secure funding, but talent isn't tied to fame. Plenty of newcomers have made stunning debuts that completely outshone the big directors. For instance, a fresh perspective often feels bolder and more original. So I'd say passion and a clear vision matter far more than a reputation.

**Q5. Do you think successful movies should have well-known actors or actresses in leading roles?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 不 一定，虽然 它 肯定 有帮助。 | Not necessarily, though it definitely helps. |
| 2 | 一个 有名的 面孔 把 人 拉 进来、还 安抚 投资者，所以 那 电影 得到 一个 更大的 预算。 | A famous face pulls people in and reassures investors, so the film gets a bigger budget. |
| 3 | 但 一个 明星 救 不了 一个 弱的 剧本。有时候 不知名的 演员 其实 感觉 更 可信，因为 你 不 想到 他们的 其他 角色。 | But a star can't rescue a weak script. Sometimes unknown actors actually feel more believable, since you don't picture their other roles. |
| 4 | 对我来说，那 写作 和 导演 顶 得 多得多、比 明星 效应。 | For me, the writing and directing count for much more than star power. |

> Not necessarily, though it definitely helps. A famous face pulls people in and reassures investors, so the film gets a bigger budget. But a star can't rescue a weak script. Sometimes unknown actors actually feel more believable, since you don't picture their other roles. For me, the writing and directing count for much more than star power.

**Q6. Why do people prefer to watch movies in the cinema?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，对我 全 关于 那 体验。 | Well, it's all about the experience for me. |
| 2 | 那 巨大的 屏幕、轰鸣的 声音、和 黑暗的 房间 把 你 拉 进 那 故事、以 一种 方式、（你的 客厅 永远 做不到的）。 | The huge screen, booming sound and dark room pull you right into the story in a way your living room never could. |
| 3 | 此外，有 某种 特别的东西、关于 笑 或 倒吸气、和 一整群人 一起。 | Plus, there's something special about laughing or gasping alongside a whole crowd. |
| 4 | 比如，一部 恐怖片 感觉 两倍 那么 吓人、被 陌生人 包围着。 | For instance, a horror film feels twice as scary surrounded by strangers. |

> Well, it's all about the experience for me. The huge screen, booming sound and dark room pull you right into the story in a way your living room never could. Plus, there's something special about laughing or gasping alongside a whole crowd. For instance, a horror film feels twice as scary surrounded by strangers.

**P3 句型/模板**：`do really well here` / `want an escape after a long day` · `don't ask too much of you` · `pack out cinemas` · `gripping historical dramas can really take off` · `if the plot grips you and the characters feel real` · `a film that drags loses people fast`（that后置）· `shine with sharp writing` / `can't save a dull script` · `flock to Hollywood blockbusters for the spectacle` / `come on hugely` · `stories that hit closer to home`（that后置）· `smash box-office records, which wasn't really the case before`（which后置）· `draw a crowd and secure funding` / `talent isn't tied to fame` · `stunning debuts that completely outshone the big directors`（that后置）· `passion and a clear vision matter far more than a reputation` · `pulls people in and reassures investors` · `can't rescue a weak script` / `you don't picture their other roles` · `count for much more than star power` · `pull you right into the story in a way your living room never could`（relative后置）· `there's something special about laughing or gasping alongside a whole crowd` · `feels twice as scary surrounded by strangers`。

## P2-老17 · An interesting building — 成都环球中心（室内海滩）（Place）

> **Cue**: 在哪 / 长什么样 / 什么功能 / 为何有趣 · 题型 Place

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那座 建筑 是 新世纪 环球中心，就 在 成都 这儿、（我 住的地方）。 | So, the building I'd like to talk about is the New Century Global Center, right here in Chengdu where I live. |
| [背景] | 据说 它 是 地球上 最大的 建筑 之一、按 建筑面积。 | It's said to be one of the biggest buildings on earth by floor area. |
| [look] | 从 外面 它 是 这个 巨大的、波浪形的 结构，全 玻璃 和 钢，而 说实话 你 没法 一次 把 它 全 收 进眼里。 | From the outside it's this vast, wave-shaped structure, all glass and steel, and honestly you can't take it all in at once. |
| [look·细] | 它 那么 巨大、你 永远 塞 不 进 一张照片，而 它 那 纯粹的 规模 令人 惊掉下巴。 | It's so enormous you'd never fit it into one photo, and the sheer scale of it is jaw-dropping. |
| [function] | 它 是 一个 混合用途的 地方，所以 里面 什么都有，比如 商场、办公室、和 酒店。 | It's a mixed-use place, so there's everything inside, like malls, offices and hotels. |
| [function·细] | 但 那 真正的 亮点，藏 在 中间，是 一个 巨大的 室内 水上乐园、带 一个 假 海滩、一个 巨型 LED 天空、和 人造 阳光。 | But the real draw, tucked away in the middle, is a huge indoor water park with a fake beach, a giant LED sky and artificial sunshine. |
| [细] | 我妻子 和 我 带 我们的 小男孩 Muye 去 那儿 一次，而 他 绝对 爱 扑腾 玩水。 | My wife and I took our little boy Muye there once and he absolutely loved splashing about. |
| [why/EL] | 让 它 这么 有趣的 是 那个 超现实的 想法、（一个 阳光的 海滩、在 室内，不管 晴雨，在 一座城市 中间）。 | What makes it so interesting is the surreal idea of a sunny beach indoors, rain or shine, in the middle of a city. |
| [closer] | 总的来说，它 是 一个 自成一体的 世界。 | All in all, it's a world of its own. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the building I'd like to talk about is the New Century Global Center, right here in Chengdu where I live. It's said to be one of the biggest buildings on earth by floor area. From the outside it's this vast, wave-shaped structure, all glass and steel, and honestly you can't take it all in at once. It's so enormous you'd never fit it into one photo, and the sheer scale of it is jaw-dropping. It's a mixed-use place, so there's everything inside, like malls, offices and hotels. But the real draw, tucked away in the middle, is a huge indoor water park with a fake beach, a giant LED sky and artificial sunshine. My wife and I took our little boy Muye there once and he absolutely loved splashing about. What makes it so interesting is the surreal idea of a sunny beach indoors, rain or shine, in the middle of a city. All in all, it's a world of its own.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **building opener + where 后置**：就在成都这儿，我住的地方 → `right here in Chengdu where I live`
- **据说是…之一**：据说它是地球上最大的建筑之一，按建筑面积 → `It's said to be one of the biggest buildings on earth by floor area`
- **一次收进眼里**：你没法一次把它全收进眼里 → `you can't take it all in at once`
- **so…（that省略）+ sheer scale**：那么巨大你塞不进一张照片；纯粹的规模令人惊掉下巴 → `so enormous you'd never fit it into one photo` / `the sheer scale of it is jaw-dropping`
- **分词后置插入**：那真正的亮点，藏在中间，是… → `the real draw, tucked away in the middle, is …`
- **What 主语 + rain or shine**：让它有趣的是室内阳光海滩、不管晴雨 → `What makes it so interesting is … a sunny beach indoors, rain or shine`
- **收尾**：一个自成一体的世界 → `a world of its own`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What types of buildings are popular in your country?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 嗯，我会说 高层 公寓 是 目前 最 受欢迎的，主要 因为 这里的 城市 那么 拥挤、以至于 往上 建 是 唯一 明智的 选项。 | Well, I'd say high-rise apartments are by far the most popular, mainly because cities here are so crowded that building upwards is the only sensible option. |
| 2 | 你 也 看到 一大堆 闪亮的 购物中心 和 混合用途的 塔 在 拔地而起。 | You also see loads of shiny shopping malls and mixed-use towers going up. |
| 3 | 传统的 四合院 还 存在，但 说实话 它们 现在 在 大 城区 变得 更 稀少。 | Traditional courtyard houses still exist, but honestly they're getting rarer in big urban areas now. |

> Well, I'd say high-rise apartments are by far the most popular, mainly because cities here are so crowded that building upwards is the only sensible option. You also see loads of shiny shopping malls and mixed-use towers going up. Traditional courtyard houses still exist, but honestly they're getting rarer in big urban areas now.

**Q2. Is it worth spending a lot of money on the exterior appearance of a building?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它 看情况，真的。 | It depends, really. |
| 2 | 对 地标、（意在 吸引 游客的），我觉得 它 是 花得 值的 钱，因为 一个 醒目的 外观 变成 那城市 的 名片。 | For landmarks meant to attract tourists, I reckon it's money well spent, since a striking exterior becomes the city's calling card. |
| 3 | 但 对 普通 办公楼，在 花哨的 外立面 上 大手花钱 感觉 浪费，当 那 钱 本 能 改善 里面的东西。 | But for ordinary offices, splashing out on fancy facades feels wasteful when that cash could improve what's inside. |
| 4 | 所以我会说 平衡 比 纯 炫耀 更 要紧。 | So I'd say balance matters more than pure showing off. |

> It depends, really. For landmarks meant to attract tourists, I reckon it's money well spent, since a striking exterior becomes the city's calling card. But for ordinary offices, splashing out on fancy facades feels wasteful when that cash could improve what's inside. So I'd say balance matters more than pure showing off.

**Q3. Is it more important for a building to look good on the outside or on the inside?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 说实话，我 倾向 那 内部 更 重要，因为 那 就是 人们 真正 花 他们 时间 的地方。 | Honestly, I lean towards the inside being more important, because that's where people actually spend their time. |
| 2 | 一个 华丽的 外观 在 第一眼 之后 褪色，但 一个 设计好的、实用的 内部 一天天 持续 要紧。 | A gorgeous exterior fades after the first glance, but a well-designed, practical interior keeps mattering day after day. |
| 3 | 话虽如此，一座 建筑 外面 也 真的 该 看起来 像样，好让 第一印象 不 被 毁。 | That said, a building really should look decent outside too, so first impressions aren't ruined. |

> Honestly, I lean towards the inside being more important, because that's where people actually spend their time. A gorgeous exterior fades after the first glance, but a well-designed, practical interior keeps mattering day after day. That said, a building really should look decent outside too, so first impressions aren't ruined.

**Q4. Why do people like to visit historical sites?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我会说 它 大多 关于 跟 过去 连接。 | I'd say it's mostly about connecting with the past. |
| 2 | 历史 遗址 让 你 几乎 回到 过去、还 想象 更早的 世代 实际上 怎么 生活，（那 教科书 不太 能 捕捉）。 | Historical sites let you almost step back in time and picture how earlier generations actually lived, which textbooks can't quite capture. |
| 3 | 此外 有 那 好奇 因素——人们 爱 看 有名的 地方、（那种 他们 只 曾 读到过的）。 | Plus there's the curiosity factor — people love seeing famous places they've only ever read about. |
| 4 | 对我来说，那 敬畏 的 感觉 是 那 真正的 吸引。 | For me, that sense of awe is the real pull. |

> I'd say it's mostly about connecting with the past. Historical sites let you almost step back in time and picture how earlier generations actually lived, which textbooks can't quite capture. Plus there's the curiosity factor — people love seeing famous places they've only ever read about. For me, that sense of awe is the real pull.

**Q5. Do you think it's reasonable to charge an entry fee for visiting interesting buildings?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 觉得 它 完全 公平，说实话。 | I think it's perfectly fair, to be honest. |
| 2 | 维护 宏伟的 老 建筑 花 一大笔，而 门票 帮 覆盖 维修 和 保养、（那种 单靠 税 覆盖不了的）。 | Maintaining grand old buildings costs a fortune, and entry fees help cover repairs and upkeep that taxes alone couldn't. |
| 3 | 但 我 会 主张 那 价格 该 保持 合理，因为 收 太多 会 把 普通 游客 挡 在外面。 | But I'd argue the price should stay reasonable, since charging too much would shut ordinary visitors out. |
| 4 | 一个 适度的 费用 感觉 像 那 正确的 中间地带。 | A modest fee feels like the right middle ground. |

> I think it's perfectly fair, to be honest. Maintaining grand old buildings costs a fortune, and entry fees help cover repairs and upkeep that taxes alone couldn't. But I'd argue the price should stay reasonable, since charging too much would shut ordinary visitors out. A modest fee feels like the right middle ground.

**Q6. Is it better to live in a new building or an old one?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 对我来说，一座 新 建筑 赢，主要 因为 现代的 地方 带 更好的 隔热、电梯、和 像样的 管道、（那种 就是 好用的）。 | For me, a new building wins, mainly because modern places come with better insulation, lifts and decent plumbing that just work. |
| 2 | 老 建筑 有 一大堆 个性，当然，但 它们 常常 藏 着 穿堂风 和 昂贵的 维护 头疼。 | Old buildings have bags of character, sure, but they often hide draughts and pricey maintenance headaches. |
| 3 | 话虽如此，我 完全 懂 为什么 有些人 迷上 一个 更老的 家 的 魅力。 | Having said that, I totally get why some folk fall for the charm of an older home. |

> For me, a new building wins, mainly because modern places come with better insulation, lifts and decent plumbing that just work. Old buildings have bags of character, sure, but they often hide draughts and pricey maintenance headaches. Having said that, I totally get why some folk fall for the charm of an older home.

**P3 句型/模板**：`so crowded that building upwards is the only sensible option`（so…that + 动名词主语）· `mixed-use towers going up` · `getting rarer in big urban areas` · `landmarks meant to attract tourists`（分词后置）/ `money well spent` / `the city's calling card` · `splashing out on fancy facades feels wasteful`（动名词主语）/ `what's inside`（embedded）· `balance matters more than pure showing off` · `the inside being more important`（动名词复合）/ `that's where people actually spend their time` · `keeps mattering day after day` · `so first impressions aren't ruined` · `connecting with the past` · `step back in time` / `picture how earlier generations actually lived`（embedded）/ `which textbooks can't quite capture`（which后置）· `famous places they've only ever read about`（relative后置）· `that sense of awe is the real pull` · `Maintaining grand old buildings costs a fortune`（动名词主语）/ `repairs and upkeep that taxes alone couldn't`（that后置）· `charging too much would shut ordinary visitors out`（动名词主语）· `the right middle ground` · `come with better insulation … that just work`（that后置）· `bags of character` / `pricey maintenance headaches` · `I totally get why some folk fall for the charm of an older home`（embedded）。

## P2-老18 · A time you needed to use your imagination — 陪 Muye 搭乐高（Event）

> **Cue**: 何时 / 为何需要想象力 / 难还是易 / 感受 · 题型 Event

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 那么，我想聊的那个时刻 是 一个 周末，当 我儿子 Muye 把 我 拽 进 一场 乐高 环节。 | So, the time I'd like to talk about is one weekend when my son Muye dragged me into a LEGO session. |
| [why need] | 他 五岁，而 他 倒 出 整盒、然后 让 我 跟 他 一起 搭 某种 疯狂的东西，比如 一座 给 恐龙 的 城堡 或 一枚 火箭、（那种 变成 一条 龙的）。 | He's five, and he tipped out the whole box and asked me to build something wild with him, like a castle for dinosaurs or a rocket that turns into a dragon. |
| [why·细] | 没有 说明书 [去]跟，只有 他的 疯狂 主意，所以 我 得 在 我脑子里 想象 它、还 从零 设计 某种东西。 | There were no instructions to follow, just his crazy idea, so I had to picture it in my head and design something from scratch. |
| [difficulty] | 说实话，起初 它 比 我 预期的 更 难。 | Honestly, it was harder than I expected at first. |
| [difficulty·细] | 在 日常生活 我 那么 习惯 跟 清晰的 步骤，所以 自由地 想 感觉 有点 生锈，像 一块 肌肉、（我 好些年 没 用 过的）。 | In daily life I'm so used to following clear steps, so thinking freely felt a bit rusty, like a muscle I hadn't used in years. |
| [turn] | 但 一旦 我 放松 下来、开始 像 一个 小孩 那样 想，它 就 顺 了。 | But once I loosened up and started to think like a kid, it flowed. |
| [turn·细] | 在 我 反应过来 之前，我们 已经 搭 了 这个 摇摇晃晃的 龙-火箭、带 翅膀、（用 车轮 做的）。 | Before I knew it, we'd built this wobbly dragon-rocket with wings made of car wheels. |
| [feel/EL] | 我的意思是，它 感觉 真的 释放、真诚 好玩。 | What I mean is, it felt really freeing and genuinely fun. |
| [EL·细] | 它 提醒 我 我们 大人 多么 少有 机会 就 玩。 | It reminded me how rarely we adults get to just play. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，差不多 就是 这样。 | So yeah, that's pretty much it. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> So, the time I'd like to talk about is one weekend when my son Muye dragged me into a LEGO session. He's five, and he tipped out the whole box and asked me to build something wild with him, like a castle for dinosaurs or a rocket that turns into a dragon. There were no instructions to follow, just his crazy idea, so I had to picture it in my head and design something from scratch. Honestly, it was harder than I expected at first. In daily life I'm so used to following clear steps, so thinking freely felt a bit rusty, like a muscle I hadn't used in years. But once I loosened up and started to think like a kid, it flowed. Before I knew it, we'd built this wobbly dragon-rocket with wings made of car wheels. What I mean is, it felt really freeing and genuinely fun. It reminded me how rarely we adults get to just play. So yeah, that's pretty much it.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **time opener + when**：一个周末，当我儿子把我拽进乐高 → `one weekend when my son … dragged me into a LEGO session`
- **that 后置**：一枚变成龙的火箭 → `a rocket that turns into a dragon`
- **没说明书可跟**：没有说明书去跟，只有他的疯狂主意 → `There were no instructions to follow, just his crazy idea`
- **动名词主语 + relative后置**：自由地想感觉生锈，像我好些年没用的肌肉 → `thinking freely felt a bit rusty, like a muscle I hadn't used in years`
- **once … it flowed**：一旦我放松、像小孩那样想，它就顺了 → `once I loosened up and started to think like a kid, it flowed`
- **Before I knew it + 过去完成 + 分词后置**：我们已搭了带车轮做翅膀的龙火箭 → `Before I knew it, we'd built … with wings made of car wheels`
- **embedded**：它提醒我我们大人多么少有机会玩 → `how rarely we adults get to just play`

---

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. Do you think adults can have lots of imagination?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，虽然 我会说 一大堆 大人 让 它 褪去。 | Definitely, though I'd say a lot of adults let it fade. |
| 2 | 我们 那么 陷 在 例行 和 截止日期 里、以至于 我们 停止 做白日梦。 | We get so caught up in routines and deadlines that we stop daydreaming. |
| 3 | 但 那 想象力 仍然 在——你 看到 它、无论 何时 某人 讲 一个 好故事 或 用 一个 聪明的 方式 解决 一个 棘手的 问题。 | But the imagination's still there — you see it whenever someone tells a good story or solves a tricky problem in a clever way. |
| 4 | 说实话，它 只 需要 一点 掸灰、时不时。 | Honestly, it just needs a bit of dusting off now and then. |

> Definitely, though I'd say a lot of adults let it fade. We get so caught up in routines and deadlines that we stop daydreaming. But the imagination's still there — you see it whenever someone tells a good story or solves a tricky problem in a clever way. Honestly, it just needs a bit of dusting off now and then.

**Q2. Do you think imagination is essential for scientists?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，我觉得 它 在 他们 做的事 的 核心。 | Absolutely, I reckon it's at the heart of what they do. |
| 2 | 在 你 能 检验 一个 理论 之前，你 得 先 想象 它——想象 那世界 可能 怎么 运作。 | Before you can test a theory, you've got to imagine it first — picture how the world might work. |
| 3 | 拿 爱因斯坦 来说，（据说 在 他脑子里 构想 出 整个 实验的）。 | Take Einstein, who supposedly dreamed up whole experiments in his head. |
| 4 | 没有 那 火花，科学 会 只是 抄 旧 结果，而 没有 什么 真正 新的 会 从 它 出来。 | Without that spark, science would just be copying old results, and nothing genuinely new would ever come out of it. |

> Absolutely, I reckon it's at the heart of what they do. Before you can test a theory, you've got to imagine it first — picture how the world might work. Take Einstein, who supposedly dreamed up whole experiments in his head. Without that spark, science would just be copying old results, and nothing genuinely new would ever come out of it.

**Q3. What kinds of jobs need imagination?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 一大堆 创意的，我会说。 | Loads of creative ones, I'd say. |
| 2 | 设计师、作家、和 建筑师 明显 靠 想象力 活——他们 每天 从 无 造 出 某种东西。 | Designers, writers and architects clearly live on imagination — they're building something from nothing every day. |
| 3 | 但 它 不 只是 那些 明显的 工作。甚至 一个 好老师 需要 它 [去]用 一个 新鲜的 方式 解释 一个 难 想法。 | But it's not just the obvious jobs. Even a good teacher needs it to explain a hard idea in a fresh way. |
| 4 | 说实话，任何 角色、（那里 你 有创意地 解决 问题的）挺 依赖 它。 | Honestly, any role where you solve problems creatively leans on it a fair bit. |

> Loads of creative ones, I'd say. Designers, writers and architects clearly live on imagination — they're building something from nothing every day. But it's not just the obvious jobs. Even a good teacher needs it to explain a hard idea in a fresh way. Honestly, any role where you solve problems creatively leans on it a fair bit.

**Q4. What subjects are helpful for children's imagination?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 会 先 指 艺术 和 创意 写作，因为 它们 基本上 奖励 孩子 [为了] 自由 发明。 | I'd point to art and creative writing first, since they basically reward kids for inventing freely. |
| 2 | 但 说句公道话，甚至 像 科学 这样的 学科 有帮助，因为 它们 推 孩子 [去]问 "要是……呢"、还 想象 各种 可能。 | But to be fair, even subjects like science help, because they push children to ask "what if" and picture possibilities. |
| 3 | 戏剧 也 很棒——假装 是 别人 真的 拉伸 那 头脑。 | Drama's brilliant too — pretending to be someone else really stretches the mind. |
| 4 | 所以 它 更少 关于 一门 学科、更多 关于 它 怎么 被 教。 | So it's less about one subject and more about how it's taught. |

> I'd point to art and creative writing first, since they basically reward kids for inventing freely. But to be fair, even subjects like science help, because they push children to ask "what if" and picture possibilities. Drama's brilliant too — pretending to be someone else really stretches the mind. So it's less about one subject and more about how it's taught.

**Q5. What games help develop children's imagination?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 开放式的 最 有效，我会说——积木、角色扮演、或 就 一个 纸板箱、（那种 变成 一艘 飞船的）。 | Open-ended ones work best, I'd say — building blocks, role-play, or just a cardboard box that becomes a spaceship. |
| 2 | 因为 没有 固定的 规则，孩子 得 自己 填 那些 空白。 | Since there's no fixed rule, kids have to fill the gaps themselves. |
| 3 | 说实话，那 玩具 越 简单，他们的 脑子 干 得 越 卖力。 | Honestly, the simpler the toy, the harder their minds work. |
| 4 | 那 就是 为什么 我 有点 警惕 花哨的 小设备、（那种 替 他们 做 所有的 想象的）。 | That's why I'm a bit wary of fancy gadgets that do all the imagining for them. |

> Open-ended ones work best, I'd say — building blocks, role-play, or just a cardboard box that becomes a spaceship. Since there's no fixed rule, kids have to fill the gaps themselves. Honestly, the simpler the toy, the harder their minds work. That's why I'm a bit wary of fancy gadgets that do all the imagining for them.

**Q6. How important is imagination to children?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 极其 重要，说实话。 | Hugely important, honestly. |
| 2 | 对 孩子，想象力 不 只是 玩——它 是 他们 理解 一个 世界、（他们 还 没 完全 领会的）的 方式。 | For kids, imagination isn't just play — it's how they make sense of a world they don't fully grasp yet. |
| 3 | 假装 建立 同理心，因为 他们 在 踏 进 其他 角色，而 它 也 激发 解决问题。 | Pretending builds empathy, since they're stepping into other roles, and it sparks problem-solving too. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，一个 没有 它 的 童年 会 感觉 挺 灰暗，而 他们 会 挣扎 [去]后来 有创意地 想。 | To be fair, a childhood without it would feel pretty grey, and they'd struggle to think creatively later on. |

> Hugely important, honestly. For kids, imagination isn't just play — it's how they make sense of a world they don't fully grasp yet. Pretending builds empathy, since they're stepping into other roles, and it sparks problem-solving too. To be fair, a childhood without it would feel pretty grey, and they'd struggle to think creatively later on.

**P3 句型/模板**：`let it fade` · `get so caught up in routines and deadlines that we stop daydreaming`（so…that）· `solves a tricky problem in a clever way` · `a bit of dusting off now and then` · `at the heart of what they do`（embedded）· `picture how the world might work`（embedded）· `Einstein, who supposedly dreamed up whole experiments in his head`（who后置）· `Without that spark, science would just be copying old results` · `live on imagination` / `building something from nothing` · `explain a hard idea in a fresh way` · `any role where you solve problems creatively leans on it`（where后置）· `reward kids for inventing freely` · `push children to ask "what if" and picture possibilities` · `pretending to be someone else really stretches the mind`（动名词主语）· `a cardboard box that becomes a spaceship`（that后置）· `fill the gaps themselves` · `the simpler the toy, the harder their minds work`（the-more）· `fancy gadgets that do all the imagining for them`（that后置）· `how they make sense of a world they don't fully grasp yet`（embedded+relative后置）· `Pretending builds empathy`（动名词主语）/ `stepping into other roles`。

## P2-老19 · A person who often helps others — wife 帮邻居老人（Person）

> **Cue**: 是谁 / 多常帮 / 怎么/为何帮 / 你的感受 · 题型 Person

### ①P2 拆解（英式中文珠子 → 范文英文）

| 句/功能 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [opener] | 我想聊的那个人 是 我的 妻子。 | The person I'd like to talk about is my wife. |
| [背景] | 她 做 一个 公务员、就 在 成都 这儿，但 真正 定义 她的 是 她 一直 有 多 善良。 | She works as a civil servant here in Chengdu, but what really defines her is just how kind-hearted she's always been. |
| [how often] | 她 差不多 一直 帮 人，但 那个 突出的 是 一位 年长的 女士、（住 楼下的）。 | She helps people pretty much all the time, but the one that stands out is an elderly lady living downstairs. |
| [how often·细] | 一周 几次，我妻子 会 特意 [去]探望 她——它 现在 只是 她 日常 的 一部分。 | A few times a week, my wife will go out of her way to check in on her — it's just part of her routine now. |
| [how/why] | 比如，她 会 为 她 买 杂货 和 药，而 她 无穷 耐心 地 教 那 老太太 怎么 用 她的 智能手机。 | For example, she'll pick up groceries and medicine for her, and she's endlessly patient teaching the old lady how to use her smartphone. |
| [why·细] | 她 为什么 做 它？基本上，她 没法 走过 一个 需要 一把手 的人——她 被 养成 [去]照看 别人，没有 一秒 犹豫。 | Why does she do it? Basically, she can't walk past someone who needs a hand — she was raised to look out for others, without a second thought. |
| [feel] | 说实话，我 感到 真的 为 她 骄傲，还 有点 自愧不如。 | Honestly, I feel really proud of her, and a bit humbled too. |
| [feel·细] | 她 安静地 做 这一切，不 期待 任何 回报。 | She does all this quietly, without expecting anything back. |
| [closer] | 所以，对，她的心 真的 在 对的 地方，而 那 就是 她 是 那种人。 | So yeah, her heart's truly in the right place, and that's the kind of person she is. |

### ②P2 整段（shadow）

> The person I'd like to talk about is my wife. She works as a civil servant here in Chengdu, but what really defines her is just how kind-hearted she's always been. She helps people pretty much all the time, but the one that stands out is an elderly lady living downstairs. A few times a week, my wife will go out of her way to check in on her — it's just part of her routine now. For example, she'll pick up groceries and medicine for her, and she's endlessly patient teaching the old lady how to use her smartphone. Why does she do it? Basically, she can't walk past someone who needs a hand — she was raised to look out for others, without a second thought. Honestly, I feel really proud of her, and a bit humbled too. She does all this quietly, without expecting anything back. So yeah, her heart's truly in the right place, and that's the kind of person she is.

### 句型/模板（可搬走）

- **what 主语 + embedded**：真正定义她的是她一直有多善良 → `what really defines her is just how kind-hearted she's always been`
- **the one that stands out is … + 分词后置**：那个突出的是一位住楼下的老太太 → `the one that stands out is an elderly lady living downstairs`
- **特意去做**：她会特意去探望她 → `go out of her way to check in on her`
- **修辞问句 + 答**：她为什么做？基本上她没法走过需要帮手的人 → `Why does she do it? Basically, she can't walk past someone who needs a hand`
- **不图回报**：她安静地做，不期待任何回报 → `without expecting anything back`
- **收尾**：她的心真的在对的地方 → `her heart's truly in the right place`

### ①P3 拆解 + ②整段

**Q1. What can children do to help their parents?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 相当 多，说实话。 | Quite a lot, honestly. |
| 2 | 更小的 孩子 能 收拾 他们的房间 或 摆 桌子，而 青少年 能 做 简单的 饭 或 跑 腿。 | Younger kids can tidy their rooms or set the table, while teenagers can cook simple meals or run errands. |
| 3 | 我会说 甚至 小 举动 也 算，因为 它们 向 父母 显示 那 努力 是 真诚的。 | I'd say even small gestures count, because they show parents the effort is genuine. |
| 4 | 比如，只是 记得 去 倒 垃圾、没 被 要求，就 能 真的 减轻 一个 父母 的 负担。 | For instance, just remembering to take the bins out without being asked can really lighten a parent's load. |

> Quite a lot, honestly. Younger kids can tidy their rooms or set the table, while teenagers can cook simple meals or run errands. I'd say even small gestures count, because they show parents the effort is genuine. For instance, just remembering to take the bins out without being asked can really lighten a parent's load.

**Q2. Should children help their parents with household chores?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 绝对，他们 应该。 | Absolutely, they should. |
| 2 | 我觉得 家务 教 孩子 责任、还 阻止 他们 假设 饭 和 干净衣服 就 变魔术般 出现。 | I reckon chores teach kids responsibility and stop them assuming meals and clean clothes just appear by magic. |
| 3 | 它 也 是 一个 公平的 方式、[去]分担 那 负担，因为 一个 父母 不该 扛 一切。 | It's also a fair way to share the load, since one parent shouldn't carry everything. |
| 4 | 比如，一个孩子、（那种 早早 学会 洗碗的）后来 长成 一个 远远 更 能干的 成年人。 | For example, a child who learns to wash up early on grows into a far more capable adult later. |

> Absolutely, they should. I reckon chores teach kids responsibility and stop them assuming meals and clean clothes just appear by magic. It's also a fair way to share the load, since one parent shouldn't carry everything. For example, a child who learns to wash up early on grows into a far more capable adult later.

**Q3. What kind of help do people need when looking for a new job?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 相当 几种，真的。 | Quite a few kinds, really. |
| 2 | 他们 常常 需要 扎实的 建议、关于 写 一份 简历 或 应付 面试，因为 那些 技能 很快 生锈。 | They often need solid advice on writing a CV or handling interviews, since those skills get rusty fast. |
| 3 | 他们 也 需要 鼓励，因为 拒绝 打击 你的 自信。 | They also need encouragement, because rejection knocks your confidence. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，一个 私人 推荐 最 要紧——某人 为 你 担保 能 打开 一扇门、（那种 单靠 一份 精致的 申请 永远 打不开的）。 | To be fair, a personal referral matters most — someone vouching for you can open a door that a polished application alone never would. |

> Quite a few kinds, really. They often need solid advice on writing a CV or handling interviews, since those skills get rusty fast. They also need encouragement, because rejection knocks your confidence. To be fair, a personal referral matters most — someone vouching for you can open a door that a polished application alone never would.

**Q4. Who should people ask for help, colleagues or family members?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 它 真的 取决于 那 问题。 | It really depends on the problem. |
| 2 | 对 实际的、工作相关的 问题，我 会 问 同事，因为 他们 理解 那 背景、还能 给 亲手的 建议。 | For practical, work-related issues, I'd ask colleagues, since they understand the context and can offer hands-on tips. |
| 3 | 但 对 情感 支持 或 大 人生 决定，家人 赢，因为 他们 深深 了解 你、还 不会 评判 你。 | But for emotional support or big life decisions, family wins, because they know you deeply and won't judge you. |
| 4 | 所以 说实话，那 聪明的 一步 是 把 那 帮手 匹配 到 你 实际上 需要 的东西。 | So honestly, the smart move is matching the helper to what you actually need. |

> It really depends on the problem. For practical, work-related issues, I'd ask colleagues, since they understand the context and can offer hands-on tips. But for emotional support or big life decisions, family wins, because they know you deeply and won't judge you. So honestly, the smart move is matching the helper to what you actually need.

**Q5. Do you think schools should teach children to do household chores?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 我 觉得 他们 应该，在 某种程度上。 | I think they should, to some extent. |
| 2 | 学校 已经 在 一些 地方 教 做饭 或 基本 生活 技能，而 那 很棒，因为 不是 每个 家庭 都 费心 教 它们。 | Schools already teach cooking or basic life skills in some places, and that's brilliant, since not every family bothers to teach them. |
| 3 | 它 也 建立 独立。话虽如此，那 主要 责任 仍然 落 在 父母 身上、在家，所以 学校 该 只 巩固 那儿 已经 开始的东西。 | It builds independence too. Having said that, the main responsibility still sits with parents at home, so schools should just reinforce what's started there. |

> I think they should, to some extent. Schools already teach cooking or basic life skills in some places, and that's brilliant, since not every family bothers to teach them. It builds independence too. Having said that, the main responsibility still sits with parents at home, so schools should just reinforce what's started there.

**Q6. Why are employees reluctant to ask their managers for help?**

| 句 | 英式中文珠子 | 范文英文 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 大多是 自尊 和 恐惧，我会说。 | Mostly it's pride and fear, I'd say. |
| 2 | 人们 担心 问 让 他们 看起来 无能，所以 他们 宁愿 安静地 挣扎、也不 冒 那 印象 的 险。 | People worry that asking makes them look incompetent, so they'd rather struggle quietly than risk that impression. |
| 3 | 还 有 那 权力 差距，因为 经理 控制 晋升 和 评估。 | There's also the power gap, since managers control promotions and reviews. |
| 4 | 说句公道话，一个 支持性的 老板、（那种 承认 他们 自己 错误的）能 真的 随时间 打破 那堵 墙。 | To be fair, a supportive boss who admits their own mistakes can really break down that wall over time. |

> Mostly it's pride and fear, I'd say. People worry that asking makes them look incompetent, so they'd rather struggle quietly than risk that impression. There's also the power gap, since managers control promotions and reviews. To be fair, a supportive boss who admits their own mistakes can really break down that wall over time.

**P3 句型/模板**：`tidy their rooms or set the table` / `run errands` · `even small gestures count` · `remembering to take the bins out without being asked` / `lighten a parent's load` · `stop them assuming meals … just appear by magic` · `a fair way to share the load` · `a child who learns to wash up early on`（who后置）· `solid advice on writing a CV or handling interviews` / `get rusty fast` · `rejection knocks your confidence` · `someone vouching for you`（动名词）/ `a door that a polished application alone never would`（that后置）· `hands-on tips` / `family wins` · `matching the helper to what you actually need`（动名词主语+embedded）· `not every family bothers to teach them` · `reinforce what's started there`（what宾语）· `asking makes them look incompetent`（动名词主语）/ `they'd rather struggle quietly than risk that impression` · `There's also the power gap` · `a supportive boss who admits their own mistakes`（who后置）/ `break down that wall over time`。
